# Glossary

Every ML term in the notes, in plain English, with its Home (the section that teaches it) and the Concept it belongs to. Each term has a fixed ID (G-118): Notes cite it right after the term, as in "**learning rate** (G-118)". IDs never change; new terms take the next free number.

| ID | Term | Meaning | Home | Concept |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| <span id="G-1">G-1</span> | $1/(1-\beta)$ | Roughly how many recent values a running average that favours new values (the EWMA) covers: about 10 for $\beta = 0.9$. | [Note DL-033](DL/03-optimizers/DL-033-exponentially-weighted-moving-average/DL-033-exponentially-weighted-moving-average.md#5-the-effect-of-beta) | Exponentially weighted moving average (EWMA) |
| <span id="G-3">G-3</span> | $\alpha$ | The share of confidence intervals that miss the true value they are meant to catch (the parameter). It equals one minus the confidence level. | [Note MA-035](MA/04-inference/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure.md#7-the-z-procedure-formula) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-2">G-2</span> | $\alpha$ (Pareto shape) | The number that sets how fat the tail of a Pareto distribution is (its shape parameter, also called the tail index). A larger $\alpha$ gives a thinner tail and fewer extreme values. | [Note MA-030](MA/03-distributions/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law.md#31-parameters) | Pareto distribution and power laws |
| <span id="G-5">G-5</span> | $\beta$ | The chance that a test misses a real effect, failing to reject a false $H_0$ (the probability of a Type II error). $1 - \beta$ is the test's power. | [Note MA-040](MA/04-inference/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails.md#2-type-i-and-type-ii-errors) | Type I and II errors, power, tails |
| <span id="G-4">G-4</span> | $\beta$ (beta) | The number between 0 and 1 that sets how much weight a running average (the EWMA) keeps on the past; usually 0.9 in deep learning. | [Note DL-033](DL/03-optimizers/DL-033-exponentially-weighted-moving-average/DL-033-exponentially-weighted-moving-average.md#4-the-formula) | Exponentially weighted moving average (EWMA) |
| <span id="G-6">G-6</span> | $\beta_1$, $\beta_2$ | Two numbers between 0 and 1 that set how much of the past Adam's two running averages keep (its decay factors): $\beta_1$ for the average of gradients $m_t$, $\beta_2$ for the average of squared gradients $v_t$. Defaults 0.9 and 0.999. | [Note DL-038](DL/03-optimizers/DL-038-adam/DL-038-adam.md#4-the-update-rule) | Adam |
| <span id="G-7">G-7</span> | $\binom{n}{k}$ ($n$ choose $k$) | The number of ways to choose $k$ items out of $n$. | [Note MA-021](MA/03-distributions/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf.md#72-binomial-distribution) | Bernoulli and binomial distributions |
| <span id="G-8">G-8</span> | $\delta$ (Huber) | The error size at which Huber loss switches from squaring the error to taking its absolute value. It is a setting chosen before training (a hyperparameter). | [Note DL-014](DL/01-basics/DL-014-dl-loss-functions/DL-014-dl-loss-functions.md#72-the-formula) | Huber loss |
| <span id="G-9">G-9</span> | $\epsilon$ (epsilon) | A tiny number added to the variance to avoid dividing by 0; 0.001 in Keras. | [Note DL-031](DL/02-training/DL-031-batch-normalization/DL-031-batch-normalization.md#43-step-1-normalise-with-the-batchs-statistics) | Batch normalisation |
| <span id="G-10">G-10</span> | $\gamma$ (gain) and $\beta$ (shift) | Two numbers per feature that layer normalisation learns in training: after the values are normalised, $\gamma$ rescales them and $\beta$ shifts them. | [Note DL-080](DL/06-transformers/DL-080-layer-normalization/DL-080-layer-normalization.md#61-how-it-works) | Layer normalisation |
| <span id="G-11">G-11</span> | $\gamma$ (scale) and $\beta$ (shift) | The two learnable parameters per node of a batch normalisation layer: after normalising, the layer multiplies by $\gamma$ and adds $\beta$, so the network can choose its own mean and spread; they start at 1 and 0 in Keras. | [Note DL-031](DL/02-training/DL-031-batch-normalization/DL-031-batch-normalization.md#44-step-2-scale-and-shift-with-gamma-and-beta) | Batch normalisation |
| <span id="G-12">G-12</span> | $\hat{\mu}$ | An estimate of the population mean $\mu$ (the hat marks an estimate): a value computed from samples, here the mean of the sample means, used in place of the unknown $\mu$. | [Note MA-034](MA/04-inference/MA-034-estimating-a-mean-with-the-clt/MA-034-estimating-a-mean-with-the-clt.md#5-the-point-estimate) | Central limit theorem |
| <span id="G-13">G-13</span> | $\int_a^b f(x)\thinspace dx$ | The area under $f$ from $a$ to $b$; for a PDF, $P(a \le X \le b)$. | [Note MA-022](MA/03-distributions/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf.md#3-area-under-the-curve-is-probability) | Probability density function (PDF) |
| <span id="G-14">G-14</span> | $\partial L/\partial b_1$ | How much the loss changes when the convolution layer's bias $b_1$ is nudged (the gradient of the loss with respect to $b_1$), used to update that bias. Because the one bias is added to every cell of the feature map, it is the sum of the feature map's gradient over all its cells. | [Note DL-048](DL/04-cnn/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers.md#81-the-bias-a-sum) | Backpropagation in a CNN |
| <span id="G-15">G-15</span> | $\partial L/\partial W_1$ | How much the loss changes when each filter weight in $W_1$ is nudged (the gradient of the loss with respect to $W_1$), used to update the filter. It is found by sliding the feature map's gradient over the input like a filter, a convolution of the input with that gradient. | [Note DL-048](DL/04-cnn/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers.md#82-the-filter-a-convolution) | Backpropagation in a CNN |
| <span id="G-16">G-16</span> | $\partial L/\partial z$ | How much the loss changes when a node's weighted sum $z$ changes (the derivative of the loss with respect to $z$). Every weight entering the node multiplies it by its own input. | [Note DL-016](DL/01-basics/DL-016-backpropagation-how/DL-016-backpropagation-how.md#73-student-1-in-numbers) | Backpropagation |
| <span id="G-17">G-17</span> | $\partial L/\partial Z_2 = a_2 - y$ | How much the loss changes with the output's weighted sum $Z_2$ (its gradient): for a sigmoid output with log loss it simplifies to prediction minus target, the error. It then gives the slope of every output weight and bias. | [Note DL-047](DL/04-cnn/DL-047-backpropagation-in-cnn/DL-047-backpropagation-in-cnn.md#6-the-ann-part-partial-lpartial-w_2-and-partial-lpartial-b_2) | Backpropagation in a CNN |
| <span id="G-18">G-18</span> | $\Phi(z)$ | The probability that a standard normal value is below $z$: the area under the bell curve to the left of $z$ (the CDF of the standard normal distribution). For example $\Phi(1) = 0.84$. | [Note MA-025](MA/03-distributions/MA-025-standard-normal-and-z-table/MA-025-standard-normal-and-z-table.md#2-the-standard-normal-distribution) | Standard normal and the z-table |
| <span id="G-19">G-19</span> | $\phi(z)$ | The height of the standard normal bell curve (mean 0, standard deviation 1) at $z$, which shows how densely values lie near $z$ (its probability density function). | [Note MA-025](MA/03-distributions/MA-025-standard-normal-and-z-table/MA-025-standard-normal-and-z-table.md#2-the-standard-normal-distribution) | Standard normal and the z-table |
| <span id="G-20">G-20</span> | $\text{Lognormal}(\mu, \sigma^2)$ | A short way to write a log-normal distribution: positive values with a long tail to the right (right-skewed), whose logarithm is normal, $\ln X \sim N(\mu, \sigma^2)$. | [Note MA-029](MA/03-distributions/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal.md#31-notation-and-parameters) | Log-normal distribution |
| <span id="G-21">G-21</span> | $\theta$ (theta) | One symbol for all the parameters of a model. | [Note MA-070](MA/08-likelihood/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation.md#71-one-symbol-for-all-parameters) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-22">G-22</span> | $A^{\mathsf T}A$ | The matrix $A$ transposed times $A$, used to find the SVD by hand: it is symmetric, its eigenvectors are the right singular vectors of $A$ and its eigenvalues are the squared singular values. | [Note MA-058](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-058-computing-the-svd/MA-058-computing-the-svd.md#2-making-one-orthogonal-matrix-disappear) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-23">G-23</span> | $b_{ij}$ | The number added to the weighted sum of node $j$ in layer $i$ before the activation (its bias), which lets the node shift its output. | [Note DL-008](DL/01-basics/DL-008-mlp-notation/DL-008-mlp-notation.md#41-biases) | MLP notation and parameter count |
| <span id="G-24">G-24</span> | $d_k$ | The length of each key vector (and query vector); attention divides its scores by $\sqrt{d_k}$ so that long vectors do not give scores so spread out that the softmax puts almost all the weight on one word. | [Note DL-075](DL/06-transformers/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention.md#1-overview) | Scaled dot-product attention |
| <span id="G-25">G-25</span> | $d_k$, $d_v$ | The size of each head's query and key vectors ($d_k$) and value vectors ($d_v$); $d_{\text{model}}/h = 64$ in the transformer. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#6-multi-head-attention-in-the-transformer) | Multi-head attention |
| <span id="G-26">G-26</span> | $d_{\text{ff}}$ | The width of the hidden layer of the transformer's feed-forward network: each word's 512-number vector is widened to $d_{\text{ff}} = 2048$ numbers, passed through ReLU, then brought back to 512. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#53-the-feed-forward-network) | Transformer encoder |
| <span id="G-27">G-27</span> | $d_{\text{model}}$ | The number of values in each word's vector at the layer's input and output; 512 in the transformer. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#6-multi-head-attention-in-the-transformer) | Multi-head attention |
| <span id="G-28">G-28</span> | $h$ | The number of attention heads in multi-head attention: self-attentions run in parallel, each with its own view of the sentence; $h = 8$ in the transformer, each head of size $d_{\text{model}}/h = 64$. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#6-multi-head-attention-in-the-transformer) | Multi-head attention |
| <span id="G-29">G-29</span> | $h_t^{(l)}$ | In a deep RNN, layer $l$'s summary of the sequence up to time step $t$ (its hidden state); it feeds both the next time step and the layer above. | [Note DL-065](DL/05-rnn/DL-065-deep-rnns/DL-065-deep-rnns.md#5-notation-time-axis-and-depth-axis) | Deep (stacked) RNNs |
| <span id="G-30">G-30</span> | $H_{\text{enc}}$ | The output of the last encoder block: one vector per input word, read by every decoder block. | [Note DL-084](DL/06-transformers/DL-084-transformer-decoder/DL-084-transformer-decoder.md#4-the-setup-one-training-pair) | Transformer decoder |
| <span id="G-32">G-32</span> | $N(\mu, \sigma^2)$ | Notation for a normal distribution, the symmetric bell curve, with mean $\mu$ (its centre) and variance $\sigma^2$ (its spread squared); $X \sim N(68, 3^2)$ reads: $X$ is normal with mean 68 and standard deviation 3. | [Note MA-024](MA/03-distributions/MA-024-normal-distribution/MA-024-normal-distribution.md#3-parameters-and-notation) | Normal distribution |
| <span id="G-33">G-33</span> | $O_{ij}$ | The one number that node $j$ in layer $i$ produces (its output), passed as input to every node of the next layer. | [Note DL-008](DL/01-basics/DL-008-mlp-notation/DL-008-mlp-notation.md#42-outputs) | MLP notation and parameter count |
| <span id="G-34">G-34</span> | $P(X = x)$ | The probability that the random variable $X$ takes the value $x$. | [Note MA-021](MA/03-distributions/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf.md#3-the-probability-mass-function) | Probability mass function (PMF) |
| <span id="G-35">G-35</span> | $P(X = x, Y = y)$ | The joint probability that $X$ takes the value $x$ and $Y$ the value $y$ together. | [Note MA-014](MA/02-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability.md#2-joint-probability) | Joint and marginal probability |
| <span id="G-36">G-36</span> | $V_0$ | The value a running average (the EWMA) starts from: 0, or the first value $\theta_1$. | [Note DL-033](DL/03-optimizers/DL-033-exponentially-weighted-moving-average/DL-033-exponentially-weighted-moving-average.md#41-where-to-start-v_0) | Exponentially weighted moving average (EWMA) |
| <span id="G-37">G-37</span> | $W_{ij}^{k}$ | The weight entering layer $k$, from node $i$ of layer $k-1$ to node $j$ of layer $k$. | [Note DL-008](DL/01-basics/DL-008-mlp-notation/DL-008-mlp-notation.md#5-naming-weights) | MLP notation and parameter count |
| <span id="G-38">G-38</span> | $w_0$ | The fixed number in the equation of a line, plane or hyperplane (the constant term). It shifts the hyperplane away from the origin, and is 0 when the hyperplane passes through the origin. | [Note MA-051](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-051-equation-of-a-hyperplane/MA-051-equation-of-a-hyperplane.md#1-overview) | Equation of a hyperplane |
| <span id="G-39">G-39</span> | $W_i$, $W_h$, $W_o$ | The three weight matrices of an RNN: $W_i$ multiplies the current input, $W_h$ multiplies the hidden state fed back from the previous time step, and $W_o$ turns the hidden state into the output. | [Note DL-056](DL/05-rnn/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation.md#43-the-network-for-our-reviews) | Recurrent neural network (RNN) |
| <span id="G-40">G-40</span> | $W_O$ | The learned output matrix that mixes the concatenated head outputs back to $d_{\text{model}}$ numbers per word. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#53-concatenate-then-mix-with-w_o) | Multi-head attention |
| <span id="G-41">G-41</span> | $W_Q$, $W_K$, $W_V$ | The learned matrices that turn an embedding into its query, key and value vectors; the same for every word. | [Note DL-074](DL/06-transformers/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step.md#1-overview) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-42">G-42</span> | $W_{\text{up}}$, $W_{\text{down}}$ | The two learned matrices of the MLP block: $W_{\text{up}}$ first widens each vector, $W_{\text{down}}$ then narrows it back. In GPT-2 they are named `c_fc` and `c_proj`. | [Note DL-089](DL/06-transformers/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts.md#3-the-mlp-block-in-one-formula) | MLP blocks as fact storage |
| <span id="G-43">G-43</span> | $x_m$ (Pareto) | The minimum possible value, where the Pareto curve starts and peaks. | [Note MA-030](MA/03-distributions/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law.md#31-parameters) | Pareto distribution and power laws |
| <span id="G-44">G-44</span> | $X_{dec}$ | The decoder's numbers for the output sentence (its representation), one row per output position, which go into cross-attention. | [Note DL-083](DL/06-transformers/DL-083-cross-attention/DL-083-cross-attention.md#4-input-two-sequences-instead-of-one) | Cross-attention |
| <span id="G-45">G-45</span> | $Y \sim \text{Po}(\lambda)$ | Notation: $Y$ follows a Poisson distribution with rate $\lambda$; likewise $\text{Bern}(p)$ and $B(n, p)$. | [Note MA-032](MA/03-distributions/MA-032-poisson-distribution/MA-032-poisson-distribution.md#22-notation) | Poisson distribution |
| <span id="G-46">G-46</span> | .dt accessor | The pandas tool that applies date and time methods to every value of a datetime column. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#5-extracting-parts-of-a-date) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-47">G-47</span> | .str accessor | The pandas tool that applies a text method to every value of a column. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#51-the-number-part) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-48">G-48</span> | 0-1 loss | A loss that counts 1 for every misclassified point and 0 for every correct one. | [Note DL-006](DL/01-basics/DL-006-perceptron-loss/DL-006-perceptron-loss.md#51-count-the-mistakes) | Perceptron loss |
| <span id="G-2236">G-2236</span> | 10% condition | When sampling without replacement, keep the sample at most 10% of the population so the observations are roughly independent. | [Note MA-042](MA/04-inference/MA-042-one-sample-t-test/MA-042-one-sample-t-test.md#42-conditions-when-the-test-may-be-used) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-49">G-49</span> | 2D density plot | A plot that shows which pairs of values of two numerical features are common and which are rare (their joint density), usually as filled contours seen from above. | [Note MA-027](MA/03-distributions/MA-027-pdf-and-cdf-in-practice/MA-027-pdf-and-cdf-in-practice.md#4-2d-density-plots) | Probability density function (PDF) |
| <span id="G-50">G-50</span> | 3D scatter plot | A scatter plot with three numerical features on three axes, one point per observation, to see how the three relate at once. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#51-the-3d-scatter-plot) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-51">G-51</span> | 4-3-2-1 network | A neural network named by its layer sizes, input first: 4 inputs, hidden layers of 3 and 2 nodes, and 1 output; the name gives the architecture in one line. | [Note DL-008](DL/01-basics/DL-008-mlp-notation/DL-008-mlp-notation.md#21-numbering-the-layers) | MLP notation and parameter count |
| <span id="G-52">G-52</span> | 5% rule of thumb | Drop the incomplete rows (complete case analysis) only for columns missing less than about 5% of their values, so only a small part of the data is lost. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#6-when-to-use-complete-case-analysis) | Complete case analysis |
| <span id="G-53">G-53</span> | 68-95-99.7 rule (empirical rule) | In a column shaped like a bell curve (a normal distribution), about 68.3%, 95.4% and 99.7% of values lie within 1, 2 and 3 standard deviations of the mean. | [Note ML-041](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-041-outliers-zscore/ML-041-outliers-zscore.md#3-the-68-95-997-rule) | Z-score outlier method |
| <span id="G-54">G-54</span> | 80-20 rule (Pareto principle) | About 20% of the causes produce about 80% of the results, e.g. 20% of people hold 80% of the wealth. | [Note MA-030](MA/03-distributions/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law.md#21-the-80-20-rule) | Pareto distribution and power laws |
| <span id="G-55">G-55</span> | @ (matrix multiplication) | Python's operator for multiplying matrices and vectors. | [Note ML-054](ML/06-regression/ML-054-multiple-lr-code/ML-054-multiple-lr-code.md#41-fit) | Multiple linear regression |
| <span id="G-56">G-56</span> | `<start>` and `<end>` tokens | Special tokens that tell the decoder to begin writing and mark the end of the output. | [Note DL-068](DL/06-transformers/DL-068-encoder-decoder/DL-068-encoder-decoder.md#42-the-decoder) | Sequence-to-sequence (encoder-decoder) |
| <span id="G-57">G-57</span> | `[CLS]`, `[SEP]` | The special tokens BERT adds at the start and the end of every sentence. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#8-the-heads-of-a-trained-model) | Multi-head attention |
| <span id="G-58">G-58</span> | `add_indicator=True` | The `SimpleImputer` setting that fills the gaps and also adds columns marking which values were missing (missing indicators), in one step. | [Note ML-037](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample.md#64-the-shortcut-add_indicatortrue) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-59">G-59</span> | `Add` | A layer that adds tensors of the same shape element by element. | [Note DL-054](DL/04-cnn/DL-054-keras-functional-api/DL-054-keras-functional-api.md#43-a-skip-connection) | Skip connections |
| <span id="G-60">G-60</span> | `alternative` (scipy) | The argument of scipy's tests that sets which direction of difference the test looks for (the direction of $H_1$): `"two-sided"`, `"less"` or `"greater"`. | [Note MA-042](MA/04-inference/MA-042-one-sample-t-test/MA-042-one-sample-t-test.md#61-the-test) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-61">G-61</span> | `base_score` | XGBoost's starting prediction, which every tree then corrects; for classification it is given as a probability, which XGBoost turns into log-odds. | [Note ML-119](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-119-xgboost-classification/ML-119-xgboost-classification.md#11-the-same-in-code) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-62">G-62</span> | `batch_size=1` | Keras setting that updates the weights after every single row. | [Note DL-016](DL/01-basics/DL-016-backpropagation-how/DL-016-backpropagation-how.md#5-the-same-training-in-keras) | Batch size in Keras |
| <span id="G-63">G-63</span> | `batch_size` | Keras setting: the number of rows used for each update; $n$, 1 or in between gives batch, stochastic or mini-batch. | [Note DL-020](DL/02-training/DL-020-gradient-descent-in-neural-networks/DL-020-gradient-descent-in-neural-networks.md#1-overview) | Batch size in Keras |
| <span id="G-64">G-64</span> | `BatchNormalization` | The Keras layer that applies batch normalisation: placed after a hidden layer, it standardises that layer's values over each mini-batch and learns $\gamma$ and $\beta$, which makes training faster. | [Note DL-031](DL/02-training/DL-031-batch-normalization/DL-031-batch-normalization.md#7-batch-normalisation-in-keras) | Batch normalisation |
| <span id="G-65">G-65</span> | `BayesianRidge` | A linear regression that keeps its weights small (built-in shrinkage); the default model of `IterativeImputer`. | [Note ML-039](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice.md#73-when-to-stop) | Iterative imputation (MICE) |
| <span id="G-66">G-66</span> | `best_params_` | The best combination of settings found by `GridSearchCV`. | [Note ML-037](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample.md#74-the-result-where-the-fill-matters-house-prices) | Grid and random search |
| <span id="G-67">G-67</span> | `Bidirectional` | The Keras wrapper that makes any recurrent layer read the sequence both forwards and backwards (bidirectional). | [Note DL-066](DL/05-rnn/DL-066-bidirectional-rnn/DL-066-bidirectional-rnn.md#51-the-wrapper) | Bidirectional RNNs |
| <span id="G-68">G-68</span> | `build_model(hp)` | The function that builds and compiles one network, asking `hp` for each tuned value. | [Note DL-039](DL/03-optimizers/DL-039-keras-tuner/DL-039-keras-tuner.md#51-step-1-the-model-building-function) | Keras Tuner |
| <span id="G-69">G-69</span> | `bw_adjust` | seaborn's multiplier on the KDE bandwidth it picks itself (not the bandwidth): values above 1 give a smoother curve, values below 1 a bumpier one. | [Note MA-023](MA/03-distributions/MA-023-density-estimation-kde/MA-023-density-estimation-kde.md#54-kde-in-seaborn) | Kernel density estimation (KDE) |

| <span id="G-147">G-147</span> | `strategy="constant"` (SimpleImputer) | The `SimpleImputer` setting that fills every gap with `fill_value`. | [Note ML-036](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-036-missing-categorical-data/ML-036-missing-categorical-data.md#6-missing-category-imputation-on-real-data) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-148">G-148</span> | `strategy="most_frequent"` (SimpleImputer) | The `SimpleImputer` setting for mode imputation: `fit` learns each column's most frequent value and `transform` fills the gaps with it. | [Note ML-036](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-036-missing-categorical-data/ML-036-missing-categorical-data.md#54-mode-imputation-with-simpleimputer) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-149">G-149</span> | `strategy` (KBinsDiscretizer) | The `KBinsDiscretizer` parameter that chooses how the bin edges are set: uniform, quantile or kmeans binning. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#9-kbinsdiscretizer-in-scikit-learn) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-150">G-150</span> | `strategy` (SimpleImputer) | The `SimpleImputer` parameter choosing the fill rule: mean, median, most_frequent or constant. | [Note ML-035](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data.md#41-the-main-parameters) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-151">G-151</span> | `to_categorical` | Keras function that turns integer class labels into 0/1 vectors with a single 1 at the label's position (one-hot encoding). | [Note DL-063](DL/05-rnn/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction.md#52-one-hot-targets) | Next-word prediction with an LSTM |
| <span id="G-152">G-152</span> | `tol` | The size of change below which `IterativeImputer` stops early; default 0.001. | [Note ML-039](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice.md#82-the-main-parameters) | Iterative imputation (MICE) |
| <span id="G-153">G-153</span> | `trainable` | The Keras attribute that decides whether a layer's weights are updated in training. | [Note DL-053](DL/04-cnn/DL-053-transfer-learning/DL-053-transfer-learning.md#71-feature-extraction) | Transfer learning (feature extraction and fine-tuning) |
| <span id="G-154">G-154</span> | `use_causal_mask` | The argument of Keras' `MultiHeadAttention` that hides future tokens, so each token can attend only to itself and earlier tokens (the causal mask). | [Note DL-082](DL/06-transformers/DL-082-masked-self-attention/DL-082-masked-self-attention.md#71-keras-causal-mask) | Masked self-attention |
| <span id="G-155">G-155</span> | `valid` padding | No padding; the output shrinks to $n - f + 1$. | [Note DL-043](DL/04-cnn/DL-043-padding-and-strides/DL-043-padding-and-strides.md#43-valid-and-same-in-keras) | Padding and strides |
| <span id="G-156">G-156</span> | `validation_split` | The share of the training rows Keras holds back as a validation set. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#73-tracking-accuracy-and-a-validation-set) | Keras workflow |
| <span id="G-157">G-157</span> | A/B testing | Comparing an old and a new version, of a model or a web page, by showing each to a random half of the users at the same time, to see which one does better. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#102-ab-testing) | Beta and A/B testing |
| <span id="G-158">G-158</span> | Absolute position | A word's place counted from the start of the sentence. | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#4-first-idea-count-the-words) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-159">G-159</span> | Absolute value | A number's size without its sign. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#8-max-abs-scaling) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-160">G-160</span> | absolute_error | A regression-tree setting (criterion) that chooses splits by the average size of the errors (mean absolute error); its leaves predict the median, so outliers pull less. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#6-hyperparameters-of-a-regression-tree) | Regression trees |
| <span id="G-161">G-161</span> | Accumulator $v_t$ | The running record of squared gradients that divides the learning rate. | [Note DL-037](DL/03-optimizers/DL-037-rmsprop/DL-037-rmsprop.md#3-adagrads-problem-in-one-formula) | RMSProp |
| <span id="G-162">G-162</span> | Accuracy | The fraction of predictions that are correct. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#9-evaluating-the-model) | Accuracy |
| <span id="G-163">G-163</span> | Acquisition function | The rule that picks the next trial using the stand-in model of the score (the surrogate), such as expected improvement. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#33-informed-search-versus-blind-search) | Bayesian optimisation |
| <span id="G-164">G-164</span> | Activation ($a^{k}$) | The vector of outputs of layer $k$; $a^{0}$ is the input row. | [Note DL-010](DL/01-basics/DL-010-forward-propagation/DL-010-forward-propagation.md#42-the-product) | Forward propagation |
| <span id="G-165">G-165</span> | Activation function | The function that turns a node's weighted sum $z$ into its output, bringing it into a fixed range. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#33-the-activation-function) | Activation functions |
| <span id="G-166">G-166</span> | Active constraint | An inequality constraint that the answer sits right on (it holds with equality), because the unconstrained minimum breaks it; it then acts like an equality constraint, and its multiplier can be positive. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#5-inequality-constraints) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-167">G-167</span> | AdaBoost (Adaptive Boosting) | A boosting algorithm that trains simple models (weak learners) one after another on reweighted data, and combines them by a vote in which each model counts by its alpha weight. | [Note ML-109](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-109-adaboost-intuition/ML-109-adaboost-intuition.md#1-overview) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-168">G-168</span> | AdaGrad | An optimizer that gives each parameter its own learning rate, $\eta/(\sqrt{v_t}+\epsilon)$, where $v_t$ sums its past squared gradients. | [Note DL-036](DL/03-optimizers/DL-036-adagrad/DL-036-adagrad.md#1-overview) | AdaGrad |
| <span id="G-169">G-169</span> | Adam | An optimizer, the rule that updates a network's weights to reduce the loss: a variant of gradient descent that keeps running averages of past gradients and of their squares, giving each weight its own step size; fairly robust to its settings, so a common default. | [Note DL-038](DL/03-optimizers/DL-038-adam/DL-038-adam.md#4-the-update-rule) | Adam |
| <span id="G-170">G-170</span> | AdamW | Adam with weight decay (shrinking the weights a little) applied directly to the weights, not through the loss. | [Note DL-026](DL/02-training/DL-026-regularization-in-dl/DL-026-regularization-in-dl.md#6-why-the-weights-shrink-weight-decay) | L1 and L2 regularisation in neural networks |
| <span id="G-171">G-171</span> | Adaptive learning rate | A learning rate that changes during training according to the gradients seen so far. | [Note DL-036](DL/03-optimizers/DL-036-adagrad/DL-036-adagrad.md#1-overview) | AdaGrad |
| <span id="G-172">G-172</span> | Add and norm | Adds a sub-layer's input back to its output (a residual connection) and then layer-normalises the sum, so the signal and gradients pass through deep stacks and the numbers stay on one scale: LayerNorm(x + Sublayer(x)). | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#52-add-and-norm) | Residual connections and add & norm |
| <span id="G-173">G-173</span> | Addition rule (general addition rule) | The rule for the probability that $A$ or $B$ (or both) happens: add their probabilities and subtract the overlap so it is not counted twice, $P(A \cup B) = P(A) + P(B) - P(A \cap B)$; for mutually exclusive events the overlap is 0. | [Note MA-017](MA/02-probability/MA-017-mutually-exclusive-events/MA-017-mutually-exclusive-events.md#5-the-addition-rule-where-mutual-exclusivity-pays-off) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-174">G-174</span> | Additive modelling | Building a complex function as a sum of simple functions, each capturing part of what the others missed. | [Note ML-115](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths.md#32-break-the-function-into-parts) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-175">G-175</span> | Adjusted Rand index (ARI) | A score for how well a clustering matches the true groups: 1 for identical, about 0 for random. | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#112-compared-with-k-means) | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) |
| <span id="G-177">G-177</span> | Adjusted R² | A version of R² with a penalty for each feature the model uses, so it rises only when a new feature truly helps; plain R² can creep up even when the new feature is useless. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#72-the-formula) | Regression metrics |
| <span id="G-178">G-178</span> | Affine transformation | A linear transformation followed by a shift, $A\mathbf{x} + \mathbf{b}$. | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#73-neural-network-layers-and-pca) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-179">G-179</span> | Agent | The learner in reinforcement learning, such as a self-driving car or a game-playing program: it acts in an environment, gets rewards or punishments, and improves its rules for acting. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#51-agent-environment-policy-and-reward) | Reinforcement learning |
| <span id="G-180">G-180</span> | Agglomerative clustering | Grouping that starts with one cluster per point and repeatedly merges the closest pair (bottom-up hierarchical clustering). | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#4-two-kinds-of-hierarchical-clustering) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-181">G-181</span> | AgglomerativeClustering | scikit-learn's class for agglomerative clustering: given the number of clusters, the distance and the linkage, `fit_predict` merges points bottom-up and returns each point's cluster. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#10-agglomerativeclustering-in-scikit-learn) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-182">G-182</span> | Aggregate | One summary number (mean, median, maximum, ...) computed from a group of values. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#41-categorical-and-numerical-any-aggregate-per-category) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-183">G-183</span> | Aggregation (in an ensemble) | Combining the base models' predictions into one: mode for classes, mean for numbers. | [Note ML-099](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-099-bagging-intuition/ML-099-bagging-intuition.md#22-aggregation) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-184">G-184</span> | AGI (artificial general intelligence) | A machine with all the abilities of human intelligence. Does not exist yet. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#22-narrow-ai-and-general-ai) | Artificial intelligence |
| <span id="G-185">G-185</span> | AI winter | A period when funding and interest in AI collapse. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#31-the-perceptron-and-the-first-ai-winter) | History of deep learning |
| <span id="G-186">G-186</span> | Alert | In a pandas profiling report, a warning about a column that may need attention, such as many missing values; a question to check, not a verdict. | [Note ML-021](ML/02-getting-data/ML-021-pandas-profiling/ML-021-pandas-profiling.md#32-alerts) | Pandas Profiling |
| <span id="G-187">G-187</span> | AlexNet | A deep convolutional neural network, trained on GPUs, that won the ImageNet contest in 2012 with about 15% error against about 26% for the next best entry, starting the current wave of deep learning. | [Note DL-051](DL/04-cnn/DL-051-pretrained-models/DL-051-pretrained-models.md#53-alexnet-the-2012-winner) | Pretrained models and ImageNet |
| <span id="G-2164">G-2164</span> | Algorithm chooser | A flowchart from a problem's properties to suitable algorithms. | [Course map](00-course-map/00-course-map.md#5-the-algorithm-chooser) |  |
| <span id="G-191">G-191</span> | Alignment | Which input words each output word relates to; read from the cross-attention weights. | [Note DL-069](DL/06-transformers/DL-069-attention-mechanism/DL-069-attention-mechanism.md#8-seeing-the-alignment) | Attention mechanism |
| <span id="G-188">G-188</span> | Alignment grid | A plot of how much each output word draws on each input word (all attention weights $\alpha_{ij}$ of a sentence pair), output words against input words. | [Note DL-069](DL/06-transformers/DL-069-attention-mechanism/DL-069-attention-mechanism.md#8-seeing-the-alignment) | Attention mechanism |
| <span id="G-189">G-189</span> | Alignment model | The small feed-forward network that scores how well encoder state $h_j$ fits the previous decoder state $s_{i-1}$, giving the alignment score $e_{ij}$. | [Note DL-069](DL/06-transformers/DL-069-attention-mechanism/DL-069-attention-mechanism.md#62-let-a-neural-network-find-the-function) | Attention mechanism |
| <span id="G-190">G-190</span> | Alignment score $e_{ij}$ | How strongly encoder state $j$ fits decoder step $i$, as a raw score before the softmax turns the scores into attention weights. | [Note DL-069](DL/06-transformers/DL-069-attention-mechanism/DL-069-attention-mechanism.md#52-what-c_i-is) | Attention mechanism |
| <span id="G-192">G-192</span> | Alpha (model weight) | A base model's say in AdaBoost's final vote; larger when it made fewer mistakes. | [Note ML-109](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-109-adaboost-intuition/ML-109-adaboost-intuition.md#43-the-say-of-each-stump-alpha) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-193">G-193</span> | Alternative hypothesis ($H_1$, $H_a$) | The claim of an effect, difference or relationship: the statement that contradicts the null hypothesis $H_0$. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#4-the-alternative-hypothesis) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-2155">G-2155</span> | Ambiguity | How much the ensemble's members disagree: the spread of their predictions around their mean. It is the amount by which the vote's squared error beats the average member's. | [Note ML-098](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-098-voting-regressor/ML-098-voting-regressor.md#3-seeing-it-on-a-curve) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-195">G-195</span> | Anaconda | A free bundle (distribution) for data science that installs Python, hundreds of popular libraries and tools such as Jupyter in one go, with the conda package manager to keep them working together. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#21-what-anaconda-and-conda-are) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-194">G-194</span> | Anaconda Navigator | Anaconda's point-and-click window for environments and packages. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#24-navigator-and-spyder) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-196">G-196</span> | Analogy question | "a is to b as c is to ?", answered by the word closest to $c - a + b$. | [Note DL-072](DL/06-transformers/DL-072-meaning-as-direction/DL-072-meaning-as-direction.md#5-arithmetic-with-directions) | Meaning as direction in embedding space |
| <span id="G-197">G-197</span> | Analytic function | A function that can be written exactly as its Taylor series (an infinite sum of powers) near every point. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#64-a-polynomial-is-its-own-taylor-polynomial) | Taylor series |
| <span id="G-198">G-198</span> | AND gate | A rule that outputs "yes" only when all its inputs are "yes". | [Note DL-089](DL/06-transformers/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts.md#42-a-toy-fact-michael-jordan-plays-basketball) | MLP blocks as fact storage |
| <span id="G-199">G-199</span> | AND, OR | Logic functions: 1 when both inputs are 1 (AND), or when at least one is (OR). | [Note DL-007](DL/01-basics/DL-007-problem-with-perceptron/DL-007-problem-with-perceptron.md#3-three-tiny-datasets-and-or-and-xor) | Problem with the perceptron (XOR) |
| <span id="G-200">G-200</span> | Anderson-Darling test | A statistical test of whether data follows a given distribution, such as the normal; like the Shapiro-Wilk test, it decides with a p-value. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#6-is-a-feature-normally-distributed) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-201">G-201</span> | Anomaly detection | Finding rows that do not fit the pattern of the rest. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#34-anomaly-detection) | Anomaly detection |
| <span id="G-203">G-203</span> | ANOVA | A test of whether several groups have the same mean (analysis of variance). | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#1-overview) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-202">G-202</span> | ANOVA table | The standard table that sets out an ANOVA's results: for each source of variation (between groups, within groups) it lists the sum of squares, degrees of freedom, mean square, the F statistic and the p-value. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#52-f) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-204">G-204</span> | API (Application Programming Interface) | A service that returns data when our code asks for it; a website's API hands out its data on request. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#2-collecting-data) | APIs |
| <span id="G-205">G-205</span> | API key | A secret code that tells the API who is asking. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#41-the-api-key) | APIs |
| <span id="G-206">G-206</span> | API token | A secret file or key that lets a program use a website's API as us. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#8-kaggle-datasets-on-colab) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-207">G-207</span> | Approximate tree learning (histogram-based training) | Finding a tree split by cutting each feature into bins and trying only the bin edges instead of every value; the split may be a little worse, but training is much faster. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#84-approximate-split-finding-with-quantile-bins) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-208">G-208</span> | Arbitrary value imputation | Filling every gap with one fixed value that never occurs, such as 99 or $-1$. | [Note ML-035](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data.md#1-overview) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-209">G-209</span> | Architecture (of a network) | How a network's nodes are connected: how many, of what kind, and which connections. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#54-architectures-and-transfer-learning) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-210">G-210</span> | arg max | The value of the variable that makes an expression largest. | [Note ML-082](ML/07-classification/ML-082-naive-bayes-maths/ML-082-naive-bayes-maths.md#6-the-formula-and-the-map-rule) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-211">G-211</span> | Arg min (argmin) | The value of a variable that makes an expression smallest, written $\arg\min$. | [Note ML-115](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths.md#5-step-1-the-best-constant-is-the-mean) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-212">G-212</span> | argmax | The position of the largest value; on 10 class probabilities, the predicted class. | [Note DL-012](DL/01-basics/DL-012-mnist-ann/DL-012-mnist-ann.md#1-overview) | ANN for classification |
| <span id="G-214">G-214</span> | Array | A grid of numbers with any number of dimensions (1D a list, 2D a list of lists); the programming name for a tensor. In NumPy every tensor is an array, an `ndarray`. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#2-what-a-tensor-is) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-215">G-215</span> | Artificial Intelligence (AI) | The field of building machines that show intelligence. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#2-artificial-intelligence) | Artificial intelligence |
| <span id="G-216">G-216</span> | Artificial neural network (ANN) | The simplest neural network: neurons in layers, each layer connected to the next by weights. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#22-the-parts-of-a-neural-network) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-217">G-217</span> | ASIC | A chip custom-made for one job, such as the TPU. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#52-hardware) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-218">G-218</span> | Association rule learning | Finding items that tend to occur together. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#35-association-rule-learning) | Association rule learning |
| <span id="G-219">G-219</span> | Associativity | A property of matrix multiplication: in a product of three matrices, which pair is multiplied first does not change the result, because both groupings mean apply $C$, then $B$, then $A$; written $(AB)C = A(BC)$. | [Note MA-054](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition.md#6-grouping-does-not-matter) | Matrix multiplication as composition |
| <span id="G-220">G-220</span> | Assumption (of a model) | A condition the data must meet for the model's results to be reliable. | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#1-overview) | Assumptions of linear regression |
| <span id="G-221">G-221</span> | Asymptotic | Coming ever closer to a line without touching it, like the normal curve's tails and the x axis. | [Note MA-024](MA/03-distributions/MA-024-normal-distribution/MA-024-normal-distribution.md#2-what-the-normal-distribution-is) | Normal distribution |
| <span id="G-222">G-222</span> | Atomic value | A cell that holds one single piece of information, not several pushed into one place; tidy data needs it, because facts packed into one cell cannot be plotted, grouped or given to a model separately. | [Note ML-044](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting.md#51-tidy-data) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-226">G-226</span> | Attention | A mechanism that lets each output step weigh all input positions and focus on the useful ones. | [Note DL-069](DL/06-transformers/DL-069-attention-mechanism/DL-069-attention-mechanism.md#4-the-idea-look-back-at-the-input-while-writing) | Attention mechanism |
| <span id="G-223">G-223</span> | Attention score | A number that measures how strongly one word relates to another: the dot product of the first word's query with the second word's key. The softmax turns one word's scores into its attention weights. | [Note DL-074](DL/06-transformers/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step.md#42-softmax-turns-scores-into-weights) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-225">G-225</span> | Attention weight | How much one word draws on another word when building its new vector: an attention score after the softmax, so the weights of one word are non-negative and sum to 1. | [Note DL-074](DL/06-transformers/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step.md#42-softmax-turns-scores-into-weights) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-224">G-224</span> | Attention weight $\alpha_{ij}$ | How much encoder state $j$ counts at decoder step $i$; the weights of one step are non-negative and sum to 1. | [Note DL-069](DL/06-transformers/DL-069-attention-mechanism/DL-069-attention-mechanism.md#5-the-context-vector-at-each-step) | Attention mechanism |
| <span id="G-227">G-227</span> | Attentional hidden state $\tilde h_i$ | In Luong attention, the vector the output layer reads to predict the next word: the decoder state and the context vector joined and passed through a tanh layer, $\tilde h_i = \tanh(W_c[c_i; s_i])$. | [Note DL-070](DL/06-transformers/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention.md#52-the-current-state-and-the-context-on-the-output-side) | Luong (multiplicative) attention |
| <span id="G-228">G-228</span> | Attribute | An extra setting written inside an HTML opening tag as `name="value"`, such as a class; scrapers use attributes to pick out the right tags. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#4-html-tags-classes-and-nesting) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-229">G-229</span> | AUC | A single score for a classifier from 0.5 (random) to 1 (perfect): the area under its ROC curve. | [Note ML-077](ML/07-classification/ML-077-roc-auc/ML-077-roc-auc.md#5-auc-the-area-under-the-curve) | ROC curve and AUC |
| <span id="G-230">G-230</span> | Autocorrelation | When each residual is related to the one before it in row order (too high on one observation, too high on the next), as is common in time series; linear regression assumes there is none. | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#7-assumption-5-no-autocorrelation-of-the-residuals) | Assumptions of linear regression |
| <span id="G-231">G-231</span> | Autoencoder | A network with a narrow middle layer that learns to compress data and rebuild it. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#24-autoencoder) | Types of neural networks |
| <span id="G-232">G-232</span> | Automatic differentiation | Software that computes exact derivatives of a program by the chain rule on its elementary operations; reverse mode is backpropagation. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#10-preview-backpropagation-and-automatic-differentiation) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-233">G-233</span> | Autoregressive (causal) generation | Producing one output at a time, each chosen from the outputs already produced and fed back as input for the next. | [Note DL-082](DL/06-transformers/DL-082-masked-self-attention/DL-082-masked-self-attention.md#3-autoregressive-models) | Masked self-attention |
| <span id="G-234">G-234</span> | Autoregressive model | A model that generates a sequence one item at a time, each item conditioned on the items it generated before. | [Note DL-082](DL/06-transformers/DL-082-masked-self-attention/DL-082-masked-self-attention.md#3-autoregressive-models) | Masked self-attention |
| <span id="G-236">G-236</span> | Average linkage | A way to measure the distance between two clusters in hierarchical clustering: the mean of all distances between a point of one cluster and a point of the other; the closest two clusters are merged next. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#8-linkage-the-distance-between-two-clusters) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-237">G-237</span> | Average meaning | What a static embedding stores: the word's meaning averaged over all its uses in the training corpus. | [Note DL-073](DL/06-transformers/DL-073-what-is-self-attention/DL-073-what-is-self-attention.md#4-static-embeddings-hold-an-average-meaning) | Contextual embeddings |
| <span id="G-238">G-238</span> | Average pooling | Pooling that replaces each window of a feature map with the mean of its values, which shrinks the map; unlike max pooling, it fades strong values such as edges. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#81-max-average-and-l2-pooling) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-239">G-239</span> | Average record size | The memory one row takes, on average. | [Note ML-021](ML/02-getting-data/ML-021-pandas-profiling/ML-021-pandas-profiling.md#31-dataset-statistics) | Pandas Profiling |
| <span id="G-240">G-240</span> | Axioms of probability | The three rules every probability obeys: non-negative, $P(S) = 1$, mutually exclusive events add. | [Note MA-011](MA/02-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability.md#61-the-axioms-of-probability) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-242">G-242</span> | Axis | One direction along which a tensor's numbers are arranged, used to say which way an operation runs; a matrix has two axes, down the rows (axis 0) and across the columns (axis 1). | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#4-rank-axes-shape-and-size) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-241">G-241</span> | Axis of rotation | The line a 3D rotation leaves in place: an eigenvector with eigenvalue 1. | [Note MA-056](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues.md#21-why-eigenvectors-describe-a-transformation-well) | Eigenvectors and eigenvalues |
| <span id="G-243">G-243</span> | Axis-parallel split | A cut that tests one column, so it is a line, plane or hyperplane parallel to the other axes. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#32-lines-planes-hyperplanes) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-244">G-244</span> | B2B | Business to business: a product that helps a company run its business. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#22-b2c-and-b2b) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-245">G-245</span> | B2C | Business to customer: a product sold to ordinary users. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#22-b2c-and-b2b) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-247">G-247</span> | Backpropagation | The algorithm that computes the gradient of the loss with respect to every weight and bias by applying the chain rule backward from the output; gradient descent then uses these gradients to train the network. | [Note DL-015](DL/01-basics/DL-015-backpropagation-what/DL-015-backpropagation-what.md#1-overview) | Backpropagation |
| <span id="G-246">G-246</span> | Backpropagation through time (BPTT) | Backpropagation for an RNN: the chain rule runs back over the network unfolded in time, adding up the gradient from every time step at which a shared weight is used. | [Note DL-059](DL/05-rnn/DL-059-backpropagation-through-time/DL-059-backpropagation-through-time.md#1-overview) | Backpropagation through time (BPTT) |
| <span id="G-248">G-248</span> | Backward elimination | Feature selection that starts with all columns and removes the worst at a time. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#81-pixels-as-columns-the-mnist-dataset) | Feature selection |
| <span id="G-249">G-249</span> | Backward pass | Computing the gradient of the loss for every tensor, from the output back to the input. | [Note DL-048](DL/04-cnn/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers.md#1-overview) | Backpropagation in a CNN |
| <span id="G-250">G-250</span> | Bag of words | The simplest way to turn texts into feature vectors: list every unique word (the vocabulary), give each word one dimension, and count how often each word appears in the text. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#42-bag-of-words) | Bag of words |
| <span id="G-251">G-251</span> | Bagging (bootstrap aggregation) | Training many models on different random samples of the data and averaging them, so the result depends less on the particular sample (lower variance). | [Note ML-095](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-095-ensemble-learning/ML-095-ensemble-learning.md#43-bagging) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-252">G-252</span> | Bagging regressor | A bagging ensemble of regressors that predicts the mean of their predictions. | [Note ML-101](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-101-bagging-regressor/ML-101-bagging-regressor.md#1-overview) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-253">G-253</span> | BaggingClassifier | scikit-learn class for bagging, pasting, random subspaces and random patches in classification. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#3-baggingclassifier-in-code) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-254">G-254</span> | BaggingRegressor | scikit-learn class for bagging, pasting, random subspaces and random patches in regression. | [Note ML-101](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-101-bagging-regressor/ML-101-bagging-regressor.md#2-the-core-idea) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-255">G-255</span> | Bahdanau attention (additive) | Attention whose alignment score comes from a small network with one hidden layer, fed the previous decoder state and one encoder state, $v^\top \tanh(W_a s_{i-1} + U_a h_j)$; the score decides how much that encoder state counts. Called additive because the two terms are added inside the tanh. | [Note DL-070](DL/06-transformers/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention.md#42-the-alignment-network-step-by-step) | Bahdanau (additive) attention |
| <span id="G-256">G-256</span> | Balanced random forest | A random forest for imbalanced data: every tree is trained on a balanced sample, the minority observations plus the same number of random majority observations, and the trees then vote. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#8-ensemble-methods-the-balanced-random-forest) | Balanced random forest |
| <span id="G-257">G-257</span> | Bandwidth | How wide each kernel is in a KDE (for a Gaussian kernel, its standard deviation); it sets how smooth the estimated curve is. | [Note MA-023](MA/03-distributions/MA-023-density-estimation-kde/MA-023-density-estimation-kde.md#51-how-a-kde-is-built) | Kernel density estimation (KDE) |
| <span id="G-258">G-258</span> | Bar plot | A plot for comparing a numerical column across categories: one bar per category, its height the mean of the numerical column for that category. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#4-bar-plot-a-numerical-column-across-categories) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-259">G-259</span> | base environment | The environment the installer creates, holding conda itself. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#41-why-one-environment-per-project) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-260">G-260</span> | Base model | One of the models inside an ensemble; the ensemble combines the base models' answers into its prediction. | [Note ML-095](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-095-ensemble-learning/ML-095-ensemble-learning.md#3-how-an-ensemble-predicts) | Ensemble learning |
| <span id="G-261">G-261</span> | Base prediction ($F_0$) | The first model of gradient boosting; for regression, the mean of the target. | [Note ML-114](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-114-gradient-boosting-intuition/ML-114-gradient-boosting-intuition.md#4-stage-1-the-mean) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-2270">G-2270</span> | Baseline | A simple rule whose score a model must beat to show it has learned something. | [Note DL-063](DL/05-rnn/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction.md#72-comparing-with-simple-guessing-rules) | Next-word prediction with an LSTM |
| <span id="G-262">G-262</span> | Basis | A set of vectors that are linearly independent and whose span is the whole space, so every vector can be built by scaling the basis vectors and adding them; a vector's coordinates are those scales. | [Note MA-052](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis.md#8-basis) | Linear combinations, span and basis |
| <span id="G-263">G-263</span> | Batch (mini-batch) | A small group of training observations used for one update; Keras uses 32 by default. | [Note ML-059](ML/06-regression/ML-059-mini-batch-gradient-descent/ML-059-mini-batch-gradient-descent.md#1-overview) | Mini-batch gradient descent |
| <span id="G-264">G-264</span> | Batch gradient descent | Gradient descent that uses all training rows for every update. | [Note ML-057](ML/06-regression/ML-057-batch-gradient-descent/ML-057-batch-gradient-descent.md#1-overview) | Batch gradient descent |
| <span id="G-265">G-265</span> | Batch learning | Training on the whole dataset at once, offline, then deploying. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#31-how-batch-learning-works) | Batch (offline) learning |
| <span id="G-266">G-266</span> | Batch normalisation | A layer placed between layers of a network that re-centres and re-scales its inputs during training; this keeps the activations away from the flat ends of sigmoid and tanh, which helps against vanishing gradients. | [Note DL-018](DL/01-basics/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients.md#64-batch-normalisation) | Batch normalisation |
| <span id="G-267">G-267</span> | Batch size | The number of rows in each batch; a hyperparameter. | [Note ML-059](ML/06-regression/ML-059-mini-batch-gradient-descent/ML-059-mini-batch-gradient-descent.md#2-a-family-that-contains-the-other-two) | Mini-batch gradient descent |
| <span id="G-2215">G-2215</span> | Bayes factor | A number that says how strongly the evidence favours yes over no: how many times more likely the evidence is if the answer is yes than if it is no, $P(\text{evidence} \mid \text{yes}) / P(\text{evidence} \mid \text{no})$. Multiplying the prior odds by it gives the posterior odds. | [Note MA-019](MA/02-probability/MA-019-bayes-problem/MA-019-bayes-problem.md#61-the-size-of-the-update-the-bayes-factor) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-269">G-269</span> | Bayes' theorem | The rule that reverses a conditional probability: from $P(B \mid A)$ it gives $P(A \mid B)$, so a belief about $A$ can be updated after seeing $B$; $P(A \mid B) = P(B \mid A) P(A) / P(B)$. | [Note MA-018](MA/02-probability/MA-018-bayes-theorem/MA-018-bayes-theorem.md#4-the-formula-and-its-proof) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-270">G-270</span> | Bayesian optimisation | Tuning that builds a model of how the score depends on the settings (hyperparameters) and uses all earlier trials to choose the next one. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#3-bayesian-search) | Bayesian optimisation |
| <span id="G-271">G-271</span> | Bayesian statistics | The branch of statistics that treats probabilities as beliefs updated by Bayes' theorem. | [Note MA-018](MA/02-probability/MA-018-bayes-theorem/MA-018-bayes-theorem.md#1-overview) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-272">G-272</span> | Beam search | A way of producing the output sentence (a decoding method) that keeps several candidate sentences at each step and picks the most probable complete one. | [Note DL-085](DL/06-transformers/DL-085-transformer-inference/DL-085-transformer-inference.md#7-the-mask-stays-on-during-inference) | Transformer inference (autoregressive decoding, KV cache, beam search) |
| <span id="G-273">G-273</span> | BeautifulSoup | Python library that parses HTML into a searchable tree. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#5-parsing-the-page-with-beautifulsoup) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-274">G-274</span> | Bell curve | The curve of a normal distribution. | [Note ML-041](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-041-outliers-zscore/ML-041-outliers-zscore.md#2-the-condition-a-normal-feature) | Normal distribution |
| <span id="G-275">G-275</span> | Bernoulli distribution | The distribution of a single trial with two outcomes, such as one coin toss: 1 (success) with probability $p$, 0 (failure) with probability $1 - p$. | [Note MA-021](MA/03-distributions/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf.md#71-bernoulli-distribution) | Bernoulli and binomial distributions |
| <span id="G-276">G-276</span> | Bernoulli trial | One random experiment with exactly two outcomes, success (1) and failure (0). | [Note MA-031](MA/03-distributions/MA-031-bernoulli-and-binomial/MA-031-bernoulli-and-binomial.md#1-overview) | Bernoulli and binomial distributions |
| <span id="G-277">G-277</span> | BernoulliNB | Naive Bayes for binary (yes/no) inputs. | [Note ML-084](ML/07-classification/ML-084-gaussian-naive-bayes/ML-084-gaussian-naive-bayes.md#7-when-the-data-is-not-normal) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-278">G-278</span> | BERT | A published transformer trained on a large amount of English text; BERT-base has 12 layers of 12 heads. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#8-the-heads-of-a-trained-model) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-279">G-279</span> | Bessel's correction | Dividing by $n - 1$ instead of $n$, so the sample variance is right on average. | [Note MA-006](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion.md#61-the-sample-version) | Bessel's correction |

| <span id="G-281">G-281</span> | best_score_ | The best mean cross-validated score found by a search. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#9-hyperparameter-tuning-with-a-pipeline) | Hyperparameter tuning |
| <span id="G-282">G-282</span> | Beta testing | Releasing a new version to a small group of trusted users first. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#101-beta-testing) | Beta and A/B testing |
| <span id="G-283">G-283</span> | BFGS | The most used way to take Newton-like steps without computing second derivatives (a quasi-Newton method): it builds a stand-in for the Hessian from gradients alone, kept symmetric and positive definite. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#7-where-ml-uses-second-order-approximations) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-287">G-287</span> | Bias (of a model) | Error from a model being too simple to capture the true relationship. | [Note ML-061](ML/06-regression/ML-061-bias-variance/ML-061-bias-variance.md#2-bias) | Bias-variance trade-off |
| <span id="G-284">G-284</span> | Bias (of a perceptron) | The weight on a constant input of 1; it shifts the boundary away from the origin. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#31-inputs-weights-and-bias) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-285">G-285</span> | Bias correction | A fix for a running average (EWMA) that starts at 0 and is too small in the first steps: divide it by $1 - \beta^t$. | [Note DL-038](DL/03-optimizers/DL-038-adam/DL-038-adam.md#4-the-update-rule) | Adam |
| <span id="G-286">G-286</span> | Bias vector ($b^{k}$) | A column holding one bias per node of layer $k$; it is added to the layer's weighted sums before the activation. | [Note DL-010](DL/01-basics/DL-010-forward-propagation/DL-010-forward-propagation.md#42-the-product) | Forward propagation |
| <span id="G-288">G-288</span> | Bias-variance trade-off | Lowering bias by adding complexity tends to raise variance, and the reverse. | [Note ML-061](ML/06-regression/ML-061-bias-variance/ML-061-bias-variance.md#5-the-trade-off) | Bias-variance trade-off |
| <span id="G-289">G-289</span> | Biased estimator | A formula for estimating a value from data (an estimator) that is systematically too high or too low on average. | [Note MA-071](MA/08-likelihood/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions.md#52-biased-but-consistent) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |

| <span id="G-296">G-296</span> | Bimodal | A distribution with two peaks. | [Note ML-030](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-030-power-transformer/ML-030-power-transformer.md#63-how-normal-is-each-feature) | Kurtosis and moments |

| <span id="G-376">G-376</span> | Characteristic polynomial | A polynomial built from a square matrix $A$, $\det(A - \lambda I)$, whose roots are the eigenvalues of $A$, so solving it finds them. | [Note MA-056](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues.md#33-two-worked-examples) | Eigenvectors and eigenvalues |
