| <span id="G-378">G-378</span> | Chi-square distribution | The shape the chi-square statistic $\chi^2$ follows when the null hypothesis $H_0$ is true: never negative, skewed to the right, with one parameter, the degrees of freedom. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#31-built-from-squared-normal-draws) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-379">G-379</span> | Chi-square statistic $\chi^2$ | A number that measures the total mismatch between observed and expected counts, $\sum (O - E)^2 / E$; it is 0 when every count matches and grows as they differ. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#22-the-formula) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-381">G-381</span> | Chi-square test | A test of whether categorical counts match expected counts (goodness of fit) or whether two categorical columns are related (independence). | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#1-overview) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-380">G-380</span> | Chi-square test of independence | A chi-square test of whether two categorical columns are related; $df = (r - 1)(c - 1)$. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#51-the-question-and-the-table) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-382">G-382</span> | Chi-squared test (chi2) | A test scoring how strongly a column is linked to the target; needs values of 0 or more. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#56-step-4-feature-selection) | Feature selection |
| <span id="G-383">G-383</span> | Cholesky solver | A scikit-learn Ridge solver that computes the coefficients in one go from the formula (the closed-form solution) instead of by repeated steps. | [Note ML-063](ML/06-regression/ML-063-ridge-regression-maths/ML-063-ridge-regression-maths.md#35-by-hand-on-two-points) | Ridge regression |
| <span id="G-384">G-384</span> | Chord | The straight line joining two points on a function's graph; it is used to test convexity: a convex function never rises above any of its chords. | [Note MA-065](MA/07-optimisation/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions.md#3-the-chord-test) | Convex and non-convex loss |
| <span id="G-385">G-385</span> | Chunk | A piece of a file read as a small DataFrame, so a file too large for memory can be processed one piece at a time. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#18-reading-a-big-file-in-chunks-chunksize) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-387">G-387</span> | Churn | Customers leaving a platform or service. | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#31-churn-rate) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-386">G-386</span> | Churn rate | The percentage of customers who leave during a given period. | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#31-churn-rate) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-389">G-389</span> | Class (HTML) | An attribute that labels tags; used to select the right ones. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#4-html-tags-classes-and-nesting) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-388">G-388</span> | Class prior | The share of training rows in a class, such as 5 wins out of 8 matches; Naive Bayes uses it as the prior probability of that class. | [Note ML-081](ML/07-classification/ML-081-naive-bayes-intuition/ML-081-naive-bayes-intuition.md#5-applying-bayes-theorem) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-390">G-390</span> | class_sep | make_classification setting for how far apart the classes are. | [Note ML-070](ML/07-classification/ML-070-perceptron-code/ML-070-perceptron-code.md#2-the-data) | Perceptron trick |
| <span id="G-391">G-391</span> | class_weight | A setting that weights each class's mistakes in the loss; "balanced" helps rare classes. | [Note ML-080](ML/07-classification/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters.md#51-class_weight) | Imbalanced data |
| <span id="G-392">G-392</span> | classes_ | The attribute holding the classes `LabelEncoder` learned, in order. | [Note ML-025](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding.md#72-encoding-the-purchased-column) | Ordinal and label encoding |
| <span id="G-395">G-395</span> | Classification | Predicting which of a fixed set of categories (classes) a row belongs to, learned from examples whose answers are known (supervised learning with a categorical output). | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#23-regression-and-classification) | Classification problems |
| <span id="G-393">G-393</span> | Classification error (SVM) | The part of the soft-margin SVM loss that adds up every point's slack $\xi_i$ (how far the point sits on the wrong side of its own hyperplane): $\sum \xi_i$, the total slack of all points. | [Note ML-088](ML/07-classification/ML-088-svm-soft-margin/ML-088-svm-soft-margin.md#5-the-soft-margin-loss) | Hinge loss and soft margin |
| <span id="G-394">G-394</span> | Classification metric | A number that measures how well a classification model performs. | [Note ML-075](ML/07-classification/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix.md#1-overview) | Accuracy |
| <span id="G-396">G-396</span> | classification_report | scikit-learn function that prints precision, recall, F1 and support for every class. | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#62-combining-the-classes) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-397">G-397</span> | Client, server | The program that asks, and the computer that answers. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#32-one-company-many-platforms) | APIs |
| <span id="G-398">G-398</span> | Closed-form solution | An answer given directly by a formula of ordinary operations. | [Note ML-050](ML/06-regression/ML-050-linear-regression-maths/ML-050-linear-regression-maths.md#2-two-ways-to-find-m-and-b) | Ordinary least squares (closed form) |
| <span id="G-399">G-399</span> | Cluster | One group found by clustering. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#32-clustering) | Clustering |
| <span id="G-400">G-400</span> | cluster_centers_ | The coordinates of the final centroids of a fitted `KMeans`. | [Note ML-123](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-123-kmeans-code/ML-123-kmeans-code.md#5-training-k-means-with-k--4) | K-means |
| <span id="G-401">G-401</span> | Clustering | Splitting data into groups of similar rows. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#32-clustering) | Clustering |
| <span id="G-402">G-402</span> | Clustermap | A heatmap with rows and columns reordered so similar ones sit together. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#8-clustermap-grouping-similar-categories) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-403">G-403</span> | CNN architecture | The sequence of layers of a CNN: convolution and pooling blocks, Flatten, fully connected layers, output. | [Note DL-045](DL/04-cnn/DL-045-lenet-5/DL-045-lenet-5.md#3-the-general-cnn-architecture) | CNN architecture (LeNet-5) |
| <span id="G-404">G-404</span> | Co-occurrence probability | How likely word $k$ is to appear near word $i$ in the training text, written $P_{ik}$; GloVe trains word vectors from these probabilities. | [Note DL-072](DL/06-transformers/DL-072-meaning-as-direction/DL-072-meaning-as-direction.md#4-the-difference-between-two-words-is-a-direction) | Meaning as direction in embedding space |
| <span id="G-405">G-405</span> | Codomain | The set in which a function's outputs lie. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#2-functions-one-number-in-one-number-out) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-406">G-406</span> | coef_ | The fitted slope (one per input column) in scikit-learn. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#51-reading-m-and-b) | Simple linear regression |
| <span id="G-407">G-407</span> | Coefficient ($\beta_i$) | The weight of one input column: the change in the output per unit of that input, others fixed. | [Note ML-052](ML/06-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression.md#3-the-equation) | Multiple linear regression |
| <span id="G-408">G-408</span> | Coefficient of variation (CV) | Standard deviation divided by mean: spread relative to the average. | [Note MA-006](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion.md#8-coefficient-of-variation) | Variance |
| <span id="G-409">G-409</span> | Coefficient path | How each coefficient changes as the regularisation strength grows, drawn as one line per feature, so we can see which coefficients are shrunk towards 0. | [Note ML-065](ML/06-regression/ML-065-ridge-key-points/ML-065-ridge-key-points.md#3-point-2-the-largest-coefficients-shrink-most) | Ridge regression |
| <span id="G-410">G-410</span> | Coefficient vector ($\beta$) | All the coefficients of the model, $\beta_0$ to $\beta_m$, as one column. | [Note ML-053](ML/06-regression/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths.md#22-stacking-the-equations) | Multiple linear regression |
| <span id="G-411">G-411</span> | Colour blob | A filter that responds to one colour. | [Note DL-052](DL/04-cnn/DL-052-visualizing-cnn/DL-052-visualizing-cnn.md#52-what-the-filters-look-like) | Visualising what a CNN learns |
| <span id="G-412">G-412</span> | Column block | XGBoost's storage of the data one sorted column per block, so each core can work on one feature. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#73-column-blocks) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-413">G-413</span> | Column sampling (feature sampling) | Training each model of an ensemble (each base model) on a random subset of the columns. | [Note ML-102](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-102-random-forest-intro/ML-102-random-forest-intro.md#5-a-random-forest-by-hand) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-414">G-414</span> | Column space | Every output a matrix can produce: all the vectors made by scaling and adding its columns (the span of its columns). | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#6-reading-a-matrix-as-a-picture) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-415">G-415</span> | Column transformer | A scikit-learn class that applies different transformations to different columns at once and joins the results. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#5-the-easy-way-columntransformer) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-416">G-416</span> | Column vector | A vector written as one column, shape $n \times 1$; the default meaning of "vector". | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#5-row-vectors-and-column-vectors) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-417">G-417</span> | ColumnTransformer | The scikit-learn class (in `sklearn.compose`) that implements the column transformer. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#1-overview) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-418">G-418</span> | Combined sampling | Training each model of an ensemble (each base model) on random rows and random columns together. | [Note ML-102](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-102-random-forest-intro/ML-102-random-forest-intro.md#5-a-random-forest-by-hand) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-419">G-419</span> | Combining perceptrons | Feeding several perceptrons' outputs, weighted and with a bias, into another perceptron, so their straight boundaries combine into a curved decision boundary; this is a multi-layer perceptron. | [Note DL-009](DL/01-basics/DL-009-mlp-intuition/DL-009-mlp-intuition.md#3-combining-two-perceptrons) | Multi-layer perceptron (MLP) |
| <span id="G-420">G-420</span> | Commutative law | The rule that swapping the two vectors does not change their dot product: $a \cdot b = b \cdot a$. | [Note MA-050](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity.md#32-two-laws) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-421">G-421</span> | Compile | Choosing the loss, the optimizer and the metrics before training. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#51-compile-loss-and-optimizer) | Keras workflow |
| <span id="G-422">G-422</span> | Complement ($A^c$) | The event that $A$ does not happen: every outcome not in $A$. | [Note MA-011](MA/02-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability.md#62-the-complement-rule) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-423">G-423</span> | Complement rule | The probability that an event does not happen is 1 minus the probability that it does, $P(A^c) = 1 - P(A)$; useful when the opposite event is easier to count, as in at least one head. | [Note MA-011](MA/02-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability.md#62-the-complement-rule) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-424">G-424</span> | Complementary slackness | A rule the best answer must obey when there are inequality constraints: for each constraint, its multiplier or the constraint value is 0, $\lambda_i g_i(\mathbf{x}) = 0$, so a constraint that plays no part at the answer gets multiplier 0. It is one of the KKT conditions. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#5-inequality-constraints) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-426">G-426</span> | Complete case | A row with a value in every column used. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#4-complete-case-analysis) | Complete case analysis |
| <span id="G-425">G-425</span> | Complete case analysis (CCA) | Dropping every row that has a missing value in any chosen column; also called listwise deletion. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#4-complete-case-analysis) | Complete case analysis |
| <span id="G-427">G-427</span> | Complete linkage | A way to measure the distance between two clusters in hierarchical clustering: the distance of their farthest pair of points; the closest two clusters are merged next. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#8-linkage-the-distance-between-two-clusters) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-428">G-428</span> | Complex cell | A nerve cell in the brain's first visual area (V1) that responds to its preferred edge anywhere inside a larger patch of view (receptive field). | [Note DL-041](DL/04-cnn/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex.md#52-complex-cells) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-429">G-429</span> | Component | One number of a vector, its position along one axis. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#23-coordinates-walking-instructions) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-430">G-430</span> | components_ | The directions of the principal components found by a fitted PCA (the eigenvectors of the covariance matrix), one per row. | [Note ML-048](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-048-pca-mnist/ML-048-pca-mnist.md#6-what-a-fitted-pca-stores) | PCA |
| <span id="G-431">G-431</span> | Composition | Applying one transformation and then another, seen as one overall transformation. | [Note MA-054](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition.md#22-following-the-basis-vectors) | Matrix multiplication as composition |
| <span id="G-432">G-432</span> | Compound event | An event with two or more outcomes. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#42-compound-events) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-433">G-433</span> | Compression | Storing data in fewer bits, for example by throwing away detail the eye barely notices and not storing again what stays the same between video frames, so files take less space and are faster to send. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#65-5d-video) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-434">G-434</span> | Computation graph | A function broken into elementary steps, each a node, with arrows for the flow of values. Backpropagation walks it backwards, applying the chain rule one step at a time. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#10-preview-backpropagation-and-automatic-differentiation) | Backpropagation |
| <span id="G-435">G-435</span> | Concat score | A score for how useful an encoder state is now: join the decoder state and the encoder state into one vector and pass it through a tanh layer and one output unit to get one number, $v_a^\top \tanh(W_a[s_i; h_j])$. It is Luong's name for the additive (Bahdanau) attention score. | [Note DL-070](DL/06-transformers/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention.md#51-a-simpler-score) | Bahdanau (additive) attention |
| <span id="G-436">G-436</span> | Concatenation ($[h_{t-1}, x_t]$) | Joining two vectors end to end into one longer vector, so one layer can read both at once: 3 numbers and 4 numbers give 7. | [Note DL-062](DL/05-rnn/DL-062-lstm-architecture/DL-062-lstm-architecture.md#51-computing-f_t) | LSTM gates (forget, input, output) and cell state |
| <span id="G-438">G-438</span> | Concave function | A function shaped like an upside-down bowl, the negative of a convex function: every chord lies on or below its graph, as for $\ln x$. Maximising a concave function is the same task as minimising a convex one. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#3-convex-functions-and-their-sets) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-2256">G-2256</span> | Concave up, concave down | Calculus names for a cup-shaped (convex) and a hump-shaped (concave) part of a curve. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#41-curving-up-the-slope-keeps-increasing) | Convex and non-convex loss |
| <span id="G-2165">G-2165</span> | Concept | One idea on the Course map, such as overfitting or Ridge. Links join Concepts, and each Concept points to the Note that teaches it. | [Course map](00-course-map/00-course-map.md#1-overview) |  |
| <span id="G-2162">G-2162</span> | Concept map | The view of the Course map that joins Concepts by typed Links (needs, is a kind of, fixes, compared with, used in), so we can see what each idea depends on and what it is for. | [Course map](00-course-map/00-course-map.md#3-the-concept-map) |  |
| <span id="G-439">G-439</span> | conda | A package and environment manager: it installs Python and libraries and keeps each set in a separate environment, so different projects do not clash. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#1-overview) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-440">G-440</span> | conda-forge | A free, community-run channel (an online store of conda packages) with more packages, often newer ones, than Anaconda's default channel. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#22-miniforge-the-installer-we-use) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-441">G-441</span> | Condition number | A number that says how much a matrix can magnify small errors, such as rounding errors, when we solve with it; a large value means the answer is sensitive. It is the largest singular value over the smallest, $\sigma_1 / \sigma_n$. | [Note MA-058](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-058-computing-the-svd/MA-058-computing-the-svd.md#8-how-computers-compute-the-svd) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-442">G-442</span> | Conditional hyperparameter | A hyperparameter that exists only for some values of another, such as `units_3`. | [Note DL-039](DL/03-optimizers/DL-039-keras-tuner/DL-039-keras-tuner.md#81-one-name-per-layer) | Keras Tuner |
| <span id="G-443">G-443</span> | Conditional independence | Independence that holds once a third variable is known: given the class, knowing one feature tells nothing more about another. Naive Bayes assumes it so it can multiply one probability per feature. | [Note ML-082](ML/07-classification/ML-082-naive-bayes-maths/ML-082-naive-bayes-maths.md#5-step-3-the-naive-assumption) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-444">G-444</span> | Conditional probability | The probability of an event given that another event has happened: $P(A \mid B)$. | [Note MA-015](MA/02-probability/MA-015-conditional-probability/MA-015-conditional-probability.md#2-the-definition) | Conditional probability |
| <span id="G-445">G-445</span> | Condorcet's jury theorem | A majority of independent voters, each right with probability above 0.5, is right more often than any one voter, and more so as voters are added. | [Note ML-096](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-096-voting-ensemble/ML-096-voting-ensemble.md#51-below-50-the-vote-is-worse-than-the-worst) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-446">G-446</span> | Confidence interval | A range of values, computed from a sample, that we expect to contain a population parameter such as the mean, so it shows how uncertain the estimate is. | <span id="G-447">G-447</span> | Confidence level | The share of intervals built by the method that contain the parameter, such as 95%; written $1 - \alpha$. | [Note MA-035](MA/04-inference/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure.md#4-confidence-intervals-and-confidence-levels) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-448">G-448</span> | Confounding variable | A hidden factor that drives two variables and makes them correlated. | [Note MA-009](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation.md#5-correlation-does-not-imply-causation) | Correlation and causation |
| <span id="G-449">G-449</span> | Confusion matrix | A table counting a classifier's predictions for every pair of actual and predicted class, so we can see which kinds of mistake it makes, which accuracy alone hides. | [Note ML-075](ML/07-classification/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix.md#4-the-confusion-matrix) | Confusion matrix |
| <span id="G-450">G-450</span> | Connection object | The open link to a database (`conn`) that queries go through. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#7-connecting-python-to-the-database) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-451">G-451</span> | Connector | A library that lets Python talk to a database. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#7-connecting-python-to-the-database) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-2238">G-2238</span> | Conservative degrees of freedom | The hand-calculation shortcut $\min(n_1, n_2) - 1$ for a two-sample t-test; Welch's formula is closer to exact. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#23-the-test-statistic) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-452">G-452</span> | Consistency (of an estimator) | Getting closer to the true parameter value as the amount of data grows. | [Note MA-070](MA/08-likelihood/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation.md#11-how-good-is-the-mle) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-453">G-453</span> | Constituency parsing | Finding the grammatical tree of a sentence: its phrases and how they nest. | [Note DL-086](DL/06-transformers/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end.md#83-english-constituency-parsing) | The transformer end to end (capstone) |
| <span id="G-454">G-454</span> | Constrained form | Writing regularisation as: make the loss as small as possible while the coefficients stay inside a fixed budget (a circle for Ridge). The picture shows how the penalty limits the size of the coefficients. | [Note ML-065](ML/06-regression/ML-065-ridge-key-points/ML-065-ridge-key-points.md#51-with-two-coefficients-why-ridge) | Ridge regression |
| <span id="G-455">G-455</span> | Constrained optimisation | Maximising or minimising a function while keeping one or more constraints true. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#2-constrained-optimisation-problems) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-456">G-456</span> | Constraint | A condition the solution must satisfy; in SVM, $y_i (w^T x_i + b) \geq 1$ for every training point. | [Note ML-087](ML/07-classification/ML-087-svm-maths/ML-087-svm-maths.md#42-one-constraint-for-both-classes) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-457">G-457</span> | Constructor (`__init__`) | The method that runs when an object is created and stores its settings. | [Note ML-124](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-124-kmeans-from-scratch/ML-124-kmeans-from-scratch.md#3-the-shape-of-the-class) | K-means |
| <span id="G-458">G-458</span> | Container | A tag (often a `div`) that holds everything about one item, such as one company. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#6-finding-the-right-tags-with-inspect) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-459">G-459</span> | Context size | The largest number of tokens the model can read at once: 1,024 for GPT-2, 2,048 for GPT-3. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#8-context-size) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-461">G-461</span> | Context vector | The summary of the input that the decoder works from; one fixed vector in the plain encoder–decoder, a new one per output word with attention. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#4-stage-1-the-encoderdecoder-2014) | Sequence-to-sequence (encoder-decoder) |
| <span id="G-460">G-460</span> | Context vector $c_i$ | The summary of the input made fresh for each decoder step $i$ under attention: a weighted sum of all the encoder's hidden states, where the weights decide how much each input word counts for the word being written now. | [Note DL-069](DL/06-transformers/DL-069-attention-mechanism/DL-069-attention-mechanism.md#52-what-c_i-is) | Attention mechanism |
| <span id="G-462">G-462</span> | Contextual embedding | A vector for a word that changes with the other words of its sentence, so "bank" gets one vector in "river bank" and another in "money bank". Self-attention produces it. | [Note DL-073](DL/06-transformers/DL-073-what-is-self-attention/DL-073-what-is-self-attention.md#5-static-and-contextual-embeddings) | Contextual embeddings |
| <span id="G-463">G-463</span> | Contextual learning | Studying a maths topic together with the ML algorithm that uses it, instead of the whole subject up front. | [Note MA-002](MA/00-why-maths/MA-002-learning-maths-for-ml/MA-002-learning-maths-for-ml.md#6-habit-5-learn-in-context) | How to learn the maths for ML |
| <span id="G-464">G-464</span> | Contingency table | A table that counts the observations for every pair of categories of two categorical features, so we can see whether the two are related; also called a crosstab. | [Note MA-013](MA/02-probability/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables.md#3-contingency-tables) | Venn diagrams and contingency tables |
| <span id="G-465">G-465</span> | Continuous data | Numerical data that can take any value in a range. | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#62-discrete-and-continuous-data) | Discrete and continuous data |
| <span id="G-466">G-466</span> | Continuous random variable | A random variable that can take any value in a range, such as a CGPA. | [Note MA-020](MA/03-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions.md#23-discrete-and-continuous-random-variables) | Random variables |
| <span id="G-467">G-467</span> | Continuous uniform distribution $U(a, b)$ | A continuous variable spread evenly between $a$ and $b$, with density $1/(b - a)$. | [Note MA-029](MA/03-distributions/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal.md#2-the-uniform-distribution) | Uniform distribution |
| <span id="G-468">G-468</span> | Contour plot | A map of a surface seen from above, with lines joining points of equal height, so a 3D shape such as a loss bowl can be drawn and read on flat paper. | [Note MA-062](MA/06-calculus/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients.md#11-reading-a-contour-map) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-469">G-469</span> | Converge | To settle at a minimum, with steps becoming negligible. | [Note DL-017](DL/01-basics/DL-017-backpropagation-why/DL-017-backpropagation-why.md#9-when-to-stop-convergence) | Gradient descent |
| <span id="G-470">G-470</span> | Convergence (k-means) | The point where the centroids stop moving between rounds, so the algorithm stops. | [Note ML-122](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-122-kmeans-intuition/ML-122-kmeans-intuition.md#44-step-5-stop-when-the-centroids-stop-moving) | K-means |
| <span id="G-472">G-472</span> | Convergence (MICE) | The point where the fills hardly change between two iterations. | [Note ML-039](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice.md#73-when-to-stop) | Iterative imputation (MICE) |
| <span id="G-471">G-471</span> | Convergence speed | How many epochs a method needs to reach a good solution, as opposed to the time per epoch. | [Note DL-020](DL/02-training/DL-020-gradient-descent-in-neural-networks/DL-020-gradient-descent-in-neural-networks.md#7-which-is-faster) | Gradient descent |
| <span id="G-473">G-473</span> | ConvergenceWarning | A warning that the solver stopped at max_iter before reaching the minimum. | [Note ML-080](ML/07-classification/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters.md#4-training-settings) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-474">G-474</span> | Conversion rate | The share of people reached who become customers. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#32-customer-profiles-for-targeted-marketing) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-475">G-475</span> | Convex combination | A mix of points (or functions) using non-negative shares that add up to 1, such as 30% of one and 70% of another; for two points it gives a point on the segment between them. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#21-walking-along-the-segment) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-476">G-476</span> | Convex function | A function where a straight line between any two points of its curve never goes below the curve; it has a single minimum. | [Note ML-056](ML/06-regression/ML-056-gradient-descent/ML-056-gradient-descent.md#8-the-shape-of-the-loss-function-matters) | Convex and non-convex loss |
| <span id="G-477">G-477</span> | Convex hull | The smallest region with straight edges that contains a set of points; every weighted average of the points lies inside it. | [Note DL-076](DL/06-transformers/DL-076-self-attention-geometric-intuition/DL-076-self-attention-geometric-intuition.md#71-the-output-sits-between-the-value-vectors) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-478">G-478</span> | Convex optimisation problem | A search for the lowest point of a bowl-shaped (convex) function inside an allowed region with no dents or holes (convex inequality constraints, affine equality constraints). In such a problem every local minimum is the global one. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#61-the-definition) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-479">G-479</span> | Convex set | A set that contains the whole segment between any two of its points. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#21-walking-along-the-segment) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-480">G-480</span> | Convolution layer | A layer that slides small filters over its input to find features. | [Note DL-040](DL/04-cnn/DL-040-cnn-intuition/DL-040-cnn-intuition.md#3-what-makes-a-network-a-cnn) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-481">G-481</span> | Convolution operation | Sliding a small filter over an input and, at each position, multiplying cell by cell and adding up, so the output (a feature map) is large where the input contains the filter's pattern, such as an edge. | [Note DL-042](DL/04-cnn/DL-042-convolution-operation/DL-042-convolution-operation.md#6-the-convolution-operation) | Convolution operation and feature maps |
| <span id="G-482">G-482</span> | Convolutional base | The convolution and pooling layers of a CNN, which find features in the image. | [Note DL-053](DL/04-cnn/DL-053-transfer-learning/DL-053-transfer-learning.md#41-base-and-top) | Transfer learning (feature extraction and fine-tuning) |
| <span id="G-484">G-484</span> | Convolutional neural network (CNN) | A neural network that slides small filters over its input to find patterns such as edges, using at least one convolutional layer; the standard network for images. | [Note DL-040](DL/04-cnn/DL-040-cnn-intuition/DL-040-cnn-intuition.md#1-overview) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-485">G-485</span> | Coordinate descent | An optimisation method that updates one coefficient at a time; used by scikit-learn's Lasso. | [Note ML-067](ML/06-regression/ML-067-lasso-sparsity/ML-067-lasso-sparsity.md#5-the-dead-zone) | Lasso regression |
| <span id="G-2241">G-2241</span> | Coordinates (of a vector) | The numbers of a vector, read as walking instructions from the origin to its tip: the first says how far to walk along the $x$-axis, the second how far to walk up or down. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#23-coordinates-walking-instructions) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-486">G-486</span> | Core point | In DBSCAN, a point with at least MinPts points within distance eps of it; core points form the inside of a cluster and give it its shape. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#6-core-border-and-noise-points) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-487">G-487</span> | Corrected resampled t-test | A paired t-test for comparing two models' cross-validation scores that enlarges the variance to allow for the folds sharing most of their training data, so it does not report a "significant" difference too often. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#8-comparing-two-machine-learning-models) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-490">G-490</span> | Correlation | A number from -1 to +1 that says how strongly two numerical features rise or fall together along a straight line: the covariance divided by both standard deviations, so the units drop out. | [Note MA-009](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation.md#4-correlation) | Correlation |
| <span id="G-488">G-488</span> | Correlation between base models | How alike two base models' predictions are; the less alike, the more an ensemble cuts variance. | [Note ML-104](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-104-bagging-vs-random-forest/ML-104-bagging-vs-random-forest.md#33-why-more-randomness-helps) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-489">G-489</span> | Correlation test | A t-test of whether a sample correlation $r$ is strong enough to show that two numerical features are correlated in the population ($H_0: \rho = 0$). It uses $t = r\sqrt{n-2}/\sqrt{1-r^2}$ with $n - 2$ degrees of freedom. | [Note MA-044](MA/04-inference/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test.md#7-two-numerical-features-the-correlation-test) | Correlation significance test |
| <span id="G-491">G-491</span> | Cosine similarity | A score from -1 to 1 for how closely two vectors point the same way: the cosine of the angle between them. It ignores their lengths, so it compares texts or word embeddings by direction only. | [Note MA-050](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity.md#61-two-phrases-as-two-arrows) | Cosine similarity |
| <span id="G-492">G-492</span> | Cost function | A measure of how far a model's predictions are from the true values, averaged over the data and read as a function of the model's parameters; training looks for the parameters that make it smallest. Often another name for the loss function. | [Note MA-065](MA/07-optimisation/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions.md#2-the-cost-function-is-a-function-of-the-parameters) | Convex and non-convex loss |
| <span id="G-2222">G-2222</span> | Cost-complexity pruning | Growing a tree fully, then cutting back the leaves that buy the least fit: keep the subtree with the lowest leaf impurity + $\alpha$ × number of leaves. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#52-the-tree-score) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-493">G-493</span> | Cost-sensitive learning | Changing the learning so that mistakes on some classes cost more. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#9-cost-sensitive-learning) | Cost-sensitive learning |
| <span id="G-494">G-494</span> | Count plot | A bar chart with one bar per category, as tall as its frequency. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#4-count-plot) | Univariate analysis |
| <span id="G-2160">G-2160</span> | Course map | The overview that ties all Notes together in four views (Pipeline map, Concept map, Learning path, Algorithm chooser): where each idea sits in a project, how ideas connect, what to read first and which algorithm to choose. | [Course map](00-course-map/00-course-map.md#1-overview) |  |
| <span id="G-496">G-496</span> | Covariance | A number that says how two numerical features move together: positive if they rise together, negative if one rises as the other falls. Its size depends on the units. | [Note MA-009](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation.md#3-covariance) | Covariance and covariance matrix |
| <span id="G-495">G-495</span> | Covariance matrix | A square table with every feature's variance on the diagonal and every pair's covariance off it, so it sums up how the data spreads and which features move together; PCA takes its eigenvectors. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#33-the-covariance-matrix) | Covariance and covariance matrix |
| <span id="G-497">G-497</span> | Covariate shift | A change in the distribution of a model's inputs while the input-output relationship stays the same. | [Note DL-031](DL/02-training/DL-031-batch-normalization/DL-031-batch-normalization.md#32-covariate-shift) | Covariate shift |
| <span id="G-498">G-498</span> | Cover | XGBoost's measure of how much data a node holds, as its similarity score counts it: the sum of $p(1-p)$ over the node's observations in classification, the number of observations in regression. The tree dump prints it as `cover`. | [Note ML-119](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-119-xgboost-classification/ML-119-xgboost-classification.md#11-the-same-in-code) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-499">G-499</span> | Coverage | The share of intervals from repeated samples that contain the true parameter; equals the confidence level when the assumptions hold. | [Note MA-036](MA/04-inference/MA-036-interpreting-confidence-intervals/MA-036-interpreting-confidence-intervals.md#2-what-95-confident-means) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-500">G-500</span> | Cramér's V | A measure of how strongly two categorical features are related, from 0 (no relationship) to 1 (perfect relationship), made by rescaling the chi-square statistic. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#72-strength-of-a-relationship-cramérs-v) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-501">G-501</span> | Credible interval | The Bayesian counterpart of a confidence interval: a range that, after seeing the data, holds the parameter with a stated probability such as 0.95. So it can be read as "95% probability the parameter is in here", which a confidence interval cannot. | [Note MA-036](MA/04-inference/MA-036-interpreting-confidence-intervals/MA-036-interpreting-confidence-intervals.md#3-common-misreadings) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-502">G-502</span> | Credit scoring | Predicting whether a loan applicant will repay. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#41-loan-approval) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-503">G-503</span> | criterion | The DecisionTreeClassifier hyperparameter choosing the impurity measure: "gini" (default), "entropy" or "log_loss". | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#81-why-another-measure) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-504">G-504</span> | Critical value | The cut-off on a z (or t) curve that leaves $\alpha/2$ in each tail; a confidence interval reaches this many standard errors either side of the estimate. It is 1.96 for 95% on the standard normal curve. | [Note MA-035](MA/04-inference/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure.md#7-the-z-procedure-formula) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-505">G-505</span> | Cross entropy | A loss for classifiers that grows when the model gives a low probability to the true class. It is the negative log-likelihood, so minimising it maximises the likelihood; smaller is better. | [Note ML-072](ML/07-classification/ML-072-log-loss/ML-072-log-loss.md#43-changing-the-sign) | Log loss (binary cross entropy) |
| <span id="G-506">G-506</span> | Cross product (vector product) | A product of two 3D vectors that gives a vector perpendicular to both. | [Note MA-050](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity.md#2-products-of-two-vectors) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-507">G-507</span> | Cross-attention | Attention between the two halves of a transformer: the queries come from the decoder and the keys and values from the encoder's output, so each output word can use the input sentence. | [Note DL-083](DL/06-transformers/DL-083-cross-attention/DL-083-cross-attention.md#5-processing-queries-from-one-side-keys-and-values-from-the-other) | Cross-attention |
| <span id="G-508">G-508</span> | Cross-correlation | The slide, multiply and add of a CNN filter done without first flipping the filter; a true convolution flips it. Most deep learning libraries compute cross-correlation and call it convolution, which does not matter because the filter values are learned. | [Note DL-042](DL/04-cnn/DL-042-convolution-operation/DL-042-convolution-operation.md#64-other-filters-find-other-edges) | Convolution operation and feature maps |
| <span id="G-509">G-509</span> | Cross-validated accuracy | Accuracy averaged over several train-test splits of the data, an estimate of performance on new data. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#101-the-data-and-the-baseline) | Cross-validation |
| <span id="G-510">G-510</span> | Cross-validation | Testing a model by training and testing it several times on different parts of the training data. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#8-cross-validation-with-a-pipeline) | Cross-validation |
| <span id="G-511">G-511</span> | Crosstab | A table that counts the rows for every pair of categories of two categorical columns, so we can see whether the two are related; pandas builds it with `pd.crosstab`. Also called a contingency table. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#71-crosstab-and-heatmap) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-512">G-512</span> | Crowdsourcing | Splitting a large task, such as labelling, among many paid online workers. | [Note DL-051](DL/04-cnn/DL-051-pretrained-models/DL-051-pretrained-models.md#43-how-it-was-labelled) | Pretrained models and ImageNet |
| <span id="G-513">G-513</span> | CSV file | A text file holding a table, with commas between values. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#2-csv-and-tsv-files) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-514">G-514</span> | CUDA | NVIDIA's platform for programming GPUs, so a network's matrix multiplications can run in parallel on a GPU; it made training on GPUs practical. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#52-hardware) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-515">G-515</span> | Cumulative distribution function (CDF) | For every value $x$, the probability that the random variable comes out at most $x$: $P(X \le x)$. | [Note MA-021](MA/03-distributions/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf.md#8-the-cumulative-distribution-function-of-a-discrete-variable) | Cumulative distribution function (CDF) |
| <span id="G-516">G-516</span> | Cumulative explained variance | The share of the data's variance kept by the first $k$ principal components together; its curve over $k$ is used to choose how many components to keep. | [Note ML-048](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-048-pca-mnist/ML-048-pca-mnist.md#72-the-cumulative-curve-and-the-90-rule) | PCA |
| <span id="G-517">G-517</span> | Cumulative frequency | The running total of the frequencies, up to and including a category. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#23-cumulative-frequency) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-518">G-518</span> | Cumulative relative frequency | The running total of the relative frequencies; ends at 1. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#23-cumulative-frequency) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-519">G-519</span> | Cumulative sum | The running total of a list of numbers; it turns weights into ranges on the line from 0 to 1. | [Note ML-110](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step.md#9-step-7-upsampling-a-new-dataset-drawn-by-weight) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-520">G-520</span> | Curse of dimensionality | The problems that appear when data has too many dimensions: lower performance and more computation. | [Note ML-045](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-045-curse-of-dimensionality/ML-045-curse-of-dimensionality.md#2-what-the-curse-of-dimensionality-is) | Curse of dimensionality |
| <span id="G-521">G-521</span> | Curvature | How fast the slope of a surface changes; given in each direction by the Hessian. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#5-the-hessian) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-522">G-522</span> | Custom binning | Binning with edges we choose from domain knowledge; also called domain-based binning. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#4-kinds-of-discretization) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-523">G-523</span> | Custom loss function | A loss written by the user, passed to libraries such as XGBoost with its gradient and Hessian. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#92-a-custom-loss-function) | Cost-sensitive learning |
| <span id="G-524">G-524</span> | Customer profile | A summary of what kind of buyer a customer is, built from their purchases. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#32-customer-profiles-for-targeted-marketing) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-525">G-525</span> | Customer segmentation | Grouping customers by their buying behaviour. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#32-customer-profiles-for-targeted-marketing) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-526">G-526</span> | Cut-offs | The low and high percentiles chosen as outlier limits, such as the 1st and the 99th: values below the first or above the second are treated as outliers and trimmed or capped. | [Note ML-043](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-043-outliers-percentile/ML-043-outliers-percentile.md#22-from-percentiles-to-limits) | Percentile outlier method |
| <span id="G-527">G-527</span> | Cutting the dendrogram | Drawing a horizontal line through the dendrogram; the lines it crosses are the clusters. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#53-cutting-the-dendrogram) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-528">G-528</span> | Damping | Making an optimiser's back-and-forth swings (oscillations) smaller: NAG brakes before it overshoots, so its path settles faster than momentum's, though it can then stay in a small dip. | [Note DL-035](DL/03-optimizers/DL-035-nesterov-accelerated-gradient/DL-035-nesterov-accelerated-gradient.md#6-why-nag-overshoots-less) | Nesterov accelerated gradient (NAG) |
| <span id="G-529">G-529</span> | Dash | A Python library for building interactive web apps with Plotly charts. | [Note ML-080](ML/07-classification/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters.md#7-an-interactive-playground) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-542">G-542</span> | Data | Recorded examples of inputs together with their outputs; a machine learning algorithm learns the pattern that links them from these examples. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#32-learning-patterns-from-data) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-530">G-530</span> | Data analysis | Finding patterns and hidden information in data, mainly by plotting graphs. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#43-data-mining-finding-hidden-patterns) | Data mining |
| <span id="G-531">G-531</span> | Data augmentation | Enlarging a dataset by making changed copies of its examples, such as zoomed or shifted images. | [Note DL-050](DL/04-cnn/DL-050-data-augmentation/DL-050-data-augmentation.md#1-overview) | Data augmentation |
| <span id="G-532">G-532</span> | Data cleaning | Fixing errors, gaps and inconsistencies in data. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#5-poor-quality-data) | Poor-quality data |
| <span id="G-533">G-533</span> | Data engineer | The specialist who collects and organises data from company systems. | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#7-step-4-getting-data) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-534">G-534</span> | Data hungry | Needing a lot of data before results become reliable. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#41-data) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-535">G-535</span> | Data leakage | Information from the test rows reaching the model during training, for example when a scaling or feature-selection step is fitted on all the data before the split; the test scores then come out higher than the model will really do on new data. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#81-why-the-preprocessing-must-be-inside-data-leakage) | Data leakage |
| <span id="G-536">G-536</span> | Data matrix | A dataset written as one matrix $X$: each observation's feature vector is a row and each feature a column, so the whole dataset can be handled with matrix operations. | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#71-one-matrix-for-the-whole-dataset) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-537">G-537</span> | Data mining | Using ML on data to extract patterns too hidden for graphs. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#43-data-mining-finding-hidden-patterns) | Data mining |
| <span id="G-538">G-538</span> | Data pipeline | A channel that carries data from one place to another, such as an API that passes a company's data to our program. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#2-what-an-api-is) | APIs |
| <span id="G-539">G-539</span> | Data preprocessing (preprocessing) | Cleaning and preparing the data before training, so an algorithm can use it. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#51-why-raw-data-cannot-go-straight-into-a-model) | ML development life cycle |
| <span id="G-540">G-540</span> | Data type (dtype) | The kind of values a column holds, such as `int64`, `float64` or `str`. | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#5-what-type-is-each-column) | Exploratory data analysis |
| <span id="G-541">G-541</span> | Data warehouse | A separate store of copied company data, safe to analyse without touching the live database. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#42-where-data-comes-from) | ML development life cycle |
| <span id="G-544">G-544</span> | Database | A program that stores data as tables and answers queries. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#3-what-sql-is) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-543">G-543</span> | Database server | A program that holds databases and answers queries, such as MySQL. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#6-getting-an-sql-database-ready) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-545">G-545</span> | Datetime | A value pandas understands as a point in time, with date and time parts, so we can pull out parts such as the year or weekday and compute the time between two dates. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#4-converting-text-to-a-datetime-type) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-546">G-546</span> | datetime64 | The pandas column type for datetimes; `[us]` means microsecond resolution. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#4-converting-text-to-a-datetime-type) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-547">G-547</span> | Day of week | The weekday as a number, Monday = 0 to Sunday = 6 (`.dt.dayofweek`). | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#54-day-of-the-week) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-548">G-548</span> | DBSCAN | A clustering method that groups points lying in crowded (dense) regions and labels lonely points as noise. The name stands for density-based spatial clustering of applications with noise. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#1-overview) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-549">G-549</span> | ddof | The NumPy and pandas argument that sets the number subtracted from $n$ in the variance's denominator: `ddof=1` divides by $n - 1$ (sample variance), `ddof=0` by $n$. NumPy defaults to 0, pandas to 1. | [Note MA-006](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion.md#63-how-much-too-small) | Variance |
| <span id="G-550">G-550</span> | De Morgan's law | Two rules for "not" over "or" and "and": "neither A nor B" is "not A and not B", $(A \cup B)^c = A^c \cap B^c$; "not both" is "at least one fails", $(A \cap B)^c = A^c \cup B^c$. | [Note MA-013](MA/02-probability/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables.md#23-two-events-and-their-four-regions) | Venn diagrams and contingency tables |
| <span id="G-551">G-551</span> | Dead neuron | A node whose output is 0 for every input; it gets no updates and stays that way. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#3-the-dying-relu-problem) | Dying ReLU problem |
| <span id="G-552">G-552</span> | Dead zone | The range of $S$ (how strongly the data pulls the slope away from 0) for which Lasso's best slope is exactly 0: inside it the penalty wins, which is how Lasso removes features. | [Note ML-067](ML/06-regression/ML-067-lasso-sparsity/ML-067-lasso-sparsity.md#5-the-dead-zone) | Lasso regression |
| <span id="G-553">G-553</span> | Decay factor $\beta$ | In momentum, the share of the previous update (the velocity) kept at each step; 0 gives plain gradient descent, usually 0.9. | [Note DL-034](DL/03-optimizers/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum.md#7-the-role-of-beta) | SGD with momentum |
| <span id="G-554">G-554</span> | Deciles | The 10th, 20th, ..., 90th percentiles: cuts into 10 groups. | [Note MA-008](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots.md#2-quantiles) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-555">G-555</span> | Decision boundary | A line or curve that separates the classes in classification. | [Note ML-006](ML/01-foundations/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based.md#41-learning-a-decision-boundary) | Decision surface and boundary |
| <span id="G-556">G-556</span> | Decision node | A node in the middle of a tree that asks a question and splits again. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#4-terminology) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-557">G-557</span> | Decision region | The part of the input space in which a model predicts a given class. | [Note ML-078](ML/07-classification/ML-078-softmax-regression/ML-078-softmax-regression.md#5-in-scikit-learn) | Softmax regression |
| <span id="G-558">G-558</span> | Decision rule (SVM) | How a trained SVM labels a new point $u$: by which side of the separating line it falls on, predicting +1 if $w \cdot u + b \geq 0$ and −1 otherwise. | [Note ML-087](ML/07-classification/ML-087-svm-maths/ML-087-svm-maths.md#22-projecting-a-new-point-onto-w) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-559">G-559</span> | Decision stump | A decision tree with maximum depth 1: one question, two regions. Too weak alone, it is AdaBoost's usual weak learner. | [Note ML-109](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-109-adaboost-intuition/ML-109-adaboost-intuition.md#22-decision-stumps) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-560">G-560</span> | Decision surface | A plot colouring every point of the input space by the class the model would predict there. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#51-what-it-shows) | Decision surface and boundary |
| <span id="G-561">G-561</span> | Decision tree | A model that predicts by asking a chain of questions about the input columns: nested if-else conditions. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#2-a-decision-tree-is-nested-if-else) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-562">G-562</span> | DecisionTreeRegressor | scikit-learn's regression tree: a decision tree whose leaves predict a number, the mean target of the training rows in the leaf, instead of a class. It is used exactly like `DecisionTreeClassifier`. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#71-the-data-and-a-first-tree) | Regression trees |
| <span id="G-564">G-564</span> | Decoder | The part of a seq2seq model that writes the output sequence. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#4-stage-1-the-encoderdecoder-2014) | Sequence-to-sequence (encoder-decoder) |
| <span id="G-563">G-563</span> | Decoder block | One layer of the decoder: masked self-attention, cross-attention and a feed-forward network, each followed by add and norm; the decoder stacks 6. | [Note DL-084](DL/06-transformers/DL-084-transformer-decoder/DL-084-transformer-decoder.md#3-from-a-black-box-to-the-decoder-block) | Transformer decoder |
| <span id="G-565">G-565</span> | Decoder-only transformer | A transformer with the encoder removed, so no cross-attention: a stack of blocks, each with masked self-attention and an MLP, that guesses the next token at every position. GPT is built this way. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#1-overview) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-566">G-566</span> | Decoding notation | Shrinking a formula's indices to two or three cases and writing every case out by hand. | [Note MA-002](MA/00-why-maths/MA-002-learning-maths-for-ml/MA-002-learning-maths-for-ml.md#3-habit-2-decode-the-notation) | How to learn the maths for ML |
| <span id="G-567">G-567</span> | Deep belief network | A many-layered network from Hinton and colleagues (2006) whose starting weights were set by unsupervised pre-training, one layer at a time; its success led the field to be renamed deep learning. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#35-deep-belief-networks-and-the-name-deep-learning) | Types of neural networks |
| <span id="G-568">G-568</span> | Deep Learning (DL) | Machine Learning that uses neural networks with many layers; finds features by itself. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#5-deep-learning) | Deep learning |
| <span id="G-569">G-569</span> | Deep LSTM, deep GRU | Two or more LSTM (or GRU) layers stacked on top of each other: a deep RNN whose recurrent layers are LSTM or GRU layers. | [Note DL-065](DL/05-rnn/DL-065-deep-rnns/DL-065-deep-rnns.md#10-deep-lstms-and-deep-grus) | Deep (stacked) RNNs |
| <span id="G-570">G-570</span> | Deep network (deep neural network) | A neural network with many hidden layers. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#22-the-parts-of-a-neural-network) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-572">G-572</span> | Deep reinforcement learning | Reinforcement learning (an agent learning from rewards by trial and error) that uses deep neural networks; it raised the skill of game-playing programs. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#41-mainstream-applications) | Reinforcement learning |
| <span id="G-573">G-573</span> | Deep RNN (stacked RNN) | An RNN with two or more recurrent layers stacked on top of each other, all unfolded through time. | [Note DL-065](DL/05-rnn/DL-065-deep-rnns/DL-065-deep-rnns.md#1-overview) | Deep (stacked) RNNs |
| <span id="G-574">G-574</span> | Default direction | The side of a split that rows with a missing value follow. XGBoost tries sending them left and then right and keeps the side with the larger gain, so trees handle missing values without filling them in. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#82-sparsity-aware-split-finding) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-575">G-575</span> | Define-by-run | Building the search space while the objective function runs, so it can depend on earlier choices. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#82-a-dynamic-search-space) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-576">G-576</span> | Degradation problem | Deeper plain networks reaching a higher training error than shallower ones. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#71-why-residual-connections) | Residual connections and add & norm |
| <span id="G-577">G-577</span> | Degree | The highest power used in a polynomial, such as 2 for $x^2$. It sets the model's complexity: a higher degree lets the curve bend more, and too high a degree overfits. | [Note ML-060](ML/06-regression/ML-060-polynomial-regression/ML-060-polynomial-regression.md#4-choosing-the-degree) | Polynomial regression |
| <span id="G-578">G-578</span> | Degrees of freedom | The number of deviations free to vary, $n - 1$ for a sample of size $n$; it sets the shape of the t-distribution (its parameter). | [Note MA-037](MA/04-inference/MA-037-t-procedure/MA-037-t-procedure.md#51-degrees-of-freedom) | Student's t-distribution |
| <span id="G-579">G-579</span> | Delivery routing | Planning the most efficient route for deliveries. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#53-delivery-routing-in-logistics) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-580">G-580</span> | Demand forecasting | Predicting how much of something will be needed, where and when. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#31-stocking-up-before-a-sale) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-581">G-581</span> | Dendrites, nucleus, axon | The parts of a brain neuron that receive signals (dendrites), process them (nucleus) and send the result on (axon); in a perceptron they match the inputs with weights, the summation and activation, and the output. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#5-perceptron-and-biological-neuron) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-582">G-582</span> | Dendrogram | A tree showing which rows (or columns) were joined as similar, and in what order. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#8-clustermap-grouping-similar-categories) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-583">G-583</span> | Dense (fully connected) layer | A layer whose every node receives the output of every node in the layer before. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#41-the-first-architecture) | ANN for classification |
| <span id="G-584">G-584</span> | Dense region, sparse region | An area with many points close together; an area with few points. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#4-density-based-clustering) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-585">G-585</span> | Dense representation | A short vector of real numbers, most of them non-zero, such as a word embedding; unlike a long, mostly-zero one-hot vector, every number carries information. | [Note DL-057](DL/05-rnn/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis.md#61-sparse-and-dense-representations) | Word embeddings |
| <span id="G-586">G-586</span> | Density estimation | Working out, from observed data, how likely each value of a random variable is: estimating its probability density function (PDF). | [Note MA-023](MA/03-distributions/MA-023-density-estimation-kde/MA-023-density-estimation-kde.md#2-what-density-estimation-is) | Density estimation |
| <span id="G-587">G-587</span> | Density plot | A histogram with a smooth KDE curve drawn along the tops of its bars, so the shape of a numerical column's distribution is easier to see. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#7-density-plot) | Kernel density estimation (KDE) |
| <span id="G-588">G-588</span> | Density-based clustering | Clustering that finds dense regions of points separated by sparse regions. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#4-density-based-clustering) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-589">G-589</span> | Density-connected | Two points linked by a chain of core points with every step at most eps, so we can walk from one to the other through the dense region; DBSCAN puts density-connected points in the same cluster. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#7-density-connected-points) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-590">G-590</span> | Dependent events | Events that are not independent: knowing one changes the probability of the other. | [Note MA-016](MA/02-probability/MA-016-independent-events/MA-016-independent-events.md#5-independent-or-not) | Independent and mutually exclusive events |
| <span id="G-591">G-591</span> | Dependent variable | Another name for the target, the output $y$ that a model predicts; it is called dependent because it depends on the features. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#5-inputs-and-output) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-592">G-592</span> | Deployment (deploy, model deployment) | Moving a model from development to production: putting it on a server so users can reach it. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#2-development-and-production) | Deployment |
| <span id="G-594">G-594</span> | Depth | The number of questions on the longest path from a tree's root to a leaf. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#2-overfitting-and-underfitting-in-a-tree) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-595">G-595</span> | Derivative | The slope of a function at a point: how fast the output changes as the input changes there. It is zero at the lowest point of a smooth curve, which is how training finds a minimum. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#41-from-secant-to-tangent) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-596">G-596</span> | Descriptive statistics | Numbers that summarise data, such as count, mean, spread and percentiles. | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#3-descriptive-and-inferential-statistics) | Descriptive statistics |
| <span id="G-597">G-597</span> | Design matrix ($X$) | The data as a matrix, one row per observation and one column per feature, plus a first column of 1s; the 1s multiply the intercept, so it is treated like any other coefficient. | [Note ML-053](ML/06-regression/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths.md#22-stacking-the-equations) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-598">G-598</span> | Determinant | The factor by which a matrix scales areas (volumes in 3D); 0 when it squishes space into a lower dimension. | [Note MA-056](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues.md#32-when-can-a-matrix-send-a-non-zero-vector-to-zero) | Determinant |
| <span id="G-599">G-599</span> | Development environment | Our own machine, where we build and train a model. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#2-development-and-production) | Batch (offline) learning |
| <span id="G-600">G-600</span> | Diabetes dataset | scikit-learn's built-in data of 442 patients, 10 standardised inputs, and disease progression one year later. | [Note ML-054](ML/06-regression/ML-054-multiple-lr-code/ML-054-multiple-lr-code.md#2-the-data) | Multiple linear regression |
| <span id="G-601">G-601</span> | Diagonal matrix | A matrix with zeros everywhere off the diagonal; it scales each axis by its own factor. | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#72-feature-scaling-is-a-stretch-along-the-axes) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-602">G-602</span> | Diagonalisation | Rewriting a matrix so that, seen along its eigenvectors, it only stretches each axis: $A = PDP^{-1}$, where $P$ holds its eigenvectors and $D$ is diagonal. This makes its powers easy to compute. | [Note MA-056](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues.md#62-changing-to-an-eigenbasis) | Eigenvectors and eigenvalues |
| <span id="G-603">G-603</span> | Dictionary (Python) | A lookup table from keys to values, written `{key: value}`. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#5-reading-the-reply) | APIs |
| <span id="G-604">G-604</span> | Difference quotient | The average slope of a function over a step $h$: the change in output divided by the change in input, $(f(x + h) - f(x))/h$, the slope of the secant line. Shrinking $h$ towards 0 gives the derivative. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#3-the-difference-quotient-slope-of-a-secant-line) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-606">G-606</span> | Differentiable | Having a derivative (one clear slope) at a point, or at every point; the absolute value $\lvert m \rvert$ is not, at its corner $m = 0$. Gradient descent needs a differentiable loss. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#41-from-secant-to-tangent) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-605">G-605</span> | Differentiable loss | A loss function whose derivative exists at every point, so it can be minimised with derivatives. | [Note ML-115](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths.md#42-the-loss-function) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-607">G-607</span> | Differential entropy | How uncertain a continuous variable is (the entropy of a continuous variable): higher for a more spread-out distribution. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#66-entropy-of-a-numerical-feature) | Entropy, information gain and Gini |
| <span id="G-608">G-608</span> | Differentiation | Finding the slope of a curve at each point; the derivative of the CDF is the PDF. | [Note MA-022](MA/03-distributions/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf.md#8-how-the-pdf-and-cdf-are-linked) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-610">G-610</span> | Dimension | One feature (input column) of the data, seen as one axis of the space the observations live in: 10 features give 10 dimensions. A tensor's dimensions mean something else, its number of axes. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#33-dimensionality-reduction) | Dimensionality reduction |
| <span id="G-609">G-609</span> | Dimension of a vector | The dimension of the space it lives in: its number of components. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#24-from-2d-to-n-dimensions) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-612">G-612</span> | Dimensionality | The number of features (columns) in the data; one-hot encoding raises it by one column per category, and too high a dimensionality brings the curse of dimensionality. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#23-more-categories-more-columns) | Curse of dimensionality |
| <span id="G-611">G-611</span> | Dimensionality reduction | Reducing the number of input columns while keeping the information. | [Note ML-045](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-045-curse-of-dimensionality/ML-045-curse-of-dimensionality.md#6-the-solution-dimensionality-reduction) | Dimensionality reduction |
| <span id="G-613">G-613</span> | Direction | The way an arrow (vector) points, regardless of its length. In a word embedding, meaning lives in directions: the arrow from "man" to "woman" points roughly the same way as the one from "uncle" to "aunt". | [Note DL-072](DL/06-transformers/DL-072-meaning-as-direction/DL-072-meaning-as-direction.md#1-overview) | Meaning as direction in embedding space |
| <span id="G-614">G-614</span> | Directional derivative | How steeply a function rises when we step in a chosen direction, given by a unit vector $\mathbf{u}$, instead of only along an axis. It is the dot product $\nabla f \cdot \mathbf{u}$. | [Note MA-062](MA/06-calculus/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients.md#43-the-slope-in-any-direction) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-615">G-615</span> | Dirty data | Data with errors, gaps, duplicates or inconsistencies. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#51-why-raw-data-cannot-go-straight-into-a-model) | Poor-quality data |
| <span id="G-616">G-616</span> | Discrete data | Numerical data that takes only separate values, usually counts. | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#62-discrete-and-continuous-data) | Discrete and continuous data |
| <span id="G-617">G-617</span> | Discrete random variable | A random variable that takes separate values, such as a die's face. | [Note MA-020](MA/03-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions.md#23-discrete-and-continuous-random-variables) | Random variables |
| <span id="G-618">G-618</span> | Discrete uniform distribution | A discrete distribution in which every possible value is equally likely, such as a fair die. | [Note MA-021](MA/03-distributions/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf.md#4-the-pmf-of-one-die) | Uniform distribution |
| <span id="G-619">G-619</span> | Discretization | Turning a continuous column into a discrete one by cutting its range into intervals. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#1-overview) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-620">G-620</span> | Discriminator | In a GAN, the network that judges whether data is real or fake. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#25-generative-adversarial-network-gan) | Types of neural networks |
| <span id="G-623">G-623</span> | Distance | A number measuring how far apart two points are; small distance = similar. | [Note ML-006](ML/01-foundations/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based.md#31-how-it-works) | Instance-based learning |
| <span id="G-621">G-621</span> | Distance weight (nan-Euclidean) | In the nan-Euclidean distance, the factor that scales the sum up: the total number of features divided by the number present in both observations. It makes up for the skipped features, so observations with many gaps do not look too close. | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#43-the-nan-euclidean-distance) | KNN imputer |
| <span id="G-622">G-622</span> | Distance weighting | Each neighbour counts in proportion to 1 / its distance, so nearer rows count more. | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#75-distance-weighting) | KNN imputer |
