| <span id="G-624">G-624</span> | distance_threshold | Height at which `AgglomerativeClustering` stops merging, instead of a fixed number of clusters. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#101-the-main-hyperparameters) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-625">G-625</span> | Distributed computing | Sharing one job between several machines (nodes), coordinated by a master node. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#76-distributed-computing) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-626">G-626</span> | Distribution | How a column's values spread over their range. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#6-histogram) | Univariate analysis |
| <span id="G-627">G-627</span> | Distributive law | The rule that multiplying a sum gives the same result as adding the separate products, $a \cdot (b + c) = a \cdot b + a \cdot c$; the dot product obeys it, so a dot product with a sum can be split into two. | [Note MA-050](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity.md#32-two-laws) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-628">G-628</span> | Diverge (divergence) | To move further away with each step: the updates overshoot more and more, so the loss grows instead of shrinking. | [Note ML-056](ML/06-regression/ML-056-gradient-descent/ML-056-gradient-descent.md#5-the-learning-rate) | Gradient descent |
| <span id="G-630">G-630</span> | Divisive clustering | Top-down hierarchical clustering: start with one cluster and split repeatedly. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#4-two-kinds-of-hierarchical-clustering) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-632">G-632</span> | Domain | The set of allowed inputs of a function. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#2-functions-one-number-in-one-number-out) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-631">G-631</span> | Domain knowledge | Knowledge of the field the data comes from, such as medicine, property or shipping; it tells us which features make sense and which new ones would help. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#2-what-feature-engineering-is) | Feature engineering |
| <span id="G-633">G-633</span> | Donor | A row that has a value in the column being filled, so it can be a neighbour. | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#5-filling-the-gap-step-by-step) | KNN imputer |
| <span id="G-2230">G-2230</span> | Dot plot | A frequency table drawn with one dot per observation, stacked over its value. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#24-a-discrete-feature-the-dot-plot) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-634">G-634</span> | Dot product | Multiplying two vectors entry by entry and adding the products, $u^{\mathsf T}x$, which gives one number. It is large when the vectors point the same way, so it measures similarity and gives projections. | [Note MA-050](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity.md#3-computing-the-dot-product) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-635">G-635</span> | Dot score | Luong's simplest attention score: the dot product of the decoder state and an encoder state, $s_i^\top h_j$, large when the two point the same way. It needs no learned parameters, but both states must have the same size. | [Note DL-070](DL/06-transformers/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention.md#51-a-simpler-score) | Luong (multiplicative) attention |
| <span id="G-636">G-636</span> | Downsampling | Reducing the height and width of a feature map. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#41-where-pooling-sits) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-2167">G-2167</span> | Draft / confirmed | The two states of a Concept on the Course map: draft means no written Note teaches it yet (shown faint, checked against its source when the Note is written); confirmed means a written Note already teaches it. | [Course map](00-course-map/00-course-map.md#1-overview) |  |
| <span id="G-637">G-637</span> | dropna | The pandas method that drops rows (or columns) with missing values. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#4-complete-case-analysis) | Complete case analysis |
| <span id="G-639">G-639</span> | Dropout | Switching off a random set of input and hidden nodes at every training step, to reduce overfitting. | [Note DL-024](DL/02-training/DL-024-dropout/DL-024-dropout.md#41-switching-nodes-off) | Dropout |
| <span id="G-638">G-638</span> | Dropout rate ($p$) | The probability that each node of a layer is switched off in a training step. | [Note DL-024](DL/02-training/DL-024-dropout/DL-024-dropout.md#42-the-dropout-rate) | Dropout |
| <span id="G-640">G-640</span> | dtreeviz | A Python library that draws decision trees with the training data shown at every node. | [Note ML-094](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-094-dtreeviz/ML-094-dtreeviz.md#1-overview) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-642">G-642</span> | Dual problem | A second problem that looks for the highest guaranteed floor under a constrained minimum. Each price $\boldsymbol{\lambda} \ge 0$ on the constraints gives a floor, the lowest value of the Lagrangian, $D(\boldsymbol{\lambda}) = \min_{\mathbf{x}} \mathcal{L}$; the dual problem maximises $D$, and a feasible point that reaches the floor is the best. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#61-the-dual-function-and-the-dual-problem) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-643">G-643</span> | Dual vector | The one vector whose dot product does the same job as a given linear transformation from vectors to a number; for example, a linear model's weight vector is the dual vector of its scoring function. | [Note MA-055](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-055-dot-product-and-duality/MA-055-dot-product-and-duality.md#6-duality) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-644">G-644</span> | Duality | The one-to-one match between vectors and linear maps that turn a vector into a number: every such map is a dot product with exactly one vector. So a vector can be read as a scoring function, and a scoring function as a vector. | [Note MA-055](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-055-dot-product-and-duality/MA-055-dot-product-and-duality.md#6-duality) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-645">G-645</span> | Dummy copy $W^{(t)}$ | A copy of a weight that an RNN reuses at every time step, one copy for step $t$ only; the shared weight's gradient is the sum over the copies. | [Note DL-059](DL/05-rnn/DL-059-backpropagation-through-time/DL-059-backpropagation-through-time.md#62-the-compact-formula) | Backpropagation through time (BPTT) |
| <span id="G-647">G-647</span> | Dummy variable | One of the 0/1 columns made by one-hot encoding; each marks whether a row belongs to one category, so a model can use a categorical column as numbers. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#31-dummy-variables-and-the-n---1-rule) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-646">G-646</span> | Dummy variable trap | The problem caused by keeping all $n$ dummy columns of a category: they always add up to 1, so any one can be calculated from the others (multicollinearity) and a linear model's weights become unstable. Dropping one column ($n - 1$ kept) avoids it. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#32-multicollinearity-inputs-must-not-depend-on-each-other) | Multicollinearity |
| <span id="G-648">G-648</span> | Duplicate row | A row identical to another row in every column. Duplicates give some examples extra weight and distort what a model learns, so we check for them before any analysis. | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#8-are-there-duplicate-rows) | Poor-quality data |
| <span id="G-649">G-649</span> | Durbin-Watson statistic | A number from 0 to 4 that checks whether a regression's residuals are linked to the residuals next to them (autocorrelation); about 2 means no such link. | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#7-assumption-5-no-autocorrelation-of-the-residuals) | Assumptions of linear regression |
| <span id="G-650">G-650</span> | Dying ReLU problem | ReLU nodes ending up with a negative weighted sum for every input, so they output 0 and stop learning. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#3-the-dying-relu-problem) | Dying ReLU problem |
| <span id="G-652">G-652</span> | Dynamic programming | Solving a problem by storing the answer to each overlapping sub-problem the first time it is computed and looking it up afterwards, so no work is repeated; memoization is one form of it. | [Note DL-019](DL/01-basics/DL-019-mlp-memoization/DL-019-mlp-memoization.md#33-the-memoized-version) | Memoization |
| <span id="G-653">G-653</span> | Dynamic search space | A search space in which some hyperparameters exist only for some values of another, such as the algorithm; the tuner then suggests only settings that make sense for the choice already made. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#82-a-dynamic-search-space) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-654">G-654</span> | E-step | The expectation step of the EM algorithm: using the current parameters, give every point its responsibilities (the probability that it came from each component), a soft assignment that the M-step then uses. | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#31-the-two-steps) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-655">G-655</span> | Eager learning | Learning that does all its work up front, building a model from the training data before any question arrives; another name for model-based learning and the opposite of lazy learning. | [Note ML-006](ML/01-foundations/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based.md#43-examples) | Model-based learning |
| <span id="G-656">G-656</span> | Early stopping | Stopping training when the score on held-out data is best, before full convergence; this keeps coefficients small, so it also acts as regularisation. | [Note ML-057](ML/06-regression/ML-057-batch-gradient-descent/ML-057-batch-gradient-descent.md#5-early-stopping) | Early stopping |
| <span id="G-657">G-657</span> | Eckart–Young theorem | The theorem that the truncated SVD (keeping the $k$ largest singular values) is the closest rank $k$ matrix to $A$, so it is the best way to compress $A$ to rank $k$; its spectral error is $\sigma_{k+1}$. | [Note MA-059](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-059-low-rank-approximation/MA-059-low-rank-approximation.md#42-the-eckartyoung-theorem) | Low-rank approximation (truncated SVD) |
| <span id="G-658">G-658</span> | Economy rate | A cricket feature built from raw totals: a bowler's runs conceded per over. It rates bowlers better than total runs; a lower economy rate is better. | [Note ML-044](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting.md#4-constructing-features-in-other-data) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-661">G-661</span> | Edge | In an image, a place where the intensity changes sharply, such as where a dark region meets a light one. Finding edges is the first job of a CNN's filters. | [Note DL-042](DL/04-cnn/DL-042-convolution-operation/DL-042-convolution-operation.md#5-edges-are-changes-in-intensity) | Convolution operation and feature maps |
| <span id="G-659">G-659</span> | Edge detector (filter) | A filter whose feature map is large along edges of one direction. | [Note DL-042](DL/04-cnn/DL-042-convolution-operation/DL-042-convolution-operation.md#61-filter-and-feature-map) | Convolution operation and feature maps |
| <span id="G-2263">G-2263</span> | Edge detector (hidden node) | A node whose weights are positive on a strip of pixels and negative around it, so it switches on when that strip holds a stroke. | [Note DL-009](DL/01-basics/DL-009-mlp-intuition/DL-009-mlp-intuition.md#45-what-a-hidden-node-can-detect) | Multi-layer perceptron (MLP) |
| <span id="G-660">G-660</span> | Edge TPU | A small Google chip for running networks on drones, watches and glasses. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#52-hardware) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-662">G-662</span> | Effective learning rate | The learning rate a parameter actually gets after AdaGrad's division: $\eta/(\sqrt{v_t}+\epsilon)$. | [Note DL-036](DL/03-optimizers/DL-036-adagrad/DL-036-adagrad.md#8-the-weakness-the-learning-rate-only-goes-down) | AdaGrad |
| <span id="G-663">G-663</span> | Eigen-decomposition | Finding all the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of a matrix. In PCA it is done on the covariance matrix: the eigenvectors give the directions of the principal components and the eigenvalues the variance along each. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#5-pca-in-five-steps) | PCA |
| <span id="G-664">G-664</span> | Eigenbasis | A basis made of eigenvectors of a matrix. Seen in this basis the matrix only scales each basis vector, so it becomes a diagonal matrix, which makes powers such as $A^k$ easy to compute. | [Note MA-056](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues.md#62-changing-to-an-eigenbasis) | Eigenvectors and eigenvalues |
| <span id="G-665">G-665</span> | Eigenvalue | The number by which a matrix stretches or shrinks one of its eigenvectors: $A\mathbf v = \lambda\mathbf v$. In PCA, the eigenvalues of the covariance matrix are the variances along the principal components. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#42-the-vectors-that-do-not-turn) | Eigenvectors and eigenvalues |
| <span id="G-666">G-666</span> | Eigenvector | A non-zero vector that a matrix only stretches or shrinks, without turning it off its own line: $A\mathbf v = \lambda\mathbf v$. In PCA, the eigenvectors of the covariance matrix are the directions of the principal components. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#42-the-vectors-that-do-not-turn) | Eigenvectors and eigenvalues |
| <span id="G-667">G-667</span> | Elastic Net regression (Elastic Net) | Linear regression whose loss gets both the L2 (ridge) and the L1 (lasso) penalty, each with its own strength. It shrinks the coefficients and can set some to 0, so we need not choose between ridge and lasso in advance. | [Note ML-068](ML/06-regression/ML-068-elastic-net/ML-068-elastic-net.md#1-overview) | Elastic Net |
| <span id="G-669">G-669</span> | ElasticNetCV | scikit-learn's Elastic Net that picks alpha and l1_ratio by cross-validation. | [Note ML-068](ML/06-regression/ML-068-elastic-net/ML-068-elastic-net.md#6-elastic-net-on-the-diabetes-data) | Elastic Net |
| <span id="G-670">G-670</span> | Elbow curve | A plot of WCSS (how far points sit from their own cluster centre) against the number of clusters k; the point where it bends is used to choose k. | [Note ML-122](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-122-kmeans-intuition/ML-122-kmeans-intuition.md#52-the-elbow-curve) | Elbow method and WCSS |
| <span id="G-671">G-671</span> | Elbow method | Choosing k at the point where the elbow curve bends from steep to flat. | [Note ML-122](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-122-kmeans-intuition/ML-122-kmeans-intuition.md#5-choosing-k-the-elbow-method) | Elbow method and WCSS |
| <span id="G-672">G-672</span> | Elbow point | The k where the elbow curve bends, after which adding clusters barely lowers WCSS; it is the number of clusters the elbow method picks. | [Note ML-122](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-122-kmeans-intuition/ML-122-kmeans-intuition.md#54-reading-the-elbow) | Elbow method and WCSS |
| <span id="G-673">G-673</span> | Elongated bowl | A loss surface stretched far more in one direction than another, with long elliptical contours, as a sparse feature produces. Gradient descent on it wastes steps moving along the steep direction and crawls along the flat one. | [Note DL-036](DL/03-optimizers/DL-036-adagrad/DL-036-adagrad.md#4-the-problem-a-sparse-feature-stretches-the-loss) | AdaGrad |
| <span id="G-674">G-674</span> | ELU | Exponential linear unit: an activation that equals ReLU for positive inputs and follows a smooth exponential curve levelling off at $-\alpha$ for negative ones, so negative inputs still give a gradient and nodes do not die. Formula: $z$ for $z \ge 0$, $\alpha(e^{z} - 1)$ for $z < 0$. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#61-elu) | Leaky ReLU, PReLU, ELU and SELU |
| <span id="G-675">G-675</span> | EM algorithm (expectation maximization) | A method for finding the maximum likelihood when some variables are hidden (latent): it repeats two steps, an E-step and an M-step, until the fit settles. | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#3-the-two-steps-and-the-algorithm) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-677">G-677</span> | Embedding | A learned vector representing a user, item or word, compared with others by dot products. | [Note DL-057](DL/05-rnn/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis.md#6-word-embeddings) | Word embeddings |
| <span id="G-676">G-676</span> | Embedding vector | The list of numbers that represents one word, drawn as an arrow from the origin; words used in similar ways get nearby arrows, so a model can compare words through their vectors. | [Note DL-076](DL/06-transformers/DL-076-self-attention-geometric-intuition/DL-076-self-attention-geometric-intuition.md#3-words-as-arrows) | Word embeddings |
| <span id="G-678">G-678</span> | Empirical (experimental) probability | The share of trials in which an event happened, counted from data. It estimates the probability when it cannot be worked out from the sample space. | [Note MA-011](MA/02-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability.md#1-overview) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-679">G-679</span> | Empirical CDF (ECDF) | The share of a sample's values at or below $x$; a step-function estimate of the CDF. | [Note MA-027](MA/03-distributions/MA-027-pdf-and-cdf-in-practice/MA-027-pdf-and-cdf-in-practice.md#31-the-empirical-cdf) | Cumulative distribution function (CDF) |
| <span id="G-680">G-680</span> | encode | The `KBinsDiscretizer` parameter choosing ordinal (bin numbers) or one-hot output. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#9-kbinsdiscretizer-in-scikit-learn) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-682">G-682</span> | Encoder | The part of a seq2seq model that reads the input sequence and summarises it. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#4-stage-1-the-encoderdecoder-2014) | Sequence-to-sequence (encoder-decoder) |
| <span id="G-681">G-681</span> | Encoder block | One layer of the transformer encoder: multi-head attention and then a feed-forward network, each followed by add and norm. It turns each word's vector into a more context-aware one; the encoder stacks 6 of them. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#1-overview) | Transformer encoder |
| <span id="G-683">G-683</span> | Encoder-only / decoder-only transformer | A transformer that keeps only the encoder (BERT) or only the decoder (GPT). | [Note DL-071](DL/06-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers.md#8-advantages) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-684">G-684</span> | Encoder–decoder attention | The paper's name for cross-attention, also called source attention: the decoder's attention layer whose queries come from the output sentence and whose keys and values come from the encoder, so each word being written can draw on the input sentence. | [Note DL-083](DL/06-transformers/DL-083-cross-attention/DL-083-cross-attention.md#1-overview) | Cross-attention |
| <span id="G-685">G-685</span> | Encoding | Two senses: the rulebook that maps text characters to stored bytes (Video 15); or turning categories into numbers (categorical encoding, Video 26). | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#12-text-encoding-encoding) | Encoding categorical data |
| <span id="G-686">G-686</span> | End of distribution imputation | Filling every gap in a numerical column with a value at the far edge of its distribution, $\mu \pm 3\sigma$ for a normal column or $Q_3 + 1.5\thinspace\text{IQR}$ for a skewed one, so the model can tell which rows were missing. | [Note ML-035](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data.md#6-end-of-distribution-imputation) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-687">G-687</span> | End-to-end product | A complete product, from raw data to software that users use. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#23-why-the-whole-cycle-matters) | ML development life cycle |
| <span id="G-688">G-688</span> | Endpoint | One address of an API that returns one kind of data. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#4-asking-an-api-for-data) | APIs |
| <span id="G-690">G-690</span> | Ensemble | Several trained models whose predictions are combined. | [Note ML-095](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-095-ensemble-learning/ML-095-ensemble-learning.md#3-how-an-ensemble-predicts) | Ensemble learning |
| <span id="G-689">G-689</span> | Ensemble learning | Combining several models into one stronger model. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#84-ensemble-learning) | Ensemble learning |
| <span id="G-691">G-691</span> | Entropy | A measure of disorder (impurity) of the class labels in a node, $-\sum p_i \log_2 p_i$: 0 when all rows are one class, 1 for a 50/50 two-class node. A decision tree splits where entropy drops most (highest information gain). | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#61-disorder-and-uncertainty) | Entropy, information gain and Gini |
| <span id="G-694">G-694</span> | Environment | In reinforcement learning, the world the agent acts in, such as the road or a game board; it rewards or punishes each action, and the agent learns from that. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#51-agent-environment-policy-and-reward) | Reinforcement learning |
| <span id="G-692">G-692</span> | Environment file | A file (`environment.yml`) listing an environment's name, channel and every package with its exact version, so anyone can rebuild the same environment and get the same results. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#51-recreating-the-environment-from-a-file) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-693">G-693</span> | Environment variable | A named value stored on the computer outside the code and read with `os.environ`; used to keep secrets such as API keys out of notebooks that get shared. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#41-the-api-key) | APIs |
| <span id="G-695">G-695</span> | Epigraph | The region on and above a function's graph; convex exactly when the function is convex. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#3-convex-functions-and-their-sets) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-696">G-696</span> | Epoch | One full update of the parameters using the whole training set. | [Note DL-005](DL/01-basics/DL-005-perceptron-trick/DL-005-perceptron-trick.md#7-loops-and-epochs) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-697">G-697</span> | eps (epsilon) | The radius of the neighbourhood DBSCAN examines around each point. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#5-measuring-density-eps-and-minpts) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-698">G-698</span> | eps-neighbourhood | All points within distance eps of a point (the circle of radius eps around it); DBSCAN counts the points in it to judge how dense the data is there. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#5-measuring-density-eps-and-minpts) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-699">G-699</span> | Equal frequency binning | Binning into bins holding the same number of rows, with the quantiles as edges; also called quantile binning. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#4-kinds-of-discretization) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-700">G-700</span> | Equal variances (homogeneity of variance) | The assumption that two populations have the same variance, $\sigma_1^2 = \sigma_2^2$. Student's t-test needs it; Welch's t-test does not, and Levene's test checks it. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#21-assumptions) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-701">G-701</span> | Equal width binning | Binning into bins of the same width, $(\max - \min)/k$; also called uniform binning. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#4-kinds-of-discretization) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-702">G-702</span> | Equally likely outcomes | Outcomes that all have the same probability, such as the faces of a fair die. | [Note MA-011](MA/02-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability.md#4-theoretical-probability) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-703">G-703</span> | Equation of a hyperplane | One equation for a flat boundary in any number of dimensions, a line in 2D, a plane in 3D, a hyperplane beyond: $w^{\mathsf T}x + w_0 = 0$. | [Note MA-051](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-051-equation-of-a-hyperplane/MA-051-equation-of-a-hyperplane.md#4-the-vector-form) | Equation of a hyperplane |
| <span id="G-704">G-704</span> | Equiprobable | Equally likely: every outcome has the same probability, as the six faces of a fair die, so each has probability 1 divided by the number of outcomes. | [Note MA-020](MA/03-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions.md#3-probability-distributions-as-tables) | Probability distributions |
| <span id="G-705">G-705</span> | Error (residual) | The gap between an actual value and the model's prediction on one data point: actual minus predicted. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#34-the-best-fit-line) | Best-fit line and squared error |
| <span id="G-707">G-707</span> | errors="coerce" | The `pd.to_numeric` option that turns values it cannot convert into NaN instead of stopping. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#41-the-numerical-part) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-708">G-708</span> | Estimated PMF | Each value's share of many repeated trials, used as its probability. | [Note MA-021](MA/03-distributions/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf.md#5-estimating-a-pmf-by-simulation) | Probability mass function (PMF) |
| <span id="G-709">G-709</span> | estimator | The scikit-learn bagging setting that names the model to train many copies of (the base model); formerly `base_estimator`. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#2-seeing-bagging-on-decision-surfaces) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-710">G-710</span> | estimators_features_ | The column numbers each trained base model was given. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#33-which-observations-and-features-each-tree-got) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-711">G-711</span> | estimators_samples_ | The row numbers each trained base model was given. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#33-which-observations-and-features-each-tree-got) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-712">G-712</span> | Eta ($\eta$) | XGBoost's name for the learning rate; default 0.3. | [Note ML-118](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-118-xgboost-regression/ML-118-xgboost-regression.md#9-stage-2-adding-the-tree-with-a-learning-rate) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-713">G-713</span> | eta0 | The starting learning rate in SGDRegressor. | [Note ML-058](ML/06-regression/ML-058-stochastic-gradient-descent/ML-058-stochastic-gradient-descent.md#7-sgd-in-scikit-learn) | Stochastic gradient descent |
| <span id="G-714">G-714</span> | ETL | Extract, transform, load: copying data from source systems into a warehouse. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#42-where-data-comes-from) | ML development life cycle |
| <span id="G-715">G-715</span> | Euclidean distance | The straight-line distance between two points. | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#41-the-euclidean-distance) | KNN imputer |
| <span id="G-716">G-716</span> | Euler's number $e$ | A fixed number, about 2.71828, that appears in the Poisson PMF. | [Note MA-032](MA/03-distributions/MA-032-poisson-distribution/MA-032-poisson-distribution.md#32-two-pieces-of-the-formula) | Poisson distribution |
| <span id="G-717">G-717</span> | Event | A set of outcomes, such as "the sum is at most 10". | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#25-event) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-718">G-718</span> | Evidence | The overall chance of seeing what we observed, counted over every possible hypothesis, written $P(E)$. Bayes' theorem divides by it: posterior = likelihood × prior / evidence. | [Note MA-018](MA/02-probability/MA-018-bayes-theorem/MA-018-bayes-theorem.md#3-the-names-of-the-four-parts) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-719">G-719</span> | Exact greedy algorithm | Finding a split by trying the midpoint between every pair of neighbouring sorted values. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#84-approximate-split-finding-with-quantile-bins) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-720">G-720</span> | Excess kurtosis | Kurtosis minus 3, so a normal distribution scores 0. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#4-excess-kurtosis-and-the-three-types) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-721">G-721</span> | Exhaustive events | Events that together cover the whole sample space, so at least one always happens. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#46-exhaustive-events) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-722">G-722</span> | Expected complete-data log-likelihood $Q$ | A score for how well the model explains the data if we also knew which group made each point: the log-likelihood of the observations together with their hidden labels, averaged over the labels with the responsibilities, written $Q$. The M-step of EM maximises it, which never lowers the real log-likelihood. | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#64-the-ascent-argument) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-723">G-723</span> | Expected count $E$ | The number of rows a category or cell would hold on average if the null hypothesis $H_0$ were true. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#21-observed-and-expected-counts) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-724">G-724</span> | Expected improvement | A score that picks which point Bayesian optimisation tries next: how much better than the best score so far a candidate point is expected to be, judged from the surrogate model's predicted mean and uncertainty. The point with the highest expected improvement is tried next. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#33-informed-search-versus-blind-search) | Bayesian optimisation |
| <span id="G-725">G-725</span> | Expected value $E[X]$ | The average value we would get over many repeats: each possible value times its probability, added up (the probability-weighted average of a random variable). Also called the long-run mean and written $\mu$. | [Note MA-012](MA/02-probability/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance.md#3-expected-value) | Expected value and variance of a random variable |
| <span id="G-726">G-726</span> | Expert system | Early AI: a human expert's knowledge written as rules, plus a program that applies them. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#31-how-an-expert-system-works) | Symbolic AI and expert systems |
| <span id="G-728">G-728</span> | Explained variance | How much of the data's spread one principal component keeps: the variance of the data along that component, equal to its eigenvalue. | [Note ML-048](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-048-pca-mnist/ML-048-pca-mnist.md#71-explained-variance) | PCA |
| <span id="G-727">G-727</span> | Explained variance ratio | The share of the data's total spread that one principal component keeps: its eigenvalue divided by the sum of all eigenvalues (its share of the total variance). Adding these shares from the largest down tells us how many components to keep. | [Note ML-048](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-048-pca-mnist/ML-048-pca-mnist.md#71-explained-variance) | PCA |
| <span id="G-729">G-729</span> | explained_variance_ | How much of the data's spread each principal component keeps (its eigenvalue), stored on a fitted PCA, largest first. | [Note ML-048](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-048-pca-mnist/ML-048-pca-mnist.md#6-what-a-fitted-pca-stores) | PCA |
| <span id="G-730">G-730</span> | Explicit programming | A human writing out every rule the computer follows. ML avoids it. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#2-defining-machine-learning) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-731">G-731</span> | Exploding gradient | Gradients growing huge as they pass back through many layers, making updates erratic. | [Note DL-018](DL/01-basics/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients.md#1-overview) | Exploding gradient and gradient clipping |
| <span id="G-732">G-732</span> | Exploratory data analysis (EDA) | Exploring data with summaries and plots to find patterns. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#6-exploratory-data-analysis-eda) | Exploratory data analysis |
| <span id="G-733">G-733</span> | Exponential distribution | The pattern of waiting times between random events: short waits are common and long waits rare (a right-skewed continuous distribution). | [Note MA-071](MA/08-likelihood/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions.md#31-the-distribution-of-waiting-times) | Exponential distribution |
| <span id="G-734">G-734</span> | Exponential time | Work that is multiplied by a constant factor with every step of the input size. | [Note DL-019](DL/01-basics/DL-019-mlp-memoization/DL-019-mlp-memoization.md#32-why-it-is-slow) | Memoization |
| <span id="G-735">G-735</span> | Exponentially weighted moving average (EWMA) | A running average in which recent values count most and older values count less and less, updated as $V_t = \beta V_{t-1} + (1-\beta)\theta_t$. Momentum and Adam use it to average the gradients. | [Note DL-033](DL/03-optimizers/DL-033-exponentially-weighted-moving-average/DL-033-exponentially-weighted-moving-average.md#1-overview) | Exponentially weighted moving average (EWMA) |
| <span id="G-736">G-736</span> | export_graphviz | scikit-learn function that writes a trained decision tree as text in Graphviz's DOT format, which Graphviz then draws as a tree diagram. | [Note ML-094](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-094-dtreeviz/ML-094-dtreeviz.md#2-the-problem-with-the-default-tree-drawing) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-737">G-737</span> | export_text | scikit-learn function that prints a trained tree as indented text. | [Note ML-104](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-104-bagging-vs-random-forest/ML-104-bagging-vs-random-forest.md#41-the-bagged-trees) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-738">G-738</span> | Extrapolation | Predicting for inputs outside the range of the training data. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#53-the-intercept-b-the-starting-value) | Simple linear regression |
| <span id="G-739">G-739</span> | F distribution | The shape that a ratio of two independent spreads (variances) follows: a right-skewed continuous distribution with two degrees-of-freedom parameters. ANOVA compares its F statistic with this distribution to get a p-value. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#6-the-f-distribution-and-the-decision) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-740">G-740</span> | F statistic | One number that compares how far apart the group means are with how spread out the values are inside each group: the variance between the group means divided by the variance within the groups, $MSB / MSW$. One-way ANOVA computes it; a large F means the group means differ more than the spread inside the groups would explain. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#5-the-f-statistic) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-741">G-741</span> | f-string | Text starting with `f` in which `{name}` is replaced by a value. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#7-looping-over-every-page) | APIs |
| <span id="G-742">G-742</span> | F-test | A check of whether two groups have the same spread, made from the ratio of the two sample variances (a hypothesis test of equal population variances); an alternative to Levene's test. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#22-checking-equal-variances-levenes-test) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-743">G-743</span> | F1 score | One number that combines precision and recall as their harmonic mean, $2PR/(P + R)$; it is high only when both are high, so it is used when both kinds of mistake matter. | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#4-f1-score) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-744">G-744</span> | Facet grid | The same plot repeated side by side, one panel per category of another column. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#52-the-facet-grid) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-745">G-745</span> | Fact (here) | An athlete's sport, as the model's ranking of 10 sports after "⟨name⟩ plays the sport of". | [Note DL-089](DL/06-transformers/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts.md#6-a-real-model-does-gpt-2-small-know-sports) | MLP blocks as fact storage |
| <span id="G-746">G-746</span> | Fail to reject $H_0$ | The decision that the evidence against the null hypothesis $H_0$ is not strong enough to drop it; it does not prove $H_0$. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#53-failing-to-reject-h_0-does-not-prove-it) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-747">G-747</span> | False negative (FN) | Predicted negative, but actually positive; a Type II error. | [Note ML-075](ML/07-classification/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix.md#42-reading-it) | Confusion matrix |
| <span id="G-748">G-748</span> | False positive (FP) | Predicted positive, but actually negative; a Type I error. | [Note ML-075](ML/07-classification/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix.md#42-reading-it) | Confusion matrix |
| <span id="G-749">G-749</span> | False positive rate (FPR) | The fraction of real negatives the model wrongly flags. | [Note ML-077](ML/07-classification/ML-077-roc-auc/ML-077-roc-auc.md#32-false-positive-rate) | ROC curve and AUC |
| <span id="G-750">G-750</span> | Family size | `SibSp` + `Parch` + 1: the number of people in a passenger's travelling family. | [Note ML-044](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting.md#32-building-the-family-size) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-751">G-751</span> | Family type | Family size grouped into alone, small family (2 to 4) and large family (5 or more). | [Note ML-044](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting.md#33-grouping-the-size-into-a-family-type) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-752">G-752</span> | Familywise error rate | The chance of at least one false alarm (Type I error) when running several tests: $1 - (1 - \alpha)^m$ for $m$ tests, each at significance level $\alpha$. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#2-why-not-several-t-tests) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-753">G-753</span> | Famous probability distributions | Common named shapes such as normal, uniform, binomial and Poisson. | [Note MA-020](MA/03-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions.md#6-famous-distributions) | Probability distributions |
| <span id="G-754">G-754</span> | Fan-in | The number of inputs coming into a node, equal to the size of the previous layer. Xavier and He initialisation use it to set the spread of the starting weights. | [Note DL-030](DL/02-training/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization.md#32-fan-in-and-fan-out) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-755">G-755</span> | Fan-out | The number of outputs leaving a node, equal to the size of the next layer. Xavier initialisation uses it, with fan-in, to set the spread of the starting weights. | [Note DL-030](DL/02-training/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization.md#32-fan-in-and-fan-out) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-756">G-756</span> | Fashion-MNIST | A dataset of 28 × 28 greyscale images of 10 kinds of clothing, in the same format as MNIST. | [Note DL-046](DL/04-cnn/DL-046-cnn-vs-ann/DL-046-cnn-vs-ann.md#6-the-comparison-on-real-data) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-757">G-757</span> | Fat tail (heavy tail) | A tail that falls to zero slowly, so extreme values are relatively common. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#3-kurtosis-a-measure-of-tailedness) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-758">G-758</span> | Favourable outcome | An outcome that belongs to the event we are measuring. | [Note MA-011](MA/02-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability.md#4-theoretical-probability) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-759">G-759</span> | Feasible region | All the points that obey every rule (constraint) of an optimisation problem; the answer must be one of them. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#2-constrained-optimisation-problems) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-772">G-772</span> | Feature | One piece of information about each example that a model uses (e.g. a student's CGPA). | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#52-features-chosen-by-us-or-learned) | Features |
| <span id="G-760">G-760</span> | Feature construction | Creating a new column by hand from existing ones, e.g. rooms + washrooms into area. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#5-the-four-parts-of-feature-engineering) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-761">G-761</span> | Feature engineering | Choosing, removing and creating input columns (features) so that the model gets the information it needs in a form it can use. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#71-feature-engineering) | Feature engineering |
| <span id="G-762">G-762</span> | Feature extraction | Letting an algorithm such as PCA produce new columns from the existing ones (compare feature construction, where we make them by hand). | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#5-the-four-parts-of-feature-engineering) | Feature extraction |
| <span id="G-764">G-764</span> | Feature importance | How much a column helped a tree: its share of all the impurity reduction (how much purer the nodes became) achieved by the tree's splits; the shares add up to 1. It shows which columns the tree relied on and helps with feature selection. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#75-feature-importance) | Feature importance |
| <span id="G-763">G-763</span> | Feature importance (weights) | Reading the size of a weight as how much its input matters, fair only on scaled inputs. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#6-weights-as-feature-importance) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-765">G-765</span> | Feature map ($\phi$) | A rule that gives each point extra coordinates built from its own values, such as $\phi(x) = (x, x^2)$, moving the data into a higher-dimensional space. Data that no straight line can separate becomes linearly separable there. | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#32-the-feature-map-and-the-kernel) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-766">G-766</span> | Feature map (CNN) | The grid of numbers a filter produces; large where the filter's pattern is present. | [Note DL-042](DL/04-cnn/DL-042-convolution-operation/DL-042-convolution-operation.md#61-filter-and-feature-map) | Convolution operation and feature maps |
| <span id="G-767">G-767</span> | Feature scaling (scaling) | Putting columns on the same scale, so no column dominates distances. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#7-scaling-the-inputs) | Feature scaling |
| <span id="G-768">G-768</span> | Feature selection | Keeping only the useful input columns and dropping the rest. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#5-the-four-parts-of-feature-engineering) | Feature selection |
| <span id="G-2219">G-2219</span> | Feature space | The space whose axes are the features; the feature map takes the data into a higher-dimensional one. | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#31-lifting-1d-data-into-2d) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-769">G-769</span> | Feature splitting | Breaking a column that holds several facts into one column per fact. | [Note ML-044](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting.md#52-splitting-a-feature) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-770">G-770</span> | Feature transformation | Changing a column into a form the model can use better. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#5-the-four-parts-of-feature-engineering) | Feature transformation |
| <span id="G-771">G-771</span> | Feature vector | The vector of input values of one data point. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#31-the-iris-flowers) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-773">G-773</span> | feature_importances_ | The value stored on a trained tree model that holds the feature importance of every column. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#75-feature-importance) | Feature importance |
| <span id="G-775">G-775</span> | Feed-forward network | A network in which information moves only from the first layer to the last. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#23-recurrent-neural-network-rnn-and-lstm) | Types of neural networks |
| <span id="G-774">G-774</span> | Feed-forward network (FFN) | A small two-layer network inside each transformer block that works on each word's vector on its own: dense layers 512 → 2048 with ReLU → 512. Attention mixes the words; this network transforms each word and adds the block's only per-word non-linearity. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#53-the-feed-forward-network) | Transformer encoder |
| <span id="G-776">G-776</span> | Fence (fences) | A cut-off for spotting outliers in a box plot: 1.5 times the IQR (the width of the middle half of the data) beyond the box, $Q_1 - 1.5\thinspace\text{IQR}$ or $Q_3 + 1.5\thinspace\text{IQR}$; values past it are possible outliers. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#81-whiskers-and-outliers) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-2197">G-2197</span> | Fill error | The average gap between filled values and the true values they replace, measured on values we hid on purpose. | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#73-is-the-knn-fill-closer-to-the-truth) | KNN imputer |
| <span id="G-777">G-777</span> | Filter (kernel) | A small grid of weights slid over the image; at each position it multiplies and adds, so it responds strongly where its pattern (such as an edge) appears and produces a feature map. Its depth equals the input's number of channels. | [Note DL-042](DL/04-cnn/DL-042-convolution-operation/DL-042-convolution-operation.md#41-a-moving-average) | Convolution operation and feature maps |
| <span id="G-779">G-779</span> | Fine-tuning | Training a pre-trained model further on a small dataset for a specific task. | [Note DL-053](DL/04-cnn/DL-053-transfer-learning/DL-053-transfer-learning.md#62-fine-tuning) | Transfer learning (feature extraction and fine-tuning) |
| <span id="G-780">G-780</span> | First moment $m_t$ | A running average of the recent gradients in which older ones count less (an EWMA), so it estimates the gradient's mean; written $m_t$. Adam uses it in its update. | [Note DL-038](DL/03-optimizers/DL-038-adam/DL-038-adam.md#4-the-update-rule) | Adam |
| <span id="G-781">G-781</span> | First-order condition | A test for a bowl-shaped (convex) function: a differentiable function is convex exactly when every tangent plane lies on or below its graph. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#42-first-order-condition-tangents-lie-below) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-782">G-782</span> | Fisher's exact test | A test for small 2 by 2 count tables that computes the p-value exactly instead of approximating it; used when expected counts fall below 5. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#71-conditions) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-783">G-783</span> | fit / transform | Learn the scaler's numbers from the training set / apply them to any data. | [Note ML-023](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-023-standardization/ML-023-standardization.md#63-fit-on-the-training-set-transform-both) | Standardization |
| <span id="G-784">G-784</span> | fit_predict | Trains a clustering model and returns the cluster of every row. | [Note ML-123](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-123-kmeans-code/ML-123-kmeans-code.md#5-training-k-means-with-k--4) | K-means |
| <span id="G-785">G-785</span> | fit_transform | Learns the numbers from the data and applies them in one call (fit, then transform); used on the training set only. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#41-fever-fill-the-missing-values) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-786">G-786</span> | Fitting a distribution | Choosing a family of distributions for data, then choosing its parameters. | [Note MA-070](MA/08-likelihood/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation.md#2-fitting-a-distribution-to-data) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-787">G-787</span> | Five-number summary | The five numbers that describe a column's centre and spread: minimum, Q1, median, Q3 and maximum. A box plot draws them. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#8-box-plot) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-788">G-788</span> | Flatten layer | A layer that reshapes a multi-dimensional input into one dimension; no parameters. | [Note DL-012](DL/01-basics/DL-012-mnist-ann/DL-012-mnist-ann.md#41-flatten-from-an-image-to-a-row) | ANN for classification |
| <span id="G-789">G-789</span> | Flattening | Reshaping a matrix into one long vector so that derivatives stay matrices. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#8-gradients-of-matrices) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-790">G-790</span> | FLOP | One floating-point operation; training cost is counted in FLOPs. | [Note DL-086](DL/06-transformers/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end.md#72-hardware-and-training-time) | The transformer end to end (capstone) |
| <span id="G-2227">G-2227</span> | Fold | One of the equal blocks the data is cut into for cross-validation; each fold is the test block once. | [Note ML-106](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-106-random-forest-tuning/ML-106-random-forest-tuning.md#4-one-split-is-not-enough) | Cross-validation |
| <span id="G-791">G-791</span> | For loop | Code that repeats once for each item of a collection. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#18-reading-a-big-file-in-chunks-chunksize) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-792">G-792</span> | Forest-level hyperparameters | The settings that shape the forest itself: n_estimators, max_features, bootstrap, max_samples. | [Note ML-105](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-105-random-forest-hyperparameters/ML-105-random-forest-hyperparameters.md#2-the-three-groups) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-793">G-793</span> | Forget gate ($f_t$) | The LSTM gate that decides what to erase from the memory (cell state): a sigmoid layer whose outputs, between 0 and 1, scale each entry of the previous cell state $c_{t-1}$. | [Note DL-062](DL/05-rnn/DL-062-lstm-architecture/DL-062-lstm-architecture.md#3-what-the-cell-must-do) | LSTM gates (forget, input, output) and cell state |
| <span id="G-795">G-795</span> | Format string | A pattern such as `"%d/%m/%Y"` telling `pd.to_datetime` how dates are written. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#4-converting-text-to-a-datetime-type) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-796">G-796</span> | Forward and backward RNN | The left-to-right and the right-to-left halves of a bidirectional RNN. | [Note DL-066](DL/05-rnn/DL-066-bidirectional-rnn/DL-066-bidirectional-rnn.md#41-two-rnns-opposite-directions) | Bidirectional RNNs |
| <span id="G-797">G-797</span> | Forward propagation | Passing one row of inputs through the network, layer by layer, to get the prediction. | [Note DL-010](DL/01-basics/DL-010-forward-propagation/DL-010-forward-propagation.md#1-overview) | Forward propagation |
| <span id="G-798">G-798</span> | Forward selection | Feature selection that starts empty and adds the best column at a time. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#81-pixels-as-columns-the-mnist-dataset) | Feature selection |
| <span id="G-799">G-799</span> | Four fundamental subspaces | Four sets of directions tied to every matrix that show what it does: which input directions it uses, which it squashes to zero, which outputs it can reach and which it never reaches. They are the row space, null space, column space and left null space. | [Note MA-058](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-058-computing-the-svd/MA-058-computing-the-svd.md#6-the-four-subspaces) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-800">G-800</span> | FPGA | A reprogrammable chip: fast and low-power, but expensive. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#52-hardware) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-801">G-801</span> | Frame | One image in a video. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#65-5d-video) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-802">G-802</span> | Framing an ML problem | Turning a business problem into a precise ML task that can be built and measured. | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#1-overview) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-804">G-804</span> | Fraud detection | Spotting dishonest transactions; here the outliers are what we want to find. | [Note ML-040](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-040-what-are-outliers/ML-040-what-are-outliers.md#42-keep-when-outliers-are-the-point) | Outliers |
| <span id="G-805">G-805</span> | Freeze | Mark a layer's weights as not trainable, so that training leaves them unchanged. | [Note DL-053](DL/04-cnn/DL-053-transfer-learning/DL-053-transfer-learning.md#42-replace-the-top-freeze-the-base) | Transfer learning (feature extraction and fine-tuning) |
| <span id="G-808">G-808</span> | Frequency | How many times a value or category occurs. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#4-count-plot) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-806">G-806</span> | Frequency and wavelength | How fast a wave repeats, and the distance after which it repeats; one is the inverse of the other. | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#6-the-formula-of-attention-is-all-you-need) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-807">G-807</span> | Frequency distribution table | A table of each value or category with the number of times it occurs. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#21-frequency-distribution-table) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-809">G-809</span> | Frobenius norm | One number for the size of a matrix: the square root of the sum of all its squared entries, also equal to $\sqrt{\sum\sigma_i^2}$ over its singular values $\sigma_i$. | [Note MA-059](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-059-low-rank-approximation/MA-059-low-rank-approximation.md#42-the-eckartyoung-theorem) | Low-rank approximation (truncated SVD) |
| <span id="G-810">G-810</span> | Full SVD | The version of the SVD that keeps every column of $U$ and $V$, so both are square and $\Sigma$ has the same shape as $A$, with the singular values on its diagonal padded with zeros. It gives complete orthonormal bases for both the input and the output space. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#61-the-full-svd) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-813">G-813</span> | Fully grown tree | A tree split until every leaf is pure; usually overfits. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#21-a-fully-grown-tree-overfits) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-814">G-814</span> | func | The `FunctionTransformer` parameter that holds the function to apply. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#74-applying-the-log-with-functiontransformer) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-816">G-816</span> | Function | A rule that assigns exactly one output to every input, written $f: \mathbb{R} \to \mathbb{R}$, $x \mapsto f(x)$. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#2-functions-one-number-in-one-number-out) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-815">G-815</span> | Function of several variables | A function that takes several numbers in (a vector of $n$ inputs) and gives one number out, $f: \mathbb{R}^n \to \mathbb{R}$; a loss that depends on many weights is one. | [Note MA-062](MA/06-calculus/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients.md#2-functions-of-several-variables) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-817">G-817</span> | Function, lambda | A named reusable piece of code (`def`), and a one-line unnamed one. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#16-transforming-values-while-reading-converters) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-818">G-818</span> | Functional API | The Keras way of building a model as a graph of layers, by calling each layer on the output of the one before; unlike a Sequential model it allows several inputs or outputs, branches and skip connections. | [Note DL-054](DL/04-cnn/DL-054-keras-functional-api/DL-054-keras-functional-api.md#1-overview) | Keras functional API |
| <span id="G-819">G-819</span> | FunctionTransformer | scikit-learn's class that applies any function we give it to the data. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#3-mathematical-transformers-in-scikit-learn) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-820">G-820</span> | Gain (XGBoost) | How much a split improves an XGBoost tree: the similarity scores of the two children minus the similarity score of the parent; the split with the largest gain is chosen. | [Note ML-118](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-118-xgboost-regression/ML-118-xgboost-regression.md#6-gain-choosing-the-root-split) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-2213">G-2213</span> | Gambler's fallacy | The mistaken belief that after a run of heads, tails is "due"; independent tosses have no memory. | [Note MA-016](MA/02-probability/MA-016-independent-events/MA-016-independent-events.md#3-examples) | Independent and mutually exclusive events |
| <span id="G-821">G-821</span> | Gamma ($\gamma$, `min_split_loss`) | A setting that stops weak splits in XGBoost: a split is kept only if its gain exceeds gamma; default 0. | [Note ML-118](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-118-xgboost-regression/ML-118-xgboost-regression.md#13-gamma-pruning-weak-splits) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-823">G-823</span> | gamma (RBF kernel) | How far one point's influence reaches in the RBF kernel; large gamma gives tighter boundaries. | [Note ML-090](ML/07-classification/ML-090-kernel-trick-code/ML-090-kernel-trick-code.md#8-gamma-how-far-one-points-influence-reaches) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-822">G-822</span> | Gamma distribution | A family of right-skewed continuous distributions with a shape and a scale parameter. | [Note MA-033](MA/04-inference/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt.md#5-simulating-the-clt) | Central limit theorem |
| <span id="G-824">G-824</span> | Garbage in, garbage out | Bad input data always gives bad results. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#5-poor-quality-data) | Poor-quality data |
| <span id="G-825">G-825</span> | Gate | A part of the LSTM cell that controls what moves into, out of, or along the cell state. | [Note DL-061](DL/05-rnn/DL-061-lstm/DL-061-lstm.md#8-the-three-gates-in-one-line-each) | LSTM gates (forget, input, output) and cell state |
| <span id="G-826">G-826</span> | Gated recurrent unit (GRU) | An RNN architecture with one memory (the hidden state) and two gates, reset and update. | [Note DL-064](DL/05-rnn/DL-064-gru/DL-064-gru.md#1-overview) | GRU (gated recurrent unit) |
| <span id="G-827">G-827</span> | Gaussian distribution | Another name for the normal distribution: the symmetric, bell-shaped continuous distribution set by its mean and standard deviation, used to model many measurements. | [Note MA-024](MA/03-distributions/MA-024-normal-distribution/MA-024-normal-distribution.md#2-what-the-normal-distribution-is) | Normal distribution |
| <span id="G-828">G-828</span> | Gaussian kernel | In kernel density estimation, a bump shaped like the normal curve placed on every data point; adding the bumps gives a smooth estimate of the density. It is the usual default kernel. | [Note MA-023](MA/03-distributions/MA-023-density-estimation-kde/MA-023-density-estimation-kde.md#51-how-a-kde-is-built) | Kernel density estimation (KDE) |
| <span id="G-829">G-829</span> | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) | A model for data made of several overlapping groups: one normal curve per group, combined as a weighted sum $\sum_k \pi_k N(x \mid \mu_k, \sigma_k^2)$. Each point is shared among the curves by probabilities instead of being given to one group. | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#32-the-standard-terms) | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) |
| <span id="G-830">G-830</span> | Gaussian Naive Bayes | Naive Bayes that models each numerical input as normally distributed within each class. | [Note ML-084](ML/07-classification/ML-084-gaussian-naive-bayes/ML-084-gaussian-naive-bayes.md#1-overview) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-831">G-831</span> | Gaussian noise | Random errors that follow a bell curve centred on 0, $N(0, \sigma^2)$. Assuming them and maximising the likelihood (MLE) gives the squared-error loss. | [Note MA-072](MA/08-likelihood/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning.md#31-the-model) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-832">G-832</span> | Gaussian process | A model that predicts a value and its uncertainty at every point; a common choice of surrogate, the stand-in model that Bayesian optimisation searches. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#33-informed-search-versus-blind-search) | Bayesian optimisation |
| <span id="G-833">G-833</span> | GaussianNB | scikit-learn's Gaussian Naive Bayes. | [Note ML-084](ML/07-classification/ML-084-gaussian-naive-bayes/ML-084-gaussian-naive-bayes.md#4-the-numbers) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-834">G-834</span> | GELU | A smooth activation function that multiplies each input $x$ by $\Phi(x)$, the chance that a standard normal value is below $x$: $x\thinspace\Phi(x)$ (Gaussian Error Linear Unit). GPT's MLPs use it. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#64-gelu) | GELU activation |
| <span id="G-836">G-836</span> | General contextual embedding | A word vector that changes with the surrounding words but is computed without any learned parameters, so it is the same for every task. | [Note DL-074](DL/06-transformers/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step.md#6-the-problem-nothing-to-learn) | Contextual embeddings |
| <span id="G-837">G-837</span> | General score | A way to score how well a decoder state $s_i$ matches an encoder state $h_j$ through a learned matrix, $s_i^\top W_a h_j$ (Luong's general score). It lets the two states have different sizes and lets the model learn which directions of similarity matter. | [Note DL-070](DL/06-transformers/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention.md#51-a-simpler-score) | Bahdanau (additive) attention |
| <span id="G-838">G-838</span> | Generalisation (generalise) | How well a model performs on new data it was not trained on. | [Note ML-070](ML/07-classification/ML-070-perceptron-code/ML-070-perceptron-code.md#62-why-it-matters) | Overfitting |
| <span id="G-840">G-840</span> | Generative adversarial network (GAN) | A generator and a discriminator competing, so that the generator learns to create realistic new data. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#25-generative-adversarial-network-gan) | Types of neural networks |
| <span id="G-841">G-841</span> | Generative AI | Models that create new content such as text, images, code or video. | [Note DL-071](DL/06-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers.md#64-generative-ai) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-842">G-842</span> | Generative process | A step-by-step recipe that produces data from a model. | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#51-the-two-steps) | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) |
| <span id="G-843">G-843</span> | Generator | The GAN network that creates new data. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#25-generative-adversarial-network-gan) | Types of neural networks |
| <span id="G-844">G-844</span> | Geometric mean | An average for values that multiply, such as growth factors: multiply the $n$ values together and take the $n$-th root. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#8-choosing-a-measure) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-2248">G-2248</span> | Geometric understanding | Knowing what a computation means as a picture: which tool fits, why it works, how to read the result. | [Note MA-002](MA/00-why-maths/MA-002-learning-maths-for-ml/MA-002-learning-maths-for-ml.md#51-two-halves-of-understanding) | How to learn the maths for ML |
| <span id="G-845">G-845</span> | get_dummies | pandas function that turns each category into its own 0/1 column (one-hot encoding); `drop_first=True` keeps $n - 1$ of them. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#61-get_dummies) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-846">G-846</span> | get_feature_names_out | `OneHotEncoder` method that returns the names of the new columns. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#74-fit-on-the-training-set-transform-both) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-847">G-847</span> | Gini impurity | A number for how mixed the class labels in a node are (impurity), $1 - \sum p_i^2$: 0 when all rows are one class, 0.5 for a 50/50 two-class node. A decision tree uses it, like entropy, to pick the split whose children are purest. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#82-the-formula) | Entropy, information gain and Gini |
| <span id="G-848">G-848</span> | Global minimum | The lowest point of the whole function. | [Note ML-056](ML/06-regression/ML-056-gradient-descent/ML-056-gradient-descent.md#8-the-shape-of-the-loss-function-matters) | Gradient descent |
| <span id="G-849">G-849</span> | Global pooling | Pooling that uses the whole feature map as one window, giving one number per map (its max or its average). It can replace the Flatten layer and needs far fewer parameters in the next layer, which reduces overfitting. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#82-global-pooling) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-850">G-850</span> | Glorot (Xavier) and He initialisation | Ways to choose the spread of random starting weights from the layer sizes. | [Note DL-018](DL/01-basics/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients.md#63-initialise-the-weights-properly) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-851">G-851</span> | GloVe | Word vectors learned from how often words appear near each other: training makes the dot product of two words' vectors match the log of their co-occurrence probability (Pennington et al. 2014). | [Note DL-072](DL/06-transformers/DL-072-meaning-as-direction/DL-072-meaning-as-direction.md#1-overview) | Word embeddings |
| <span id="G-852">G-852</span> | Good fit | Capturing the pattern while ignoring the noise. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#72-underfitting) | Overfitting |
| <span id="G-853">G-853</span> | Goodness-of-fit test | A check of whether the counts in one categorical column match the shares we claimed (a chi-square test against claimed proportions); $df = k - 1$ for $k$ categories. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#41-the-question) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-854">G-854</span> | Google Colab | Google's browser-based Jupyter notebooks, saved in Google Drive. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#7-google-colab) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-855">G-855</span> | GPT | A language model that writes text by predicting the next token again and again (Generative Pre-trained Transformer); it is built from transformer decoder blocks only. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#1-overview) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-856">G-856</span> | GPU | A graphics chip that runs deep learning maths much faster than a CPU. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#6-kaggle-notebooks) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-857">G-857</span> | Gradient $g_i$ | How fast row $i$'s loss changes as the previous prediction for that row moves (the first derivative of its loss with respect to that prediction). XGBoost uses it with the Hessian $h_i$ in a second-order approximation of the loss to build the next tree. | [Note ML-120](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-120-xgboost-maths/ML-120-xgboost-maths.md#8-the-second-order-approximation-of-the-objective) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-863">G-863</span> | Gradient (of the loss) | How fast the loss changes with each weight and bias, collected in one vector (the partial derivatives of the loss); it points in the direction in which the loss rises fastest. | [Note DL-015](DL/01-basics/DL-015-backpropagation-what/DL-015-backpropagation-what.md#1-overview) | Backpropagation |
| <span id="G-858">G-858</span> | Gradient as a row vector | Writing the gradient as one row of partial derivatives, $\nabla f = [\partial f/\partial x_1, \dots, \partial f/\partial x_n] \in \mathbb{R}^{1 \times n}$; this convention makes the chain rule a matrix product. | [Note MA-062](MA/06-calculus/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients.md#41-collecting-the-partial-derivatives) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-859">G-859</span> | Gradient boosting | A way to build a model from many trees added one by one: it starts from a simple guess, and each new tree is trained on the mistakes (pseudo-residuals) of the ensemble so far (a boosting algorithm). | [Note ML-114](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-114-gradient-boosting-intuition/ML-114-gradient-boosting-intuition.md#1-overview) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-860">G-860</span> | Gradient checking | Checking that a gradient formula is right by comparing it with a numerical estimate made by nudging each weight a tiny amount (finite differences), measured by the relative error. | [Note MA-062](MA/06-calculus/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients.md#7-checking-a-gradient-numerically) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-861">G-861</span> | Gradient clipping | Capping the gradient before each update: if its overall size (its norm) is above a chosen limit, it is scaled down to that limit with its direction kept, so exploding gradients cannot make huge weight jumps. | [Note DL-018](DL/01-basics/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients.md#73-gradient-clipping) | Exploding gradient and gradient clipping |
| <span id="G-862">G-862</span> | Gradient descent | Finding the lowest point of a function by repeated small steps downhill. | [Note ML-056](ML/06-regression/ML-056-gradient-descent/ML-056-gradient-descent.md#1-overview) | Gradient descent |
| <span id="G-864">G-864</span> | Gradient routing | In max pooling's backward pass, sending each gradient only to the position of its window's maximum. | [Note DL-048](DL/04-cnn/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers.md#6-back-through-max-pooling-route-to-the-maximum) | Backpropagation in a CNN |
| <span id="G-866">G-866</span> | Grand mean | The mean of all values from all groups pooled together; ANOVA measures how far the values and the group means lie from it to split the total variation. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#3-splitting-the-variation) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-867">G-867</span> | Grand total | The sum of every cell of a contingency table: the size of the whole sample. | [Note MA-013](MA/02-probability/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables.md#32-a-class-of-100-students) | Venn diagrams and contingency tables |
| <span id="G-868">G-868</span> | Graphviz | The graph-drawing program (`dot`) that lays out tree diagrams for dtreeviz and export_graphviz. | [Note ML-094](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-094-dtreeviz/ML-094-dtreeviz.md#3-installing-and-calling-dtreeviz) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-869">G-869</span> | GRE, TOEFL | Exams taken by students applying to graduate programmes abroad; the first two inputs of the admission data. | [Note DL-013](DL/01-basics/DL-013-graduate-admission-ann/DL-013-graduate-admission-ann.md#2-the-graduate-admission-data) | ANN for regression |
| <span id="G-870">G-870</span> | Greedy decoding | Predicting by choosing the most likely token at each step and feeding it back. | [Note DL-068](DL/06-transformers/DL-068-encoder-decoder/DL-068-encoder-decoder.md#6-prediction) | Sequence-to-sequence (encoder-decoder) |
| <span id="G-871">G-871</span> | Greedy search | Taking the best split at each node without looking ahead. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#73-greedy-top-down-recursive) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-872">G-872</span> | Grid search | Training a model for every combination of listed settings and keeping the best by cross-validation. | [Note ML-037](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample.md#71-the-idea) | Grid and random search |
| <span id="G-873">G-873</span> | Grid-like topology | Data arranged on a regular grid, such as a time series (1D, one value per time step) or an image (2D, rows and columns of pixels); the kind of data CNNs are built to process. | [Note DL-040](DL/04-cnn/DL-040-cnn-intuition/DL-040-cnn-intuition.md#1-overview) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
