| <span id="G-874">G-874</span> | Grouping effect | Elastic Net's tendency to give correlated inputs similar coefficients instead of keeping only one. | [Note ML-068](ML/06-regression/ML-068-elastic-net/ML-068-elastic-net.md#5-correlated-features-the-grouping-effect) | Elastic Net |
| <span id="G-875">G-875</span> | handle_unknown | `OneHotEncoder` parameter that decides what happens to categories never seen in training. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#74-fit-on-the-training-set-transform-both) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-876">G-876</span> | handle_unknown="ignore" | `OneHotEncoder` setting that outputs all zeros for a category not seen in training. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#42-one-hot-encoding-sex-and-embarked) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-877">G-877</span> | Hard assignment | Giving each observation wholly to one cluster (responsibility 0 or 1). | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#8-k-means-as-hard-em) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-878">G-878</span> | Hard voting | Predicting the label that most base models predict. | [Note ML-097](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-097-voting-classifier/ML-097-voting-classifier.md#31-hard-voting) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-879">G-879</span> | Hard-margin SVM | The SVM that allows no point inside the margin or on the wrong side; it needs perfectly separable data. | [Note ML-087](ML/07-classification/ML-087-svm-maths/ML-087-svm-maths.md#7-the-limit-hard-margin-svm) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-880">G-880</span> | Harmonic mean | An average that stays close to the smaller of the values, so one low value pulls it down; F1 uses it to combine precision and recall. For two values: $2ab/(a + b)$. | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#42-why-the-harmonic-mean) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-881">G-881</span> | He normal | A way to set starting weights for ReLU layers: draw them from a normal distribution with standard deviation $\sqrt{2/\text{fan-in}}$, so the signal keeps roughly the same size from layer to layer. | [Note DL-030](DL/02-training/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization.md#62-with-keras-initialisers) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-882">G-882</span> | He uniform | A way to set starting weights for ReLU layers: draw them evenly between $-\sqrt{6/\text{fan-in}}$ and $+\sqrt{6/\text{fan-in}}$, so the signal keeps roughly the same size from layer to layer. | [Note DL-030](DL/02-training/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization.md#62-with-keras-initialisers) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-883">G-883</span> | Head | One of several attention units that run side by side in a multi-head attention layer, each a complete self-attention with its own learned $W_Q$, $W_K$, $W_V$; each head gives its own table of attention weights, its own point of view. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#1-overview) | Multi-head attention |
| <span id="G-884">G-884</span> | Header (of a file) | The line of a file that holds the column names. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#2-csv-and-tsv-files) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-885">G-885</span> | Headers (HTTP) | Extra information sent with a request, such as the User-Agent. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#32-looking-like-a-browser) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-886">G-886</span> | Heatmap | A table drawn as coloured cells, darker for larger values. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#71-crosstab-and-heatmap) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-887">G-887</span> | Hessian $h_i$ | How fast row $i$'s gradient changes as the previous prediction moves (the second derivative of that row's loss with respect to the prediction). XGBoost uses it together with the gradient to compute leaf weights and split gains. | [Note ML-120](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-120-xgboost-maths/ML-120-xgboost-maths.md#8-the-second-order-approximation-of-the-objective) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-888">G-888</span> | Hessian matrix | The table of how a many-input function's slopes change, which measures its curvature: the symmetric $n \times n$ matrix of all second partial derivatives of $f: \mathbb{R}^n \to \mathbb{R}$. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#51-the-hessian-matrix) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-889">G-889</span> | Heteroscedasticity | Prediction errors (residuals) whose spread changes with the predicted value, often widening like a funnel; it breaks the equal-spread assumption (homoscedasticity) of linear regression. | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#6-assumption-4-homoscedasticity) | Assumptions of linear regression |
| <span id="G-890">G-890</span> | Hidden layer | A layer of neurons between the input and output layers; it works on the output of the layer before and passes what it finds on, so the network can build up more complex patterns. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#22-the-parts-of-a-neural-network) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-891">G-891</span> | Hidden state ($h_t$) | The RNN's running summary of the inputs so far: the recurrent layer's output at time step $t$. | [Note DL-056](DL/05-rnn/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation.md#42-two-differences-from-an-ann) | Recurrent neural network (RNN) |
| <span id="G-892">G-892</span> | hidden_layer_sizes | MLPClassifier setting: the number of nodes in each hidden layer, e.g. (4, 4). | [Note DL-009](DL/01-basics/DL-009-mlp-intuition/DL-009-mlp-intuition.md#52-other-shapes) | Multi-layer perceptron (MLP) |
| <span id="G-893">G-893</span> | Hierarchical clustering | Clustering that builds a hierarchy of clusters, from single points up to one cluster. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#1-overview) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-894">G-894</span> | Hierarchical representation | Features built in levels: simple ones in lower layers, more abstract ones in higher layers. | [Note DL-065](DL/05-rnn/DL-065-deep-rnns/DL-065-deep-rnns.md#71-hierarchical-representation) | Deep (stacked) RNNs |
| <span id="G-895">G-895</span> | High bias, low variance algorithm | An algorithm too simple to fit the training data well but stable across samples, such as linear regression; it underfits. | [Note ML-099](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-099-bagging-intuition/ML-099-bagging-intuition.md#31-the-bias-variance-problem) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-896">G-896</span> | High cardinality | A categorical column with very many different categories. | [Note ML-021](ML/02-getting-data/ML-021-pandas-profiling/ML-021-pandas-profiling.md#31-dataset-statistics) | Pandas Profiling |
| <span id="G-897">G-897</span> | High-dimensional data | Data with a very large number of columns. | [Note ML-045](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-045-curse-of-dimensionality/ML-045-curse-of-dimensionality.md#2-what-the-curse-of-dimensionality-is) | Curse of dimensionality |
| <span id="G-898">G-898</span> | Hinge loss | The SVM's error for one point: 0 when the point is on the correct side and outside the margin, growing in a straight line the further it goes past the margin; formula $\max(0, 1 - y(w^T x + b))$. | [Note ML-088](ML/07-classification/ML-088-svm-soft-margin/ML-088-svm-soft-margin.md#7-comparison-with-logistic-regression) | Hinge loss and soft margin |
| <span id="G-899">G-899</span> | Histogram | A bar chart of how many values fall in each equal range (bin) of a numerical column. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#6-histogram) | Univariate analysis |
| <span id="G-900">G-900</span> | History object | The object Keras' `fit` returns: its `.history` holds the loss and metrics of every epoch, used to draw the training curves. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#8-training-curves) | Training curves (History) |
| <span id="G-901">G-901</span> | Hold-out set | Rows set aside before training, used only to produce honest predictions or scores. | [Note ML-121](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-121-stacking-blending/ML-121-stacking-blending.md#5-the-overfitting-problem) | Stacking and blending |
| <span id="G-902">G-902</span> | Homoscedasticity | Prediction errors (residuals) with the same spread for every predicted value; an assumption of linear regression, checked by plotting residuals against predictions. | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#6-assumption-4-homoscedasticity) | Assumptions of linear regression |
| <span id="G-903">G-903</span> | Horizontal flip | Mirroring an image left to right; a data augmentation step that gives the model new training images of the same class. | [Note DL-050](DL/04-cnn/DL-050-data-augmentation/DL-050-data-augmentation.md#41-five-common-transformations) | Data augmentation |
| <span id="G-904">G-904</span> | HTML | The language web pages are written in: a tree of nested tags. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#1-overview) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-905">G-905</span> | Huber loss | A regression loss that is half the squared error for errors up to $\delta$ and grows in a straight line beyond; it acts like MSE for small errors and like MAE for large ones, so outliers pull less. | [Note DL-014](DL/01-basics/DL-014-dl-loss-functions/DL-014-dl-loss-functions.md#71-when-neither-mse-nor-mae-fits) | Huber loss |
| <span id="G-906">G-906</span> | Hue, style, size | Plot settings that show an extra column by colour, marker shape or dot size. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#5-graphs-for-more-than-two-features) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-907">G-907</span> | Hyper-cuboid | A box in many dimensions: the region a tree's cuts carve out. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#32-lines-planes-hyperplanes) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-910">G-910</span> | Hyperparameter | A setting of an algorithm chosen before training, such as a tree's `max_depth`. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#9-hyperparameter-tuning-with-a-pipeline) | Hyperparameter tuning |
| <span id="G-908">G-908</span> | Hyperparameter importance | How much each hyperparameter affected the score in a study; the values add up to 1. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#74-hyperparameter-importances) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-909">G-909</span> | Hyperparameter tuning | Trying several hyperparameter values and keeping the best. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#9-hyperparameter-tuning-with-a-pipeline) | Hyperparameter tuning |
| <span id="G-911">G-911</span> | Hyperplane | A flat surface in more than three dimensions; the model for three or more input columns. | [Note ML-052](ML/06-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression.md#22-more-inputs-a-hyperplane) | Equation of a hyperplane |
| <span id="G-2214">G-2214</span> | Hypothesis (Bayes' theorem) | The statement whose probability we want, such as "Steve is a librarian" or "the email is spam". | [Note MA-018](MA/02-probability/MA-018-bayes-theorem/MA-018-bayes-theorem.md#3-the-names-of-the-four-parts) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-912">G-912</span> | Hypothesis function | A model written as a function $h(x)$ that maps an input to a prediction. | [Note ML-109](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-109-adaboost-intuition/ML-109-adaboost-intuition.md#5-the-final-prediction-a-weighted-vote) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-913">G-913</span> | Hypothesis testing | Checking a claim about a population parameter with a sample. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#2-the-problem-hypothesis-testing-solves) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-914">G-914</span> | Identity initialisation | Starting the recurrent weight matrix $W_h$ as the identity matrix (1 on the diagonal, 0 elsewhere), so at the start of training multiplying by it leaves the gradient unchanged instead of shrinking it. | [Note DL-060](DL/05-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn.md#5-fixing-the-vanishing-gradient) | Long-term dependency problem |
| <span id="G-915">G-915</span> | Identity matrix | The matrix that leaves every vector unchanged. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#41-a-matrix-is-a-transformation) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-916">G-916</span> | If-else ladder | A long chain of hand-written `if` conditions, one per case; the traditional way to code rules, which breaks down when the cases are too many or keep changing. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#41-when-the-rules-keep-changing-spam-filtering) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-917">G-917</span> | ILSVRC | The ImageNet Large Scale Visual Recognition Challenge: 1,000 classes, about 1.2 million training photos. | [Note DL-051](DL/04-cnn/DL-051-pretrained-models/DL-051-pretrained-models.md#51-the-competition) | Pretrained models and ImageNet |
| <span id="G-918">G-918</span> | Image captioning | Producing a sentence that describes an image. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#4-one-to-many) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-919">G-919</span> | Image classification | Deciding what a picture contains, e.g. dog or not dog. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#42-when-there-are-too-many-cases-recognising-dogs) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-2268">G-2268</span> | Image segmentation | Dividing an image into regions by saying which pixels belong to which object. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#9-disadvantages-of-pooling) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-920">G-920</span> | ImageNet | A very large labelled image dataset with a yearly classification competition. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#36-2012-imagenet-and-after) | History of deep learning |

| <span id="G-922">G-922</span> | imbalanced-learn | A Python library (`imblearn`) of tools that rebalance the classes by adding or removing rows (resampling techniques), plus balanced ensembles, with a `fit_resample` method. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#5-random-undersampling) | Random under- and oversampling |
| <span id="G-923">G-923</span> | IMDB dataset | 50,000 film reviews labelled positive or negative, a standard sentiment-analysis dataset. | [Note DL-057](DL/05-rnn/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis.md#4-the-imdb-dataset) | Recurrent neural network (RNN) |
| <span id="G-924">G-924</span> | Immediate derivative | The part of a derivative that comes only from a weight's direct use at step $t$: the derivative of $h_t$ with respect to the weight with $h_{t-1}$ held fixed. | [Note DL-059](DL/05-rnn/DL-059-backpropagation-through-time/DL-059-backpropagation-through-time.md#61-three-paths) | Backpropagation through time (BPTT) |
| <span id="G-925">G-925</span> | Importance $I_i$ | The weight of feature $i$ in the toy model's loss. | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#81-the-toy-model) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-926">G-926</span> | Impossible event | An event that contains no outcome, so it can never happen, such as rolling a 7 with one die; it is the empty set $\varnothing$ and has probability 0. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#47-impossible-and-sure-events) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-927">G-927</span> | Imputation | Filling in missing values, for example with the mean, median or mode. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#61-handling-missing-values) | Missing values |
| <span id="G-928">G-928</span> | Inactive constraint | An inequality constraint that the unconstrained minimum already satisfies, so it plays no part in the answer; its Lagrange multiplier is 0. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#5-inequality-constraints) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-929">G-929</span> | include_bias | PolynomialFeatures setting that adds a column of 1s (the power 0), which acts as an intercept; LinearRegression fits its own intercept anyway, so either setting works. | [Note ML-060](ML/06-regression/ML-060-polynomial-regression/ML-060-polynomial-regression.md#3-adding-powers-as-new-features) | Polynomial features |
| <span id="G-930">G-930</span> | Increasing function | A function whose output grows whenever its input grows, such as the log; it keeps the position of a maximum. | [Note MA-070](MA/08-likelihood/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation.md#81-the-log-does-not-move-the-peak) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-931">G-931</span> | Incremental learning (incremental training) | Training on small pieces of data over time, keeping what was learned before (the opposite of batch learning). | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#31-how-batch-learning-works) | Online learning |
| <span id="G-933">G-933</span> | Independent and identically distributed (i.i.d.) | Values that do not affect each other and all come from the same distribution. | [Note MA-033](MA/04-inference/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt.md#41-conditions) | Central limit theorem |
| <span id="G-934">G-934</span> | Independent events | Events where one happening does not change the probability of the other. | [Note MA-016](MA/02-probability/MA-016-independent-events/MA-016-independent-events.md#2-the-definition) | Independent and mutually exclusive events |
| <span id="G-935">G-935</span> | Independent models | Models whose mistakes are unrelated, so one being wrong says nothing about the others. | [Note ML-096](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-096-voting-ensemble/ML-096-voting-ensemble.md#3-the-puzzle) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-936">G-936</span> | Independent two-sample t-test | A t-test comparing the means of two separate, non-overlapping groups. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#2-the-independent-two-sample-t-test) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-937">G-937</span> | Independent variables | Another name for the features: the input columns X that a model uses to predict the output. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#5-inputs-and-output) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-938">G-938</span> | Index | The labels down the left side of a DataFrame, one per row (0, 1, 2, ... by default); pandas uses them to name and select rows. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#7-choosing-the-index-index_col) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-939">G-939</span> | Inertia (of an organisation) | A big organisation's resistance to changing direction once it has started. | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#11-think-before-coding) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-940">G-940</span> | inertia_ | The number stored on a trained `KMeans` model that measures how tight its clusters are: the total squared distance from each point to its cluster's centroid (its WCSS). The elbow method plots it against k. | [Note ML-123](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-123-kmeans-code/ML-123-kmeans-code.md#4-choosing-k-with-the-elbow-method) | Elbow method and WCSS |

| <span id="G-941">G-941</span> | Inference (vs prediction) | Learning how the inputs affect the output, rather than only predicting it; not the deployment sense (running a trained model) nor inferential statistics. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#75-inference-which-feature-mattered) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-942">G-942</span> | Inference engine | The part of an expert system that applies the rules to answer a question. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#31-how-an-expert-system-works) | Symbolic AI and expert systems |
| <span id="G-944">G-944</span> | Inferential statistics | Statistics that draws conclusions about a population from a sample. | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#3-descriptive-and-inferential-statistics) | Inferential statistics |
| <span id="G-945">G-945</span> | Inflection point | A point where a curve switches between bending down and bending up; for the normal curve, at $\mu \pm \sigma$. | [Note MA-024](MA/03-distributions/MA-024-normal-distribution/MA-024-normal-distribution.md#5-the-pdf-of-the-normal-distribution) | Normal distribution |
| <span id="G-946">G-946</span> | Information gain | How much a split reduces the mix of labels: the parent's entropy minus the weighted entropy of its children; the tree splits on the highest. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#71-the-definition) | Entropy, information gain and Gini |
| <span id="G-947">G-947</span> | Initialisation | Giving every weight and bias its starting value before training begins; a bad start can cause vanishing or exploding gradients or slow convergence. | [Note DL-029](DL/02-training/DL-029-weight-initialization/DL-029-weight-initialization.md#1-overview) | Weight initialisation |
| <span id="G-948">G-948</span> | Input / output | The columns we know / the column we want to predict. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#21-learning-from-inputs-and-outputs) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-949">G-949</span> | Input feeding | Joining the previous step's attention output $\tilde h_{i-1}$ to the next decoder input, so the model remembers its past alignment choices; an option in Luong's attention. | [Note DL-070](DL/06-transformers/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention.md#52-the-current-state-and-the-context-on-the-output-side) | Luong (multiplicative) attention |

| <span id="G-952">G-952</span> | Input layer | The first layer of a neural network, with one node per input column; it takes in the data and passes the values on without calculating anything. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#22-the-parts-of-a-neural-network) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-953">G-953</span> | Inspect | Browser tool that shows which tag draws each part of a page. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#6-finding-the-right-tags-with-inspect) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-954">G-954</span> | Instance set $I_j$ | The rows that land in leaf $j$. | [Note ML-120](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-120-xgboost-maths/ML-120-xgboost-maths.md#9-from-a-sum-over-observations-to-a-sum-over-leaves) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-955">G-955</span> | Instance-based learning | Learning by storing the training data and comparing new points with it. | [Note ML-006](ML/01-foundations/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based.md#2-two-ways-to-learn) | Instance-based learning |
| <span id="G-956">G-956</span> | Integer encoding | Replacing each word by its index (a whole number) in the vocabulary, so a network such as an RNN can read text as numbers. | [Note DL-057](DL/05-rnn/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis.md#31-the-three-steps) | Tokenization and integer encoding of text |
| <span id="G-957">G-957</span> | Integration | Finding the area under a curve by adding up infinitely many thin strips. | [Note MA-022](MA/03-distributions/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf.md#3-area-under-the-curve-is-probability) | Probability density function (PDF) |
| <span id="G-958">G-958</span> | Inter-sequence attention | Attention between two different sequences, such as an output and an input sentence. | [Note DL-077](DL/06-transformers/DL-077-why-self-attention/DL-077-why-self-attention.md#6-why-it-is-called-self) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |

| <span id="G-961">G-961</span> | intercept_ | The attribute of a fitted scikit-learn linear model that holds the learned intercept $b$, the prediction when every input is 0. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#51-reading-m-and-b) | Simple linear regression |
| <span id="G-962">G-962</span> | Interference | The part of a feature's reading that comes from other active features, in proportion to the cosines between their directions. | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#3-perpendicular-directions-do-not-interfere-but-only-d-of-them-fit) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-963">G-963</span> | Internal covariate shift | The way the values a layer receives keep changing their spread and centre during training, because the layers before it keep updating (the change in the distribution of a network's activations). | [Note DL-031](DL/02-training/DL-031-batch-normalization/DL-031-batch-normalization.md#33-internal-covariate-shift) | Covariate shift |
| <span id="G-964">G-964</span> | Interpolation | Placing a new point on the segment between two existing points. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#71-interpolation) | SMOTE |
| <span id="G-965">G-965</span> | Interpretability | How well people can understand why a model makes its decisions. | [Note ML-108](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-108-feature-importance/ML-108-feature-importance.md#22-interpretability) | Feature importance |
| <span id="G-966">G-966</span> | Interquartile range (IQR) | The width of the middle half of the data: Q3 - Q1. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#8-box-plot) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-967">G-967</span> | Intersection (A ∩ B) | The event that both A and B happen. | [Note MA-015](MA/02-probability/MA-015-conditional-probability/MA-015-conditional-probability.md#2-the-definition) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-968">G-968</span> | Inverse matrix | The matrix that undoes another: their product is the identity matrix. | [Note ML-053](ML/06-regression/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths.md#6-the-normal-equation) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-969">G-969</span> | Inverse square root decay | Lowering the learning rate in proportion to $1/\sqrt{\text{step}}$ after the warm-up, so the steps get smaller and the weights can settle near a minimum; the transformer paper's schedule. | [Note DL-086](DL/06-transformers/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end.md#73-the-optimizer-and-the-warm-up-learning-rate) | Learning-rate warm-up schedule |
| <span id="G-970">G-970</span> | Inverted dropout | Dropout that scales the kept outputs up by $1/(1-p)$ during training, so prediction needs no change; what Keras does. | [Note DL-024](DL/02-training/DL-024-dropout/DL-024-dropout.md#6-dropout-at-prediction-time) | Dropout |
| <span id="G-971">G-971</span> | IoT sensor | A device that measures something and sends the readings over the internet. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#62-predictive-maintenance) | Applications of ML |

| <span id="G-976">G-976</span> | Iteration (MICE) | One pass that re-predicts the gaps of every column once, in order. | [Note ML-039](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice.md#63-the-marketing-column) | Iterative imputation (MICE) |
| <span id="G-977">G-977</span> | Iteration 0 | The starting table, with every gap filled by its column mean. | [Note ML-039](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice.md#5-step-0-fill-with-the-column-means) | Iterative imputation (MICE) |
| <span id="G-978">G-978</span> | Iterative imputer | Filling missing values by predicting each column from the other columns, over and over (multivariate imputation); its algorithm is MICE. | [Note ML-039](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice.md#1-overview) | Iterative imputation (MICE) |
| <span id="G-980">G-980</span> | Jacobian | The table (matrix) of all first partial derivatives of a function with several inputs and outputs, one row per output and one column per input, $J_{ij} = \partial f_i/\partial x_j$; it shows how every output changes with every input near a point. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#42-the-formula-every-partial-derivative-in-one-grid) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-979">G-979</span> | Jacobian determinant | The factor by which a function stretches or shrinks small areas or volumes near a point: the determinant of its Jacobian, $\det J$. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#52-det-j-how-a-function-scales-small-areas) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-982">G-982</span> | Jensen's inequality | For a convex function, the function of a weighted average is at most the weighted average of the function. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#3-convex-functions-and-their-sets) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-981">G-981</span> | Jensen's inequality (for the log) | The log of a weighted average is at least the weighted average of the logs. | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#62-jensens-inequality-for-the-log) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-983">G-983</span> | joblib | A library that saves and loads Python objects like pickle, better suited to large arrays. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#10-exporting-the-pipeline-for-production) | Saving models with pickle |
| <span id="G-984">G-984</span> | Johnson–Lindenstrauss lemma | A cloud of $n$ points in many dimensions can be copied into far fewer dimensions, about $\log n/\varepsilon^2$, with every squared distance kept within a factor $1 \pm \varepsilon$. | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#6-the-room-grows-exponentially-with-the-dimension) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-986">G-986</span> | Joint probability | The probability that two events happen together, $P(A \cap B)$. | [Note MA-014](MA/02-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability.md#2-joint-probability) | Joint and marginal probability |
| <span id="G-985">G-985</span> | Joint probability distribution | The joint probabilities of every combination of values of two variables; they sum to 1. | [Note MA-014](MA/02-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability.md#22-the-joint-probability-distribution) | Joint and marginal probability |
| <span id="G-987">G-987</span> | JSON (JavaScript Object Notation) | A plain-text format for structured data, made of objects and arrays, that almost every language can read; used by APIs. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#2-what-json-is) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-988">G-988</span> | JSON Lines | A JSON file with one object per line, read with `lines=True`. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#41-useful-options) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-989">G-989</span> | JSON viewer | A tool that lays out JSON text as a tree to show its structure. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#5-reading-the-reply) | APIs |
| <span id="G-990">G-990</span> | Jupyter | Tool for notebooks that mix code, output and text. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#31-starting-jupyter) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-991">G-991</span> | JupyterLab | The program that runs Jupyter notebooks in a web browser. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#31-starting-jupyter) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-993">G-993</span> | k (k-means) | The number of clusters k-means makes; chosen by us. | [Note ML-122](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-122-kmeans-intuition/ML-122-kmeans-intuition.md#41-step-1-choose-k) | K-means |
| <span id="G-992">G-992</span> | k (n_neighbors) | The number of neighbours that vote; KNN's main hyperparameter. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#4-choosing-k) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-994">G-994</span> | k-distance plot | A plot of every point's distance to its k-th nearest point, sorted from small to large; the bend where it shoots up gives a good eps for DBSCAN. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#12-choosing-eps-the-k-distance-plot) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-996">G-996</span> | k-means | A clustering algorithm that repeatedly assigns points to the nearest centre and moves each centre to the mean of its points. | [Note ML-122](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-122-kmeans-intuition/ML-122-kmeans-intuition.md#4-the-five-steps-of-k-means) | K-means |
| <span id="G-995">G-995</span> | k-means binning | Binning whose edges lie halfway between the centres of the groups found by k-means. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#4-kinds-of-discretization) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-997">G-997</span> | k-means++ | The default way `KMeans` picks its starting centroids: one by one, with far-away points more likely to be picked. | [Note ML-123](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-123-kmeans-code/ML-123-kmeans-code.md#5-training-k-means-with-k--4) | K-means |
| <span id="G-998">G-998</span> | K-nearest neighbours (KNN) | Predicting from the answers of the k closest stored points. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#2-how-knn-predicts) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-999">G-999</span> | Kaggle | A website for sharing datasets and notebooks and for ML competitions. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#34-loading-a-dataset) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1000">G-1000</span> | KBinsDiscretizer | scikit-learn's class for equal width, equal frequency and k-means binning. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#9-kbinsdiscretizer-in-scikit-learn) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-1003">G-1003</span> | Keras | The high-level interface built into TensorFlow for defining and training networks. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#4-building-a-network-in-keras) | Keras workflow |
| <span id="G-1001">G-1001</span> | Keras Tuner | A Python library (`keras_tuner`) that searches for good hyperparameters of a Keras model. | [Note DL-039](DL/03-optimizers/DL-039-keras-tuner/DL-039-keras-tuner.md#1-overview) | Keras Tuner |
| <span id="G-1002">G-1002</span> | Keras workflow | Build, compile, fit, predict: the four steps of every Keras model. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#1-overview) | Keras workflow |
| <span id="G-1009">G-1009</span> | Kernel (Jupyter) | The running Python process behind a notebook. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#33-the-kernel) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-2273">G-2273</span> | Kernel (KDE) | A small symmetric bump with area 1, placed on each point to build a KDE curve. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#7-density-plot) | Kernel density estimation (KDE) |
| <span id="G-2274">G-2274</span> | Kernel (Keras) | Keras' name for a layer's weight matrix. | [Note DL-026](DL/02-training/DL-026-regularization-in-dl/DL-026-regularization-in-dl.md#73-with-l2) | L1 and L2 regularisation in neural networks |
| <span id="G-1004">G-1004</span> | Kernel (SVM) | A function of two points, $K(a, b)$, that returns their dot product in the higher-dimensional space, $\phi(a) \cdot \phi(b)$, directly from the original values (not the Jupyter kernel). | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#32-the-feature-map-and-the-kernel) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-1005">G-1005</span> | Kernel density estimate (KDE) | A smooth curve that estimates a column's PDF from its values, built by adding a kernel centred on every data point; a KDE plot draws it. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#7-density-plot) | Kernel density estimation (KDE) |
| <span id="G-1006">G-1006</span> | Kernel function $K(a, b)$ | A function of two points that returns the dot product they would have after lifting to a higher-dimensional space, $\phi(a) \cdot \phi(b)$, computed straight from the original points, so the lifted features are never built. | [Note ML-090](ML/07-classification/ML-090-kernel-trick-code/ML-090-kernel-trick-code.md#7-why-it-is-a-trick) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-1007">G-1007</span> | Kernel transformation | A loose name for lifting the data to the higher-dimensional space with the feature map $\phi$. | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#32-the-feature-map-and-the-kernel) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-1008">G-1008</span> | Kernel trick | Making non-linear data separable by mapping it to a higher dimension, without building the new columns. | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#1-overview) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-1011">G-1011</span> | Key | In attention, the vector each word offers to be compared with a query; the dot product of the query with a key scores how much attention that word gets. | [Note DL-074](DL/06-transformers/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step.md#7-three-roles-query-key-and-value) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-1010">G-1010</span> | Key and value | In attention, the vectors of the words being looked at: keys are compared with the query to get the weights, and values are mixed with those weights; in cross-attention both come from the encoder's input words. | [Note DL-074](DL/06-transformers/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step.md#7-three-roles-query-key-and-value) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-1012">G-1012</span> | Key, value (in an MLP) | Geva et al.'s names for a row of the first matrix (a pattern detector) and the matching direction of the second matrix (what gets written). | [Note DL-089](DL/06-transformers/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts.md#5-columns-write-directions) | MLP blocks as fact storage |
| <span id="G-1013">G-1013</span> | KKT conditions | The four checks a point must pass to be a minimum when some constraints are inequalities: the Lagrangian's gradient is zero (stationarity), the point obeys every constraint (primal feasibility), every multiplier is at least 0 (dual feasibility), and each multiplier is 0 unless its constraint is tight (complementary slackness). | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#5-inequality-constraints) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-1014">G-1014</span> | KL divergence, focal loss, triplet loss | Losses for autoencoders, object detection and embeddings, taught with those networks. | [Note DL-014](DL/01-basics/DL-014-dl-loss-functions/DL-014-dl-loss-functions.md#11-choosing-a-loss) | Loss functions in deep learning |
| <span id="G-1015">G-1015</span> | KMeans | scikit-learn's k-means class, in `sklearn.cluster`. | [Note ML-123](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-123-kmeans-code/ML-123-kmeans-code.md#4-choosing-k-with-the-elbow-method) | K-means |
| <span id="G-1016">G-1016</span> | KNeighborsClassifier | scikit-learn's KNN classifier; `n_neighbors=5` by default. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#3-knn-in-scikit-learn-the-breast-cancer-data) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-1017">G-1017</span> | KNN imputer | Filling a missing value from the most similar rows (multivariate imputation, `KNNImputer`). | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#2-univariate-and-multivariate-imputation) | KNN imputer |
| <span id="G-1018">G-1018</span> | Knowledge base | The collection of rules inside an expert system. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#31-how-an-expert-system-works) | Symbolic AI and expert systems |
| <span id="G-1019">G-1019</span> | Kruskal-Wallis test | A test of whether several groups differ that works on the ranks of the values, so it does not assume normality; an alternative to one-way ANOVA. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#7-assumptions) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-1021">G-1021</span> | Kurtosis | How heavy the tails of a distribution are compared with a normal curve; the fourth moment. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#1-overview) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-1020">G-1020</span> | Kurtosis risk | In finance, the risk of extreme gains or losses from fat-tailed returns. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#5-where-kurtosis-matters-kurtosis-risk) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-1022">G-1022</span> | KV cache | Storing the key and value vectors of earlier positions so that each new step computes them only for the new word. | [Note DL-085](DL/06-transformers/DL-085-transformer-inference/DL-085-transformer-inference.md#7-the-mask-stays-on-during-inference) | Transformer inference (autoregressive decoding, KV cache, beam search) |
| <span id="G-1023">G-1023</span> | L-BFGS | An optimiser that uses the curvature of the loss without storing the full Hessian: it builds a stand-in for it from only the last few steps and gradient changes, saving memory (limited-memory BFGS, a quasi-Newton method). scikit-learn's default logistic regression solver. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#7-where-ml-uses-second-order-approximations) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1024">G-1024</span> | L1 loss | Another name for the mean absolute error used as a training loss. | [Note DL-014](DL/01-basics/DL-014-dl-loss-functions/DL-014-dl-loss-functions.md#6-mean-absolute-error) | Loss functions in deep learning |
| <span id="G-1025">G-1025</span> | L1 norm | The sum of the absolute values of the components. | [Note MA-049](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations.md#22-in-n-dimensions) | Vector magnitude, distance and scalar operations |
| <span id="G-1026">G-1026</span> | L1 regularisation | Another name for the absolute-value penalty used by Lasso. | [Note ML-066](ML/06-regression/ML-066-lasso-regression/ML-066-lasso-regression.md#1-overview) | Lasso regression |
| <span id="G-1027">G-1027</span> | l1_ratio | The share of the total penalty given to the L1 (Lasso) part. | [Note ML-068](ML/06-regression/ML-068-elastic-net/ML-068-elastic-net.md#3-alpha-and-l1_ratio) | Elastic Net |
| <span id="G-1028">G-1028</span> | L2 norm (norm, magnitude, length of a vector) | The usual magnitude of a vector, its distance from the origin: the square root of the sum of squared components, $\lVert w \rVert = \sqrt{w_1^2 + w_2^2 + \dots}$. | [Note MA-049](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations.md#22-in-n-dimensions) | Vector magnitude, distance and scalar operations |
| <span id="G-1029">G-1029</span> | L2 regularisation | Another name for the squared-coefficient penalty used by Ridge. | [Note ML-062](ML/06-regression/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition.md#1-overview) | Ridge regression |
| <span id="G-1032">G-1032</span> | Label | The answer an example comes with, the value a model learns to predict, such as the digit an image shows; another name for the target or output. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#21-learning-from-inputs-and-outputs) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-1030">G-1030</span> | Label encoding | Replacing the classes of the target by 0, 1, 2, ...; for the output column only. | [Note ML-025](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding.md#3-why-categories-must-become-numbers) | Ordinal and label encoding |
| <span id="G-2172">G-2172</span> | Label propagation | Semi-supervised method in which labels hop from labelled observations to their nearest neighbours, step by step. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#4-semi-supervised-learning) | Semi-supervised learning |
| <span id="G-1031">G-1031</span> | Label smoothing | Replacing the one-hot target by a slightly softer one, $(1-\varepsilon)$ on the correct word plus $\varepsilon/K$ on every word, so training stops pushing the model towards complete certainty. | [Note DL-086](DL/06-transformers/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end.md#74-regularisation-residual-dropout-and-label-smoothing) | Label smoothing |
| <span id="G-1033">G-1033</span> | LabelEncoder | scikit-learn's class for label encoding the target. | [Note ML-025](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding.md#72-encoding-the-purchased-column) | Ordinal and label encoding |
| <span id="G-1034">G-1034</span> | Labelled data | Data that includes the output column. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#21-learning-from-inputs-and-outputs) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-1035">G-1035</span> | labels_ | The cluster number of every training row, after fitting. | [Note ML-123](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-123-kmeans-code/ML-123-kmeans-code.md#5-training-k-means-with-k--4) | K-means |
| <span id="G-1036">G-1036</span> | Lagrange multiplier | A number attached to one constraint; at the answer it scales the constraint's gradient to match the objective's, and measures how much the constraint costs. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#32-tangent-curves-have-parallel-gradients) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-1037">G-1037</span> | Lagrangian | One function that packs an objective and its constraints together: the objective plus each constraint times its multiplier, $f + \sum_i \lambda_i g_i$; setting its gradient to zero gives the conditions for the constrained minimum. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#41-one-function-holds-all-the-conditions) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-1039">G-1039</span> | Lambda ($\lambda$, `reg_lambda`) | XGBoost's setting that holds its trees back (regularisation): it is added to the number of residuals in the denominators of the similarity score and leaf output, which shrinks scores, gains and outputs, most for leaves with few residuals; default 1. | [Note ML-118](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-118-xgboost-regression/ML-118-xgboost-regression.md#12-lambda-shrinking-scores-and-outputs) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1038">G-1038</span> | Lambda ($\lambda$, power transform) | The power used by a power transform, learned separately for each column. | [Note ML-030](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-030-power-transformer/ML-030-power-transformer.md#1-overview) | Power transformer |
| <span id="G-1040">G-1040</span> | Lambda (Python) | A one-line Python function without a name, such as `lambda x: x**2`. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#8-trying-the-other-transforms) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-1041">G-1041</span> | lambdas_ | The `PowerTransformer` attribute holding the learned $\lambda$ of each column. | [Note ML-030](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-030-power-transformer/ML-030-power-transformer.md#64-box-cox) | Power transformer |
| <span id="G-1042">G-1042</span> | Language modelling | Training a model to predict the next word of a text. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#7-stage-4-transfer-learning-for-language-2018) | Large language models (LLMs) |
| <span id="G-1043">G-1043</span> | Laplace approximation | Replacing a distribution near its peak with a normal distribution whose width comes from the curvature there (the Hessian). | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#7-where-ml-uses-second-order-approximations) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1044">G-1044</span> | Laplace distribution | A peaked, heavy-tailed distribution with density $e^{-\lvert x - \mu\rvert/b}/(2b)$. | [Note MA-072](MA/08-likelihood/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning.md#34-estimating-the-noise) | MAP estimation |
| <span id="G-1045">G-1045</span> | Laplace smoothing | Adding a small count (usually 1) to every count so that no probability is 0. | [Note ML-083](ML/07-classification/ML-083-naive-bayes-code/ML-083-naive-bayes-code.md#7-the-fix-laplace-smoothing) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-1046">G-1046</span> | Large language model (LLM) | A transformer language model with billions of parameters, trained on a vast amount of text. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#8-stage-5-large-language-models-2018-onward) | Large language models (LLMs) |
| <span id="G-1047">G-1047</span> | Lasso regression | Linear regression with a penalty on the sum of absolute coefficients (L1); it shrinks coefficients and can set some exactly to 0, which removes those features. | [Note ML-066](ML/06-regression/ML-066-lasso-regression/ML-066-lasso-regression.md#1-overview) | Lasso regression |
| <span id="G-1048">G-1048</span> | Latency | The delay between a request and its answer; high for KNN on large data. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#71-large-datasets-slow-predictions) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-1049">G-1049</span> | Latent semantic analysis (LSA) | Describing each document by a few topic directions of the document-word matrix (its top $k$ singular directions, found with the SVD), so that texts on one topic line up. | [Note MA-060](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning.md#32-topics-from-the-svd) | Latent semantic analysis |
| <span id="G-1050">G-1050</span> | Latent variable | A variable in a model that is never observed, such as the component that produced a point. | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#52-the-latent-variable) | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) |
| <span id="G-1051">G-1051</span> | Lateral geniculate nucleus (LGN) | A region of the thalamus that passes visual signals on to V1. | [Note DL-041](DL/04-cnn/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex.md#3-how-visual-information-flows) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-1052">G-1052</span> | Law of large numbers | The more trials, the closer a share of trials gets to the true probability. | [Note MA-021](MA/03-distributions/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf.md#5-estimating-a-pmf-by-simulation) | Probability mass function (PMF) |
| <span id="G-1053">G-1053</span> | Law of total probability | The rule for the overall probability of an event: add its probability under each case, weighted by how likely that case is, $P(B) = \sum_i P(B \mid A_i) P(A_i)$, when the cases $A_i$ are mutually exclusive and cover every possibility. | [Note MA-019](MA/02-probability/MA-019-bayes-problem/MA-019-bayes-problem.md#1-overview) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-1056">G-1056</span> | Layer | A group of neurons in a neural network that all work on the output of the layer before; stacking layers lets the network find more complex patterns. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#53-layers-build-up-understanding) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-1054">G-1054</span> | Layer normalisation | Putting each observation's numbers on one scale by itself, without depending on the batch: subtract the mean and divide by the standard deviation across its own features, then scale and shift each feature by learned $\gamma_j$ and $\beta_j$. | [Note DL-080](DL/06-transformers/DL-080-layer-normalization/DL-080-layer-normalization.md#1-overview) | Layer normalisation |
| <span id="G-1055">G-1055</span> | Layer number | The position of a layer, from 0 for the input layer to the output layer. | [Note DL-008](DL/01-basics/DL-008-mlp-notation/DL-008-mlp-notation.md#21-numbering-the-layers) | MLP notation and parameter count |
| <span id="G-1057">G-1057</span> | Lazy learning | Another name for instance-based learning: no work until a question arrives. | [Note ML-006](ML/01-foundations/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based.md#32-no-real-training) | Instance-based learning |
| <span id="G-1058">G-1058</span> | lbfgs | The default solver of LogisticRegression; supports L2 or no penalty. | [Note ML-080](ML/07-classification/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters.md#3-the-solver) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1059">G-1059</span> | LDA | Linear discriminant analysis: a way to build new columns that uses the class labels (a supervised method), along the directions that best separate the classes. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#9-feature-extraction) | Feature extraction |
| <span id="G-1060">G-1060</span> | Leaf node | A node that is not split; it gives the prediction. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#4-terminology) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1061">G-1061</span> | Leaf value ($\gamma_{jm}$) | The constant a leaf adds to the model, chosen to minimise the loss of the rows in that leaf. | [Note ML-115](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths.md#8-step-2c-the-best-value-for-each-leaf) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1062">G-1062</span> | Leaf value in log-odds | The amount a leaf adds to the log-odds in gradient boosting for classification, converted from the leaf's residuals because a probability cannot be added to log-odds: $\sum r / \sum p(1-p)$ over the leaf's rows. | [Note ML-116](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-116-gradient-boosting-classification/ML-116-gradient-boosting-classification.md#7-stage-2-a-regression-tree-on-the-residuals) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1063">G-1063</span> | Leaf weight $w_j$ | The output value of leaf $j$ of a tree: the number the tree gives every observation that lands in that leaf; XGBoost solves for these values. | [Note ML-120](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-120-xgboost-maths/ML-120-xgboost-maths.md#3-what-we-must-find-the-leaf-weights) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1064">G-1064</span> | Leaky ReLU | A ReLU variant with a small slope for negative inputs, so nodes do not die. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#51-leaky-relu) | Leaky ReLU, PReLU, ELU and SELU |
| <span id="G-1065">G-1065</span> | Learnable (trainable) parameters | The weights and biases that training changes; for a convolution layer, the filter values and one bias per filter. | [Note DL-046](DL/04-cnn/DL-046-cnn-vs-ann/DL-046-cnn-vs-ann.md#51-counting-the-parameters-of-a-convolution-layer) | CNN architecture (LeNet-5) |
| <span id="G-1066">G-1066</span> | Learned positional embedding | A trainable vector per position, learned like a word embedding. | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#82-the-dot-product-depends-only-on-the-distance) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-1073">G-1073</span> | Learning | Finding rules (patterns) from examples. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#41-learning-rules-from-data) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-2163">G-2163</span> | Learning path | Which Notes to read before which. | [Course map](00-course-map/00-course-map.md#1-overview) |  |
| <span id="G-1068">G-1068</span> | Learning rate ($\eta$) | How strongly each update changes the model; in gradient descent, the number the slope is multiplied by to get the step size. | [Note ML-005](ML/01-foundations/ML-005-online-learning/ML-005-online-learning.md#5-the-learning-rate) | Learning rate |
| <span id="G-1067">G-1067</span> | Learning rate ($\eta$) in gradient boosting | The fraction of each tree's output that is added to the model, the same for every tree; typically 0.1. | [Note ML-114](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-114-gradient-boosting-intuition/ML-114-gradient-boosting-intuition.md#8-the-learning-rate-small-steps-in-the-right-direction) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1069">G-1069</span> | Learning rate (AdaBoost) | A number that scales how much each weak learner's vote (its alpha) counts in AdaBoost; values below 1 slow learning. | [Note ML-112](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters.md#23-learning_rate-the-say-of-every-stump-scaled) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1070">G-1070</span> | Learning rate scheduler (learning schedule) | A rule that changes the learning rate as training goes on, usually lowering it. | [Note DL-021](DL/02-training/DL-021-improving-a-neural-network/DL-021-improving-a-neural-network.md#34-batch-size) | Improving a neural network |
| <span id="G-1071">G-1071</span> | Learning rate warm-up | Starting training with a very small learning rate and raising it over the first epochs or steps (4,000 steps in the transformer paper). | [Note DL-021](DL/02-training/DL-021-improving-a-neural-network/DL-021-improving-a-neural-network.md#34-batch-size) | Learning-rate warm-up schedule |
| <span id="G-1076">G-1076</span> | Least-squares loss | The total squared gap between the targets and a model's predictions, $\lVert \mathbf{y} - \Phi\boldsymbol{\theta} \rVert^2$; fitting picks the parameters $\boldsymbol{\theta}$ that make it smallest. Its gradient is $-2(\mathbf{y} - \Phi\boldsymbol{\theta})^{\mathsf T}\Phi$. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#7-example-the-gradient-of-the-least-squares-loss) | Ordinary least squares (closed form) |
| <span id="G-1077">G-1077</span> | LeCun initialisation | A way to pick a network's random starting weights: draw them from a normal distribution with standard deviation $\sqrt{1/\text{fan-in}}$ (fan-in is the number of inputs to a node), so the signal keeps a steady spread from layer to layer; the start SELU needs (Keras `lecun_normal`). | [Note DL-030](DL/02-training/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization.md#41-xavier-normal) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-1078">G-1078</span> | Left null space | The set of output vectors perpendicular to every column of a matrix, so the matrix can never produce them; in the SVD it is spanned by the left singular vectors $\mathbf u_{r+1}, \dots, \mathbf u_m$. | [Note MA-058](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-058-computing-the-svd/MA-058-computing-the-svd.md#6-the-four-subspaces) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1079">G-1079</span> | Left singular vector ($\mathbf u_i$) | One of the output directions of the SVD: the unit vector that a right singular vector lands on, stretched by its singular value ($A\mathbf v_i = \sigma_i \mathbf u_i$); the columns of $U$. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#41-from-the-singular-value-equation-to-the-factorisation) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1080">G-1080</span> | LeNet-5 | An early network for reading handwritten digits, built by LeCun et al. in 1998 (a CNN): two blocks of convolution then pooling (conv-pool blocks), then layers of 120, 84 and 10 nodes. | [Note DL-045](DL/04-cnn/DL-045-lenet-5/DL-045-lenet-5.md#41-background) | CNN architecture (LeNet-5) |
| <span id="G-1081">G-1081</span> | Leptokurtic | Said of a distribution whose tails are fatter than the normal's (excess kurtosis above 0), so extreme values and outliers are more common. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#4-excess-kurtosis-and-the-three-types) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-1082">G-1082</span> | Levene's test | A check of whether two or more groups have the same spread (a hypothesis test for equal variances, $H_0$: they do). Used before a two-sample t-test to choose between the equal-variance test and Welch's t-test. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#22-checking-equal-variances-levenes-test) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1083">G-1083</span> | LiDAR | A sensor that measures distances to nearby objects with laser light. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#41-mainstream-applications) | History of deep learning |
| <span id="G-1084">G-1084</span> | LightGBM | Microsoft's gradient boosting library, aimed at speed and low memory use. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#9-lightgbm-and-catboost) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1086">G-1086</span> | Likelihood | How probable the observed data is under given parameter values; read as a function of the parameters with the data fixed. | [Note MA-069](MA/08-likelihood/MA-069-probability-vs-likelihood/MA-069-probability-vs-likelihood.md#22-likelihood-from-the-event-back-to-the-parameter) | Likelihood |
| <span id="G-1085">G-1085</span> | Likelihood function $L(\theta \mid \text{data})$ | The likelihood as a function of the parameters, with the data held fixed: the product of the densities (or probabilities) of all observations. | [Note MA-069](MA/08-likelihood/MA-069-probability-vs-likelihood/MA-069-probability-vs-likelihood.md#43-the-likelihood-function-slide-the-parameter) | Likelihood |
| <span id="G-1087">G-1087</span> | Limit | The value an expression approaches as a quantity (such as $h$) gets arbitrarily close to a target (such as 0). | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#41-from-secant-to-tangent) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-1088">G-1088</span> | Line plot | A scatter plot with the dots joined in order, used when x is time. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#10-line-plot-a-number-over-time) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-1089">G-1089</span> | Linear activation | An activation that passes a node's weighted sum through unchanged, $f(z) = z$. Used in the output layer for regression. | [Note DL-013](DL/01-basics/DL-013-graduate-admission-ann/DL-013-graduate-admission-ann.md#41-the-linear-output-node) | Activation functions |
| <span id="G-1090">G-1090</span> | Linear algebra | The branch of mathematics that studies linear equations, vectors and matrices; ML uses it to store data (tables, text, images) as vectors and matrices and to work on a whole dataset in one step. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#61-what-linear-algebra-is) | Linear algebra roadmap |
| <span id="G-1091">G-1091</span> | Linear combination | A sum of vectors each multiplied by a number, $a_1\mathbf v_1 + \dots + a_k\mathbf v_k$; changing the numbers reaches different vectors, and all the vectors it can reach form the span. | [Note MA-052](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis.md#5-linear-combinations) | Linear combinations, span and basis |
| <span id="G-1092">G-1092</span> | Linear interpolation | Placing a percentile between two neighbouring sorted values, in proportion to its position; the pandas default. | [Note ML-043](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-043-outliers-percentile/ML-043-outliers-percentile.md#22-from-percentiles-to-limits) | Percentile outlier method |
| <span id="G-1093">G-1093</span> | Linear program | A problem of making a straight-line (linear) function as small as possible when the allowed choices are limited by linear inequalities (linear constraints). It is a common kind of convex problem with its own fast solvers. | [Note MA-068](MA/07-optimisation/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming.md#21-the-problem) | Linear and quadratic programming |
| <span id="G-1094">G-1094</span> | Linear regression | A model that predicts a number from the inputs by fitting the straight line (or, with several inputs, the plane) that runs closest to all the training points. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#1-overview) | Simple linear regression |
| <span id="G-1095">G-1095</span> | Linear relationship | A relationship between two columns that follows a straight line. | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#2-assumption-1-a-linear-relationship) | Assumptions of linear regression |
| <span id="G-1097">G-1097</span> | Linear transformation | A change of the whole plane by a matrix that keeps grid lines straight and evenly spaced, and the origin fixed; algebraically, $L(\mathbf{v} + \mathbf{w}) = L(\mathbf{v}) + L(\mathbf{w})$ and $L(c\mathbf{v}) = cL(\mathbf{v})$. | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#3-what-makes-a-transformation-linear) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-1096">G-1096</span> | Linear transformation to the number line | A linear transformation that sends each vector to a single number, keeping evenly spaced dots evenly spaced; its matrix is one row ($1 \times n$), so applying it is the same as a dot product with that row. | [Note MA-055](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-055-dot-product-and-duality/MA-055-dot-product-and-duality.md#4-linear-transformations-from-vectors-to-numbers) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-1098">G-1098</span> | Linear variants of ReLU | ReLU variants that replace ReLU's flat zero on the negative side with a straight line of small slope (Leaky ReLU and PReLU), so a node with negative inputs still has a gradient and keeps learning. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#5-linear-variants) | Leaky ReLU, PReLU, ELU and SELU |
| <span id="G-1099">G-1099</span> | Linearisation | Replacing a function near a point by its tangent line (its first-order Taylor polynomial). | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#63-degree-1-the-tangent-line) | Taylor series |
| <span id="G-1100">G-1100</span> | Linearity of expectation | The rule that the expected value of a sum is the sum of the expected values, with constants scaling and shifting it: $E[aX + bY + c] = a\thinspace E[X] + b\thinspace E[Y] + c$, for any random variables. | [Note MA-012](MA/02-probability/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance.md#43-the-shortcut-formula) | Expected value and variance of a random variable |
| <span id="G-1101">G-1101</span> | Linearly dependent | Said of a set of vectors in which at least one can be built from the others by scaling and adding (a linear combination), so it adds nothing to the span. | [Note MA-052](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis.md#7-linear-dependence-and-independence) | Linear combinations, span and basis |
| <span id="G-1102">G-1102</span> | Linearly independent | Said of a set of vectors in which none can be built from the others, so each one adds a new direction to the span. | [Note MA-052](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis.md#7-linear-dependence-and-independence) | Linear combinations, span and basis |
| <span id="G-1103">G-1103</span> | Linearly separable | Data whose classes a straight line, plane or hyperplane can split. | [Note ML-069](ML/07-classification/ML-069-perceptron-trick/ML-069-perceptron-trick.md#2-when-logistic-regression-works) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-2166">G-2166</span> | Link | A labelled arrow on the course map from one Concept to another that says how they relate, with one of five types: needs, is a kind of, fixes, compared with, used in. | [Course map](00-course-map/00-course-map.md#3-the-concept-map) |  |
| <span id="G-1104">G-1104</span> | Linkage | The rule that measures the distance between two clusters, for example the distance between their closest points or their average distance; hierarchical clustering merges the two closest clusters by this rule, so the linkage shapes the result. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#8-linkage-the-distance-between-two-clusters) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-1105">G-1105</span> | List of grids | Several separate sets of hyperparameter values to try (parameter grids), passed to the search together, so values that do not work together never meet in one combination. | [Note ML-106](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-106-random-forest-tuning/ML-106-random-forest-tuning.md#72-a-trap-bootstrap-and-max_samples) | Grid and random search |
| <span id="G-1106">G-1106</span> | List, dictionary | Python's ordered collection `[...]`, and its `key: value` pairs `{...}`. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#6-separators-and-column-names-sep-names) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-1107">G-1107</span> | Load balancing | Spreading requests across servers so all users are served quickly. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#111-launching-at-scale) | Deployment |
| <span id="G-2204">G-2204</span> | Loading (loading score) | How much one original feature counts in a principal component (its weight there); it is one entry of that component's eigenvector. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#61-reading-the-result-the-recipe-and-the-scree-plot) | PCA |
| <span id="G-1108">G-1108</span> | Local linear map | The linear transformation a smooth function behaves like near a point; its matrix is the Jacobian. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#3-zoom-in-the-function-looks-linear) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-1109">G-1109</span> | Local maximum | A point higher than everything near it but not the highest overall. | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#7-local-maxima-and-starting-points) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-1110">G-1110</span> | Local minimum | A point lower than everything around it, but not the lowest overall. | [Note ML-056](ML/06-regression/ML-056-gradient-descent/ML-056-gradient-descent.md#8-the-shape-of-the-loss-function-matters) | Local minima and saddle points |
| <span id="G-1111">G-1111</span> | Local optimum (k-means) | A clustering where k-means has stopped but a better one exists, caused by a bad start. | [Note ML-124](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-124-kmeans-from-scratch/ML-124-kmeans-from-scratch.md#9-bad-random-starts) | K-means |
| <span id="G-2250">G-2250</span> | Locally linear | Said of a function that looks more and more like a linear transformation as we zoom in on any point. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#3-zoom-in-the-function-looks-linear) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-1112">G-1112</span> | Log transform | Replacing each value with its logarithm; pulls in a long right tail. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#5-log-transform) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-1113">G-1113</span> | Log-likelihood | The logarithm of the likelihood, which turns the product of probabilities into a sum of log probabilities; it peaks at the same parameters as the likelihood and is easier to compute and maximise. | [Note ML-072](ML/07-classification/ML-072-log-loss/ML-072-log-loss.md#42-taking-logs) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-1114">G-1114</span> | Log-log plot | A plot with both axes on a log scale: it plots $\ln y$ against $\ln x$. On it a power law shows as a straight line. | [Note MA-030](MA/03-distributions/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law.md#41-the-log-log-plot) | Pareto distribution and power laws |
| <span id="G-1115">G-1115</span> | Log-normal distribution | A distribution with a long tail to the right (right-skewed, continuous) whose values follow a normal distribution once you take their logarithm. | [Note MA-029](MA/03-distributions/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal.md#3-the-log-normal-distribution) | Log-normal distribution |
| <span id="G-1116">G-1116</span> | Log-odds | The natural log of the odds, $\ln(p/(1-p))$: it turns a probability between 0 and 1 into any number (0 at $p = 0.5$), so a model such as logistic regression can predict it with a straight line, and the sigmoid turns it back into a probability. | [Note ML-116](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-116-gradient-boosting-classification/ML-116-gradient-boosting-classification.md#4-stage-1-the-log-odds-of-class-1) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1117">G-1117</span> | log1p | NumPy's function that adds 1 to each value and then takes the log, $\log(1 + x)$, so the log transform also works when a value is 0. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#51-log1--x-for-columns-with-zeros) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-1118">G-1118</span> | Logical diagram | A drawing of a neural network as a chain of data arrays (tensors) linked by the operations between them. | [Note DL-047](DL/04-cnn/DL-047-backpropagation-in-cnn/DL-047-backpropagation-in-cnn.md#4-forward-propagation) | Backpropagation in a CNN |
| <span id="G-1119">G-1119</span> | Logistic function | Another name for the sigmoid, $1/(1 + e^{-z})$: the S-shaped function that squashes any number into a value between 0 and 1, read as a probability. | [Note ML-071](ML/07-classification/ML-071-sigmoid-function/ML-071-sigmoid-function.md#4-the-sigmoid-function) | Sigmoid function |
| <span id="G-1120">G-1120</span> | Logistic regression | A classification algorithm that passes a weighted sum of the inputs through the sigmoid to give the probability of a class; the line (or plane) where that probability is 0.5 is the boundary it learns between the classes. | [Note ML-069](ML/07-classification/ML-069-perceptron-trick/ML-069-perceptron-trick.md#1-overview) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1122">G-1122</span> | Logit | The raw score the final linear layer gives each vocabulary word, any number and not yet a probability; the softmax turns the logits into the probabilities of the next word. | [Note DL-084](DL/06-transformers/DL-084-transformer-decoder/DL-084-transformer-decoder.md#8-the-output-layer-linear-and-softmax) | Unembedding, logits, temperature and sampling |
| <span id="G-1121">G-1121</span> | Logit lens | A way to see what a language model would predict partway through: after each block, pass its running vector (the residual stream) through the final LayerNorm and the unembedding to read which tokens it would predict if it stopped there. It shows the guess forming over the blocks. | [Note DL-088](DL/06-transformers/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling.md#7-the-logit-lens-watching-the-guess-form) | Logit lens |
| <span id="G-1123">G-1123</span> | Long short-term memory (LSTM) | A recurrent network (RNN) that carries two memories from one time step to the next, a long-term one (the cell state) and a short-term one (the hidden state), with gates that control what each memory keeps and passes on. | [Note DL-061](DL/05-rnn/DL-061-lstm/DL-061-lstm.md#6-the-core-idea-a-second-path-for-long-term-memory) | LSTM (long short-term memory) |
| <span id="G-1124">G-1124</span> | Long-term context | What matters for the sequence as a whole, kept from earlier steps. | [Note DL-061](DL/05-rnn/DL-061-lstm/DL-061-lstm.md#4-how-we-read-a-story-two-kinds-of-context) | Long-term dependency problem |
| <span id="G-1126">G-1126</span> | Long-term dependency | An output that depends on an input many time steps earlier. | [Note DL-060](DL/05-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn.md#32-an-example-predicting-the-next-word) | Long-term dependency problem |
| <span id="G-1125">G-1125</span> | Long-term dependency problem | A simple RNN's failure to link an output to an input many time steps earlier (a long-term dependency), because the gradient shrinks away as it flows back through time (the vanishing gradient through time). | [Note DL-060](DL/05-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn.md#32-an-example-predicting-the-next-word) | Long-term dependency problem |
