| <span id="G-1127">G-1127</span> | Look-ahead point | The point that the momentum step alone would take the weights to, $w_t - \beta v_{t-1}$; Nesterov momentum measures the gradient there instead of at the current weights, so it corrects sooner and overshoots less. | [Note DL-035](DL/03-optimizers/DL-035-nesterov-accelerated-gradient/DL-035-nesterov-accelerated-gradient.md#4-two-pushes-at-once-or-one-after-the-other) | Nesterov accelerated gradient (NAG) |
| <span id="G-1128">G-1128</span> | Lookup table | The stored probabilities (class priors, and each feature value's probability within each class) that Naive Bayes computes once in training and then looks up to score new rows. | [Note ML-083](ML/07-classification/ML-083-naive-bayes-code/ML-083-naive-bayes-code.md#3-two-phases) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-706">G-706</span> | Loss function (error function) | A formula that measures how wrong a model's predictions are; in deep learning, the error on one training row. | [Note ML-050](ML/06-regression/ML-050-linear-regression-maths/ML-050-linear-regression-maths.md#32-adding-the-errors-up) | Ordinary least squares (closed form) |
| <span id="G-1131">G-1131</span> | Loss weight | A number that scales one output's loss in the total loss of a multi-output model. | [Note DL-054](DL/04-cnn/DL-054-keras-functional-api/DL-054-keras-functional-api.md#53-one-loss-per-output) | Keras functional API |
| <span id="G-1132">G-1132</span> | Low bias, high variance algorithm | An algorithm that fits its training data very well but changes a lot with the data, such as a fully grown tree; it overfits. | [Note ML-099](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-099-bagging-intuition/ML-099-bagging-intuition.md#31-the-bias-variance-problem) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-1133">G-1133</span> | Lower and upper limit (of a confidence interval) | The two ends of a confidence interval: the point estimate minus and plus the margin of error, the range in which the parameter is estimated to lie. | [Note MA-035](MA/04-inference/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure.md#4-confidence-intervals-and-confidence-levels) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-1134">G-1134</span> | Lower bound $B(\theta; q)$ | A simpler function that always sits at or below the log-likelihood and touches it when $q$ are the current responsibilities. EM's M-step climbs this bound, which pushes the log-likelihood up at least as much. | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#63-the-lower-bound) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-1135">G-1135</span> | LPA | Lakh rupees per annum: a salary in hundreds of thousands of rupees per year. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#2-the-problem) | Simple linear regression |
| <span id="G-1137">G-1137</span> | Luong attention (multiplicative, dot) | Attention between a decoder state and the encoder states, scored by multiplying them: $s_i^\top h_j$ (dot) or $s_i^\top W_a h_j$. | [Note DL-070](DL/06-transformers/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention.md#5-luong-attention) | Luong (multiplicative) attention |
| <span id="G-1139">G-1139</span> | M-step | The second step of each EM round (maximisation): with the responsibilities held fixed, re-estimate each component's mean, covariance and weight as responsibility-weighted averages, so the fit to the data improves. | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#21-one-round-recalled) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-1140">G-1140</span> | Machine Learning (ML) | Using statistics to let a machine find patterns (rules) in data by itself. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#2-defining-machine-learning) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-1141">G-1141</span> | Machine translation | Translating a sentence from one language into another. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#52-different-lengths) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-1142">G-1142</span> | Maclaurin series | The Taylor series around $x_0 = 0$: an infinite sum of powers of $x$, built from a function's derivatives at 0, that approximates the function near 0. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#62-the-taylor-polynomial-of-degree-n) | Taylor series |
| <span id="G-1143">G-1143</span> | Macro average | The plain mean of a metric (such as precision) over all classes, so every class counts equally however many rows it has. | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#62-combining-the-classes) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-1144">G-1144</span> | Magnitude (of a quantity) | The number part of a quantity, as opposed to its unit. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#2-what-normalization-is) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-1145">G-1145</span> | Majority class | The class with the most rows in imbalanced data. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#2-what-imbalanced-data-looks-like) | Imbalanced data |
| <span id="G-1146">G-1146</span> | Majority vote | Predicting the class that most voters choose: the k neighbours in KNN, or the base models of an ensemble. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#21-the-steps) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-1147">G-1147</span> | make_blobs | scikit-learn function that generates points around chosen centres. | [Note ML-123](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-123-kmeans-code/ML-123-kmeans-code.md#8-k-means-in-three-dimensions) | K-means |
| <span id="G-1149">G-1149</span> | make_classification | scikit-learn function that creates random classification data. | [Note ML-070](ML/07-classification/ML-070-perceptron-code/ML-070-perceptron-code.md#2-the-data) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1150">G-1150</span> | make_column_transformer | scikit-learn function that sets up different preprocessing for different columns (a column transformer) from (transformer, columns) pairs, without you naming the steps. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#59-pipeline-compared-with-make_pipeline) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-1152">G-1152</span> | make_pipeline | scikit-learn function that chains steps such as preprocessing and a model into one object (a pipeline) from the objects alone, naming each step after its class. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#59-pipeline-compared-with-make_pipeline) | ML pipelines |
| <span id="G-1153">G-1153</span> | make_regression | scikit-learn function that generates data following a linear pattern plus noise. | [Note ML-052](ML/06-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression.md#4-multiple-linear-regression-in-scikit-learn) | Multiple linear regression |
| <span id="G-1154">G-1154</span> | Many-to-many RNN | An RNN that takes a sequence and produces a sequence; also called sequence-to-sequence. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#5-many-to-many) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-1155">G-1155</span> | Many-to-one RNN | An RNN that reads a sequence and gives one output at the end. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#3-many-to-one) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-1157">G-1157</span> | MAP rule | Predict the class that is most probable for the row, given its features (the class with the largest posterior probability; maximum a posteriori). | [Note ML-082](ML/07-classification/ML-082-naive-bayes-maths/ML-082-naive-bayes-maths.md#6-the-formula-and-the-map-rule) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-1158">G-1158</span> | MAR | Missing at random: the gaps depend on another, recorded column. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#52-the-other-two-kinds-mar-and-mnar) | Missing values |
| <span id="G-1159">G-1159</span> | Margin (gap) | The distance from a separating line to the nearest point of a class. | [Note ML-070](ML/07-classification/ML-070-perceptron-code/ML-070-perceptron-code.md#61-comparing-with-logistic-regression) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1160">G-1160</span> | Margin (SVM) | The full width of the empty band around an SVM's separating line, from the nearest point of one class to the nearest point of the other (later shown to be $2/\lVert w \rVert$); the SVM picks the line that makes this band widest. | [Note ML-086](ML/07-classification/ML-086-svm-intuition/ML-086-svm-intuition.md#2-the-idea-in-one-dimension) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1161">G-1161</span> | Margin error | The part of the soft-margin SVM loss, $\lVert w \rVert / 2$, that grows as the margin narrows; minimising it pushes the margin wider. | [Note ML-088](ML/07-classification/ML-088-svm-soft-margin/ML-088-svm-soft-margin.md#5-the-soft-margin-loss) | Hinge loss and soft margin |
| <span id="G-1162">G-1162</span> | Margin of error | The distance from the point estimate to either end of a confidence interval. | [Note MA-035](MA/04-inference/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure.md#4-confidence-intervals-and-confidence-levels) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-1163">G-1163</span> | Margin-maximising hyperplane | The flat boundary (hyperplane) that splits the classes while staying as far as possible from the nearest points of each (the largest margin); it is the decision boundary an SVM learns. | [Note ML-086](ML/07-classification/ML-086-svm-intuition/ML-086-svm-intuition.md#4-the-margin) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1164">G-1164</span> | Marginal (simple, unconditional) probability | The probability of one variable's value whatever the other variable does. | [Note MA-014](MA/02-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability.md#3-marginal-probability) | Joint and marginal probability |
| <span id="G-1165">G-1165</span> | Marginal probability distribution | All the marginal probabilities of one variable, read from a joint table. | [Note MA-014](MA/02-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability.md#32-marginal-probability-distributions) | Joint and marginal probability |
| <span id="G-1166">G-1166</span> | Marginalising | Adding up a table of probabilities of two variables (a joint distribution) over every value of one variable, which removes that variable and leaves the other's probabilities. | [Note MA-014](MA/02-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability.md#31-reading-the-margins) | Joint and marginal probability |
| <span id="G-1167">G-1167</span> | Margins | The row and column totals of a contingency table, where marginal probabilities are read. | [Note MA-014](MA/02-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability/MA-014-joint-marginal-conditional-probability.md#31-reading-the-margins) | Joint and marginal probability |
| <span id="G-1168">G-1168</span> | Markdown | A simple way to format text with symbols such as `#` and `**`. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#32-code-cells-and-markdown-cells) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1169">G-1169</span> | Mask matrix $M$ | A matrix added to the attention scores: 0 where attention is allowed, $-\infty$ where it is blocked. | [Note DL-082](DL/06-transformers/DL-082-masked-self-attention/DL-082-masked-self-attention.md#61-where-the-mask-goes) | Masked self-attention |
| <span id="G-1170">G-1170</span> | Masked language model | A pre-training task that hides some words and asks the model to predict them from both sides (BERT). | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#8-stage-5-large-language-models-2018-onward) | Large language models (LLMs) |
| <span id="G-1171">G-1171</span> | Masked multi-head attention | Multi-head attention with the causal mask in every head, so no word can take information from the words after it; the first sub-layer of the transformer decoder. | [Note DL-082](DL/06-transformers/DL-082-masked-self-attention/DL-082-masked-self-attention.md#8-masked-multi-head-attention) | Masked self-attention |
| <span id="G-1172">G-1172</span> | Masked self-attention | Self-attention in which each word can take information only from itself and earlier words: the later words' scores get $-\infty$ before the softmax, so their weights are 0, $\text{softmax}(QK^T/\sqrt{d_k} + M)\thinspace V$. It lets the decoder train on whole target sentences in one pass without seeing the future. | [Note DL-082](DL/06-transformers/DL-082-masked-self-attention/DL-082-masked-self-attention.md#6-the-fix-mask-the-future) | Masked self-attention |
| <span id="G-1173">G-1173</span> | Mathematical problem | A business goal restated as a measurable target, such as a churn rate to reach. | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#4-step-1-business-problem-to-ml-problem) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-1174">G-1174</span> | Mathematical transformation | Applying one mathematical formula, such as the log or the square root, to every value of a column, usually to bring its distribution closer to normal. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#1-overview) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-1180">G-1180</span> | Matrix | A table of numbers in rows and columns (a 2D tensor); it can hold a dataset (one row per observation), and multiplying a vector by it transforms the vector. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#2-what-a-tensor-is) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-1175">G-1175</span> | Matrix (of a transformation) | The grid of numbers that records a linear transformation: its columns are where the basis vectors land, which is enough to find where every vector lands. | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#5-the-matrix-of-a-transformation) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-1176">G-1176</span> | Matrix calculus | Rules for differentiating expressions that contain vectors and matrices, such as a loss written in matrix form, so all the partial derivatives come out in one formula. | [Note ML-053](ML/06-regression/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths.md#51-three-rules-of-matrix-calculus-each-checked-on-numbers) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-1177">G-1177</span> | Matrix factorisation (decomposition) | Writing a matrix as a product of simpler matrices. | [Note MA-047](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-047-linear-algebra-roadmap/MA-047-linear-algebra-roadmap.md#46-matrix-factorisation) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1178">G-1178</span> | Matrix factorisation (recommenders) | Predicting ratings as a viewer vector times a film vector, fitted on the known ratings only. | [Note MA-060](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning.md#42-reading-the-singular-vectors) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1179">G-1179</span> | Matrix product | The single matrix $BA$ that does the same as applying $A$ and then $B$ (their composition), so a chain of transformations can be done as one multiplication. | [Note MA-054](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition.md#3-why-the-product-is-written-right-to-left) | Matrix multiplication as composition |
| <span id="G-1181">G-1181</span> | Matrix-vector multiplication | Multiplying a matrix by a vector, $A\mathbf{x}$: each column of $A$ is scaled by the matching entry of $\mathbf{x}$ and the results are added (a linear combination of the columns). | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#5-the-matrix-of-a-transformation) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-1182">G-1182</span> | Max pooling | Pooling that keeps only the largest value in each window (usually 2 × 2), which shrinks the feature map while keeping the strongest response in each region. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#4-max-pooling) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-1183">G-1183</span> | Max-abs scaling | Divide by the largest absolute value in the column, giving values from -1 to 1. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#8-max-abs-scaling) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-1184">G-1184</span> | max_depth | The cap on a tree's depth; None lets it grow until every leaf is pure. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#2-overfitting-and-underfitting-in-a-tree) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1185">G-1185</span> | max_features | The number of randomly chosen columns a tree considers at each split. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#46-max_features) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1186">G-1186</span> | max_leaf_nodes | The cap on the number of leaves; the tree grows best-first until it is reached. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#47-max_leaf_nodes) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1187">G-1187</span> | max_samples | The number or share of rows each base model gets. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#32-bagging) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-1188">G-1188</span> | MaxAbsScaler | scikit-learn's class for max-abs scaling. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#8-max-abs-scaling) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-2217">G-2217</span> | Maximal margin classifier | The classifier that separates the classes with the largest possible margin; the hard-margin SVM. | [Note ML-086](ML/07-classification/ML-086-svm-intuition/ML-086-svm-intuition.md#2-the-idea-in-one-dimension) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1189">G-1189</span> | Maximum a posteriori (MAP) estimation | Choosing the parameters with the largest posterior probability given the data, which weighs the likelihood by a prior belief about the parameters; in practice, minimise the negative log-likelihood minus the log prior. | [Note MA-072](MA/08-likelihood/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning.md#71-the-posterior-over-the-parameters) | MAP estimation |
| <span id="G-1190">G-1190</span> | Maximum likelihood estimate $\hat\theta_{\text{ML}}$ | The parameter value where the likelihood function is highest. | [Note MA-070](MA/08-likelihood/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation.md#3-the-idea-slide-the-curve-keep-the-peak) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-1191">G-1191</span> | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) | Fitting parameters by making the likelihood of the observed data as large as possible. | [Note MA-070](MA/08-likelihood/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation.md#72-the-maximum-likelihood-estimate) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-1192">G-1192</span> | MCAR | Missing completely at random: the gaps have no relation to any value in the data. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#51-missing-completely-at-random-mcar) | Missing values |
| <span id="G-1203">G-1203</span> | Mean | The average of the values; the centre of the data. | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#71-count-mean-standard-deviation-minimum-and-maximum) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-1193">G-1193</span> | Mean absolute deviation | The average absolute distance of the points from their mean. Sometimes also abbreviated MAD, which clashes with the median absolute deviation. | [Note MA-006](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion.md#7-mean-absolute-deviation) | Variance |
| <span id="G-1194">G-1194</span> | Mean absolute error (MAE) | The average absolute difference between actual and predicted values. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#2-mean-absolute-error-mae) | Regression metrics |
| <span id="G-1195">G-1195</span> | Mean centring | Subtracting the mean from every value, so the column's mean becomes 0. | [Note MA-049](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations.md#5-mean-centring-shifting-in-ml) | Vector magnitude, distance and scalar operations |
| <span id="G-1196">G-1196</span> | Mean decrease in impurity (MDI) | A score of how much a column helps the trees: its share of the total drop in impurity from all splits made on it, weighted and averaged over the trees (impurity-based feature importance, also called Gini importance). | [Note ML-108](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-108-feature-importance/ML-108-feature-importance.md#4-how-a-decision-tree-computes-feature-importance) | Feature importance |
| <span id="G-2237">G-2237</span> | Mean difference ($\mu_d$) | The average of each subject's difference between its two measurements (a paired design); the paired t-test tests whether it is 0, $\mu_d = 0$. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#51-when-to-use-it) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1197">G-1197</span> | Mean imputation | Filling every gap with the mean of the column's known values. | [Note ML-035](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data.md#2-mean-and-median-imputation) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-1198">G-1198</span> | Mean normalization | Subtract the mean and divide by the range, giving values from -1 to 1 centred on 0. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#7-mean-normalization) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-1199">G-1199</span> | Mean of a random variable | Another name for its expected value: the probability-weighted average of its possible values, the average outcome over many repeats. | [Note MA-012](MA/02-probability/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance.md#3-expected-value) | Expected value and variance of a random variable |
| <span id="G-1200">G-1200</span> | Mean square (MSB, MSW) | A sum of squared distances divided by its degrees of freedom, which turns it into a variance (a measure of spread). ANOVA divides the between-group mean square (MSB) by the within-group one (MSW) to get the F statistic. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#51-mean-squares) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-1201">G-1201</span> | Mean squared error (MSE) | The average squared difference between actual and predicted values. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#3-mean-squared-error-mse) | Regression metrics |
| <span id="G-1204">G-1204</span> | mean(axis=0) | The mean of each column of an array. | [Note ML-124](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-124-kmeans-from-scratch/ML-124-kmeans-from-scratch.md#6-step-4-moving-the-centroids) | K-means |
| <span id="G-1205">G-1205</span> | Measure of central tendency | A single number for the typical, central value of a column. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#2-what-central-tendency-means) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-1206">G-1206</span> | Measure of dispersion | A number that describes how spread out a column is around its centre. | [Note MA-006](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion.md#1-overview) | Variance |
| <span id="G-1209">G-1209</span> | Median | The middle value of sorted data; the 50% percentile. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#4-median) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-1207">G-1207</span> | Median absolute deviation (MAD) | The median distance of the values from their median. | [Note ML-021](ML/02-getting-data/ML-021-pandas-profiling/ML-021-pandas-profiling.md#42-a-numerical-column-age) | Outliers |
| <span id="G-1208">G-1208</span> | Median imputation | Filling every gap with the median of the column's known values; better for skewed columns. | [Note ML-035](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data/ML-035-imputing-numerical-data.md#2-mean-and-median-imputation) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-1210">G-1210</span> | Memoization | Storing the result of a function call and returning the stored result when the same input comes again. | [Note DL-019](DL/01-basics/DL-019-mlp-memoization/DL-019-mlp-memoization.md#3-memoization-on-the-fibonacci-numbers) | Memoization |
| <span id="G-1211">G-1211</span> | meshgrid | NumPy function that builds every combination of x and y values: the grid for a decision surface. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#52-how-it-is-built) | Decision surface and boundary |
| <span id="G-1212">G-1212</span> | Mesokurtic | Said of a distribution whose tails are like the normal's (excess kurtosis 0), so extreme values are about as common as under a normal distribution. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#4-excess-kurtosis-and-the-three-types) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-1213">G-1213</span> | Meta-model | The final model in stacking: it takes the other models' (base models') predictions as its inputs and is trained to combine them. | [Note ML-095](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-095-ensemble-learning/ML-095-ensemble-learning.md#42-stacking) | Stacking and blending |
| <span id="G-1214">G-1214</span> | method | The `PowerTransformer` setting that picks which formula reshapes the column: `"box-cox"` or `"yeo-johnson"`. | [Note ML-030](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-030-power-transformer/ML-030-power-transformer.md#2-power-transformer-in-scikit-learn) | Power transformer |
| <span id="G-1215">G-1215</span> | Metric (performance metric) | A number that measures how well a model works, and so tells whether the work is moving in the right direction. | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#8-step-5-metrics) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-1216">G-1216</span> | MICE | A way to fill gaps: each column's missing values are predicted by a model trained on the other columns, and the rounds repeat until the filled values settle (Multivariate Imputation by Chained Equations, the algorithm behind the iterative imputer). | [Note ML-039](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice.md#1-overview) | Iterative imputation (MICE) |
| <span id="G-1217">G-1217</span> | Min-max scaling | Subtract the column's minimum and divide by its range, giving values from 0 to 1; the main normalization technique. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#41-the-formula) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-1218">G-1218</span> | min_frequency | `OneHotEncoder` parameter that merges rare categories into one column. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#8-top-categories-on-the-brand-column) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-1219">G-1219</span> | min_impurity_decrease | A tree setting: a split is made only if it makes the nodes purer by at least this much (the smallest weighted impurity decrease allowed). | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#48-min_impurity_decrease) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1220">G-1220</span> | min_samples_leaf | The smallest number of rows every leaf must keep. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#45-min_samples_leaf) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1221">G-1221</span> | min_samples_split | The smallest number of rows a node must hold to be split. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#44-min_samples_split) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1222">G-1222</span> | Mini-batch gradient descent | Gradient descent that uses a small random group of rows for every update. | [Note ML-059](ML/06-regression/ML-059-mini-batch-gradient-descent/ML-059-mini-batch-gradient-descent.md#3-how-it-works) | Mini-batch gradient descent |
| <span id="G-1224">G-1224</span> | Miniforge | A small installer with only conda and Python, using conda-forge. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#22-miniforge-the-installer-we-use) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1225">G-1225</span> | Minimax inequality | Taking the smallest value over one argument and then the largest over the other never gives more than doing it in the other order: $\max_{\mathbf{y}} \min_{\mathbf{x}} \varphi \le \min_{\mathbf{x}} \max_{\mathbf{y}} \varphi$ for any function of two arguments. It is why the dual problem's answer is never above the primal's (weak duality). | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#62-weak-and-strong-duality) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-1226">G-1226</span> | Minimum-norm solution | When many solutions fit equally well (the same least-squares error), the shortest one, with the smallest length (norm); what $A^{+}\mathbf{b}$ returns. | [Note MA-060](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning.md#53-when-the-normal-equation-breaks) | Moore-Penrose pseudo-inverse |
| <span id="G-1227">G-1227</span> | Minkowski distance | A family of distances between two points, $\left(\sum_i \lvert x_i - y_i \rvert^p\right)^{1/p}$, set by the number $p$: $p = 2$ gives the Euclidean distance and $p = 1$ the Manhattan distance; KNN uses it to find the nearest neighbours. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#21-the-steps) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-1228">G-1228</span> | MinMaxScaler | scikit-learn's class for min-max scaling. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#52-split-fit-and-transform) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-1229">G-1229</span> | Minority class | The rare class in imbalanced data, usually the one we need to find. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#2-what-imbalanced-data-looks-like) | Imbalanced data |
| <span id="G-1230">G-1230</span> | Minorizer / majorizer | A simpler function that lies below (minorizer) or above (majorizer) the function being optimised (the objective) and touches it at the current point; maximising a minorizer (or minimising a majorizer) moves the objective the same way, which is how EM makes progress. | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#9-em-is-a-minorizemaximize-mm-algorithm) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-1231">G-1231</span> | MinPts (min_samples) | The DBSCAN setting for how many points a point's eps-neighbourhood (the circle of radius eps around it) must hold for the region to count as dense, which makes the point a core point. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#5-measuring-density-eps-and-minpts) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-1232">G-1232</span> | Missing category imputation | Filling every gap in a categorical column with a new category, "Missing". | [Note ML-036](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-036-missing-categorical-data/ML-036-missing-categorical-data.md#31-the-idea) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-1233">G-1233</span> | Missing indicator | A 0/1 (True/False) column recording whether a value was missing, added next to the imputed column so the model can learn whether being missing itself matters. | [Note ML-037](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample.md#6-missing-indicator) | Missing indicator |
| <span id="G-1234">G-1234</span> | Missing value | An empty entry, shown by pandas as `NaN`. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#9-loading-only-some-columns-usecols) | Missing values |
| <span id="G-1236">G-1236</span> | Mixed partial derivative | A second partial derivative taken with respect to two different variables, such as $\partial^2 f/\partial y\thinspace\partial x$: how the slope along $x$ changes as $y$ changes; the off-diagonal entries of the Hessian. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#41-second-partial-derivatives) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1237">G-1237</span> | Mixed variable | A column holding both numerical and categorical data. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#2-what-a-mixed-variable-is) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-1238">G-1238</span> | Mixture weight $\pi_k$ | How big a share of a mixture one component $k$ gets; the weights are non-negative and add up to 1. | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#61-the-formula) | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) |
| <span id="G-1239">G-1239</span> | ML algorithm | A general method that finds the pattern between inputs and outputs in data. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#32-learning-patterns-from-data) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-1240">G-1240</span> | MLDLC | Machine learning development life cycle: the guidelines for building an ML product from idea to product. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#22-the-machine-learning-development-life-cycle) | ML development life cycle |
| <span id="G-1241">G-1241</span> | MLE of a binomial $p$ | The success probability of a binomial that makes the observed data most likely: the observed share of successes, $\hat p = x/n$. | [Note MA-071](MA/08-likelihood/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions.md#23-the-general-formula) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-1242">G-1242</span> | MLE of a normal distribution | The normal curve that makes the data most likely: its mean is the sample mean and its variance the average squared distance from that mean (dividing by $n$), $\hat\mu = \bar{x}$ and $\hat\sigma^2 = \sum(x_i - \bar{x})^2/n$. | [Note MA-071](MA/08-likelihood/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions.md#44-the-mle-of-the-standard-deviation) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-1243">G-1243</span> | MLE of an exponential rate | The rate of an exponential distribution that makes the observed waiting times most likely: one over their mean, $\hat\lambda = n/\sum x_i = 1/\bar{x}$. | [Note MA-071](MA/08-likelihood/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions.md#33-the-derivation) | Exponential distribution |
| <span id="G-1244">G-1244</span> | MLOps | Running and maintaining ML models in production. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#10-cost) | MLOps and cost |
| <span id="G-1245">G-1245</span> | MLP block | The feed-forward part of a transformer block: it widens each token's vector with a matrix (up-projection), applies an activation, shrinks it back (down-projection) and adds the result to the token's vector. | [Note DL-089](DL/06-transformers/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts.md#3-the-mlp-block-in-one-formula) | MLP blocks as fact storage |
| <span id="G-1246">G-1246</span> | MLPClassifier | scikit-learn's class for a classifier built from layers of perceptrons (a multi-layer perceptron). | [Note DL-009](DL/01-basics/DL-009-mlp-intuition/DL-009-mlp-intuition.md#52-other-shapes) | Multi-layer perceptron (MLP) |
| <span id="G-1247">G-1247</span> | MM algorithm | A way to optimise a hard function by repeatedly optimising a simpler one (a surrogate) that lies on one side of it and touches it at the current point; the name stands for minorize–maximize (or majorize–minimize). | [Note MA-074](MA/08-likelihood/MA-074-expectation-maximization/MA-074-expectation-maximization.md#9-em-is-a-minorizemaximize-mm-algorithm) | Expectation maximization (EM) |
| <span id="G-1248">G-1248</span> | MNAR | Missing not at random: the gaps depend on the missing value itself. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#52-the-other-two-kinds-mar-and-mnar) | Missing values |
| <span id="G-1249">G-1249</span> | MNIST | A dataset of about 70,000 handwritten-digit images of 28 × 28 pixels. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#81-pixels-as-columns-the-mnist-dataset) | Feature extraction |
| <span id="G-1250">G-1250</span> | Modality | A kind of data, such as text, images or audio. | [Note DL-071](DL/06-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers.md#63-multimodal-models) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-1251">G-1251</span> | Mode | The most common value of a column. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#5-mode) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-1256">G-1256</span> | Model | The logic produced by training, used to give outputs for new inputs. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#32-learning-patterns-from-data) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-1253">G-1253</span> | Model drift / concept drift | A model's accuracy dropping as the real world changes; sometimes called model rot. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#41-models-go-stale) | Model drift |
| <span id="G-1254">G-1254</span> | Model selection | Training several algorithms and keeping the best. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#83-model-selection-and-hyperparameter-tuning) | ML development life cycle |
| <span id="G-1257">G-1257</span> | Model-based learning | Learning a mathematical function from the data and predicting with it. | [Note ML-006](ML/01-foundations/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based.md#2-two-ways-to-learn) | Model-based learning |
| <span id="G-1258">G-1258</span> | Momentum (optimizer) | Gradient descent that keeps part of its past movement: each step follows an average of past gradients in which older ones count less and less (a velocity, an exponentially decaying average). | [Note DL-034](DL/03-optimizers/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum.md#6-the-update-rule) | SGD with momentum |
| <span id="G-1259">G-1259</span> | monotonic_cst | Setting that forces predictions to only rise or only fall as a column grows. | [Note ML-105](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-105-random-forest-hyperparameters/ML-105-random-forest-hyperparameters.md#5-the-general-hyperparameters) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-1260">G-1260</span> | Monotonicity | Whether a column's values only go up, or only go down, from row to row. | [Note ML-021](ML/02-getting-data/ML-021-pandas-profiling/ML-021-pandas-profiling.md#42-a-numerical-column-age) | Pandas Profiling |
| <span id="G-1261">G-1261</span> | Moore's law | The number of transistors on a chip doubles about every two years. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#52-hardware) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-1262">G-1262</span> | Moore–Penrose pseudo-inverse ($A^{+}$) | The closest thing to an inverse for a matrix that is not square or has no inverse (a generalised inverse): the SVD inverted with zero singular values left at zero, $V\Sigma^{+}U^{\mathsf T}$. | [Note MA-060](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning.md#51-inverting-the-svd) | Moore-Penrose pseudo-inverse |
| <span id="G-1263">G-1263</span> | More extreme | Said of a possible test result with at least as much evidence against $H_0$ as the observed one, in the direction(s) $H_1$ points to; the p-value is the probability of a result this extreme or more extreme when $H_0$ is true. | [Note MA-041](MA/04-inference/MA-041-p-values/MA-041-p-values.md#2-definition) | P-values |
| <span id="G-1264">G-1264</span> | Most frequent value imputation (mode imputation) | Filling every gap in a column with its mode. | [Note ML-036](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-036-missing-categorical-data/ML-036-missing-categorical-data.md#21-the-idea) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-2267">G-2267</span> | Moving average | The list of averages a sliding window gives: each output is the mean of the numbers under the window, so sharp jumps are smoothed out. | [Note DL-042](DL/04-cnn/DL-042-convolution-operation/DL-042-convolution-operation.md#41-a-moving-average) | Convolution operation and feature maps |
| <span id="G-1265">G-1265</span> | Moving mean and moving variance | Running averages of each node's mean and variance over the training batches, kept during training (in batch normalization) and used in place of the batch's own values at prediction. | [Note DL-031](DL/02-training/DL-031-batch-normalization/DL-031-batch-normalization.md#5-batch-normalisation-during-prediction) | Batch normalisation |
| <span id="G-1266">G-1266</span> | Multi-class classification | Classification where the output is one of more than two classes, such as the next word out of a whole vocabulary; the output layer has one node per class. | [Note DL-063](DL/05-rnn/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction.md#51-why-not-regression) | Next-word prediction with an LSTM |
| <span id="G-1267">G-1267</span> | Multi-class output layer | An output layer with one node per class; the highest output gives the prediction. | [Note DL-009](DL/01-basics/DL-009-mlp-intuition/DL-009-mlp-intuition.md#43-more-nodes-in-the-output-layer) | Multi-layer perceptron (MLP) |
| <span id="G-1268">G-1268</span> | Multi-head attention | Several self-attentions (heads) run in parallel on the same input, each with its own weights so each can capture a different kind of relation between words; their outputs are joined and mixed by one more learned matrix, $W_O$. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#5-the-idea-several-self-attentions-in-parallel) | Multi-head attention |
| <span id="G-1269">G-1269</span> | Multi-input model | A model that takes several inputs, such as a photo and a table. | [Note DL-054](DL/04-cnn/DL-054-keras-functional-api/DL-054-keras-functional-api.md#3-when-a-straight-line-is-not-enough) | Keras functional API |
| <span id="G-1270">G-1270</span> | Multi-layer perceptron (MLP) | Many perceptrons organised in layers: input, hidden and output. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#21-multi-layer-perceptron-mlp) | Multi-layer perceptron (MLP) |
| <span id="G-1271">G-1271</span> | Multi-layer stacking | Stacking with more than one layer of base models below the meta-model. | [Note ML-121](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-121-stacking-blending/ML-121-stacking-blending.md#8-stacking-in-several-layers) | Stacking and blending |
| <span id="G-1272">G-1272</span> | Multi-output model | A model that predicts several targets at once, each from its own output layer. | [Note DL-054](DL/04-cnn/DL-054-keras-functional-api/DL-054-keras-functional-api.md#3-when-a-straight-line-is-not-enough) | Keras functional API |
| <span id="G-1273">G-1273</span> | Multicollinearity | A relationship between input columns, so that one can be calculated (or largely predicted) from the others; it makes a linear model's coefficients unstable and hard to interpret. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#32-multicollinearity-inputs-must-not-depend-on-each-other) | Multicollinearity |
| <span id="G-1275">G-1275</span> | Multimodal | Having more than one mode (two modes: bimodal). | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#5-mode) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-1274">G-1274</span> | Multimodal model | A model that takes in or produces more than one modality. | [Note DL-071](DL/06-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers.md#63-multimodal-models) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-1276">G-1276</span> | Multinomial logistic regression | Logistic regression extended to more than two classes: it gives each class a probability with the softmax. Also called softmax regression. | [Note ML-078](ML/07-classification/ML-078-softmax-regression/ML-078-softmax-regression.md#1-overview) | Softmax regression |
| <span id="G-1277">G-1277</span> | MultinomialNB | Naive Bayes for count data, such as word counts. | [Note ML-084](ML/07-classification/ML-084-gaussian-naive-bayes/ML-084-gaussian-naive-bayes.md#7-when-the-data-is-not-normal) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-1278">G-1278</span> | Multiple imputation | Making several filled copies of the data to see how unsure the fills are. | [Note ML-039](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice/ML-039-iterative-imputer-mice.md#85-several-imputations) | Iterative imputation (MICE) |
| <span id="G-1279">G-1279</span> | Multiple linear regression | Linear regression with several input columns. | [Note ML-052](ML/06-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression.md#1-overview) | Multiple linear regression |
| <span id="G-2212">G-2212</span> | Multiplication rule | The chance that both A and B happen is the chance of B times the chance of A given B: $P(A \cap B) = P(A \mid B) \times P(B)$. | [Note MA-015](MA/02-probability/MA-015-conditional-probability/MA-015-conditional-probability.md#5-the-multiplication-rule) | Conditional probability |
| <span id="G-1280">G-1280</span> | Multivariate analysis | Studying more than two variables together. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#2-univariate-bivariate-and-multivariate-analysis) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-1281">G-1281</span> | Multivariate chain rule | How to take a derivative when a function depends on its inputs through in-between (intermediate) variables: multiply the derivatives along each path and add the paths. In matrix form, a row gradient times a matrix of inner derivatives. | [Note MA-062](MA/06-calculus/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients.md#6-the-chain-rule-with-several-variables) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-1282">G-1282</span> | Multivariate imputation | Filling the gaps in one column using the values of the other columns too (as the KNN and iterative imputers do), so each fill fits the rest of its row. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#32-imputing-filling-in-the-gaps) | Missing values |
| <span id="G-1283">G-1283</span> | Multivariate normal distribution | The bell-shaped (normal) distribution for several numbers at once, a vector; it is set by a mean vector (its centre) and a covariance matrix (its spread and how the numbers vary together). | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#71-the-multivariate-normal) | Multivariate normal distribution |
| <span id="G-1284">G-1284</span> | Multivariate Taylor polynomial | An approximation of a function of several variables near a point, built from its derivatives there up to order $n$ (the multivariate Taylor series cut after the $k = n$ term); the second-order one uses the gradient and the Hessian. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#61-the-terms-up-to-second-order) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1285">G-1285</span> | Multivariate Taylor series | An approximation of a function of several variables near a point $\mathbf x_0$ from all its derivatives there, each term adding one more order of correction: $\sum_k D^k f(\mathbf x_0)\thinspace\boldsymbol{\delta}^k / k!$. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#62-higher-orders-tensors-and-outer-products) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1286">G-1286</span> | Mutually exclusive events | Events that cannot happen at the same time; their intersection has probability 0. | [Note MA-017](MA/02-probability/MA-017-mutually-exclusive-events/MA-017-mutually-exclusive-events.md#2-the-definition) | Independent and mutually exclusive events |
| <span id="G-1287">G-1287</span> | n-gram | A sequence of $n$ consecutive words. | [Note DL-063](DL/05-rnn/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction.md#32-every-prefix-predicts-its-next-word) | Next-word prediction with an LSTM |
| <span id="G-1288">G-1288</span> | n_bins | The `KBinsDiscretizer` parameter for the number of bins. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#9-kbinsdiscretizer-in-scikit-learn) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-1289">G-1289</span> | n_components | The number of principal components PCA keeps; a number between 0 and 1 means a share of the variance. | [Note ML-048](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-048-pca-mnist/ML-048-pca-mnist.md#41-the-steps) | PCA |
| <span id="G-1291">G-1291</span> | n_estimators | The number of base models in an ensemble. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#2-seeing-bagging-on-decision-surfaces) | Ensemble learning |
| <span id="G-1290">G-1290</span> | n_estimators (AdaBoost) | The largest number of simple models (weak learners) AdaBoost builds, one per boosting round (stage). | [Note ML-112](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters.md#22-n_estimators-the-number-of-stages) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1292">G-1292</span> | n_init | How many times `KMeans` starts again from new starting centres (centroids); the run whose points sit closest to their centres (the lowest inertia) is kept. | [Note ML-123](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-123-kmeans-code/ML-123-kmeans-code.md#5-training-k-means-with-k--4) | K-means |
| <span id="G-1293">G-1293</span> | n_iter | RandomizedSearchCV's setting for how many hyperparameter combinations it draws at random and tries (default 10); trying a few instead of every combination makes the search much faster. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#72-tuning-with-gridsearchcv-and-randomizedsearchcv) | Grid and random search |
| <span id="G-1294">G-1294</span> | n_jobs | scikit-learn setting for how many CPU cores to use in parallel; -1 means all. | [Note ML-098](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-098-voting-regressor/ML-098-voting-regressor.md#42-the-voting-regressor) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-1295">G-1295</span> | Nabla ($\nabla$) | The upside-down triangle $\nabla$, read "nabla" or "grad"; $\nabla f$ means the gradient of $f$, the vector that collects all its partial derivatives. | [Note MA-062](MA/06-calculus/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients.md#41-collecting-the-partial-derivatives) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-1296">G-1296</span> | Naive assumption | The assumption that, within each class, the inputs are independent of each other (conditional independence given the class); it lets Naive Bayes multiply one probability per input instead of counting rare full combinations. | [Note ML-081](ML/07-classification/ML-081-naive-bayes-intuition/ML-081-naive-bayes-intuition.md#7-the-naive-assumption) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-1297">G-1297</span> | Naive Bayes classifier (Naive Bayes) | A classifier that applies Bayes' theorem with the assumption that inputs are independent within each class. | [Note ML-081](ML/07-classification/ML-081-naive-bayes-intuition/ML-081-naive-bayes-intuition.md#1-overview) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-1299">G-1299</span> | Name tokens | The tokens that spell the athlete's name in a prompt such as "Michael Jordan plays the sport of"; zeroing the MLP outputs at these positions tests whether the MLPs there recall the sport. | [Note DL-089](DL/06-transformers/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts.md#71-switching-mlps-off) | MLP blocks as fact storage |
| <span id="G-1300">G-1300</span> | Named entity recognition (NER) | Marking which words of a sentence are entities, specific things a program must act on, such as "7 pm" and "airport"; chatbots use it to pick out times and places. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#51-same-length) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-1301">G-1301</span> | named_steps | A pipeline's dictionary from each step's name to its object; used to reach one step and look inside it, for example when debugging. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#72-pulling-out-fitted-steps) | ML pipelines |
| <span id="G-1302">G-1302</span> | nan-Euclidean distance | A distance between two rows that may have missing values: the Euclidean distance over only the columns both rows have, scaled up by (all columns / used columns) so rows with gaps do not look too close. scikit-learn uses it to find neighbours for KNN imputation. | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#43-the-nan-euclidean-distance) | KNN imputer |
| <span id="G-1303">G-1303</span> | Narrow AI | AI that does one specific task. All AI today is narrow. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#22-narrow-ai-and-general-ai) | Artificial intelligence |
| <span id="G-1304">G-1304</span> | NaT | "Not a time": the missing value of a datetime column. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#4-converting-text-to-a-datetime-type) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-1305">G-1305</span> | Natural language processing (NLP) | The part of ML that works with human language. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#71-sentiment-analysis) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-1306">G-1306</span> | Nearest neighbours (neighbours) | The rows at the smallest distance from a given row; in KNN, the k training points closest to the query point. | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#10-key-terms) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-1307">G-1307</span> | Nearly perpendicular | Two directions whose angle is close to 90 degrees, so their cosine (and dot product) is close to 0; letting feature directions be only nearly perpendicular lets a vector of $d$ numbers store more than $d$ features, at the price of a little interference. | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#2-prerequisites) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-1308">G-1308</span> | Negative gradient | The direction that lowers the loss fastest: minus the derivative of the loss with respect to the prediction. | [Note ML-115](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths.md#6-step-2a-pseudo-residuals-are-negative-gradients) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1309">G-1309</span> | Negative hyperplane ($\pi^-$) | In an SVM, the copy of the separating hyperplane pushed out, parallel to it, until it touches the first negative-class point; with $\pi^+$ on the other side it marks the edges of the margin. | [Note ML-086](ML/07-classification/ML-086-svm-intuition/ML-086-svm-intuition.md#51-the-procedure) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1310">G-1310</span> | Negative log-likelihood (NLL) | The log-likelihood with its sign flipped, so a better fit gives a smaller number; fitting minimises it, which is the same as maximising the likelihood. | [Note MA-070](MA/08-likelihood/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation/MA-070-maximum-likelihood-estimation.md#9-the-negative-log-likelihood) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-1311">G-1311</span> | Negative power | A power with a minus sign, which means one over the positive power: $a^{-n} = 1/a^{n}$, so $2^{-3} = 1/8$. | [Note MA-032](MA/03-distributions/MA-032-poisson-distribution/MA-032-poisson-distribution.md#32-two-pieces-of-the-formula) | Poisson distribution |
| <span id="G-1312">G-1312</span> | Negative skew (left skew) | A long tail on the left: a few very small values. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#10-skewness) | Skewness |
| <span id="G-1314">G-1314</span> | Neocognitron | An early pattern-recognition network, built by Fukushima in 1980 from S-cells and C-cells; a forerunner of CNNs. | [Note DL-041](DL/04-cnn/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex.md#61-the-neocognitron) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-2158">G-2158</span> | Nested cross-validation | A way to score a tuned model fairly: the whole tuning search runs inside each outer fold of cross-validation, so the chosen model is scored on data the search never saw. | [Note ML-106](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-106-random-forest-tuning/ML-106-random-forest-tuning.md#64-did-tuning-really-help) | Cross-validation |
| <span id="G-1315">G-1315</span> | Nesterov accelerated gradient (NAG) | A version of momentum that first takes the momentum jump and then measures the gradient where it lands (the look-ahead point); seeing the slope ahead lets it brake before the minimum and swing less. | [Note DL-035](DL/03-optimizers/DL-035-nesterov-accelerated-gradient/DL-035-nesterov-accelerated-gradient.md#1-overview) | Nesterov accelerated gradient (NAG) |
| <span id="G-1316">G-1316</span> | Neural network | A model made of many simple connected units (neurons) in layers, loosely inspired by the brain; it learns the weights of its connections from data and is the model deep learning uses. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#51-neural-networks) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-1318">G-1318</span> | Neuron (biological) | A brain cell: dendrites take signals in, the nucleus processes them, the axon sends the result on. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#5-perceptron-and-biological-neuron) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-1317">G-1317</span> | Neuron (in a network) | One unit of a neural network (in an ANN, a perceptron): it takes inputs from the layer before, combines them with its learned weights and passes its output on. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#22-the-parts-of-a-neural-network) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-1319">G-1319</span> | Neuroplasticity | The brain's connections strengthening, weakening, vanishing or forming over time. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#51-three-differences) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-1320">G-1320</span> | Newton step | Minimising a function by fitting a parabola from its first and second derivatives and jumping to the parabola's lowest point. | [Note ML-116](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-116-gradient-boosting-classification/ML-116-gradient-boosting-classification.md#8-leaf-values-in-log-odds) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1321">G-1321</span> | Newton's method | A way to find a minimum: at the current point, fit a bowl (the second-order Taylor polynomial, from the first and second derivatives), jump to its lowest point, and repeat; the step is $\boldsymbol{\delta} = -H^{-1}\nabla f^{\mathsf T}$. XGBoost uses one such step for every tree. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#7-where-ml-uses-second-order-approximations) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1322">G-1322</span> | Next-word predictor | A model that takes some text and predicts the word that comes next. | [Note DL-063](DL/05-rnn/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction.md#1-overview) | Next-word prediction with an LSTM |
| <span id="G-1324">G-1324</span> | Node number | A node's index in a fitted tree, counted depth-first from 0 at the root; dtreeviz can print it so a node in the picture can be found in the arrays of `clf.tree_`. | [Note ML-094](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-094-dtreeviz/ML-094-dtreeviz.md#6-useful-view-options) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1325">G-1325</span> | Node-level feature sampling (node-level column sampling) | Drawing a new random set of features before every split (random forest). | [Note ML-104](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-104-bagging-vs-random-forest/ML-104-bagging-vs-random-forest.md#32-random-forest-node-level-sampling) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-1326">G-1326</span> | Noise ($\varepsilon$) | The random part of a target that the model's prediction does not explain. | [Note MA-072](MA/08-likelihood/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning.md#31-the-model) | Log loss (binary cross entropy) |
| <span id="G-1327">G-1327</span> | Noise (irreducible error) | Randomness in the data that no model can predict. | [Note ML-061](ML/06-regression/ML-061-bias-variance/ML-061-bias-variance.md#5-the-trade-off) | Bias-variance trade-off |
| <span id="G-1328">G-1328</span> | Noise floor | The flat run of small singular values that random noise produces; the few singular values standing above it carry the real structure, so keeping only those removes most of the noise. | [Note MA-059](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-059-low-rank-approximation/MA-059-low-rank-approximation.md#7-removing-noise) | Low-rank approximation (truncated SVD) |
| <span id="G-1329">G-1329</span> | Noise point | In DBSCAN, a point in a sparse area that is neither a core point nor a border point; it is treated as an outlier and labelled -1. | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#6-core-border-and-noise-points) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-1330">G-1330</span> | Nominal data | Categorical data whose categories have no order, such as states. | [Note ML-025](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding.md#21-nominal-data) | Encoding categorical data |
| <span id="G-1331">G-1331</span> | Non-autoregressive | Producing all output positions at once, as the decoder does during training. | [Note DL-084](DL/06-transformers/DL-084-transformer-decoder/DL-084-transformer-decoder.md#9-training-every-position-in-one-pass) | Transformer decoder |
| <span id="G-1332">G-1332</span> | Non-closed-form solution | An answer reached by improving a guess step by step. | [Note ML-050](ML/06-regression/ML-050-linear-regression-maths/ML-050-linear-regression-maths.md#2-two-ways-to-find-m-and-b) | Gradient descent |
| <span id="G-1333">G-1333</span> | Non-convex function | A function whose curve is not one simple bowl: some straight line between two of its points (a chord) lies below part of the curve. It can have several local minima. | [Note MA-065](MA/07-optimisation/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions.md#31-the-definition-as-a-formula) | Convex and non-convex loss |
| <span id="G-1334">G-1334</span> | Non-Gaussian distribution | Any distribution that is not normal. | [Note MA-029](MA/03-distributions/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal.md#1-overview) | Log-normal distribution |
| <span id="G-1335">G-1335</span> | Non-linear data | Data whose classes no straight line, plane or hyperplane can separate. | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#2-data-that-no-straight-line-can-split) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-1336">G-1336</span> | Non-linear variants of ReLU | ReLU variants with a curve on the negative side: ELU and SELU. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#1-overview) | Leaky ReLU, PReLU, ELU and SELU |
| <span id="G-1337">G-1337</span> | Non-null | A cell that holds a value rather than a missing one; counting the non-null values of each column shows how much of it is missing. | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#51-reading-the-output-of-info) | Exploratory data analysis |
| <span id="G-1338">G-1338</span> | Non-parametric density estimation | Estimating the shape of the data's distribution (its PDF) straight from the data, without first assuming a shape such as the normal curve. | [Note MA-023](MA/03-distributions/MA-023-density-estimation-kde/MA-023-density-estimation-kde.md#4-non-parametric-density-estimation) | Density estimation |
| <span id="G-1339">G-1339</span> | Non-saturating function | A function with no ceiling on its output, so it does not flatten out, such as ReLU for positive inputs; its slope does not shrink to 0, which avoids the vanishing gradient that saturating functions cause. | [Note DL-027](DL/02-training/DL-027-activation-functions/DL-027-activation-functions.md#55-non-saturating) | Activation functions |
| <span id="G-1340">G-1340</span> | Non-sequential data | Data whose features can be listed in any order without changing the meaning, such as a table row. | [Note DL-055](DL/05-rnn/DL-055-why-rnn/DL-055-why-rnn.md#3-sequential-data) | Sequential data |
| <span id="G-1341">G-1341</span> | Non-trainable parameter | A stored number that gradient descent does not update, such as a moving mean. | [Note DL-031](DL/02-training/DL-031-batch-normalization/DL-031-batch-normalization.md#5-batch-normalisation-during-prediction) | Batch normalisation |
| <span id="G-1343">G-1343</span> | Normal distribution | A symmetric, bell-shaped distribution: most values sit near the mean and they get rarer the further out we go; its mean and standard deviation set its centre and width. | [Note MA-024](MA/03-distributions/MA-024-normal-distribution/MA-024-normal-distribution.md#2-what-the-normal-distribution-is) | Normal distribution |
| <span id="G-1344">G-1344</span> | Normal equation | A formula that gives all linear-regression coefficients in one step, with no iterations (a closed-form solution): $\beta = (X^{\mathsf T}X)^{-1}X^{\mathsf T}y$. | [Note ML-053](ML/06-regression/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths.md#6-the-normal-equation) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-1345">G-1345</span> | Normal equations | A set of equations, one per coefficient, whose solution is the best-fit (least-squares) coefficients: $X^{\mathsf T}X\beta = X^{\mathsf T}y$. | [Note ML-053](ML/06-regression/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths.md#52-the-gradient-then-zero) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-1346">G-1346</span> | Normal vector | A vector perpendicular to a line, plane or hyperplane; for $w^{\mathsf T}x + w_0 = 0$ it is $w$. | [Note MA-051](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-051-equation-of-a-hyperplane/MA-051-equation-of-a-hyperplane.md#61-the-argument) | Equation of a hyperplane |
| <span id="G-1348">G-1348</span> | Normalisation (in a network) | Transforming values so they have chosen statistics, usually mean 0 and variance 1; in a network it is applied to the inputs and hidden activations so they stay in a fixed range and training is more stable and faster. | [Note DL-080](DL/06-transformers/DL-080-layer-normalization/DL-080-layer-normalization.md#3-normalisation-in-deep-learning) | Layer normalisation |
| <span id="G-1347">G-1347</span> | Normalisation (of weights) | Dividing every weight by their sum so they add up to 1. | [Note ML-111](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-111-adaboost-from-scratch/ML-111-adaboost-from-scratch.md#6-normalising-and-drawing-the-next-dataset) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1349">G-1349</span> | Normalization (feature scaling) | The type of feature scaling that squeezes values into a fixed range, such as 0 to 1. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#2-what-normalization-is) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-1350">G-1350</span> | Normalized importances | Importances divided by their total, so they add up to 1. | [Note ML-108](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-108-feature-importance/ML-108-feature-importance.md#42-from-node-decreases-to-feature-importance) | Feature importance |
| <span id="G-1351">G-1351</span> | Normalizing inputs | Bringing every input column of a network to the same scale before training, by standardization or normalization; without it, the weights of large-valued inputs get huge gradients and training goes badly. | [Note DL-023](DL/02-training/DL-023-data-scaling-in-ann/DL-023-data-scaling-in-ann.md#5-the-fix-scale-the-inputs) | Scaling inputs for neural networks |
| <span id="G-1352">G-1352</span> | Not commutative | The order of the factors matters: $AB \neq BA$ in general. | [Note MA-054](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition.md#5-order-matters) | Matrix multiplication as composition |
| <span id="G-1353">G-1353</span> | Notebook | A `.ipynb` file of cells, each with its output underneath. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#31-starting-jupyter) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1354">G-1354</span> | np.argmin | NumPy function returning the position of the smallest value. | [Note ML-124](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-124-kmeans-from-scratch/ML-124-kmeans-from-scratch.md#52-the-nearest-centroid) | K-means |
| <span id="G-1355">G-1355</span> | np.concatenate | NumPy function that joins arrays; with `axis=1` it puts them side by side. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#44-age-and-the-final-join) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-1356">G-1356</span> | np.insert | NumPy function that inserts values into an array at a given position. | [Note ML-054](ML/06-regression/ML-054-multiple-lr-code/ML-054-multiple-lr-code.md#41-fit) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-1357">G-1357</span> | np.linalg.inv | NumPy function that computes the inverse of a square matrix. | [Note ML-054](ML/06-regression/ML-054-multiple-lr-code/ML-054-multiple-lr-code.md#41-fit) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-1358">G-1358</span> | np.linalg.lstsq | NumPy function that finds the best-fit answer to a set of linear equations, the one with the smallest squared error (the least-squares solution). | [Note ML-054](ML/06-regression/ML-054-multiple-lr-code/ML-054-multiple-lr-code.md#6-solving-without-an-explicit-inverse) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-1359">G-1359</span> | NPU | A neural processing unit: a chip in phones that speeds up networks. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#52-hardware) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-1360">G-1360</span> | Nucleus (top $p$) sampling | A sampling rule for text generation that keeps only the smallest set of most likely tokens whose probabilities add up to at least $p$, then samples among them; it cuts off the unlikely tail and adapts the cut to the context. | [Note DL-088](DL/06-transformers/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling.md#62-sampling-at-different-temperatures) | Unembedding, logits, temperature and sampling |

| <span id="G-1362">G-1362</span> | Null space | All vectors a matrix sends to $\mathbf{0}$; spanned by the $\mathbf v_i$ with $\sigma_i = 0$. | [Note MA-058](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-058-computing-the-svd/MA-058-computing-the-svd.md#5-a-matrix-of-rank-1) | Singular value decomposition |

| <span id="G-1364">G-1364</span> | Nullity matrix | A picture of the whole table with each missing value drawn as a white line; it shows where values are missing and whether gaps in different columns fall in the same rows. | [Note ML-021](ML/02-getting-data/ML-021-pandas-profiling/ML-021-pandas-profiling.md#7-missing-values-how-many-and-where) | Missing values |
| <span id="G-1365">G-1365</span> | Number of samples ($k$) | How many samples are drawn; different from the sample size $n$. | [Note MA-034](MA/04-inference/MA-034-estimating-a-mean-with-the-clt/MA-034-estimating-a-mean-with-the-clt.md#7-a-common-mistake-dividing-by-the-wrong-square-root) | Sampling distribution and standard error |
| <span id="G-1366">G-1366</span> | Numerator layout | Writing derivatives with outputs as rows and inputs as columns. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#42-the-formula-every-partial-derivative-in-one-grid) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-2247">G-2247</span> | Numeric understanding | Knowing how to carry out a computation, such as a matrix product. | [Note MA-002](MA/00-why-maths/MA-002-learning-maths-for-ml/MA-002-learning-maths-for-ml.md#51-two-halves-of-understanding) | How to learn the maths for ML |
| <span id="G-1367">G-1367</span> | Numerical data | Data made of numbers. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#22-numerical-and-categorical-data) | Supervised learning |
