| <span id="G-1369">G-1369</span> | NumPy | Python's library for arrays and linear algebra. | [Note MA-047](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-047-linear-algebra-roadmap/MA-047-linear-algebra-roadmap.md#48-tools-and-libraries) | Linear algebra roadmap |
| <span id="G-1373">G-1373</span> | Objective (Keras Tuner) | The metric the tuner maximises or minimises, such as `val_accuracy`. | [Note DL-039](DL/03-optimizers/DL-039-keras-tuner/DL-039-keras-tuner.md#52-step-2-the-tuner-object) | Keras Tuner |
| <span id="G-1372">G-1372</span> | Objective function | The quantity an algorithm tries to make as large or as small as possible. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#2-the-problem-pca-solves) | PCA |
| <span id="G-1370">G-1370</span> | Objective function (Optuna) | The function a search optimises: it takes a trial's hyperparameter values and returns a score. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#4-optunas-vocabulary) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-1371">G-1371</span> | Objective function (XGBoost) | The quantity XGBoost minimises: the loss plus a regularisation term. | [Note ML-120](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-120-xgboost-maths/ML-120-xgboost-maths.md#4-the-objective-loss-plus-a-penalty-on-the-tree) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1374">G-1374</span> | Observation | One record of a dataset: one row of the data table. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#21-learning-from-inputs-and-outputs) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-1375">G-1375</span> | Observed count $O$ | The number of sample rows in a category or table cell. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#21-observed-and-expected-counts) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-1376">G-1376</span> | Odds | How often an event happens divided by how often it does not, e.g. 5 placed to 3 not placed is $5/3$. | [Note ML-116](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-116-gradient-boosting-classification/ML-116-gradient-boosting-classification.md#4-stage-1-the-log-odds-of-class-1) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1377">G-1377</span> | Offline learning | Training a model once on all the data away from the live system, for example on the engineer's own machine, and then deploying it; another name for batch learning. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#31-how-batch-learning-works) | Batch (offline) learning |
| <span id="G-1378">G-1378</span> | OLTP | Online transaction processing: the database that records every action as it happens. | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#7-step-4-getting-data) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-1379">G-1379</span> | One-hot encoding | Replacing a nominal column by one 0/1 column per category, with a single 1 in each row; also used to represent words. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#2-how-one-hot-encoding-works) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-1380">G-1380</span> | One-hot target | A target with probability 1 on the correct word and 0 on all others. | [Note DL-063](DL/05-rnn/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction.md#52-one-hot-targets) | Next-word prediction with an LSTM |
| <span id="G-1381">G-1381</span> | One-sample proportion test | A test of whether the share (proportion) of one category in the population equals a claimed value $\pi_0$, using a z statistic (a z-test). | [Note MA-044](MA/04-inference/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test.md#1-overview) | One-sample proportion test |
| <span id="G-1382">G-1382</span> | One-sample t-test | A test of whether a population's mean equals a claimed value, from one sample, when the population standard deviation $\sigma$ is unknown: $t = (\bar{x} - \mu_0)/(s/\sqrt{n})$. | [Note MA-042](MA/04-inference/MA-042-one-sample-t-test/MA-042-one-sample-t-test.md#3-the-three-types-of-t-test) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1383">G-1383</span> | One-sided and two-sided critical value | The cut-off beyond which the test statistic leads to rejecting $H_0$ (the critical value): one-sided puts the whole $\alpha$ in one tail, two-sided puts $\alpha/2$ in each of the two tails. | [Note MA-039](MA/04-inference/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test.md#6-the-rejection-region-and-the-critical-value) | Z-test and rejection regions |
| <span id="G-1384">G-1384</span> | One-tailed p-value | The p-value counted on one side only: the tail area beyond the test statistic on the side that $H_1$ points to. | [Note MA-041](MA/04-inference/MA-041-p-values/MA-041-p-values.md#61-one-tailed-the-training-program) | P-values |
| <span id="G-1385">G-1385</span> | One-tailed test (one-sided test) | A test whose $H_1$ has a direction ($>$ or $<$), with the whole rejection region in one tail. | [Note MA-040](MA/04-inference/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails.md#5-one-tailed-and-two-tailed-tests) | Type I and II errors, power, tails |
| <span id="G-1386">G-1386</span> | One-to-many RNN | An RNN that takes one non-sequential input and produces a sequence. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#4-one-to-many) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-1387">G-1387</span> | One-to-one | A network with non-sequential input and output: an ordinary ANN or CNN, not an RNN. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#6-one-to-one) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-1388">G-1388</span> | One-vs-rest | Training one binary classifier per class, each separating that class from all others. | [Note ML-078](ML/07-classification/ML-078-softmax-regression/ML-078-softmax-regression.md#41-an-intuition-one-model-per-class) | Softmax regression |
| <span id="G-1389">G-1389</span> | One-way ANOVA | A test of whether three or more group means are equal, with the groups defined by one categorical column. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#1-overview) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-1390">G-1390</span> | OneHotEncoder | scikit-learn's class for one-hot encoding; remembers the categories it learned. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#73-creating-the-encoder) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-1391">G-1391</span> | Online learning | Training incrementally on mini-batches while the model is live in production. | [Note ML-005](ML/01-foundations/ML-005-online-learning/ML-005-online-learning.md#21-learning-in-small-steps) | Online learning |
| <span id="G-1392">G-1392</span> | OOB prediction | A prediction for a training row made only by the trees that never saw that row, because their random sample (bootstrap sample) missed it. | [Note ML-107](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-107-oob-score/ML-107-oob-score.md#31-the-steps) | OOB score |

| <span id="G-1394">G-1394</span> | oob_prediction_ | For a regressor, each training row's prediction from only the trees that never saw it (its OOB prediction). | [Note ML-107](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-107-oob-score/ML-107-oob-score.md#7-when-the-oob-score-is-available) | OOB score |
| <span id="G-1395">G-1395</span> | oob_score_ | The accuracy (classifier) or $R^2$ (regressor) of each training row's prediction by the trees that never saw it (the OOB predictions). | [Note ML-107](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-107-oob-score/ML-107-oob-score.md#4-the-oob-score-in-scikit-learn) | OOB score |
| <span id="G-1396">G-1396</span> | OPTICS | A density-based clustering algorithm, like DBSCAN: it groups points that lie in dense areas, so clusters can have any shape (Ankerst et al. 1999). | [Note ML-126](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-126-dbscan/ML-126-dbscan.md#4-density-based-clustering) | DBSCAN |
| <span id="G-1397">G-1397</span> | Optimal number of features | The number of columns at which a model performs best. | [Note ML-045](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-045-curse-of-dimensionality/ML-045-curse-of-dimensionality.md#2-what-the-curse-of-dimensionality-is) | Curse of dimensionality |
| <span id="G-1400">G-1400</span> | Optimisation | Changing a model step by step until its error is as small as possible. | [Note MA-001](MA/00-why-maths/MA-001-role-of-maths-in-ml/MA-001-role-of-maths-in-ml.md#3-calculus-reducing-the-error) | Role of mathematics in ML |
| <span id="G-1398">G-1398</span> | Optimisation algorithm | A method for finding the parameter values that make a function as small (or large) as possible. | [Note ML-056](ML/06-regression/ML-056-gradient-descent/ML-056-gradient-descent.md#1-overview) | Gradient descent |
| <span id="G-1399">G-1399</span> | Optimisation problem | Finding the inputs (here the weights and biases) that make a function (here the loss) smallest. | [Note DL-032](DL/03-optimizers/DL-032-optimizers-in-deep-learning/DL-032-optimizers-in-deep-learning.md#3-what-an-optimizer-does) | Optimizers in deep learning |
| <span id="G-1401">G-1401</span> | Optimizer | The rule that turns gradients into weight updates, such as plain gradient descent or Adam. | [Note DL-021](DL/02-training/DL-021-improving-a-neural-network/DL-021-improving-a-neural-network.md#33-learning-rate-and-optimizer) | Optimizers in deep learning |
| <span id="G-1402">G-1402</span> | Optuna | A Python framework for hyperparameter tuning that uses the results of earlier tries to pick the next values to test (Bayesian optimisation). | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#1-overview) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-1403">G-1403</span> | Ordinal data | Categorical data whose categories have a natural order, such as Poor < Average < Good. | [Note ML-025](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding.md#22-ordinal-data) | Ordinal and label encoding |

| <span id="G-1405">G-1405</span> | OrdinalEncoder | scikit-learn's class for ordinal encoding; takes the order through `categories`. | [Note ML-025](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding/ML-025-ordinal-label-encoding.md#64-the-categories-parameter) | Ordinal and label encoding |
| <span id="G-1406">G-1406</span> | Ordinary least squares (OLS, least squares) | The way linear regression finds its line with one formula (a closed-form method): the line with the smallest sum of squared errors. | [Note ML-050](ML/06-regression/ML-050-linear-regression-maths/ML-050-linear-regression-maths.md#2-two-ways-to-find-m-and-b) | Ordinary least squares (closed form) |

| <span id="G-2240">G-2240</span> | Origin | The point where the axes cross; the tail of every vector in linear algebra. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#21-size-and-direction) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-1408">G-1408</span> | Orthogonal | At right angles (perpendicular); two non-zero vectors are orthogonal when their dot product is 0, which as data means they share nothing. | [Note MA-050](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity.md#52-perpendicular-vectors-have-dot-product-0) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-1407">G-1407</span> | Orthogonal matrix | A square matrix that only rotates or flips: its columns have length 1 and are all perpendicular (orthonormal), and its inverse is its transpose. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#23-orthonormal-columns) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1409">G-1409</span> | Orthonormal | Vectors of length 1 that are all perpendicular to each other. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#23-orthonormal-columns) | Singular value decomposition |

| <span id="G-1413">G-1413</span> | Out-of-core learning (out-of-core computing) | Training on data too big for the memory (RAM) by loading it chunk by chunk, offline. | [Note ML-005](ML/01-foundations/ML-005-online-learning/ML-005-online-learning.md#6-out-of-core-learning) | Out-of-core learning |

| <span id="G-1416">G-1416</span> | Out-of-vocabulary (OOV) token | A placeholder token, `[UNK]`, that stands for every word not in the vocabulary, so a word never seen in training still gets an index. | [Note DL-057](DL/05-rnn/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis.md#32-tokenizing-in-keras) | Tokenization and integer encoding of text |
| <span id="G-1417">G-1417</span> | Outcome | The single result of one trial, such as heads or a 3. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#23-outcome) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-1418">G-1418</span> | Outer product | Multiplying a column vector by a row vector to get a whole matrix: $\boldsymbol{\delta}\boldsymbol{\delta}^{\mathsf T}$ has entry $\delta_i\delta_j$ for every pair; it is what $\boldsymbol{\delta}^2$ means in a Taylor polynomial of several variables. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#62-higher-orders-tensors-and-outer-products) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1420">G-1420</span> | Outlier | A value far from the rest of the data. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#81-whiskers-and-outliers) | Outliers |
| <span id="G-1419">G-1419</span> | Outlier detection | Finding the values that lie far from the rest of a feature: compute a lower and an upper limit, and flag every value outside them as an outlier, ready to be removed or changed. | [Note ML-040](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-040-what-are-outliers/ML-040-what-are-outliers.md#6-handling-outliers-detect-then-treat) | Outliers |
| <span id="G-1422">G-1422</span> | Output gate ($o_t$) | The LSTM gate that decides how much of the cell's memory to show as the new hidden state: a sigmoid layer gives $o_t$, between 0 and 1, which scales $\tanh(c_t)$ to give $h_t$. | [Note DL-062](DL/05-rnn/DL-062-lstm-architecture/DL-062-lstm-architecture.md#7-the-output-gate) | LSTM gates (forget, input, output) and cell state |
| <span id="G-1424">G-1424</span> | Output layer | The last layer, which gives the prediction. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#22-the-parts-of-a-neural-network) | Neural networks |
| <span id="G-1425">G-1425</span> | Output value (classification) | The number a leaf of an XGBoost classification tree adds to the prediction, in log-odds: sum of residuals / ($\sum p(1-p) + \lambda$), with $p$ the previous predicted probabilities. | [Note ML-119](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-119-xgboost-classification/ML-119-xgboost-classification.md#7-output-values-in-log-odds) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1426">G-1426</span> | Output value (leaf weight) | The number an XGBoost regression leaf predicts: sum of residuals / (number of residuals + $\lambda$); with $\lambda = 0$ it is the mean residual, and a larger $\lambda$ pulls it towards 0. | [Note ML-118](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-118-xgboost-regression/ML-118-xgboost-regression.md#8-output-values-of-the-leaves) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1427">G-1427</span> | Output-size formula | The rule that gives the size of a convolution's output from the image size $n$, filter size $f$, padding $p$ and stride $s$: $\lfloor (n + 2p - f)/s \rfloor + 1$. | [Note DL-043](DL/04-cnn/DL-043-padding-and-strides/DL-043-padding-and-strides.md#52-the-formula-with-strides) | Padding and strides |
| <span id="G-1428">G-1428</span> | Overdispersion | Counts that are much more spread out than their mean suggests (variance clearly larger than the mean), a sign that a Poisson model does not fit. | [Note MA-032](MA/03-distributions/MA-032-poisson-distribution/MA-032-poisson-distribution.md#5-mean-and-variance) | Poisson distribution |
| <span id="G-1429">G-1429</span> | Overfitting | Learning the training data too closely, noise included; fails on new data. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#71-overfitting) | Overfitting |
| <span id="G-1430">G-1430</span> | Overshooting | Moving past the minimum because of the built-up velocity, then swinging back. | [Note DL-034](DL/03-optimizers/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum.md#8-escaping-a-local-minimum-and-the-cost-overshooting) | SGD with momentum |
| <span id="G-1433">G-1433</span> | P-value | The probability, assuming $H_0$ is true, of getting a sample as or more extreme than ours. | [Note MA-041](MA/04-inference/MA-041-p-values/MA-041-p-values.md#2-definition) | P-values |
| <span id="G-1431">G-1431</span> | P-value approach | Carrying out a test by computing a p-value, which also measures the strength of the evidence. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#6-the-eight-steps-of-a-hypothesis-test) | P-values |
| <span id="G-1432">G-1432</span> | P-value decision rule | Reject $H_0$ if $p \le \alpha$, otherwise fail to reject it. | [Note MA-041](MA/04-inference/MA-041-p-values/MA-041-p-values.md#51-the-decision-rule) | P-values |
| <span id="G-1435">G-1435</span> | Package | A library bundled so that a package manager such as pip or conda can download and install it. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#21-what-anaconda-and-conda-are) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1434">G-1434</span> | Package manager | A program that downloads and installs libraries in versions that fit together. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#21-what-anaconda-and-conda-are) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1436">G-1436</span> | Padding | Adding zeros to sequences so that all have the same length. | [Note DL-057](DL/05-rnn/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis.md#31-the-three-steps) | Sequence padding |
| <span id="G-1437">G-1437</span> | Pair plot | A grid of scatter plots of every pair of numerical columns, with histograms on the diagonal. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#9-pair-plot-every-numerical-pair-at-once) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-1438">G-1438</span> | Paired observations | Two measurements that belong to the same subject or matched pair. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#52-assumptions) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1439">G-1439</span> | Paired t-test (dependent t-test) | A test of whether two linked measurements of the same subjects differ on average; it works on the difference within each pair (a t-test on the differences). | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#5-the-paired-t-test) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1440">G-1440</span> | Pandas Profiling | A library that builds a full EDA report (column summaries, missing values, correlations and more) from a DataFrame in a few lines of code; now named `fg-data-profiling`. | [Note ML-021](ML/02-getting-data/ML-021-pandas-profiling/ML-021-pandas-profiling.md#1-overview) | Pandas Profiling |
| <span id="G-1441">G-1441</span> | pandas, DataFrame | Python's main table library, and its name for a table. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#3-loading-and-cleaning-the-data) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1442">G-1442</span> | Parallel corpus | A dataset of sentences paired with their translations, one pair per row; it is the training data of a translation model. | [Note DL-068](DL/06-transformers/DL-068-encoder-decoder/DL-068-encoder-decoder.md#51-the-data) | Sequence-to-sequence (encoder-decoder) |
| <span id="G-1443">G-1443</span> | Parallel learning | Training the base models independently, so they can all be trained at once (bagging). | [Note ML-113](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-113-bagging-vs-boosting/ML-113-bagging-vs-boosting.md#3-difference-2-parallel-against-sequential-learning) | Ensemble learning |
| <span id="G-1444">G-1444</span> | Parallel processing | Splitting one job among several processor cores working at the same time. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#72-parallel-processing) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1445">G-1445</span> | Parallel training | Training in which all positions of a sequence are computed at the same time. | [Note DL-071](DL/06-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers.md#9-disadvantages) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-1448">G-1448</span> | Parameter (of a function) | A named setting passed to a function, like `sep=";"`. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#3-the-read_csv-function) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-2276">G-2276</span> | Parameter (of a population) | A number that describes the whole population, such as its true mean $\mu$; it is usually unknown, so we estimate it from a sample statistic. | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#42-parameters-and-statistics) | Population, sample, parameter and statistic |
| <span id="G-1446">G-1446</span> | Parameter grid | A dictionary that lists the model settings to tune (hyperparameter names) and the values to try for each. | [Note ML-106](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-106-random-forest-tuning/ML-106-random-forest-tuning.md#61-the-grid) | Grid and random search |
| <span id="G-1447">G-1447</span> | Parameter sharing | Using the same weights at every position or time step; it lets a model handle sequences of any length and recognise a pattern wherever it appears. | [Note DL-055](DL/05-rnn/DL-055-why-rnn/DL-055-why-rnn.md#6-the-idea-behind-an-rnn) | Parameter sharing across time steps |
| <span id="G-1449">G-1449</span> | Parameters (of a distribution) | The numbers, such as $\mu$ and $\sigma$, that set a distribution's location, scale and shape. | [Note MA-020](MA/03-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions.md#8-parameters-of-a-distribution) | Probability distributions |
| <span id="G-1450">G-1450</span> | Parameters (of a model) | The numbers inside a model that training learns from the data, such as a line's slope and intercept; once learned, they turn inputs into predictions. | [Note ML-006](ML/01-foundations/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based.md#42-the-training-data-is-no-longer-needed) | Model-based learning |
| <span id="G-1451">G-1451</span> | Parametric density estimation | Estimating how the data is spread (its probability density) by assuming it follows a named distribution and estimating that distribution's parameters from the data. | [Note MA-023](MA/03-distributions/MA-023-density-estimation-kde/MA-023-density-estimation-kde.md#3-parametric-density-estimation) | Density estimation |
| <span id="G-1452">G-1452</span> | Parametric ReLU (PReLU) | An activation that passes positive inputs unchanged and multiplies negative ones by a small slope $a$ that is learned during training, one per node (a Leaky ReLU with a learned slope). | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#52-parametric-relu-prelu) | Leaky ReLU, PReLU, ELU and SELU |
| <span id="G-1453">G-1453</span> | Pareto distribution | A distribution of values that start at a smallest value $x_m$ and become rarer the larger they are, in a long right tail (a right-skewed power law); used to model wealth and income. PDF $\alpha x_m^{\alpha}/x^{\alpha + 1}$. | [Note MA-030](MA/03-distributions/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law.md#3-the-pareto-distribution) | Pareto distribution and power laws |
| <span id="G-1454">G-1454</span> | Parse, parser | Read text and build a structure from it; the part that does this. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#5-parsing-the-page-with-beautifulsoup) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-1456">G-1456</span> | Part-of-speech tagging | Labelling every word of a sentence with its part of speech. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#51-same-length) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-1457">G-1457</span> | Partial derivative | The slope of a function of several variables in one variable, holding the others fixed. | [Note ML-050](ML/06-regression/ML-050-linear-regression-maths/ML-050-linear-regression-maths.md#41-the-shape-of-e) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-1458">G-1458</span> | partial_fit | A scikit-learn method that continues training from where the model left off. | [Note ML-005](ML/01-foundations/ML-005-online-learning/ML-005-online-learning.md#41-scikit-learns-partial_fit) | Online learning |
| <span id="G-1459">G-1459</span> | Partition | A set of events of which exactly one happens in every trial: no two can happen together (mutually exclusive) and together they cover every outcome (exhaustive). | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#46-exhaustive-events) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-1460">G-1460</span> | passthrough (ColumnTransformer) | The `remainder` option that keeps untouched columns unchanged. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#52-the-remainder-parameter) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-1461">G-1461</span> | Passthrough (stacking) | In stacking, giving the final model that combines the base models (the meta-model) the original input columns as well as the base models' predictions. | [Note ML-121](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-121-stacking-blending/ML-121-stacking-blending.md#9-stackingclassifier-in-scikit-learn) | Stacking and blending |
| <span id="G-1462">G-1462</span> | Past defaulters | Past borrowers who did not repay their loan; a loan model compares a new applicant with them, and high similarity means high risk. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#41-loan-approval) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-1463">G-1463</span> | Pasting | Training several models, each on its own random sample of rows drawn without replacement (no row twice), and combining their predictions; it is bagging with this sample instead of a bootstrap sample. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#24-pasting-random-subspaces-and-random-patches) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-1464">G-1464</span> | Path (in a network) | A route from a node to the output along connections; a derivative sums its products over all paths. | [Note DL-019](DL/01-basics/DL-019-mlp-memoization/DL-019-mlp-memoization.md#44-a-weight-of-the-first-layer-two-paths) | Backpropagation |
| <span id="G-1465">G-1465</span> | Path (in the chain rule) | One route through the computation from the loss to a weight; the derivative is the sum over all paths. | [Note DL-059](DL/05-rnn/DL-059-backpropagation-through-time/DL-059-backpropagation-through-time.md#61-three-paths) | Backpropagation through time (BPTT) |
| <span id="G-1466">G-1466</span> | Patience | How many passes over the training data (epochs) in a row without improvement training waits before early stopping ends it. | [Note DL-022](DL/02-training/DL-022-early-stopping/DL-022-early-stopping.md#52-min_delta-and-patience) | Early stopping |
| <span id="G-1467">G-1467</span> | Pattern | The relationship between input and output that the algorithm discovers. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#32-learning-patterns-from-data) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-2169">G-2169</span> | Pattern recognition | Finding regularities in data; the measurable part of intelligence that today's AI covers. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#21-intelligence-is-many-abilities) | Artificial intelligence |
| <span id="G-1469">G-1469</span> | PCA (principal component analysis) | A way to replace many columns with a few new ones that keep most of the spread in the data, using no labels (unsupervised feature extraction for dimensionality reduction). Each new column, a principal component, follows one direction of greatest variance. Used to cut the number of features and to plot data with many columns. | [Note ML-046](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-046-pca-geometric-intuition/ML-046-pca-geometric-intuition.md#1-what-pca-is) | PCA |
| <span id="G-1468">G-1468</span> | PCA through the SVD | Computing PCA from the SVD of the centred data: the columns of $V$ are the principal components, $\sigma_i^2/n$ their variances, and $U\Sigma$ the data's new coordinates (scores). | [Note MA-060](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning.md#2-pca-through-the-svd) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1470">G-1470</span> | pd.crosstab | pandas function that counts how often each pair of values from two columns occurs. | [Note ML-083](ML/07-classification/ML-083-naive-bayes-code/ML-083-naive-bayes-code.md#4-training-the-lookup-table) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-1471">G-1471</span> | pd.cut | The pandas function that puts values into intervals we give it. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#102-binning-both-features) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-1472">G-1472</span> | pd.to_datetime | The pandas function that converts text to datetime values. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#4-converting-text-to-a-datetime-type) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-1473">G-1473</span> | pd.to_numeric | The pandas function that converts values to numbers. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#41-the-numerical-part) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-1474">G-1474</span> | Pearson correlation coefficient (Pearson's r) | The usual measure of correlation for straight-line relationships between two numerical columns, written $r$; the one `df.corr()` computes. | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#91-correlation) | Correlation |
| <span id="G-1475">G-1475</span> | Pearson's skewness coefficient | A simple measure of skew: three times the gap between the mean and the median, divided by the standard deviation, $3(\bar{x} - \text{median})/s$; positive for right skew, negative for left. | [Note MA-026](MA/03-distributions/MA-026-skewness/MA-026-skewness.md#5-the-sample-skewness-formula) | Skewness |
| <span id="G-1477">G-1477</span> | penalty | The SGDRegressor setting that chooses how large weights are penalised (regularisation), such as "l2" for Ridge. | [Note ML-064](ML/06-regression/ML-064-ridge-gradient-descent/ML-064-ridge-gradient-descent.md#6-ridge-with-gradient-descent-in-scikit-learn) | Ridge regression |
| <span id="G-1476">G-1476</span> | Penalty term | The extra term added to the cost to discourage large weights. | [Note DL-026](DL/02-training/DL-026-regularization-in-dl/DL-026-regularization-in-dl.md#5-the-penalty-term) | L1 and L2 regularisation in neural networks |
| <span id="G-1478">G-1478</span> | penalty=None | The LogisticRegression setting that switches off the penalty on large weights (regularisation). | [Note ML-074](ML/07-classification/ML-074-logistic-gradient-descent/ML-074-logistic-gradient-descent.md#8-checking-against-scikit-learn) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1479">G-1479</span> | Per-row seed | A seed taken from a row's own values, so the same input always gets the same random fill. | [Note ML-037](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample.md#42-the-fix-one-seed-per-row) | Random sample imputation |
| <span id="G-1483">G-1483</span> | Percentile | The value below which a given share of the data lies. | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#72-percentiles) | Percentile outlier method |
| <span id="G-2234">G-2234</span> | Percentile bootstrap interval | A range for a population value built by resampling: draw many resamples with replacement from one sample, compute each one's mean, and keep the middle 95% (2.5th to 97.5th percentile); it works for any statistic, even with no formula. | [Note MA-035](MA/04-inference/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure.md#10-another-way-the-bootstrap-interval) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-1480">G-1480</span> | Percentile location | Where the $p$-th percentile sits in sorted data: position $(p/100) \times (n+1)$; if it falls between two values, the percentile lies the same fraction of the way between them. | [Note MA-008](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots.md#31-the-value-at-a-given-percentile) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-1481">G-1481</span> | Percentile method (percentile rule) | A way to find outliers: flag values below a low percentile or above a high one (e.g. 1st and 99th); it works for any column. | [Note ML-043](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-043-outliers-percentile/ML-043-outliers-percentile.md#1-overview) | Percentile outlier method |
| <span id="G-1482">G-1482</span> | Percentile rank | The percentile a given value falls at: the values below it plus half of those equal to it, as a share of all values, $(X + 0.5Y)/n \times 100$. | [Note MA-008](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots.md#32-the-percentile-of-a-given-value) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-1486">G-1486</span> | Perceptron | The smallest building block of a neural network, one artificial neuron: it multiplies each input by a weight, adds the results and turns the sum into an output. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#3-the-parts-of-a-perceptron) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-1484">G-1484</span> | Perceptron loss | A loss for the perceptron that is 0 for a point on its correct side and $\lvert f(x) \rvert$ for a point on the wrong side, so worse mistakes cost more: $\max(0, -y f(x))$ per point, labels $\pm 1$. Minimising it trains the line. | [Note DL-006](DL/01-basics/DL-006-perceptron-loss/DL-006-perceptron-loss.md#61-the-formula) | Perceptron loss |
| <span id="G-1485">G-1485</span> | Perceptron trick | Moving a line towards each misclassified point until the classes are separated. | [Note ML-069](ML/07-classification/ML-069-perceptron-trick/ML-069-perceptron-trick.md#1-overview) | Perceptron trick |
| <span id="G-1487">G-1487</span> | Perfect separation | When a line splits the training classes with no mistakes; unregularised weights then grow without limit. | [Note ML-074](ML/07-classification/ML-074-logistic-gradient-descent/ML-074-logistic-gradient-descent.md#9-perfectly-separable-data) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1489">G-1489</span> | Periodic function | A function that repeats after a fixed interval, its period. | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#4-first-idea-count-the-words) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-1490">G-1490</span> | Permutation importance | A feature's importance measured as the drop in a model's test score when that column's values are shuffled; a big drop means the model relies on it, and unlike impurity-based importance it is not fooled by columns with many unique values. | [Note ML-108](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-108-feature-importance/ML-108-feature-importance.md#7-permutation-importance) | Permutation importance |
| <span id="G-1491">G-1491</span> | permutation_importance | scikit-learn function (in `sklearn.inspection`) that computes permutation importance. | [Note ML-108](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-108-feature-importance/ML-108-feature-importance.md#7-permutation-importance) | Permutation importance |
| <span id="G-1492">G-1492</span> | Perplexity | A score of how surprised a language model is by the correct tokens: the exponential of the mean cross-entropy per token; lower means it gave the correct tokens higher probability. | [Note DL-086](DL/06-transformers/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end.md#74-regularisation-residual-dropout-and-label-smoothing) | Label smoothing |
| <span id="G-1493">G-1493</span> | phpMyAdmin | A web page for creating and managing MySQL databases. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#6-getting-an-sql-database-ready) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-1494">G-1494</span> | pickle | A Python module that saves objects to a file and loads them back. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#101-saving-the-model) | Saving models with pickle |
| <span id="G-1495">G-1495</span> | Pie chart | A circle split into slices sized by each category's share. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#5-pie-chart) | Univariate analysis |
| <span id="G-1496">G-1496</span> | Piecewise linear | Made of straight segments joined at bends; the shape of a ReLU network's decision boundary. | [Note DL-026](DL/02-training/DL-026-regularization-in-dl/DL-026-regularization-in-dl.md#3-why-neural-networks-overfit) | L1 and L2 regularisation in neural networks |
| <span id="G-1497">G-1497</span> | pip | Python's own package manager, which installs from PyPI. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#21-what-anaconda-and-conda-are) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1499">G-1499</span> | Pipeline | One object that chains preprocessing steps and a model, each step's output feeding the next; every input, including new ones after deployment, then gets exactly the same preprocessing. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#2-what-a-pipeline-is) | ML pipelines |
| <span id="G-1498">G-1498</span> | Pipeline (class) | The scikit-learn class (in `sklearn.pipeline`) that builds a pipeline from a list of (name, object) tuples. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#58-chaining-the-steps-with-pipeline) | ML pipelines |
| <span id="G-2161">G-2161</span> | Pipeline map | A diagram of the 14 steps of an ML project, from framing the problem to deployment, that shows where each Concept belongs. | [Course map](00-course-map/00-course-map.md#2-the-pipeline-map) |  |
| <span id="G-1500">G-1500</span> | Pivot table | A grid with one column's values as rows, another's as columns, and a third in the cells. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#111-pivot-table-and-heatmap) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-1501">G-1501</span> | Pixel | One dot of an image, stored as one or more numbers. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#64-4d-images) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-1502">G-1502</span> | Plane | A flat surface in 3D; the model for two input columns. | [Note ML-052](ML/06-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression/ML-052-multiple-linear-regression.md#21-two-inputs-a-plane) | Multiple linear regression |
| <span id="G-1503">G-1503</span> | Plateau | A nearly flat region of the loss, where steps become very small. | [Note ML-056](ML/06-regression/ML-056-gradient-descent/ML-056-gradient-descent.md#8-the-shape-of-the-loss-function-matters) | Gradient descent |
| <span id="G-1504">G-1504</span> | Platykurtic | A distribution with thinner tails than the normal, so fewer extreme values; its excess kurtosis is below 0. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#4-excess-kurtosis-and-the-three-types) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-1505">G-1505</span> | Plausibility | How believable a parameter value is in the light of the data; measured by its likelihood relative to other values. | [Note MA-069](MA/08-likelihood/MA-069-probability-vs-likelihood/MA-069-probability-vs-likelihood.md#5-the-two-definitions) | Likelihood |
| <span id="G-1506">G-1506</span> | Plural direction | A direction in embedding space: the average of the arrows from singular to plural nouns, scaled to length 1; a word's dot product with it scores how plural the word is. | [Note DL-072](DL/06-transformers/DL-072-meaning-as-direction/DL-072-meaning-as-direction.md#6-a-dot-product-reads-how-much-of-a-direction-a-word-has) | Meaning as direction in embedding space |
| <span id="G-1507">G-1507</span> | Point estimate | A single number computed from sample data as the best guess for an unknown population parameter. | [Note MA-034](MA/04-inference/MA-034-estimating-a-mean-with-the-clt/MA-034-estimating-a-mean-with-the-clt.md#1-overview) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-1508">G-1508</span> | Pointwise operation | An operation done entry by entry on vectors of the same length, such as $\odot$. | [Note DL-062](DL/05-rnn/DL-062-lstm-architecture/DL-062-lstm-architecture.md#44-pointwise-operations) | LSTM gates (forget, input, output) and cell state |
| <span id="G-1509">G-1509</span> | Poisson distribution | A distribution that gives the probability of each possible count of events (0, 1, 2, ...) in a fixed interval of time or space, from the average count $\lambda$; used for counts such as questions per day. | [Note MA-032](MA/03-distributions/MA-032-poisson-distribution/MA-032-poisson-distribution.md#21-counts-in-an-interval) | Poisson distribution |
| <span id="G-1510">G-1510</span> | Poisson regression | A model for a count target, such as bike rentals per hour: it predicts the average count $\lambda$ for each row from its features, and the Poisson distribution then gives the probability of each count. | [Note MA-032](MA/03-distributions/MA-032-poisson-distribution/MA-032-poisson-distribution.md#8-when-a-count-is-poisson-and-where-it-is-used) | Poisson distribution |
| <span id="G-1511">G-1511</span> | Polar coordinates | Describing a point by its distance $r$ from the origin and its angle $\theta$: $(r\cos\theta, r\sin\theta)$. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#22-functions-with-several-outputs) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-1512">G-1512</span> | Policy | The agent's rules for which action to take. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#51-agent-environment-policy-and-reward) | Reinforcement learning |
| <span id="G-1513">G-1513</span> | Polynomial features | New input columns made from powers and products of the original inputs, such as $x_1^2$ and $x_1 x_2$; they let a linear model fit curves and curved decision boundaries. | [Note ML-079](ML/07-classification/ML-079-polynomial-logistic-regression/ML-079-polynomial-logistic-regression.md#22-the-formal-version-polynomial-features) | Polynomial features |
| <span id="G-1514">G-1514</span> | Polynomial kernel | A function that gives the dot product of two points after their inputs are expanded into powers, such as $x^2$ (a kernel whose feature map is built from powers of the inputs); it lets an SVM draw curved boundaries. | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#32-the-feature-map-and-the-kernel) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-1515">G-1515</span> | Polynomial regression | Linear regression on powers (and products) of the inputs, to fit curves. | [Note ML-060](ML/06-regression/ML-060-polynomial-regression/ML-060-polynomial-regression.md#1-overview) | Polynomial regression |
| <span id="G-1516">G-1516</span> | PolynomialFeatures | scikit-learn transformer that creates the power and product columns. | [Note ML-060](ML/06-regression/ML-060-polynomial-regression/ML-060-polynomial-regression.md#3-adding-powers-as-new-features) | Polynomial features |
| <span id="G-1517">G-1517</span> | Polysemantic neuron | A neuron that responds to several unrelated features. | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#8-the-superposition-hypothesis-and-a-toy-model) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-1518">G-1518</span> | Polytope | The region where a set of linear inequalities all hold, such as the feasible region of a linear program: a polygon in two dimensions. | [Note MA-068](MA/07-optimisation/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming.md#21-the-problem) | Linear and quadratic programming |
| <span id="G-1519">G-1519</span> | Pooled standard deviation | The combined standard deviation of two groups used by Student's two-sample t-test. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#23-the-test-statistic) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1521">G-1521</span> | Pooling | Replacing each small window of a feature map with one summary number, to shrink the map. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#41-where-pooling-sits) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-1520">G-1520</span> | Pooling layer | A CNN layer that shrinks the feature map from a convolution layer by replacing each small window with one number, such as its maximum; this cuts the computation and makes the features less sensitive to small shifts. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#41-where-pooling-sits) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-1525">G-1525</span> | Population | The entire group of individuals or objects we want to study. | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#4-population-and-sample) | Population, sample, parameter and statistic |
| <span id="G-1522">G-1522</span> | Population correlation $\rho$ | The correlation between two columns in the whole population; $r$ estimates it. | [Note MA-044](MA/04-inference/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test.md#71-correlation-as-a-test) | Correlation significance test |
| <span id="G-1523">G-1523</span> | Population covariance ($\sigma_{xy}$) | The covariance computed from every member of a population: the average product of the two columns' distances from their means, dividing by $N$; it shows whether the two columns move together. | [Note MA-009](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation.md#3-covariance) | Covariance and covariance matrix |
| <span id="G-1524">G-1524</span> | Population mean ($\mu$) | The mean of every value in the population. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#3-mean) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-1526">G-1526</span> | Position embedding matrix $W_P$ | A learned table with one vector per position in the text (1,024 rows in GPT-2); a token's position row is added to its token embedding, so the model knows where the token sits. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#5-from-tokens-to-vectors-token-and-position-embeddings) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-1527">G-1527</span> | Position-wise | Applied to each word's vector separately, with the same weights for every position. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#53-the-feed-forward-network) | Transformer encoder |
| <span id="G-1528">G-1528</span> | Positional encoding | A vector added to each word's embedding that tells the model the word's position. | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#1-overview) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-1529">G-1529</span> | Positive and negative side | The two halves of the plane on either side of a line $Ax + By + C = 0$: where $Ax + By + C$ is above 0 and where it is below 0; the perceptron predicts one class on each side. | [Note ML-069](ML/07-classification/ML-069-perceptron-trick/ML-069-perceptron-trick.md#32-positive-and-negative-sides) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1530">G-1530</span> | Positive definite matrix | A symmetric matrix that curves upward in every direction: its eigenvalues are all positive, and its quadratic form is a strictly convex bowl. | [Note MA-068](MA/07-optimisation/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming.md#31-the-problem) | Linear and quadratic programming |
| <span id="G-1531">G-1531</span> | Positive hyperplane ($\pi^+$) | A copy of the separating hyperplane moved out, parallel to itself, until it touches the first positive point; with the negative hyperplane it marks the two edges of the SVM's margin. | [Note ML-086](ML/07-classification/ML-086-svm-intuition/ML-086-svm-intuition.md#51-the-procedure) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1532">G-1532</span> | Positive semi-definite | A symmetric matrix that never curves downward in any direction: its eigenvalues are all 0 or positive. For such a matrix the eigenvectors are orthonormal, so its eigen-decomposition and its SVD are the same. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#7-svd-and-eigen-decomposition-compared) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1533">G-1533</span> | Positive skew (right skew) | A long tail on the right: a few very large values. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#10-skewness) | Skewness |
| <span id="G-1534">G-1534</span> | Post-hoc test | A test run after ANOVA rejects, to find which groups differ. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#83-which-classes-differ-tukeys-test) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-1536">G-1536</span> | Posterior | The probability of an event after the evidence is taken into account. | [Note MA-018](MA/02-probability/MA-018-bayes-theorem/MA-018-bayes-theorem.md#3-the-names-of-the-four-parts) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-1535">G-1535</span> | Posterior $p(\theta \mid \text{data})$ | The distribution over the parameters after seeing the data; proportional to likelihood × prior. | [Note MA-072](MA/08-likelihood/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning.md#71-the-posterior-over-the-parameters) | MAP estimation |
| <span id="G-1537">G-1537</span> | Power iteration | Finding the top eigenvector by multiplying a vector by the matrix again and again. | [Note MA-056](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues/MA-056-eigenvectors-and-eigenvalues.md#7-where-ml-uses-eigenvectors) | Eigenvectors and eigenvalues |
| <span id="G-1538">G-1538</span> | Power law | A relationship $y = k\thinspace x^{a}$: one variable proportional to a power of the other. | [Note MA-030](MA/03-distributions/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law/MA-030-pareto-and-power-law.md#2-power-laws) | Pareto distribution and power laws |
| <span id="G-1539">G-1539</span> | Power of a test | The probability that a test detects a real effect, that is, rejects $H_0$ when $H_0$ is false; it equals $1 - \beta$, where $\beta$ is the probability of a Type II error. | [Note MA-040](MA/04-inference/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails.md#3-power-of-a-test) | Type I and II errors, power, tails |
| <span id="G-1540">G-1540</span> | Power rule | The rule for differentiating a power of $x$: bring the power down in front and lower the power by one, $(x^n)' = n x^{n-1}$. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#42-the-power-rule-from-the-definition) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-1541">G-1541</span> | Power series | A polynomial with infinitely many terms, $\sum a_k (x - c)^k$; the Taylor series is one example, and it lets functions such as $e^x$ and $\sin x$ be computed from polynomials. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#64-a-polynomial-is-its-own-taylor-polynomial) | Taylor series |
| <span id="G-1542">G-1542</span> | Power transformer | A transform that raises each column to a learned power $\lambda$ to make it close to normal. | [Note ML-030](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-030-power-transformer/ML-030-power-transformer.md#1-overview) | Power transformer |
| <span id="G-1543">G-1543</span> | PowerTransformer | scikit-learn's class that applies the Box-Cox or Yeo-Johnson power transform to each column, to make its distribution closer to normal. | [Note ML-030](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-030-power-transformer/ML-030-power-transformer.md#2-power-transformer-in-scikit-learn) | Power transformer |
| <span id="G-1544">G-1544</span> | PPMI | Positive pointwise mutual information: a score of how much more often two words appear together than by chance, with negative scores set to 0. | [Note DL-073](DL/06-transformers/DL-073-what-is-self-attention/DL-073-what-is-self-attention.md#42-the-same-effect-in-real-text) | Word embeddings |
| <span id="G-1545">G-1545</span> | Pre-LN transformer (prenorm) | A transformer variant with layer normalisation at the input of each sub-layer, inside the residual branch, instead of after the addition; it trains without warm-up. | [Note DL-086](DL/06-transformers/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end.md#73-the-optimizer-and-the-warm-up-learning-rate) | Layer normalisation |
| <span id="G-1546">G-1546</span> | Pre-training | The first, general training of a model on a large dataset, such as ImageNet or a huge amount of unlabelled text, so that it learns general features; fine-tuning then adapts it to a task with little labelled data. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#7-stage-4-transfer-learning-for-language-2018) | Transfer learning (feature extraction and fine-tuning) |
| <span id="G-1547">G-1547</span> | Precision | Of all items predicted positive, the fraction that really are positive. | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#2-precision) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-1548">G-1548</span> | Predict (prediction, inference) | Use a trained model to give an answer for new data it has not seen, with no correct output available. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#41-learning-rules-from-data) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-1549">G-1549</span> | predict_proba | scikit-learn method that returns predicted probabilities instead of classes. | [Note ML-077](ML/07-classification/ML-077-roc-auc/ML-077-roc-auc.md#43-choosing-the-threshold) | ROC curve and AUC |
| <span id="G-1550">G-1550</span> | Prediction ($\hat{y}$) | The value the model gives for an input whose true output is not known; the hat marks a prediction. | [Note ML-050](ML/06-regression/ML-050-linear-regression-maths/ML-050-linear-regression-maths.md#31-the-error-at-one-point) | Best-fit line and squared error |
| <span id="G-1552">G-1552</span> | Prediction path | The nodes a row passes through, from the root to the leaf that predicts it. | [Note ML-094](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-094-dtreeviz/ML-094-dtreeviz.md#62-the-prediction-path-of-one-observation) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1553">G-1553</span> | Predictive maintenance | Repairing a machine before it breaks, based on predicted faults. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#62-predictive-maintenance) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-1554">G-1554</span> | Preferred stimulus | The pattern, such as an edge of one orientation, that a cell responds to most. | [Note DL-041](DL/04-cnn/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex.md#51-simple-cells) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-1555">G-1555</span> | Prefix | The first few words of a sentence; in next-word prediction each prefix is one training input and the word that follows it is the target. | [Note DL-063](DL/05-rnn/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction.md#32-every-prefix-predicts-its-next-word) | Next-word prediction with an LSTM |
| <span id="G-1558">G-1558</span> | Pretrained model | A network already trained by someone else on a large dataset, reused for our own predictions. | [Note DL-051](DL/04-cnn/DL-051-pretrained-models/DL-051-pretrained-models.md#1-overview) | Pretrained models and ImageNet |
| <span id="G-1559">G-1559</span> | Primal problem | The original constrained optimisation problem, in its own variables $\mathbf{x}$; it is called primal to tell it apart from the dual problem built from it with Lagrange multipliers. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#61-the-dual-function-and-the-dual-problem) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-1560">G-1560</span> | Primary visual cortex (V1) | The first area of the cortex that processes visual signals. | [Note DL-041](DL/04-cnn/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex.md#3-how-visual-information-flows) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-1561">G-1561</span> | Primitive feature | A basic feature such as an edge, found by the first layers. | [Note DL-040](DL/04-cnn/DL-040-cnn-intuition/DL-040-cnn-intuition.md#51-breaking-a-9-into-features) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-1563">G-1563</span> | Principal component | A new axis found by PCA, a direction through the data: PC1 holds the most variance, PC2 the next most at right angles to it; keeping only the first few cuts the number of features. | [Note ML-046](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-046-pca-geometric-intuition/ML-046-pca-geometric-intuition.md#4-how-pca-finds-the-new-features) | PCA |
| <span id="G-1565">G-1565</span> | Prior | The probability of an event before any evidence is seen. | [Note MA-018](MA/02-probability/MA-018-bayes-theorem/MA-018-bayes-theorem.md#3-the-names-of-the-four-parts) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-1564">G-1564</span> | Prior $p(\theta)$ | A distribution over the parameters expressing what we believe before seeing data. | [Note MA-072](MA/08-likelihood/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning.md#71-the-posterior-over-the-parameters) | MAP estimation |
| <span id="G-1566">G-1566</span> | Probabilistic interpretation | Reading the model's output as the probability of the positive class. | [Note ML-071](ML/07-classification/ML-071-sigmoid-function/ML-071-sigmoid-function.md#53-a-map-of-probabilities) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1567">G-1567</span> | Probabilistic model | A model that gives a probability for every possible target value, $p(y \mid x, \theta)$, instead of a single predicted value. | [Note MA-072](MA/08-likelihood/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning/MA-072-mle-in-machine-learning.md#2-a-model-as-a-distribution-of-the-target) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-1574">G-1574</span> | Probability | A number from 0 to 1 measuring how likely an event is; written $P(A)$. | [Note MA-011](MA/02-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability.md#2-what-a-probability-measures) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-1569">G-1569</span> | Probability density | The height of a continuous distribution's curve; compares how likely nearby values are. | [Note MA-022](MA/03-distributions/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf.md#5-what-the-density-at-a-point-means) | Probability density function (PDF) |
| <span id="G-1568">G-1568</span> | Probability density function (PDF) | A curve showing how likely each value is; areas under it are probabilities. | [Note MA-022](MA/03-distributions/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf/MA-022-pdf-and-continuous-cdf.md#4-bars-whose-area-is-probability-the-pdf) | Probability density function (PDF) |
| <span id="G-1571">G-1571</span> | Probability distribution | A list of every possible outcome of a random variable with its probability. | [Note MA-020](MA/03-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions.md#3-probability-distributions-as-tables) | Probability distributions |
| <span id="G-1570">G-1570</span> | Probability distribution function | A formula $y = f(x)$ giving the probability of each outcome; the umbrella term for PMF and PDF. | [Note MA-020](MA/03-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions.md#1-overview) | Probability distributions |
| <span id="G-1572">G-1572</span> | Probability mass function (PMF) | The function that gives the probability of each exact value of a discrete random variable, such as $P(X = 3) = 1/6$ for a fair die; its probabilities add up to 1. | [Note MA-021](MA/03-distributions/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf/MA-021-pmf-and-discrete-cdf.md#3-the-probability-mass-function) | Probability mass function (PMF) |
| <span id="G-1573">G-1573</span> | Probability tree | A diagram that draws each stage of a problem as branches labelled with probabilities; multiplying along a path gives that path's probability, and adding paths gives a total probability. | [Note MA-019](MA/02-probability/MA-019-bayes-problem/MA-019-bayes-problem.md#4-the-evidence-total-probability) | Bayes' theorem |
| <span id="G-1575">G-1575</span> | Probe | A fixed direction used to score words: the dot product of a word's vector with it. | [Note DL-072](DL/06-transformers/DL-072-meaning-as-direction/DL-072-meaning-as-direction.md#6-a-dot-product-reads-how-much-of-a-direction-a-word-has) | Meaning as direction in embedding space |
| <span id="G-1577">G-1577</span> | Product rule | The rule for the derivative of a product of two functions: differentiate one factor at a time, keep the other, and add: $(fg)' = f'g + fg'$. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#52-sum-product-and-quotient-rules) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-1576">G-1576</span> | Product rule for independent events | For independent events, the probability that both happen is the product of their separate probabilities: $P(A \cap B) = P(A) \times P(B)$. | [Note MA-016](MA/02-probability/MA-016-independent-events/MA-016-independent-events.md#2-the-definition) | Independent and mutually exclusive events |
| <span id="G-1578">G-1578</span> | Production code | The code that runs the deployed model on a server, for example behind a website. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#45-what-goes-wrong-without-a-pipeline) | ML pipelines |
| <span id="G-1579">G-1579</span> | Production environment | The server where a model serves real users. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#2-development-and-production) | Deployment |
| <span id="G-1580">G-1580</span> | Profiling report | An automatic EDA report describing every column and pair of columns of a dataset. | [Note ML-021](ML/02-getting-data/ML-021-pandas-profiling/ML-021-pandas-profiling.md#1-overview) | Pandas Profiling |
| <span id="G-1581">G-1581</span> | Program | Logic written by us that turns an input into an output. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#31-explicit-programming) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-1583">G-1583</span> | Projection | Dropping a point or vector straight onto an axis, a line or another vector's direction, like casting a shadow. | [Note ML-046](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-046-pca-geometric-intuition/ML-046-pca-geometric-intuition.md#21-the-rule-keep-the-bigger-spread) | PCA |
| <span id="G-1582">G-1582</span> | Projection view of the dot product | Reading the dot product as a shadow: $\mathbf{v} \cdot \mathbf{w}$ is the signed length of $\mathbf{w}$'s shadow (projection) on the line of $\mathbf{v}$ times the length $\lVert \mathbf{v} \rVert$, negative when the shadow points away from $\mathbf{v}$. | [Note MA-055](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-055-dot-product-and-duality/MA-055-dot-product-and-duality.md#2-the-dot-product-as-a-projection) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-1584">G-1584</span> | Proportional to (∝) | Equal up to a constant factor, written $\propto$; in Naive Bayes the evidence term is the same for every class, so it is dropped and the classes are compared with $\propto$. | [Note ML-082](ML/07-classification/ML-082-naive-bayes-maths/ML-082-naive-bayes-maths.md#3-step-1-bayes-theorem-without-the-evidence) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-1585">G-1585</span> | Proximity matrix | An n × n table of the distances between every pair of points or clusters. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#7-the-algorithm-the-proximity-matrix) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-1587">G-1587</span> | Pruning | Stopping a tree early or cutting it back so it does not overfit. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#44-min_samples_split) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1586">G-1586</span> | Pruning (Optuna) | Stopping an unpromising trial early, before its training finishes. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#9-other-features-of-optuna) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-1588">G-1588</span> | Pruning, quantization, knowledge distillation | Ways to make a model smaller: remove weights, store them with fewer bits, or train a small model to imitate a large one. | [Note DL-071](DL/06-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers.md#10-the-future) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-1589">G-1589</span> | Pseudo-residual | The mistake on one row that the next tree learns; for squared error it is actual minus predicted. | [Note ML-114](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-114-gradient-boosting-intuition/ML-114-gradient-boosting-intuition.md#5-pseudo-residuals-the-mistakes-of-the-current-model) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1590">G-1590</span> | Public dataset | A labelled dataset released for anyone to use. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#51-datasets) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-1591">G-1591</span> | Pure leaf | A leaf whose training rows all belong to one class. | [Note ML-094](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-094-dtreeviz/ML-094-dtreeviz.md#4-reading-a-dtreeviz-tree) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1592">G-1592</span> | Push and pull | Moving the line away from a correctly classified point, or towards a misclassified one. | [Note ML-071](ML/07-classification/ML-071-sigmoid-function/ML-071-sigmoid-function.md#21-push-and-pull) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1593">G-1593</span> | PyPI | The Python Package Index, the public store of Python packages. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#21-what-anaconda-and-conda-are) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1594">G-1594</span> | Pyramid structure | Hidden layers with fewer neurons in each later layer, such as 64-32-16; an old rule of thumb, not needed. | [Note DL-021](DL/02-training/DL-021-improving-a-neural-network/DL-021-improving-a-neural-network.md#32-neurons-per-layer) | Improving a neural network |
| <span id="G-1595">G-1595</span> | PyTorch | Meta's deep learning library, most used in research. | [Note DL-001](DL/01-basics/DL-001-dl-scope-and-prerequisites/DL-001-dl-scope-and-prerequisites.md#23-the-software) | What deep learning is |
| <span id="G-1596">G-1596</span> | Q-Q plot | A plot of a column's sorted values against the values a theoretical distribution, often the normal, would have; points on the line mean the data follows it. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#41-how-a-q-q-plot-is-built) | Q-Q plot |
| <span id="G-2252">G-2252</span> | Quadratic approximation | A curved surface that matches a function near a point in value, slopes and bending: the tangent plane plus the terms $\tfrac12 f_{xx}\delta_x^2 + f_{xy}\delta_x\delta_y + \tfrac12 f_{yy}\delta_y^2$; it is the second-order Taylor polynomial, and Newton's method steps to its minimum. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#4-bending-the-plane-the-quadratic-approximation) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1597">G-1597</span> | Quadratic form | An expression such as $\mathbf{x}^{\mathsf T}A\mathbf{x}$ that turns a vector into one number made only of squared entries and products of pairs of entries, such as $x_1^2 + 3x_2^2$; it is the curved part of a quadratic program's objective. | [Note MA-068](MA/07-optimisation/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming.md#31-the-problem) | Linear and quadratic programming |
| <span id="G-1598">G-1598</span> | Quadratic program | A problem of finding the lowest point of a bowl-shaped function with squared terms (a convex quadratic) while meeting limits written as linear inequalities (constraints). | [Note MA-068](MA/07-optimisation/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming.md#1-overview) | Linear and quadratic programming |
| <span id="G-1599">G-1599</span> | Quantiles | Values that cut sorted data into equal-sized groups; a fraction $p$ of the values lie below the quantile $Q(p)$. | [Note MA-008](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots.md#2-quantiles) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-1600">G-1600</span> | QuantileTransformer | A scikit-learn transformer that maps each column's values, through their quantiles, onto a uniform or normal distribution; it is used much less than the log and power transforms. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#3-mathematical-transformers-in-scikit-learn) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-1601">G-1601</span> | Quarter | One of four three-month parts of a year. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#57-quarter) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-1602">G-1602</span> | Quartiles | The 25%, 50% and 75% percentiles, which cut the data into four equal groups. | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#72-percentiles) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-1603">G-1603</span> | Quasi-Newton method | Newton's method with the Hessian replaced by a stand-in matrix built from how the gradient changes between steps, so no second derivatives are computed; BFGS and L-BFGS are examples. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#7-where-ml-uses-second-order-approximations) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1606">G-1606</span> | Query (SQL) | A request for data, written in SQL. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#3-what-sql-is) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-1604">G-1604</span> | Query parameters | Settings written after the `?` in an API's URL and joined by `&`, such as `page=1` or the API key; they tell the API which data to return. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#4-asking-an-api-for-data) | APIs |
| <span id="G-1605">G-1605</span> | Query point | The new point whose class we want to predict. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#21-the-steps) | K-nearest neighbours |
| <span id="G-1607">G-1607</span> | Query, key, value vectors | Three vectors made from each word's embedding by learned matrices: a word's query is compared with every word's key by dot products to get attention weights, and those weights mix the value vectors into the word's new vector. | [Note DL-078](DL/06-transformers/DL-078-multi-head-attention/DL-078-multi-head-attention.md#3-self-attention-in-one-paragraph) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-1608">G-1608</span> | Quintiles | The 20th, 40th, 60th and 80th percentiles: cuts into 5 groups. | [Note MA-008](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots/MA-008-percentiles-and-box-plots.md#2-quantiles) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-1609">G-1609</span> | Quotient rule | The rule for the derivative of a fraction of two functions: $(f/g)' = (f'g - fg')/g^2$. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#52-sum-product-and-quotient-rules) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-1610">G-1610</span> | Random experiment | An experiment whose outcome cannot be predicted, such as a coin toss. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#21-random-experiment) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-1611">G-1611</span> | Random forest | Bagging with decision trees as the base models: many trees, each trained on a random sample of the rows with a random choice of features at every split, vote (classification) or are averaged (regression), which lowers the variance of a single tree. | [Note ML-102](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-102-random-forest-intro/ML-102-random-forest-intro.md#1-overview) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-1612">G-1612</span> | Random initialization | Starting a model's parameters at random values, often drawn from a uniform distribution. | [Note MA-029](MA/03-distributions/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal.md#23-where-the-continuous-uniform-appears) | Weight initialisation |
| <span id="G-1613">G-1613</span> | Random oversampling | Copying randomly chosen minority rows until the classes are equal. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#6-random-oversampling) | Random under- and oversampling |
| <span id="G-1614">G-1614</span> | Random patches | Training several models, each on a random subset of rows and a random subset of columns, and combining their predictions (a kind of bagging). | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#24-pasting-random-subspaces-and-random-patches) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-1615">G-1615</span> | Random preprocessing layer | A Keras layer, such as `RandomFlip`, that changes images at random during training and does nothing at prediction. | [Note DL-050](DL/04-cnn/DL-050-data-augmentation/DL-050-data-augmentation.md#5-augmentation-in-keras) | Data augmentation |
| <span id="G-1616">G-1616</span> | Random sample imputation | Filling each gap with a value drawn at random from the column's known values. | [Note ML-037](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample.md#1-overview) | Random sample imputation |
| <span id="G-1617">G-1617</span> | Random seed | A number that fixes a random number generator so that a run can be repeated exactly. | [Note ML-111](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-111-adaboost-from-scratch/ML-111-adaboost-from-scratch.md#6-normalising-and-drawing-the-next-dataset) | Random sample imputation |
| <span id="G-1618">G-1618</span> | Random subspaces | Training several models, each on all rows but a random subset of columns, and combining their predictions (a kind of bagging). | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#24-pasting-random-subspaces-and-random-patches) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-1619">G-1619</span> | Random undersampling | Dropping randomly chosen majority rows until the classes are equal. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#5-random-undersampling) | Random under- and oversampling |
| <span id="G-1620">G-1620</span> | Random variable | The possible numerical outcomes of a random experiment; strictly, a function from outcomes to numbers. | [Note MA-020](MA/03-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions/MA-020-random-variables-and-distributions.md#21-algebra-variables-and-random-variables) | Random variables |
| <span id="G-1621">G-1621</span> | RandomForestClassifier | scikit-learn's random forest for classification. | [Note ML-102](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-102-random-forest-intro/ML-102-random-forest-intro.md#54-what-a-real-random-forest-does-differently) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-1622">G-1622</span> | RandomForestRegressor | scikit-learn's random forest for regression. | [Note ML-102](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-102-random-forest-intro/ML-102-random-forest-intro.md#2-why-random-forests-are-so-popular) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-1623">G-1623</span> | Randomised controlled trial | An experiment that assigns a treatment at random, to test causation. | [Note MA-009](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation.md#5-correlation-does-not-imply-causation) | Correlation and causation |
| <span id="G-1624">G-1624</span> | Randomized SVD | A fast method that finds only the top $k$ singular vectors, used by scikit-learn for large data. | [Note MA-060](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning.md#23-why-compute-it-this-way) | Latent semantic analysis |
| <span id="G-1625">G-1625</span> | RandomizedSearchCV | A scikit-learn tool that tries a fixed number of randomly drawn combinations of model settings (hyperparameters) and scores each with cross-validation, instead of trying every combination. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#72-tuning-with-gridsearchcv-and-randomizedsearchcv) | Grid and random search |
| <span id="G-1626">G-1626</span> | Range | The simplest measure of spread: the largest value minus the smallest. | [Note MA-006](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion.md#3-range) | Variance |
| <span id="G-1627">G-1627</span> | Rank (of a matrix) | The number of linearly independent columns of a matrix, that is, how many directions its columns really span; a column that is a combination of others does not add to it. | [Note MA-058](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-058-computing-the-svd/MA-058-computing-the-svd.md#5-a-matrix-of-rank-1) | Linear combinations, span and basis |
| <span id="G-1629">G-1629</span> | Rank (of a tensor) | The number of axes of a tensor (ndim in NumPy). | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#4-rank-axes-shape-and-size) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-1628">G-1628</span> | Rank collapse | All word vectors of a sentence becoming the same vector after many attention layers without residual connections. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#71-why-residual-connections) | Residual connections and add & norm |
