| <span id="G-1630">G-1630</span> | Rank $k$ approximation (truncated SVD) | The approximation of a matrix that keeps only its $k$ largest singular values with their vectors and drops the rest, $\hat A_k = U_k\Sigma_kV_k^{\mathsf T}$; it is the best small summary of the matrix and needs far fewer numbers. | [Note MA-059](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-059-low-rank-approximation/MA-059-low-rank-approximation.md#3-the-rank-k-approximation) | Low-rank approximation (truncated SVD) |
| <span id="G-1631">G-1631</span> | Rank-1 layer | One piece of a matrix split by the SVD: a column times a row, $\sigma_i\mathbf u_i\mathbf v_i^{\mathsf T}$, weighted by its singular value $\sigma_i$; adding all the layers gives back the matrix. | [Note MA-059](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-059-low-rank-approximation/MA-059-low-rank-approximation.md#22-splitting-any-matrix-into-layers) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1632">G-1632</span> | RapidAPI | A website listing many APIs, including free ones. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#9-sharing-datasets-and-finding-more-apis) | APIs |
| <span id="G-1633">G-1633</span> | Rate $\lambda$ | The average number of events per interval; the only parameter of the Poisson distribution. | [Note MA-032](MA/03-distributions/MA-032-poisson-distribution/MA-032-poisson-distribution.md#1-overview) | Poisson distribution |
| <span id="G-1634">G-1634</span> | Rate limit | The most requests an API accepts in a given time. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#7-looping-over-every-page) | APIs |
| <span id="G-1635">G-1635</span> | Rate of change | How much one quantity changes per unit change of another; the meaning of a derivative. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#3-the-difference-quotient-slope-of-a-secant-line) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-1636">G-1636</span> | Rate parameter $\lambda$ | The average number of events per unit time; the average wait is $1/\lambda$. | [Note MA-071](MA/08-likelihood/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions.md#31-the-distribution-of-waiting-times) | Exponential distribution |
| <span id="G-1637">G-1637</span> | Raw data | Data as it arrives, before any preparation. | [Note ML-022](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering/ML-022-what-is-feature-engineering.md#2-what-feature-engineering-is) | Feature engineering |
| <span id="G-1638">G-1638</span> | Raw string | A Python string written `r"..."`, in which a backslash is kept as it is. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#52-the-category-part) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-1639">G-1639</span> | RBF kernel | Radial basis function kernel, built on $e^{-(\text{distance})^2}$: it scores two points as similar (close to 1) when they are near each other and close to 0 when far apart; the usual first-choice SVM kernel for curved boundaries. | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#4-a-2d-example-concentric-circles) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-1640">G-1640</span> | Reader | What `read_csv` returns with `chunksize`: it hands out one chunk at a time. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#18-reading-a-big-file-in-chunks-chunksize) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-1641">G-1641</span> | Recall (sensitivity) | Of all items that really are positive, the fraction the model found. | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#3-recall) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-1642">G-1642</span> | Receptive field | The area of the image that one cell (or one unit of a network) responds to. | [Note DL-041](DL/04-cnn/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex.md#51-simple-cells) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-1643">G-1643</span> | Reciprocal transform | Replacing each value with $1/x$; reverses the order of the values. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#61-reciprocal-transform) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-1644">G-1644</span> | Recommendation engine (recommender system) | A model that suggests items, such as movies, that a user is likely to like. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#32-example-a-movie-recommender) | Batch (offline) learning |
| <span id="G-1646">G-1646</span> | Recurrent layer | A hidden layer whose output at one time step is an input to itself at the next. | [Note DL-056](DL/05-rnn/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation.md#42-two-differences-from-an-ann) | Recurrent neural network (RNN) |
| <span id="G-1647">G-1647</span> | Recurrent neural network (RNN) | A network whose hidden-layer output is fed back in, so it remembers earlier steps of a sequence. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#23-recurrent-neural-network-rnn-and-lstm) | Recurrent neural network (RNN) |
| <span id="G-1648">G-1648</span> | Recursion | A function that calls itself on smaller inputs. | [Note DL-019](DL/01-basics/DL-019-mlp-memoization/DL-019-mlp-memoization.md#31-the-plain-recursive-version) | Memoization |
| <span id="G-1649">G-1649</span> | Reduced sample space | The outcomes that remain possible once the condition is known. | [Note MA-015](MA/02-probability/MA-015-conditional-probability/MA-015-conditional-probability.md#32-a-conditional-probability-by-counting) | Conditional probability |
| <span id="G-1650">G-1650</span> | Reference category | The category whose dummy column is dropped; it is shown by all zeros. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#33-dropping-one-column-loses-nothing) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-1651">G-1651</span> | Reflection | A transformation that mirrors space, without stretching it (an orthogonal transformation with determinant $-1$). | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#24-what-an-orthogonal-matrix-does-to-space) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1655">G-1655</span> | Regression | Supervised learning with a numerical output. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#23-regression-and-classification) | Regression problems |
| <span id="G-1652">G-1652</span> | Regression metric | A number that summarises how close a regression model's predictions are to the true values. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#1-overview) | Regression metrics |
| <span id="G-1653">G-1653</span> | Regression output layer | The last layer of a regression network: one node per number to predict, with the linear activation, which returns the weighted sum unchanged so the output can be any number. | [Note DL-013](DL/01-basics/DL-013-graduate-admission-ann/DL-013-graduate-admission-ann.md#41-the-linear-output-node) | ANN for regression |
| <span id="G-1654">G-1654</span> | Regression tree | A decision tree whose leaves predict numbers: the mean output of their training rows. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#1-overview) | Regression trees |
| <span id="G-1656">G-1656</span> | Regular expression | A short pattern that describes text, such as `\d+` for "one or more digits". | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#51-the-number-part) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-1659">G-1659</span> | Regularisation | Adding a penalty for large coefficients to the loss, so the model overfits less (lower variance). | [Note ML-062](ML/06-regression/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition.md#1-overview) | Regularisation |
| <span id="G-1657">G-1657</span> | Regularisation by randomisation | A name for dropout: reducing overfitting through random choices during training. | [Note DL-025](DL/02-training/DL-025-dropout-code/DL-025-dropout-code.md#42-the-results) | Dropout |
| <span id="G-1658">G-1658</span> | Regularisation term $\Omega$ | XGBoost's penalty on each tree, added to the loss so it prefers small, simple trees: $\gamma$ for every leaf plus $\frac{1}{2}\lambda$ times each squared leaf weight, $\gamma T + \frac{1}{2}\lambda\sum_j w_j^2$ with $T$ the number of leaves. | [Note ML-120](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-120-xgboost-maths/ML-120-xgboost-maths.md#4-the-objective-loss-plus-a-penalty-on-the-tree) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1660">G-1660</span> | Reinforcement learning | Learning by acting and receiving rewards or punishments. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#51-agent-environment-policy-and-reward) | Reinforcement learning |
| <span id="G-1661">G-1661</span> | Reject $H_0$ | The decision that the data gives strong enough evidence against $H_0$. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#52-the-goal-evidence-against-h_0) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-1662">G-1662</span> | Rejection region (critical region) | The values of the test statistic for which we reject $H_0$; its area under $H_0$ is $\alpha$. | [Note MA-039](MA/04-inference/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test.md#6-the-rejection-region-and-the-critical-value) | Z-test and rejection regions |
| <span id="G-1663">G-1663</span> | Rejection region approach | Carrying out a test by checking whether the test statistic falls beyond a fixed boundary. | [Note MA-039](MA/04-inference/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test.md#6-the-rejection-region-and-the-critical-value) | Z-test and rejection regions |
| <span id="G-1664">G-1664</span> | Relative frequency | A category's share of all the data: its frequency divided by the total. | [Note MA-007](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs/MA-007-frequency-tables-and-graphs.md#22-relative-frequency) | Frequency tables |
| <span id="G-1665">G-1665</span> | Relative path | A file's location, starting from the folder the code runs in. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#4-opening-a-file-on-our-machine) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-1666">G-1666</span> | Relative position | The distance between two words, such as "two words later". | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#4-first-idea-count-the-words) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-1668">G-1668</span> | ReLU | Rectified linear unit, the activation $\max(0, z)$: it passes a positive input unchanged and turns a negative one into 0; the usual default activation for hidden layers. | [Note DL-027](DL/02-training/DL-027-activation-functions/DL-027-activation-functions.md#8-relu) | ReLU |
| <span id="G-1667">G-1667</span> | ReLU mask | The 0/1 matrix $\mathbb{1}[Z > 0]$, with 1 where ReLU's input was positive and 0 elsewhere; in the backward pass the gradient is multiplied by it, so it flows back only through the positive cells. | [Note DL-048](DL/04-cnn/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers.md#7-back-through-relu-a-mask) | Backpropagation in a CNN |
| <span id="G-1669">G-1669</span> | remainder | The `ColumnTransformer` parameter for untouched columns: `"drop"` (default) or `"passthrough"`. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#52-the-remainder-parameter) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-2260">G-2260</span> | Representation | A set of features that describes the data; a good one stays the same when unimportant details (such as handwriting) change. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#23-the-technical-definition-representation-learning) | Representation learning |
| <span id="G-1670">G-1670</span> | Representation learning (feature learning) | Letting the algorithm discover useful features from raw data, instead of engineering them by hand. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#1-overview) | Representation learning |
| <span id="G-1671">G-1671</span> | Representation power | The variety of patterns a network can express; grows with more nodes and layers. | [Note DL-065](DL/05-rnn/DL-065-deep-rnns/DL-065-deep-rnns.md#3-why-stack-recurrent-layers) | Deep (stacked) RNNs |
| <span id="G-1672">G-1672</span> | Representative sample | A sample that reflects the whole situation fairly. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#41-a-sample-that-tells-the-wrong-story) | Sampling noise and bias |
| <span id="G-1673">G-1673</span> | Request, response | What we send to a server, and what it sends back. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#31-the-request-and-the-status-code) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-1674">G-1674</span> | requests | Python library that sends web requests. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#6-fetching-one-page-in-python) | APIs |
| <span id="G-1675">G-1675</span> | Required sample size | The smallest sample size that gives a chosen margin of error $E$, found by solving the margin formula for $n$: $n = (z_{\alpha/2}\thinspace\sigma/E)^2$. | [Note MA-036](MA/04-inference/MA-036-interpreting-confidence-intervals/MA-036-interpreting-confidence-intervals.md#43-sample-size) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-1676">G-1676</span> | Resampling | Changing the number of rows per class to balance the data. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#1-overview) | Random under- and oversampling |
| <span id="G-1677">G-1677</span> | Research hypothesis | Another name for the alternative hypothesis: the idea that came out of research. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#4-the-alternative-hypothesis) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-1678">G-1678</span> | Reset (modulated) memory | The GRU's old memory scaled entry by entry by the reset gate, $r_t \odot h_{t-1}$; it sets how much of the past goes into building the candidate hidden state. | [Note DL-064](DL/05-rnn/DL-064-gru/DL-064-gru.md#82-step-2-the-candidate-hidden-state) | GRU (gated recurrent unit) |
| <span id="G-1679">G-1679</span> | Reset gate ($r_t$) | A sigmoid layer's output that decides how much of each entry of the old memory is used to build the candidate. | [Note DL-064](DL/05-rnn/DL-064-gru/DL-064-gru.md#4-the-big-idea-one-state-two-gates) | GRU (gated recurrent unit) |
| <span id="G-1680">G-1680</span> | Residual block | A block whose input is added to its output, giving the gradient a shortcut. | [Note DL-018](DL/01-basics/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients.md#65-residual-networks) | Skip connections |
| <span id="G-1681">G-1681</span> | Residual connection | A path that skips a sub-layer and adds the sub-layer's input to its output; also called a skip connection. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#1-overview) | Residual connections and add & norm |
| <span id="G-1682">G-1682</span> | Residual dropout | Dropout applied to each sub-layer's output before it is added back to the sub-layer's input (and to the embedding sums); the original transformer uses it to reduce overfitting. | [Note DL-086](DL/06-transformers/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end/DL-086-transformer-end-to-end.md#74-regularisation-residual-dropout-and-label-smoothing) | Label smoothing |
| <span id="G-2207">G-2207</span> | Residual plot | The residuals (errors) plotted above and below a flat zero line, against a feature or the predicted value; a funnel or a wave in it shows that an assumption of linear regression is broken. | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#4-the-residual-plot) | Assumptions of linear regression |
| <span id="G-1683">G-1683</span> | Residual stream | The token vector that passes from block to block, to which every sub-layer adds its output. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#6-inside-a-gpt-2-block) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-1684">G-1684</span> | Residual sum of squares (sum of squared errors, SSE) | The squares of all the errors (residuals) added up; the quantity the best-fit line makes smallest, and a regression tree splits where the SSE of the two sides is smallest. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#62-the-formula) | Regression metrics |
| <span id="G-1686">G-1686</span> | Response | What `requests.get` returns: the status code plus the reply. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#6-fetching-one-page-in-python) | APIs |
| <span id="G-1687">G-1687</span> | Responsibility $r_{nk}$ | In a mixture model, how likely it is that component $k$ produced point $n$, given that point (the posterior probability). | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#32-the-standard-terms) | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) |
| <span id="G-1688">G-1688</span> | Retina | The 2D sheet of light-sensitive cells at the back of the eye. | [Note DL-041](DL/04-cnn/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex.md#3-how-visual-information-flows) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-1689">G-1689</span> | Retrain | Train a model again, here from scratch on old + new data. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#42-retraining-on-a-schedule) | Retraining |
| <span id="G-1690">G-1690</span> | Reward / punishment | Good / bad feedback after an action. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#51-agent-environment-policy-and-reward) | Reinforcement learning |
| <span id="G-1691">G-1691</span> | Ridge regression | Linear regression with a penalty on the sum of squared coefficients added to the loss; the penalty keeps the coefficients small, which reduces overfitting (L2 regularisation). | [Note ML-062](ML/06-regression/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition.md#1-overview) | Ridge regression |
| <span id="G-1692">G-1692</span> | Right singular vector ($\mathbf v_i$) | A column $\mathbf v_i$ of $V$ in the SVD: a unit-length input direction that the matrix sends to $\sigma_i$ times the matching left singular vector $\mathbf u_i$. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#41-from-the-singular-value-equation-to-the-factorisation) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1693">G-1693</span> | Right-tailed and left-tailed test | One-tailed tests that count only one tail as evidence against $H_0$: a right-tailed test rejects for large values of the test statistic (as for $H_1: \mu > \mu_0$), a left-tailed test for small ones (as for $H_1: \mu < \mu_0$). | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#32-the-curves-and-the-tail-area) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-1694">G-1694</span> | River | A Python library for online machine learning. | [Note ML-005](ML/01-foundations/ML-005-online-learning/ML-005-online-learning.md#4-implementing-online-learning) | Online learning |
| <span id="G-1695">G-1695</span> | RLHF | Reinforcement learning from human feedback: improving a model with a reward model trained on human rankings of its outputs. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#9-from-gpt-3-to-chatgpt) | Large language models (LLMs) |
| <span id="G-1696">G-1696</span> | RMSNorm | A simpler version of layer normalisation that divides each vector by its root mean square and skips subtracting the mean; many current transformers use it. | [Note DL-080](DL/06-transformers/DL-080-layer-normalization/DL-080-layer-normalization.md#72-where-it-sits) | Layer normalisation |
| <span id="G-1697">G-1697</span> | RMSProp | An optimizer that divides each parameter's step by the root of a running average (EWMA) of its recent squared gradients, $v_t = \beta v_{t-1} + (1-\beta)g_t^2$; old gradients fade, so unlike AdaGrad the learning rate does not shrink to nothing. | [Note DL-037](DL/03-optimizers/DL-037-rmsprop/DL-037-rmsprop.md#1-overview) | RMSProp |
| <span id="G-1698">G-1698</span> | robots.txt | A file at a site's root listing what bots are asked not to visit. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#32-looking-like-a-browser) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-1699">G-1699</span> | Robust scaling | Subtract the median and divide by the interquartile range; copes well with outliers. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#9-robust-scaling) | Feature scaling |
| <span id="G-1700">G-1700</span> | Robustness | Performing well even when the data changes somewhat. | [Note ML-095](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-095-ensemble-learning/ML-095-ensemble-learning.md#64-benefit-3-robustness) | Ensemble learning |
| <span id="G-1701">G-1701</span> | RobustScaler | scikit-learn's class for robust scaling. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#9-robust-scaling) | Feature scaling |
| <span id="G-1702">G-1702</span> | ROC curve | A plot of the true positive rate against the false positive rate as the classification threshold moves; it shows a binary classifier's trade-off at every threshold, and a curve nearer the top-left corner is better. | [Note ML-077](ML/07-classification/ML-077-roc-auc/ML-077-roc-auc.md#4-the-roc-curve) | ROC curve and AUC |
| <span id="G-1703">G-1703</span> | Rollback | Restoring a model to an earlier, good version. | [Note ML-005](ML/01-foundations/ML-005-online-learning/ML-005-online-learning.md#72-bad-data-can-damage-the-model) | Online learning |
| <span id="G-1704">G-1704</span> | Root mean square | The square root of the average of squared values: the typical size of the gradients. | [Note DL-037](DL/03-optimizers/DL-037-rmsprop/DL-037-rmsprop.md#4-the-fix-an-average-that-forgets) | RMSProp |
| <span id="G-1705">G-1705</span> | Root mean squared error (RMSE) | The square root of the average squared error (MSE), so the error is back in the output's units. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#4-root-mean-squared-error-rmse) | Regression metrics |
| <span id="G-1706">G-1706</span> | Root node | The first node of a tree, holding all the training rows. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#4-terminology) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1709">G-1709</span> | Rotation | A transformation that turns space without stretching or flipping it (an orthogonal matrix with determinant $+1$). | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#24-what-an-orthogonal-matrix-does-to-space) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1708">G-1708</span> | Rotation matrix | The matrix of a rotation about the origin; by 90°, columns $[0, 1]$ and $[-1, 0]$. | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#81-one-matrix-per-distance) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-1707">G-1707</span> | Rotation matrix $M_k$ | The fixed matrix that turns the encoding of any position $p$ into that of $p + k$. | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#81-one-matrix-per-distance) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-1710">G-1710</span> | Rotation, shift, zoom, shear | Four common data-augmentation changes to an image: turning it, moving it, enlarging or shrinking it, and slanting it; each makes a new training image with the same label. | [Note DL-050](DL/04-cnn/DL-050-data-augmentation/DL-050-data-augmentation.md#41-five-common-transformations) | Data augmentation |
| <span id="G-1711">G-1711</span> | Row and column totals | The sums in the margins of a contingency table; each counts one whole event. | [Note MA-013](MA/02-probability/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables.md#31-the-die-as-a-table) | Venn diagrams and contingency tables |
| <span id="G-2245">G-2245</span> | Row form | Writing points as rows and multiplying $\mathbf{x}^{\mathsf T}W^{\mathsf T}$; the matrix applied first is on the left. | [Note MA-054](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition.md#72-points-as-rows-the-same-product-transposed) | Matrix multiplication as composition |
| <span id="G-1712">G-1712</span> | Row sampling | Giving each base model a random subset of the rows. | [Note ML-102](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-102-random-forest-intro/ML-102-random-forest-intro.md#5-a-random-forest-by-hand) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-1713">G-1713</span> | Row space | All the combinations of a matrix's rows (their span), a subspace of the input space; the SVD's right singular vectors $\mathbf v_i$ with $\sigma_i > 0$ are a perpendicular basis of it. | [Note MA-058](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-058-computing-the-svd/MA-058-computing-the-svd.md#6-the-four-subspaces) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1714">G-1714</span> | Row vector | A vector written as one row, shape $1 \times n$. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#5-row-vectors-and-column-vectors) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-1715">G-1715</span> | RPM | Revolutions per minute: how fast a motor turns. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#62-predictive-maintenance) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-1716">G-1716</span> | Runge's phenomenon | The large swings of a high-degree polynomial near the ends of the interval it is fitted on. | [Note ML-115](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths.md#31-when-lines-and-polynomials-are-not-enough) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1717">G-1717</span> | R² score (coefficient of determination) | 1 minus the model's squared error divided by the squared error of always predicting the mean: 1 is perfect, 0 is no better than the average. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#6-r²-score) | Regression metrics |
| <span id="G-1718">G-1718</span> | Saddle point | A flat point that curves up in one direction and down in another. | [Note DL-032](DL/03-optimizers/DL-032-optimizers-in-deep-learning/DL-032-optimizers-in-deep-learning.md#55-saddle-points) | Local minima and saddle points |
| <span id="G-1719">G-1719</span> | Safety (of an augmentation) | Whether a transformation keeps the label true. | [Note DL-050](DL/04-cnn/DL-050-data-augmentation/DL-050-data-augmentation.md#42-only-transformations-that-keep-the-label) | Data augmentation |
| <span id="G-1720">G-1720</span> | saga | A solver (the method that finds the weights) that updates them from randomly chosen rows (stochastic) and supports every penalty, including Elastic Net. | [Note ML-080](ML/07-classification/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters.md#3-the-solver) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1721">G-1721</span> | Same-length many-to-many | Many-to-many with one output per input time step. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#51-same-length) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-1722">G-1722</span> | SAMME | The AdaBoost variant in scikit-learn: alpha without the factor 1/2, only misclassified rows reweighted; same decisions. | [Note ML-111](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-111-adaboost-from-scratch/ML-111-adaboost-from-scratch.md#10-weights-instead-of-upsampling-how-scikit-learn-does-it) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1723">G-1723</span> | SAMME.R | An AdaBoost variant that used predicted probabilities; removed from scikit-learn. | [Note ML-112](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters.md#24-algorithm-removed) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1731">G-1731</span> | Sample | The part of a population that we actually measure, such as 50,000 people asked about their salary instead of everyone in India; we study it because measuring the whole population is usually impossible, and use it to draw conclusions about the population. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#41-a-sample-that-tells-the-wrong-story) | Population, sample, parameter and statistic |
| <span id="G-1724">G-1724</span> | Sample covariance ($s_{xy}$) | Covariance computed from a sample of $n$ observations: the products of the two features' distances from their means, added up and divided by $n - 1$; its sign says whether the two features rise together or move in opposite directions. | [Note MA-009](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation.md#3-covariance) | Covariance and covariance matrix |
| <span id="G-1725">G-1725</span> | Sample mean ($\bar{x}$) | The average of the values in a sample, $\bar{x}$: add them up and divide by $n$; it is used as the estimate of the population mean $\mu$. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#3-mean) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-1726">G-1726</span> | Sample proportion $\hat{p}$ | The share of a sample that falls in one category, written $\hat{p}$, such as 26 men out of 60; it estimates that share in the whole population. | [Note MA-044](MA/04-inference/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test.md#41-the-question) | One-sample proportion test |
| <span id="G-1727">G-1727</span> | Sample size ($n$) | The number of values in one sample, written $n$, such as 50 people; a larger $n$ makes the sample mean vary less from sample to sample (its spread is $\sigma/\sqrt{n}$). | [Note MA-033](MA/04-inference/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt.md#3-sampling-distributions) | Sampling distribution and standard error |
| <span id="G-1728">G-1728</span> | Sample skewness $G_1$ | The skewness formula used on a sample, $G_1$: the average cubed standardized distance from the mean, with a small-sample correction; it measures how lopsided the data is, and it is what pandas' `skew()` returns. | [Note MA-026](MA/03-distributions/MA-026-skewness/MA-026-skewness.md#5-the-sample-skewness-formula) | Skewness |
| <span id="G-1729">G-1729</span> | Sample space | The set of all possible outcomes of an experiment, such as $\lbrace1, \dots, 6\rbrace$ for a die; every event is a subset of it, so probabilities are worked out inside it. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#24-sample-space) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-2229">G-2229</span> | Sample variance $s^2$ | The variance computed from a sample, $s^2$: the squared distances from the sample mean, summed and divided by $n - 1$; dividing by $n - 1$ instead of $n$ makes it right on average as an estimate of the population variance. | [Note MA-006](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion.md#61-the-sample-version) | Variance |
| <span id="G-1730">G-1730</span> | Sample weight | A number attached to each row saying how important it is; AdaBoost starts every row at $1/n$ and raises the weights of misclassified rows so the next model focuses on them. | [Note ML-110](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step.md#3-step-1-every-observation-gets-the-same-weight) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1732">G-1732</span> | sample_weight | The argument of `fit` that tells a scikit-learn model how much each row counts. | [Note ML-111](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-111-adaboost-from-scratch/ML-111-adaboost-from-scratch.md#10-weights-instead-of-upsampling-how-scikit-learn-does-it) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1733">G-1733</span> | Sampler | In Optuna, the algorithm that suggests the next trial's hyperparameter values. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#4-optunas-vocabulary) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-1739">G-1739</span> | Sampling (text generation) | Choosing the next token at random according to the softmax probabilities, instead of always taking the most probable token, so the generated text does not keep repeating itself. | [Note DL-088](DL/06-transformers/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling.md#6-sampling-choosing-the-next-token) | Unembedding, logits, temperature and sampling |
| <span id="G-1734">G-1734</span> | Sampling bias | A sample that misrepresents the population because of how the data was collected, such as asking only Indian fans who should win a cricket match; a larger sample does not fix it. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#42-sampling-noise-and-sampling-bias) | Sampling noise and bias |
| <span id="G-1736">G-1736</span> | Sampling distribution | The distribution of a statistic (such as the mean) computed from many independent samples of the same size from one population; it shows how much that statistic varies from sample to sample. | [Note MA-033](MA/04-inference/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt.md#1-overview) | Sampling distribution and standard error |
| <span id="G-1735">G-1735</span> | Sampling distribution of the sample mean | The distribution of the means of many samples of size $n$; by the central limit theorem it is close to normal, centred on the population mean, with spread $\sigma/\sqrt{n}$. | [Note MA-033](MA/04-inference/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt.md#3-sampling-distributions) | Sampling distribution and standard error |
| <span id="G-1737">G-1737</span> | Sampling noise | Error in a sample's result because the sample is too small, so the answer depends on luck, such as asking only 5 fans; a larger sample reduces it. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#42-sampling-noise-and-sampling-bias) | Sampling noise and bias |
| <span id="G-1738">G-1738</span> | Sampling techniques | Ways of drawing a sample from a population so that it is random and representative, and its conclusions hold for the population. | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#41-what-makes-a-good-sample) | Population, sample, parameter and statistic |
| <span id="G-1740">G-1740</span> | Saturated softmax | A softmax whose inputs are so far apart that one weight is near 1 and the rest near 0; its gradient is then near 0, so training barely changes the scores. | [Note DL-075](DL/06-transformers/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention.md#5-why-a-wide-spread-is-a-problem-the-softmax-saturates) | Scaled dot-product attention |
| <span id="G-1741">G-1741</span> | Saturating function | A function that squeezes any input into a bounded range, such as the sigmoid into 0 to 1, so its slope goes to 0 at the ends; those near-zero slopes make gradients vanish in deep networks. | [Note DL-027](DL/02-training/DL-027-activation-functions/DL-027-activation-functions.md#55-non-saturating) | Activation functions |
| <span id="G-1743">G-1743</span> | Scalar | A single number, such as 5 or -6: a 0D tensor; multiplying a vector by it scales the vector, which is where the name comes from. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#2-what-a-tensor-is) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-1742">G-1742</span> | Scalar product | Another name for the dot product: multiply two vectors component by component and add, giving one number (a scalar) that is large when the vectors point the same way. | [Note MA-050](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity.md#2-products-of-two-vectors) | Dot product |
| <span id="G-1744">G-1744</span> | Scale (in SciPy) | SciPy's parameter for the exponential distribution, the average wait $1/\lambda$. | [Note MA-071](MA/08-likelihood/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions.md#33-the-derivation) | Exponential distribution |
| <span id="G-1745">G-1745</span> | Scaled dot-product attention | Self-attention in which every query–key dot-product score is divided by $\sqrt{d_k}$ before the softmax, so the scores do not spread so wide that the softmax saturates: $\text{softmax}(QK^T/\sqrt{d_k})\thinspace V$. | [Note DL-075](DL/06-transformers/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention.md#1-overview) | Scaled dot-product attention |
| <span id="G-1746">G-1746</span> | Scaling (a vector) | Multiplying or dividing every component of a vector by the same scalar, such as $2 \times [1, 3] = [2, 6]$; it makes the arrow longer or shorter along the same line. | [Note MA-049](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations.md#6-multiplying-or-dividing-by-a-scalar-scaling) | Vector magnitude, distance and scalar operations |
| <span id="G-1747">G-1747</span> | Scaling factor | The number $1/\sqrt{d_k}$ that multiplies every attention score; it shrinks the spread of the scores so the softmax does not saturate. | [Note DL-075](DL/06-transformers/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention.md#6-choosing-the-scaling-factor) | Scaled dot-product attention |
| <span id="G-1749">G-1749</span> | Scatter plot | A chart with one dot per row and one numerical column on each axis; it shows by eye whether two features rise together, fall together or are unrelated. | [Note ML-020](ML/02-getting-data/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis/ML-020-bivariate-multivariate-analysis.md#31-total-bill-against-tip) | Bivariate and multivariate analysis |
| <span id="G-1750">G-1750</span> | scikit-learn | Python's main library for classical ML (imported as `sklearn`); it gives ready-made models and tools for splitting, scaling and scoring data. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#6-training-and-test-sets) | Train-test split |
| <span id="G-1751">G-1751</span> | SciPy | A Python library built on NumPy that adds more scientific routines, such as extra linear algebra and statistics functions. | [Note MA-047](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-047-linear-algebra-roadmap/MA-047-linear-algebra-roadmap.md#48-tools-and-libraries) | Linear algebra roadmap |
| <span id="G-1753">G-1753</span> | Score | In naive Bayes, the likelihood times the prior for one class; it is not the probability of the class, only proportional to it, which is enough to pick the class with the largest score. | [Note ML-081](ML/07-classification/ML-081-naive-bayes-intuition/ML-081-naive-bayes-intuition.md#2-the-method-on-one-picture) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-1752">G-1752</span> | Score function | In attention, the rule that gives the raw score $e_{ij}$ of how well encoder state $j$ matches decoder step $i$; a softmax turns these scores into attention weights, and Bahdanau and Luong attention differ in this rule. | [Note DL-070](DL/06-transformers/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention/DL-070-bahdanau-vs-luong-attention.md#3-recap-what-both-must-compute) | Attention mechanism |
| <span id="G-1754">G-1754</span> | Scott's rule | A rule of thumb that picks a KDE's bandwidth from the data's standard deviation $s$ and size $n$, $s \times n^{-1/5}$, so more data gives a narrower bandwidth; seaborn and SciPy use it by default. | [Note MA-023](MA/03-distributions/MA-023-density-estimation-kde/MA-023-density-estimation-kde.md#54-kde-in-seaborn) | Kernel density estimation (KDE) |
| <span id="G-2205">G-2205</span> | Scree plot | A bar chart with one bar per principal component, showing its share of the total variance (eigenvalue ÷ sum of eigenvalues); it shows how many components are worth keeping. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#61-reading-the-result-the-recipe-and-the-scree-plot) | PCA |
| <span id="G-1755">G-1755</span> | SDLC | Software development life cycle: the standard sequence of steps for building a software product from start to end; the ML development life cycle adapts it to ML projects. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#21-the-software-development-life-cycle) | ML development life cycle |
| <span id="G-1756">G-1756</span> | Search space | The ranges or lists of values each hyperparameter may take during tuning; the tuner only tries values from it, and each added value multiplies the number of combinations. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#21-the-problem-a-random-forest-for-placement) | Bayesian optimisation |
| <span id="G-1757">G-1757</span> | Secant equation | The condition $B_{k+1}\mathbf{s} = \mathbf{y}$ on the matrix that stands in for the Hessian: the new matrix must reproduce the gradient change $\mathbf{y}$ seen over the last step $\mathbf{s}$, so it captures the curvature just observed. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#7-where-ml-uses-second-order-approximations) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1758">G-1758</span> | Secant line | A straight line through two points of a curve; as the second point slides towards the first, it turns into the tangent line, whose slope is the derivative. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#1-overview) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-2249">G-2249</span> | Second derivative | The derivative of the derivative; it measures how the curve bends. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#2-the-second-derivative-how-a-curve-bends) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-2257">G-2257</span> | Second derivative test | A test that tells what a flat point (where $f' = 0$) is from the sign of the second derivative: $f'' > 0$ a local minimum, $f'' < 0$ a local maximum, $f'' = 0$ no conclusion. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#41-curving-up-the-slope-keeps-increasing) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1759">G-1759</span> | Second moment $v_t$ | In Adam, the running average (EWMA) of the squared gradient, an estimate of its mean square; dividing the step by $\sqrt{v_t}$ gives each weight its own step size, as in RMSProp. | [Note DL-038](DL/03-optimizers/DL-038-adam/DL-038-adam.md#4-the-update-rule) | Adam |
| <span id="G-1760">G-1760</span> | Second partial derivative | A partial derivative taken of a partial derivative, such as $\partial^2 f/\partial x^2$; it measures how the slope along one input changes, that is, how the surface bends. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#41-second-partial-derivatives) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-2253">G-2253</span> | Second partial derivative test | A test that tells what a stationary point of a function of two inputs is from one number, $D = f_{xx}f_{yy} - f_{xy}^2$: $D > 0$ a minimum or maximum (by the sign of $f_{xx}$), $D < 0$ a saddle point, $D = 0$ no answer. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#53-curvature-what-the-hessian-says-about-the-shape) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1761">G-1761</span> | Second-order condition | A test of whether a function is bowl-shaped (convex) by its curvature: a twice-differentiable function is convex exactly when its Hessian is positive semi-definite everywhere (with one input, when $f'' \ge 0$ everywhere). | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#43-second-order-condition-in-several-variables) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-2157">G-2157</span> | Selection bias | The optimism of a score that was picked as the best of many noisy scores: the winner partly won by luck on the same folds, so it looks a little better than it will on new data. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#74-the-best-score-of-a-grid-is-a-little-lucky) | Grid and random search |
| <span id="G-1762">G-1762</span> | SelectKBest | scikit-learn class that gives every column a score and keeps the `k` best, so only the most useful features go into the model. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#56-step-4-feature-selection) | Feature selection |
| <span id="G-1763">G-1763</span> | Self-attention (intra-attention) | Attention in which the words of one sequence attend to each other (the queries, keys and values all come from that sequence); it gives each word a new vector that depends on the words around it. | [Note DL-077](DL/06-transformers/DL-077-why-self-attention/DL-077-why-self-attention.md#1-overview) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-1765">G-1765</span> | Self-normalising | Keeping the activations of every layer at mean 0 and standard deviation 1 without a separate normalisation step. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#62-selu) | Leaky ReLU, PReLU, ELU and SELU |
| <span id="G-1766">G-1766</span> | SELU | Scaled exponential linear unit: an activation that is ELU with $\alpha \approx 1.6733$, multiplied by $\lambda \approx 1.0507$; these constants make a network self-normalising, keeping each layer's outputs near mean 0 and standard deviation 1. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#62-selu) | Leaky ReLU, PReLU, ELU and SELU |
| <span id="G-1767">G-1767</span> | Semester | One of two six-month halves of a year. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#58-semester) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-1768">G-1768</span> | Semi-supervised learning | Learning from a few labelled rows and many unlabelled ones: the algorithm labels the rest from the few labels, which saves the cost of labelling everything by hand. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#4-semi-supervised-learning) | Semi-supervised learning |
| <span id="G-2275">G-2275</span> | Sensitivity (of a model) | How much a model's prediction changes when its input changes a little; smaller weights make it lower, so the decision boundary is smoother. | [Note DL-026](DL/02-training/DL-026-regularization-in-dl/DL-026-regularization-in-dl.md#77-how-strong-should-the-penalty-be) | L1 and L2 regularisation in neural networks |
| <span id="G-1769">G-1769</span> | Sentiment analysis | Deciding whether a text expresses a positive or negative opinion. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#71-sentiment-analysis) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-1770">G-1770</span> | Separator | The character between values on a line, such as `,` or a tab. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#6-separators-and-column-names-sep-names) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-1771">G-1771</span> | Sequence-to-sequence (seq2seq) model | A model whose input and output are both sequences, such as a translator from English to Hindi; in RNNs it is the many-to-many type. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#5-many-to-many) | Sequence-to-sequence (encoder-decoder) |
| <span id="G-1772">G-1772</span> | Sequence-to-sequence (seq2seq) task | A task with a sequence as input and a sequence as output, possibly of different lengths, such as translation. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#3-sequence-to-sequence-problems) | Sequence-to-sequence (encoder-decoder) |
| <span id="G-1774">G-1774</span> | Sequential data | Data that comes as an ordered series of pieces whose order matters, such as text or a time series; models read it one piece after another, in order. | [Note DL-055](DL/05-rnn/DL-055-why-rnn/DL-055-why-rnn.md#1-overview) | Sequential data |
| <span id="G-1775">G-1775</span> | Sequential learning | Training the base models one after another, each learning from the mistakes of the ones before; this is how boosting works. | [Note ML-113](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-113-bagging-vs-boosting/ML-113-bagging-vs-boosting.md#3-difference-2-parallel-against-sequential-learning) | Boosting |
| <span id="G-1776">G-1776</span> | Sequential model | A Keras model whose layers form one stack, each feeding the next. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#4-building-a-network-in-keras) | Keras workflow |
| <span id="G-1777">G-1777</span> | Sequential training | Training in which step $t$ must wait for step $t-1$, as in an RNN; it cannot be spread over many processors. | [Note DL-071](DL/06-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers/DL-071-introduction-to-transformers.md#4-why-transformers-were-needed) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-1778">G-1778</span> | Series | pandas' one-column structure: a list of values with an index; a DataFrame is several Series side by side. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#10-one-column-as-a-series-squeeze) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-1779">G-1779</span> | Server | A computer that is always on and that users reach over the internet; a model runs on one so that other people can use it. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#2-development-and-production) | Deployment |
| <span id="G-1780">G-1780</span> | set_output | Method that, called as `set_output(transform="pandas")`, makes a scikit-learn transformer return a pandas DataFrame with column names instead of a bare NumPy array. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#62-a-dataframe-instead-of-an-array) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-1781">G-1781</span> | set_params | Method that changes a model's settings after it is created. | [Note ML-105](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-105-random-forest-hyperparameters/ML-105-random-forest-hyperparameters.md#5-the-general-hyperparameters) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-1782">G-1782</span> | SGDClassifier | scikit-learn's linear classifier that learns its weights by stochastic gradient descent (SGD), small steps from one row at a time, with a choice of loss (perceptron, log loss, hinge, ...). | [Note DL-006](DL/01-basics/DL-006-perceptron-loss/DL-006-perceptron-loss.md#8-one-perceptron-many-models) | Perceptron loss |
| <span id="G-1783">G-1783</span> | SGDRegressor | A scikit-learn model that does linear regression with stochastic gradient descent, step by step, so it can also learn from data arriving in small pieces. | [Note ML-005](ML/01-foundations/ML-005-online-learning/ML-005-online-learning.md#41-scikit-learns-partial_fit) | Online learning |
| <span id="G-1784">G-1784</span> | Shadow price | The Lagrange multiplier read in economic terms: how much the best value would improve per extra unit of a limited resource, so it tells how much loosening a constraint is worth. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#42-what-the-multiplier-measures) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-1785">G-1785</span> | Shallow decision tree | A decision tree with a small maximum depth: high bias, low variance; boosting uses such trees as its base models. | [Note ML-113](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-113-bagging-vs-boosting/ML-113-bagging-vs-boosting.md#21-boosting-high-bias-low-variance-models) | Boosting |
| <span id="G-1787">G-1787</span> | Shape | The number of items along each axis of a tensor, such as (2, 3) for a matrix with 2 rows and 3 columns; it tells how the data is laid out. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#4-rank-axes-shape-and-size) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-1786">G-1786</span> | Shape rule | The rule for which matrices can be multiplied: an $m \times n$ matrix times an $n \times p$ matrix gives an $m \times p$ matrix, so the inner sizes must match. | [Note MA-054](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition/MA-054-matrix-multiplication-as-composition.md#71-the-shape-rule) | Matrix multiplication as composition |
| <span id="G-1788">G-1788</span> | Shapiro-Wilk test | A statistical test of whether data follows a normal distribution: it takes the values and returns a statistic and a p-value, and a small p-value says the data is unlikely to be normal. | [Note MA-042](MA/04-inference/MA-042-one-sample-t-test/MA-042-one-sample-t-test.md#5-checking-normality-the-shapiro-wilk-test) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1789">G-1789</span> | Shear | A linear transformation that keeps $\hat{\imath}$ fixed and slants $\hat{\jmath}$, so horizontal lines slide sideways, more the higher they are. | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#6-reading-a-matrix-as-a-picture) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-1790">G-1790</span> | Shift right | Putting `<start>` in front of the target sentence to form the decoder's input, so each position predicts the next word. | [Note DL-084](DL/06-transformers/DL-084-transformer-decoder/DL-084-transformer-decoder.md#51-shifting-right) | Transformer decoder |
| <span id="G-1791">G-1791</span> | Shifting | Adding or subtracting the same scalar to every component of a vector, such as $[2, 3] + 1 = [3, 4]$; it moves the point to a new place. | [Note MA-049](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations/MA-049-magnitude-distance-and-scalar-operations.md#41-adding-or-subtracting-a-scalar-shifting) | Vector magnitude, distance and scalar operations |
| <span id="G-1792">G-1792</span> | Short-term and long-term contributions | When an RNN learns, the signal that adjusts its weights (the BPTT gradient) is a sum of two kinds of parts: short-term ones measure how the loss changes through recent inputs, long-term ones through distant inputs. The long-term ones shrink towards 0, so the RNN fails to learn from distant inputs. | [Note DL-060](DL/05-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn.md#41-short-term-and-long-term-terms) | Long-term dependency problem |
| <span id="G-1793">G-1793</span> | Short-term context | What is happening in the sequence right now; an LSTM keeps it apart from the long-term context, which holds what matters for the whole sequence. | [Note DL-061](DL/05-rnn/DL-061-lstm/DL-061-lstm.md#4-how-we-read-a-story-two-kinds-of-context) | LSTM (long short-term memory) |
| <span id="G-1794">G-1794</span> | Shortcut variance formula | A quicker way to compute a variance: the mean of the squares minus the square of the mean, $\mathrm{Var}(X) = E[X^2] - (E[X])^2$; it gives the same number as the definition. | [Note MA-012](MA/02-probability/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance.md#43-the-shortcut-formula) | Expected value and variance of a random variable |
| <span id="G-1796">G-1796</span> | Shrinkage | The pulling of a model's coefficients towards 0 by a penalty, as when Ridge's alpha grows; it makes the model rely less on any one feature and can reduce overfitting. | [Note ML-062](ML/06-regression/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition.md#43-many-features-the-diabetes-data) | Ridge regression |
| <span id="G-1795">G-1795</span> | Shrinkage (boosting) | Scaling down each base model's contribution so the ensemble learns in small steps and overfits less. | [Note ML-112](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters.md#42-why-a-smaller-alpha-slows-learning) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1797">G-1797</span> | Shuffling | Putting the rows in a new random order before each epoch, so the mini-batches differ from one epoch to the next. | [Note ML-059](ML/06-regression/ML-059-mini-batch-gradient-descent/ML-059-mini-batch-gradient-descent.md#3-how-it-works) | Mini-batch gradient descent |
| <span id="G-1798">G-1798</span> | Sigmoid function | An S-shaped function that squashes any number into the range 0 to 1, $\sigma(z) = 1/(1 + e^{-z})$; it turns a score into a probability, as in logistic regression. | [Note ML-071](ML/07-classification/ML-071-sigmoid-function/ML-071-sigmoid-function.md#4-the-sigmoid-function) | Sigmoid function |
| <span id="G-1799">G-1799</span> | Sigmoid kernel | A way to score how alike two points are, built from an S-shaped curve, $\tanh(\gamma\thinspace x \cdot x' + r)$ (a kernel). Like other kernels, it lets an SVM draw a curved boundary without computing new features. | [Note ML-089](ML/07-classification/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition/ML-089-kernel-trick-intuition.md#32-the-feature-map-and-the-kernel) | Kernel trick |
| <span id="G-1800">G-1800</span> | Sign function | Returns +1 for a positive number and -1 for a negative one; AdaBoost uses it to turn the weighted vote of its stumps into a class. | [Note ML-109](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-109-adaboost-intuition/ML-109-adaboost-intuition.md#5-the-final-prediction-a-weighted-vote) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-1801">G-1801</span> | Significance level ($\alpha$) | The chance, fixed before the test, of wrongly declaring an effect when there is none (rejecting $H_0$ when it is actually true); usually 0.05. | [Note MA-039](MA/04-inference/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test.md#5-how-rare-is-too-rare-the-significance-level) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-2266">G-2266</span> | SiLU (Swish) | A smooth version of ReLU that multiplies each input by its sigmoid, $z\thinspace\sigma(z)$; it is used as an activation and is close to GELU. SiLU stands for sigmoid linear unit; it is also called Swish. | [Note DL-028](DL/02-training/DL-028-relu-variants/DL-028-relu-variants.md#63-gelu-and-silu-smooth-versions-of-relu) | Leaky ReLU, PReLU, ELU and SELU |
| <span id="G-1805">G-1805</span> | Similarity | How alike two data points are, measured as a distance: the closer two points are, the more similar; instance-based methods such as KNN use it to find the nearest examples. | [Note ML-006](ML/01-foundations/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based/ML-006-instance-vs-model-based.md#31-how-it-works) | Instance-based learning |
| <span id="G-1802">G-1802</span> | Similarity measure | A number that says how alike two vectors are, such as the cosine similarity; ML uses it to compare data points or words. | [Note MA-050](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity/MA-050-dot-product-and-cosine-similarity.md#62-definition-and-range) | Cosine similarity |
| <span id="G-1804">G-1804</span> | Similarity score | A number that says how well the leftover errors (residuals) in one node of an XGBoost regression tree agree: (sum of residuals)$^2$ / (number of residuals + $\lambda$). The tree keeps the split that raises it the most. | [Note ML-118](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-118-xgboost-regression/ML-118-xgboost-regression.md#4-the-similarity-score) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1803">G-1803</span> | Similarity score (classification) | The same score for XGBoost classification trees: how well the leftover errors (residuals) in a node agree, (sum of residuals)$^2$ / ($\sum p(1-p) + \lambda$) with $p$ the previous probabilities. As in regression, it is used to choose splits. | [Note ML-119](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-119-xgboost-classification/ML-119-xgboost-classification.md#5-the-similarity-score-for-classification) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1806">G-1806</span> | Simple (elementary) event | An event with exactly one outcome. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#41-simple-events) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-1807">G-1807</span> | Simple cell | A nerve cell in the brain's first visual area (V1) that responds to an edge of one orientation at one place in a small patch of the view (its receptive field). | [Note DL-041](DL/04-cnn/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex/DL-041-cnn-vs-visual-cortex.md#51-simple-cells) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-1808">G-1808</span> | Simple linear regression | Linear regression with one input column: it fits a straight line $y = mx + b$ to predict the output from that one feature. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#1-overview) | Simple linear regression |
| <span id="G-1809">G-1809</span> | SimpleImputer | scikit-learn's class that fills missing values, by default with the column's mean. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#41-fever-fill-the-missing-values) | Simple imputation (mean, median, mode, constant) |
| <span id="G-2259">G-2259</span> | Simplex algorithm | A way to find the best corner of the region allowed by straight-line limits (solving a linear program): walk from one corner (vertex) to a neighbouring, better one until no neighbour is better. | [Note MA-068](MA/07-optimisation/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming.md#22-the-answer-is-at-a-corner) | Linear and quadratic programming |
| <span id="G-1810">G-1810</span> | Simulated annealing | An optimisation method named after slowly cooling metal, which then settles into a stable, low-energy state: it lowers a temperature step by step so the search settles down; a learning schedule that lowers the learning rate resembles it. | [Note ML-058](ML/06-regression/ML-058-stochastic-gradient-descent/ML-058-stochastic-gradient-descent.md#6-learning-schedules) | Stochastic gradient descent |
| <span id="G-1811">G-1811</span> | Single linkage | In hierarchical clustering, a way to measure the distance between two clusters: the distance of their closest pair of points; the two closest clusters are merged next. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#8-linkage-the-distance-between-two-clusters) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-1812">G-1812</span> | Singular value ($\sigma_i$) | A stretch factor of a matrix: the length of $A\mathbf v_i$; never negative, listed largest first. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#33-the-ellipse) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1813">G-1813</span> | Singular value decomposition (SVD) | A way to write any matrix, square or not, as three simple steps: turn, stretch by the singular values, turn ($A = U\Sigma V^{\mathsf T}$: orthogonal, diagonal, orthogonal; a factorisation). ML uses it for PCA and to compress data. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#1-overview) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1814">G-1814</span> | Singular value equation | The equation $A\mathbf v_i = \sigma_i\mathbf u_i$: the matrix sends each input direction $\mathbf v_i$ to its output direction $\mathbf u_i$, stretched by the singular value $\sigma_i$; it is used to compute each $\mathbf u_i$. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#34-the-singular-value-equation) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1815">G-1815</span> | Sinusoidal positional encoding | The fixed position code of the original transformer (Vaswani et al.): each position gets a vector of sine waves (even dimensions) and cosine waves (odd dimensions) of different wavelengths, added to the word's embedding so the model knows word order. | [Note DL-079](DL/06-transformers/DL-079-positional-encoding/DL-079-positional-encoding.md#62-the-values) | Positional encoding |
| <span id="G-1816">G-1816</span> | Size | The total number of items in an array: the numbers in its shape multiplied together. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#4-rank-axes-shape-and-size) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-1817">G-1817</span> | Skewness | A number for how lopsided a distribution is: 0 symmetric, positive right tail, negative left tail. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#10-skewness) | Skewness |
| <span id="G-1819">G-1819</span> | Skip connection through time | A connection from a hidden state several steps back directly to the present; it gives the gradient a shorter path, so it vanishes less. | [Note DL-060](DL/05-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn.md#5-fixing-the-vanishing-gradient) | Long-term dependency problem |
| <span id="G-1820">G-1820</span> | Slack (ξ) | A number for each training point in the soft-margin SVM that says how far the point lies on the wrong side of its own margin line, in margin units: 0 on the correct side, above 1 on the wrong side of $\pi$ (slack $\xi_i$). The loss adds up the slacks, so each violation has a cost. | [Note ML-088](ML/07-classification/ML-088-svm-soft-margin/ML-088-svm-soft-margin.md#41-the-new-term) | Hinge loss and soft margin |
| <span id="G-1821">G-1821</span> | Slater's condition | There is at least one point that meets every inequality limit with room to spare (strictly). Together with convexity, this guarantees that the related helper (dual) problem gives the same best value as the original (strong duality). | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#62-what-convexity-guarantees) | Convex sets and convex optimisation |
| <span id="G-1822">G-1822</span> | slice(0, 10) | Python object meaning positions 0 up to, not including, 10. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#55-step-3-scaling) | ML pipelines |
| <span id="G-1823">G-1823</span> | Slope | How much the output changes for one unit of change in the input; $m$ in $y = mx + b$. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#32-if-the-data-were-perfectly-linear) | Simple linear regression |
| <span id="G-1824">G-1824</span> | Slow convergence | Reaching a good solution only after very many epochs. | [Note DL-029](DL/02-training/DL-029-weight-initialization/DL-029-weight-initialization.md#1-overview) | Weight initialisation |
| <span id="G-1825">G-1825</span> | SMOTE | Synthetic Minority Over-sampling Technique: it grows the minority class until the classes are equal by making new rows on the line between a minority row and one of its minority neighbours. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#7-smote) | SMOTE |
| <span id="G-1826">G-1826</span> | Soft assignment | Sharing a point among clusters by probabilities instead of giving it to one. | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#32-the-standard-terms) | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) |
| <span id="G-1827">G-1827</span> | Soft thresholding | Moving a value towards 0 by a fixed amount, and setting it to 0 if it would cross 0; this is how Lasso sets coefficients to exactly 0. | [Note ML-067](ML/06-regression/ML-067-lasso-sparsity/ML-067-lasso-sparsity.md#5-the-dead-zone) | Lasso regression |
| <span id="G-1828">G-1828</span> | Soft voting | Predicting the class with the highest average predicted probability across the base models. | [Note ML-097](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-097-voting-classifier/ML-097-voting-classifier.md#32-soft-voting) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-1829">G-1829</span> | Soft-margin SVM | The SVM that allows points inside the margin or on the wrong side, at a cost controlled by C. | [Note ML-088](ML/07-classification/ML-088-svm-soft-margin/ML-088-svm-soft-margin.md#1-overview) | Hinge loss and soft margin |
| <span id="G-1830">G-1830</span> | Softmax function | A function that turns a list of scores into probabilities that add up to 1: raise $e$ to each score and divide by the total, $e^{z_k} / \sum_j e^{z_j}$; it gives one probability per class in multi-class models. | [Note ML-078](ML/07-classification/ML-078-softmax-regression/ML-078-softmax-regression.md#22-the-formula) | Softmax regression |
| <span id="G-1831">G-1831</span> | Softmax gradient | How much each softmax output changes when one input score changes: $\partial\alpha_i/\partial s_i = \alpha_i(1 - \alpha_i)$ and $\partial\alpha_i/\partial s_j = -\alpha_i\alpha_j$; training changes the scores through it, and it is near 0 everywhere when the softmax is saturated. | [Note DL-075](DL/06-transformers/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention/DL-075-scaled-dot-product-attention.md#5-why-a-wide-spread-is-a-problem-the-softmax-saturates) | Scaled dot-product attention |
| <span id="G-1832">G-1832</span> | Softmax output layer | An output layer with one node per class whose outputs are probabilities adding up to 1. | [Note DL-012](DL/01-basics/DL-012-mnist-ann/DL-012-mnist-ann.md#42-the-architecture) | ANN for classification |
| <span id="G-1833">G-1833</span> | Softmax regression | Logistic regression extended to any number of classes using the softmax function. | [Note ML-078](ML/07-classification/ML-078-softmax-regression/ML-078-softmax-regression.md#1-overview) | Softmax regression |
| <span id="G-1834">G-1834</span> | Softplus | A smooth curve, $\ln(1 + e^z)$, that bends upward (convex) and whose slope at every point is the sigmoid. | [Note MA-067](MA/07-optimisation/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions/MA-067-convex-sets-and-functions.md#41-curving-up-the-slope-keeps-increasing) | Convex and non-convex loss |
| <span id="G-1835">G-1835</span> | Software integration | Building a model into the software that users use. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#8-software-integration) | Software integration |
| <span id="G-1836">G-1836</span> | Solver | The method a model uses to find its best settings during training. | [Note ML-080](ML/07-classification/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters/ML-080-logistic-hyperparameters.md#3-the-solver) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1837">G-1837</span> | Spam classifier | A program that decides whether an email is spam or not. | [Note ML-001](ML/01-foundations/ML-001-what-is-ml/ML-001-what-is-ml.md#41-when-the-rules-keep-changing-spam-filtering) | Machine learning |
| <span id="G-1838">G-1838</span> | Span | The set of all linear combinations of some vectors: every point reachable by scaling and adding them, such as the whole plane for $\hat{\imath}$ and $\hat{\jmath}$. | [Note MA-052](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis.md#6-span) | Linear combinations, span and basis |
| <span id="G-1846">G-1846</span> | Sparse | Mostly made of zeros, such as a feature map whose values are almost all 0, or a model whose coefficients are mostly exactly 0. | [Note DL-052](DL/04-cnn/DL-052-visualizing-cnn/DL-052-visualizing-cnn.md#7-feature-maps-get-sparser-with-depth) | Visualising what a CNN learns |
| <span id="G-1839">G-1839</span> | Sparse categorical cross-entropy | The same loss as categorical cross-entropy, but it takes the labels as plain integers (such as the digits 0 to 9) instead of one-hot vectors, so the labels need no encoding. | [Note DL-012](DL/01-basics/DL-012-mnist-ann/DL-012-mnist-ann.md#51-the-loss-for-many-classes) | Categorical and sparse categorical cross-entropy |
| <span id="G-1840">G-1840</span> | Sparse data | Two senses: a table that is mostly zeros (as after one-hot encoding); or, in many dimensions, a space where most regions hold no points. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#8-max-abs-scaling) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-1841">G-1841</span> | Sparse feature | A feature whose values are mostly zero, such as "studied at an IIT". | [Note DL-036](DL/03-optimizers/DL-036-adagrad/DL-036-adagrad.md#3-when-adagrad-helps) | AdaGrad |
| <span id="G-1842">G-1842</span> | Sparse interactions | Each output of a convolution depends only on a small window of the input, because the filter is small; so a convolution needs far fewer weights than a fully connected layer. | [Note DL-046](DL/04-cnn/DL-046-cnn-vs-ann/DL-046-cnn-vs-ann.md#4-how-they-are-similar-a-filter-is-a-node) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-1843">G-1843</span> | Sparse matrix | A table stored as only its non-zero entries, to save memory. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#73-creating-the-encoder) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-1844">G-1844</span> | Sparse model | A model in which many coefficients are exactly 0; those features have no effect and can be dropped, so the model also does feature selection. | [Note ML-066](ML/06-regression/ML-066-lasso-regression/ML-066-lasso-regression.md#4-feature-selection) | Lasso regression |
| <span id="G-1845">G-1845</span> | Sparse representation | A representation where most values are 0, such as a one-hot vector over a 10,000-word vocabulary (9,999 zeros and one 1); it wastes space compared with a dense one. | [Note DL-057](DL/05-rnn/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis.md#61-sparse-and-dense-representations) | Word embeddings |
| <span id="G-1847">G-1847</span> | sparse_output | The `OneHotEncoder` setting that picks the output format: `False` returns a normal NumPy array instead of a sparse matrix (a table stored as only its non-zero entries). | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#73-creating-the-encoder) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-1849">G-1849</span> | Sparsity | Having many coefficients exactly equal to 0. | [Note ML-067](ML/06-regression/ML-067-lasso-sparsity/ML-067-lasso-sparsity.md#1-overview) | Lasso regression |
| <span id="G-1848">G-1848</span> | Sparsity $S$ | The probability that a feature is 0 in an observation; high sparsity means the feature is rarely active. | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#81-the-toy-model) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-1850">G-1850</span> | Sparsity-aware split finding | XGBoost's way of handling missing values: at each split it tries sending them to the left and to the right side and keeps the side with the higher gain. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#82-sparsity-aware-split-finding) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1851">G-1851</span> | Spatial arrangement | Where each pixel sits relative to the others; an ANN that flattens the image loses it, while a CNN's filters use it by looking at neighbouring pixels. | [Note DL-040](DL/04-cnn/DL-040-cnn-intuition/DL-040-cnn-intuition.md#44-problem-3-the-spatial-arrangement-is-lost) | Convolutional neural network (CNN) |
| <span id="G-2209">G-2209</span> | Specificity | Of all items that really are negative, the fraction the model cleared: TN / (TN + FP). | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#32-the-formula) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-1852">G-1852</span> | Spectral norm | The largest stretch a matrix gives to any input of length 1, $\lVert M\rVert_2 = \sigma_1$; it is one way to measure the size of a matrix. | [Note MA-059](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-059-low-rank-approximation/MA-059-low-rank-approximation.md#41-measuring-the-size-of-a-matrix) | Low-rank approximation (truncated SVD) |
| <span id="G-1853">G-1853</span> | splitter | A decision tree setting for how each split's threshold is chosen: "best" searches every threshold, "random" draws thresholds at random, which adds randomness that pays off when many trees are averaged. | [Note ML-092](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters/ML-092-decision-tree-hyperparameters.md#42-splitter-best-or-random) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1855">G-1855</span> | Splitting | Dividing a node's rows into parts according to a question. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#4-terminology) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1854">G-1854</span> | Splitting criterion (threshold) | The value a numerical question in a decision tree compares against, such as petal length $\le$ 2.45; the tree keeps the threshold with the highest information gain. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#9-splitting-on-a-numerical-feature) | Decision trees |
| <span id="G-1856">G-1856</span> | Spyder | A Python code editor that shows variables and tables in memory. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#24-navigator-and-spyder) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1857">G-1857</span> | SQL (Structured Query Language) | The language for asking a database for data. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#3-what-sql-is) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-1858">G-1858</span> | SQLAlchemy | A Python library that connects to many kinds of database; pandas supports it fully. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#7-connecting-python-to-the-database) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-1859">G-1859</span> | SQLite | A database stored in a single file, built into Python, needing no server. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#6-getting-an-sql-database-ready) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-1860">G-1860</span> | Square root transform | Replacing each value with $\sqrt{x}$; it pulls a right tail in, more gently than the log. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#63-square-root-transform) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-1861">G-1861</span> | Square transform | Replacing each value with $x^2$; used for left-skewed data. | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#62-square-transform) | Function transformer |
| <span id="G-1862">G-1862</span> | Square-root rule | A rough starting value for k, the number of neighbours in KNN: about $\sqrt{n}$, where $n$ is the number of training rows, made odd to avoid ties. | [Note ML-085](ML/07-classification/ML-085-knn/ML-085-knn.md#4-choosing-k) | K-nearest neighbours |
