| <span id="G-1863">G-1863</span> | Squared loss (L2 loss) | The squared difference between target and prediction, $(y - \hat{y})^2$, used as the error a model lowers during training, so large errors cost much more than small ones; averaged over the rows it is the mean squared error. | [Note DL-014](DL/01-basics/DL-014-dl-loss-functions/DL-014-dl-loss-functions.md#51-the-formula) | Loss functions in deep learning |
| <span id="G-1864">G-1864</span> | squared_error | The default rule `DecisionTreeRegressor` uses to judge splits: it splits by mean squared error, and each leaf predicts the mean of its rows. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#43-trying-every-threshold) | Regression trees |
| <span id="G-1865">G-1865</span> | Stacked (deep) LSTM | Several LSTM layers on top of each other, each feeding its outputs to the next; the extra layers give the model more room and capacity, with higher layers working closer to the meaning. | [Note DL-068](DL/06-transformers/DL-068-encoder-decoder/DL-068-encoder-decoder.md#82-deep-stacked-lstms) | Deep (stacked) RNNs |
| <span id="G-1866">G-1866</span> | Stacking | An ensemble in which the base models' predictions become the input columns of a new dataset, and a final meta-model learns how best to combine them. | [Note ML-121](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-121-stacking-blending/ML-121-stacking-blending.md#1-overview) | Stacking and blending |
| <span id="G-1867">G-1867</span> | Stage-wise additive model | A model built as a sum of base models added one at a time, one per stage; boosting builds this kind of model, each new part learning from the mistakes of the ones before. | [Note ML-109](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-109-adaboost-intuition/ML-109-adaboost-intuition.md#3-a-stage-wise-additive-model) | Boosting |
| <span id="G-1868">G-1868</span> | staged_score | A method that gives an ensemble's score after each added stage. | [Note ML-112](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters/ML-112-adaboost-hyperparameters.md#43-the-trade-off-with-n_estimators) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1869">G-1869</span> | Standard basis ($\hat{\imath}$, $\hat{\jmath}$) | The unit vectors along the axes, $[1, 0]$ and $[0, 1]$ in 2D; every vector is a sum of scaled copies of them, and its coordinates are those scales. | [Note MA-052](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis.md#4-coordinates-are-scalars-basis-vectors) | Linear combinations, span and basis |
| <span id="G-1871">G-1871</span> | Standard deviation | How far values typically lie from the mean; divide by $n - 1$ for a sample (pandas default) or by $n$ for a population (NumPy default). | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#71-count-mean-standard-deviation-minimum-and-maximum) | Variance |
| <span id="G-1870">G-1870</span> | Standard deviation of a random variable | The square root of a random variable's variance, in the units of $X$; it says how far values typically fall from the expected value. | [Note MA-012](MA/02-probability/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance.md#41-variance-term-by-term) | Expected value and variance of a random variable |
| <span id="G-1872">G-1872</span> | Standard error | How much a statistic, such as the sample mean, changes from one sample to the next, so how precise it is as an estimate. It is the standard deviation of the sampling distribution; for the mean, $\sigma/\sqrt{n}$. | [Note MA-033](MA/04-inference/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt/MA-033-sampling-distribution-and-clt.md#42-mean-and-variance-of-the-sample-means) | Sampling distribution and standard error |
| <span id="G-1873">G-1873</span> | Standard normal distribution | The normal distribution with mean 0 and standard deviation 1, written $Z \sim N(0, 1)$; any normal variable can be converted to it with z-scores, so one table of probabilities serves every normal distribution. | [Note MA-025](MA/03-distributions/MA-025-standard-normal-and-z-table/MA-025-standard-normal-and-z-table.md#2-the-standard-normal-distribution) | Standard normal and the z-table |
| <span id="G-1874">G-1874</span> | Standardization | Scaling a column to mean 0 and standard deviation 1 (subtract the mean, divide by the standard deviation), so features measured on different scales become comparable. | [Note ML-023](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-023-standardization/ML-023-standardization.md#41-the-idea-how-many-standard-deviations-from-the-mean) | Standardization |
| <span id="G-1875">G-1875</span> | standardize | The `PowerTransformer` parameter (on by default) that rescales the output to mean 0 and standard deviation 1. | [Note ML-030](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-030-power-transformer/ML-030-power-transformer.md#65-yeo-johnson) | Power transformer |
| <span id="G-1876">G-1876</span> | StandardScaler | scikit-learn's class that standardizes columns: `fit` learns each column's mean and standard deviation from the training set, and `transform` applies $(x - \bar{x})/\sigma$. | [Note ML-023](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-023-standardization/ML-023-standardization.md#63-fit-on-the-training-set-transform-both) | Standardization |
| <span id="G-1877">G-1877</span> | Static embedding | An embedding that gives a word the same vector in every sentence, so "bank" gets one vector in both "money bank" and "river bank"; self-attention fixes this by making the vector depend on context. | [Note DL-073](DL/06-transformers/DL-073-what-is-self-attention/DL-073-what-is-self-attention.md#1-overview) | Contextual embeddings |
| <span id="G-1878">G-1878</span> | Static model | A model that learns nothing new after deployment. | [Note ML-004](ML/01-foundations/ML-004-batch-learning/ML-004-batch-learning.md#41-models-go-stale) | Batch (offline) learning |
| <span id="G-1879">G-1879</span> | Stationary point | A point where a function is flat, its slope is zero (the derivative, or every partial derivative, is zero); it is a minimum, a maximum or a saddle point. | [Note MA-065](MA/07-optimisation/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions.md#32-why-convexity-matters-one-minimum) | Convex and non-convex loss |
| <span id="G-1880">G-1880</span> | Statistic | A number computed from a sample, such as the sample mean $\bar{x}$; it is used as an estimate of the matching number for the whole population (a parameter). | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#42-parameters-and-statistics) | Population, sample, parameter and statistic |
| <span id="G-1881">G-1881</span> | Statistical hypothesis test | A method that uses sample data to decide whether a claim about a whole population (a hypothesis about a population parameter) is supported strongly enough; it is a form of statistical inference. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#23-definition) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-1882">G-1882</span> | Statistical moments | Averages of distances from the mean raised to a power: mean, variance, skewness, kurtosis. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#21-statistical-moments) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-1884">G-1884</span> | Statistics | The branch of mathematics for collecting, analysing, interpreting and presenting data. | [Note MA-004](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-004-what-is-statistics/MA-004-what-is-statistics.md#2-what-statistics-is) | Inferential statistics |
| <span id="G-1885">G-1885</span> | statsmodels | A Python library for statistical models and tests, such as the VIF and the Durbin-Watson statistic used to check the assumptions of linear regression. | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#32-checking-it) | Assumptions of linear regression |
| <span id="G-1886">G-1886</span> | Status code | A number saying how a request went: 200 OK, 401, 404, 500. | [Note ML-016](ML/02-getting-data/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api/ML-016-fetching-data-from-api.md#6-fetching-one-page-in-python) | APIs |
| <span id="G-1887">G-1887</span> | Status quo | Another name for the null hypothesis $H_0$: the current state of things, which the test keeps unless the data gives strong evidence against it. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#4-the-alternative-hypothesis) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-1888">G-1888</span> | Steepest ascent | The direction in which $f$ increases fastest: the direction of the gradient; gradient descent steps the opposite way to go downhill fastest. | [Note MA-062](MA/06-calculus/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients/MA-062-partial-derivatives-and-gradients.md#43-the-slope-in-any-direction) | Partial derivatives and gradients |
| <span id="G-1889">G-1889</span> | Step function | A function that outputs 1 for positive inputs and 0 otherwise; the classic perceptron uses it to turn its score into a class. | [Note ML-069](ML/07-classification/ML-069-perceptron-trick/ML-069-perceptron-trick.md#72-predicting) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-1893">G-1893</span> | Stochastic | Involving randomness, as in stochastic gradient descent, which picks rows at random. | [Note ML-058](ML/06-regression/ML-058-stochastic-gradient-descent/ML-058-stochastic-gradient-descent.md#3-how-stochastic-gradient-descent-works) | Stochastic gradient descent |
| <span id="G-1891">G-1891</span> | Stochastic error | A random, unmeasurable influence that scatters data around its trend. | [Note ML-049](ML/06-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression/ML-049-simple-linear-regression.md#33-why-real-data-is-not-on-a-line) | Simple linear regression |
| <span id="G-1892">G-1892</span> | Stochastic gradient descent (SGD) | Gradient descent that uses one random row for every update, so each update is cheap and many updates happen per epoch, at the cost of a noisier path. | [Note ML-058](ML/06-regression/ML-058-stochastic-gradient-descent/ML-058-stochastic-gradient-descent.md#3-how-stochastic-gradient-descent-works) | Stochastic gradient descent |
| <span id="G-1894">G-1894</span> | Stopping rule | The condition that ends a training loop: a fixed number of loops, or convergence. | [Note DL-005](DL/01-basics/DL-005-perceptron-trick/DL-005-perceptron-trick.md#6-when-to-stop) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-1895">G-1895</span> | str dtype | The pandas 3 type for text columns, replacing `object`. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#3-the-data) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-1896">G-1896</span> | str.extract | The pandas method that returns the part of each value matching a regular expression. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#51-the-number-part) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-1897">G-1897</span> | Strength of a relationship | How closely the points follow a straight line; measured by $\lvert r \rvert$. | [Note MA-009](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation/MA-009-covariance-and-correlation.md#32-the-flaw-covariance-depends-on-the-scale) | Covariance and covariance matrix |
| <span id="G-1898">G-1898</span> | Strength of evidence | How strongly the data speaks against the starting claim of no effect ($H_0$); the rejection region approach does not measure it. | [Note MA-039](MA/04-inference/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test.md#11-the-weakness-of-the-rejection-region-approach) | Z-test and rejection regions |
| <span id="G-1899">G-1899</span> | Strictly convex function | A function whose curve lies strictly below the straight line (chord) joining any two different points of it; it has at most one minimum. | [Note MA-065](MA/07-optimisation/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions/MA-065-convex-and-non-convex-cost-functions.md#31-the-definition-as-a-formula) | Convex and non-convex loss |
| <span id="G-1900">G-1900</span> | Stride | The number of pixels the filter moves at each step; a stride larger than 1 makes the output smaller. | [Note DL-043](DL/04-cnn/DL-043-padding-and-strides/DL-043-padding-and-strides.md#51-what-a-stride-is) | Padding and strides |
| <span id="G-1901">G-1901</span> | Strided convolution | A convolution with a stride larger than 1, used to make the feature map smaller. | [Note DL-043](DL/04-cnn/DL-043-padding-and-strides/DL-043-padding-and-strides.md#51-what-a-stride-is) | Padding and strides |
| <span id="G-1902">G-1902</span> | Strike rate | A batter's runs per 100 balls faced. | [Note ML-044](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting.md#4-constructing-features-in-other-data) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-1903">G-1903</span> | Strong duality | The best lower bound given by a related helper problem (the dual problem) equals the true minimum of the original (primal) problem, so solving the dual solves the original; it holds for convex problems. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#62-weak-and-strong-duality) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-1904">G-1904</span> | Strong learner | A model with high accuracy. | [Note ML-109](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-109-adaboost-intuition/ML-109-adaboost-intuition.md#21-weak-learners) | Boosting |
| <span id="G-1905">G-1905</span> | Structure score | XGBoost's number for how good one tree's shape is: the best value of its loss-plus-penalty objective, $-\frac{1}{2}\sum_j G_j^2/(H_j + \lambda) + \gamma T$. Lower is better, so it is used to compare candidate splits. | [Note ML-120](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-120-xgboost-maths/ML-120-xgboost-maths.md#13-the-best-objective-and-the-similarity-score) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1906">G-1906</span> | Student's t-distribution | A bell-shaped distribution like the standard normal but with fatter tails. It is used in place of the standard normal when the population's $\sigma$ is unknown and estimated by the sample's $s$; it is the distribution of $(\bar{X} - \mu)/(s/\sqrt{n})$ and approaches the standard normal as $n$ grows. | [Note MA-037](MA/04-inference/MA-037-t-procedure/MA-037-t-procedure.md#5-students-t-distribution) | Student's t-distribution |
| <span id="G-1907">G-1907</span> | Study | In Optuna, one full search for the best settings: a collection of trials, each scoring one set of values, aimed at optimising the objective function. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#4-optunas-vocabulary) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-1908">G-1908</span> | Sub-layer | One of the two parts of an encoder block: multi-head attention or the feed-forward network. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#1-overview) | Transformer encoder |
| <span id="G-1909">G-1909</span> | Sub-network | The smaller network left after dropout switches some nodes off; it shares the full network's weights, and each training step trains a different one. | [Note DL-024](DL/02-training/DL-024-dropout/DL-024-dropout.md#41-switching-nodes-off) | Dropout |
| <span id="G-1910">G-1910</span> | Subgradient | A slope used at a corner of a function, where the ordinary derivative does not exist. | [Note DL-006](DL/01-basics/DL-006-perceptron-loss/DL-006-perceptron-loss.md#71-the-derivatives) | Perceptron loss |
| <span id="G-1911">G-1911</span> | Subscription | A model where customers pay a fixed amount every month (or year). | [Note ML-013](ML/01-foundations/ML-013-framing-ml-problem/ML-013-framing-ml-problem.md#3-the-running-example-netflix-revenue) | Framing an ML problem |
| <span id="G-1914">G-1914</span> | Sum of squares (SST, SSB, SSW) | The squared distances from a mean added up, before dividing: total (SST), between-group (SSB) and within-group (SSW), with $SST = SSB + SSW$; ANOVA compares the between-group and within-group parts to test whether group means differ. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#3-splitting-the-variation) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-1915">G-1915</span> | Summation ($z$) | The weighted sum $w_1x_1 + w_2x_2 + \dots + b$ inside a perceptron. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#32-the-summation) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-1917">G-1917</span> | Superposition | A network storing more features than it has dimensions by giving each feature its own nearly perpendicular direction. | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#8-the-superposition-hypothesis-and-a-toy-model) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-1916">G-1916</span> | Superposition hypothesis | The proposal that real neural networks store features in superposition (Elhage et al. 2022). | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#1-overview) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-1918">G-1918</span> | Supervised binning | Grouping a column's values into ranges (binning) with edges chosen using the target too, such as decision tree binning. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#4-kinds-of-discretization) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-1919">G-1919</span> | Supervised learning | Learning from data with inputs and outputs, to predict outputs. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#21-learning-from-inputs-and-outputs) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-1920">G-1920</span> | Supervision | Correct answers that guide an algorithm while it learns. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#1-overview) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-1924">G-1924</span> | Support | The number of items that really belong to a class. | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#61-per-class) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-1921">G-1921</span> | Support vector machine (SVM) | A classifier that separates the classes with the line, or flat surface in more dimensions (a hyperplane), that leaves the widest gap (margin) between the classes. | [Note ML-086](ML/07-classification/ML-086-svm-intuition/ML-086-svm-intuition.md#1-overview) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1922">G-1922</span> | Support vector regression (SVR) | The regression version of SVM: the same ideas, used to predict a number instead of a class. | [Note ML-086](ML/07-classification/ML-086-svm-intuition/ML-086-svm-intuition.md#7-strengths-of-svm) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1923">G-1923</span> | Support vectors | The training points that lie on the two margin lines, $\pi^+$ or $\pi^-$; they alone fix the SVM line. | [Note ML-086](ML/07-classification/ML-086-svm-intuition/ML-086-svm-intuition.md#6-support-vectors) | Support vector machines |
| <span id="G-1925">G-1925</span> | Sure (certain) event | The event that contains every outcome, the whole sample space, so it always happens; its probability is 1. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#47-impossible-and-sure-events) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-1926">G-1926</span> | Surge pricing | Raising fares when demand is much higher than supply. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#51-surge-pricing) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-2221">G-2221</span> | Surprise | How unexpected a class is: $\log_2(1/p)$ for a class with share $p$; entropy is the average surprise. | [Note ML-091](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition/ML-091-decision-trees-intuition.md#62-the-formula) | Entropy, information gain and Gini |
| <span id="G-1927">G-1927</span> | Surrogate model | A cheap stand-in for the unknown score function, built by Bayesian optimisation from the trials so far; it is used to guess which hyperparameter values to try next. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#33-informed-search-versus-blind-search) | Bayesian optimisation |
| <span id="G-1928">G-1928</span> | Survival function | The probability of a value above $x$: one minus the CDF; `sf` in scipy. | [Note MA-031](MA/03-distributions/MA-031-bernoulli-and-binomial/MA-031-bernoulli-and-binomial.md#5-the-binomial-formula) | Bernoulli and binomial distributions |
| <span id="G-1930">G-1930</span> | Switches | The stored positions of the largest values that max pooling kept; max pooling's backward pass needs them. | [Note DL-048](DL/04-cnn/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers/DL-048-backpropagation-cnn-layers.md#6-back-through-max-pooling-route-to-the-maximum) | Backpropagation in a CNN |
| <span id="G-1931">G-1931</span> | Symbolic AI | Early AI where humans write the knowledge as rules. | [Note ML-002](ML/01-foundations/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl/ML-002-ai-vs-ml-vs-dl.md#3-symbolic-ai-and-expert-systems) | Symbolic AI and expert systems |
| <span id="G-1932">G-1932</span> | Symmetric matrix | A square matrix equal to its own transpose, such as $X^{\mathsf T}X$ or a covariance matrix; it always has a full set of perpendicular eigenvectors. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#7-svd-and-eigen-decomposition-compared) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1933">G-1933</span> | Symmetry problem | Nodes that start with equal weights get equal updates and stay identical, so a layer acts like one node. | [Note DL-029](DL/02-training/DL-029-weight-initialization/DL-029-weight-initialization.md#43-sigmoid-every-node-becomes-the-same-node) | Weight initialisation |
| <span id="G-1934">G-1934</span> | Synthetic data | Rows created by an algorithm rather than collected. | [Note ML-127](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-127-imbalanced-data/ML-127-imbalanced-data.md#21-our-dataset) | Random under- and oversampling |
| <span id="G-1935">G-1935</span> | System prompt | Text placed before the user's message that sets the scene for a chatbot. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#74-generation-predict-pick-append-repeat) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-1936">G-1936</span> | T critical value | The cut-off from Student's t-distribution that leaves $\alpha/2$ in each tail; the t-interval multiplies the standard error by it in place of $z_{\alpha/2}$. For 95% and $n = 30$ it is 2.045. | [Note MA-037](MA/04-inference/MA-037-t-procedure/MA-037-t-procedure.md#6-the-t-procedure-formula) | Student's t-distribution |
| <span id="G-1937">G-1937</span> | T statistic | The number a t-test computes from the sample: how many estimated standard errors ($s/\sqrt{n}$) the sample mean lies from the mean claimed by $H_0$; the test decides from its size. | [Note MA-042](MA/04-inference/MA-042-one-sample-t-test/MA-042-one-sample-t-test.md#41-the-t-statistic-step-by-step) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1938">G-1938</span> | T-procedure | The way to build a confidence interval for a mean when the population standard deviation $\sigma$ is unknown: use the sample's $s$ and a t critical value in place of $z$, giving $\bar{x} \pm t_{\alpha/2,\thinspace n-1}\thinspace s/\sqrt{n}$. | [Note MA-037](MA/04-inference/MA-037-t-procedure/MA-037-t-procedure.md#6-the-t-procedure-formula) | Student's t-distribution |
| <span id="G-1939">G-1939</span> | T-table | A table of t critical values with one row per degrees of freedom and one column per tail area; we read the cut-off for a t-interval or t-test from it. | [Note MA-037](MA/04-inference/MA-037-t-procedure/MA-037-t-procedure.md#61-reading-a-t-table) | Student's t-distribution |
| <span id="G-1940">G-1940</span> | T-test | A hypothesis test about means that uses the sample standard deviation and Student's t-distribution. | [Note MA-042](MA/04-inference/MA-042-one-sample-t-test/MA-042-one-sample-t-test.md#1-overview) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-1941">G-1941</span> | Tag | One labelled box of a web page in HTML, such as a heading or a paragraph (`<h2>TCS</h2>`); a scraper finds the values it wants by their tags. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#4-html-tags-classes-and-nesting) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-1942">G-1942</span> | Tail (of a distribution) | The part of the curve far from the centre, where values are rare. | [Note ML-041](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-041-outliers-zscore/ML-041-outliers-zscore.md#31-reading-the-two-tails) | Z-score outlier method |
| <span id="G-1943">G-1943</span> | Tail event | An event with a very low probability but a very large effect. | [Note MA-026](MA/03-distributions/MA-026-skewness/MA-026-skewness.md#3-the-tail-and-tail-events) | Skewness |
| <span id="G-1944">G-1944</span> | Tailedness | How much probability lies far from the mean, in the tails. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#3-kurtosis-a-measure-of-tailedness) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-1945">G-1945</span> | Tangent line | The line that touches a curve at one point with the curve's slope there; the limit of secant lines. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#1-overview) | Derivatives of one variable |
| <span id="G-1946">G-1946</span> | Tangent plane | The flat plane that touches a surface at one point with the same slopes (gradient) there; it approximates the surface near that point and is the first-order Taylor approximation. | [Note MA-064](MA/06-calculus/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor/MA-064-hessian-and-multivariate-taylor.md#1-overview) | Hessian and multivariate Taylor |
| <span id="G-1947">G-1947</span> | Tanh (hyperbolic tangent) | An activation function that squashes any number into an S-curve from $-1$ to 1, centred on 0: $\tanh(z) = (e^{z}-e^{-z})/(e^{z}+e^{-z})$, with derivative $1 - \tanh^2(z)$. | [Note DL-027](DL/02-training/DL-027-activation-functions/DL-027-activation-functions.md#3-what-an-activation-function-is) | Tanh |
| <span id="G-1949">G-1949</span> | Target | The output column we predict, such as the class; also called the label. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#21-learning-from-inputs-and-outputs) | Supervised learning |
| <span id="G-2185">G-2185</span> | Target encoding | Replacing each category by the mean of the target for that category, learned on training rows only. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#4-columns-with-many-categories) | Encoding categorical data |
| <span id="G-1948">G-1948</span> | Target leakage | Building an input column from the answer itself, so the model sees information it would not have in real use. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#72-the-formula) | Data leakage |
| <span id="G-1951">G-1951</span> | Targeted marketing | Advertising only to the people most likely to buy. | [Note ML-008](ML/01-foundations/ML-008-applications-of-ml/ML-008-applications-of-ml.md#32-customer-profiles-for-targeted-marketing) | Applications of ML |
| <span id="G-1952">G-1952</span> | Task-specific contextual embedding | A contextual embedding (a word's vector built from its neighbouring words) whose way of mixing the neighbours is learned from the task's data, so a phrase such as "piece of cake" gets the meaning the task needs. | [Note DL-074](DL/06-transformers/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step.md#6-the-problem-nothing-to-learn) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-1953">G-1953</span> | Taylor polynomial | A polynomial built from a function's value and its first $n$ derivatives at one point $x_0$, used to approximate the function near $x_0$; it is the Taylor series cut after the $(x - x_0)^n$ term. | [Note MA-061](MA/06-calculus/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable/MA-061-derivatives-of-one-variable.md#62-the-taylor-polynomial-of-degree-n) | Taylor series |
| <span id="G-1954">G-1954</span> | Taylor series | Approximation of a function near a point by a polynomial built from its derivatives there. | [Note ML-120](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-120-xgboost-maths/ML-120-xgboost-maths.md#7-the-taylor-series) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-1955">G-1955</span> | Teacher forcing | Feeding the correct (gold) previous token to the decoder during training instead of its own prediction, so every step learns from a correct history and training goes faster. | [Note DL-068](DL/06-transformers/DL-068-encoder-decoder/DL-068-encoder-decoder.md#52-the-forward-pass-and-teacher-forcing) | Teacher forcing |
| <span id="G-1956">G-1956</span> | Temperature $T$ | A number that makes a model's probabilities sharper or flatter: every raw score (logit) is divided by it before the softmax; low $T$ sharpens, high $T$ flattens. | [Note DL-088](DL/06-transformers/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling.md#1-overview) | Unembedding, logits, temperature and sampling |
| <span id="G-1957">G-1957</span> | Tensor | A container of numbers arranged along one or more axes. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#2-what-a-tensor-is) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-1959">G-1959</span> | TensorFlow | Google's deep learning library for building and training neural networks; most code in these Notes uses it with Keras, its built-in high-level interface. | [Note DL-001](DL/01-basics/DL-001-dl-scope-and-prerequisites/DL-001-dl-scope-and-prerequisites.md#23-the-software) | Keras workflow |
| <span id="G-1958">G-1958</span> | TensorFlow Playground | A website that trains small neural networks in the browser and shows their boundaries. | [Note DL-007](DL/01-basics/DL-007-problem-with-perceptron/DL-007-problem-with-perceptron.md#5-the-same-failure-on-larger-data) | Problem with the perceptron (XOR) |
| <span id="G-1960">G-1960</span> | Terminal region | The part of the input space that ends in one leaf of a tree, written $R_{jm}$ for leaf $j$ of tree $m$; every point in it gets that leaf's output. | [Note ML-115](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths/ML-115-gradient-boosting-regression-maths.md#7-step-2b-fit-a-tree-it-creates-terminal-regions) | Gradient boosting |
| <span id="G-1961">G-1961</span> | Terminal velocity | The step size momentum reaches when every gradient is the same: $\eta g/(1-\beta)$. | [Note DL-034](DL/03-optimizers/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum.md#6-the-update-rule) | SGD with momentum |
| <span id="G-2211">G-2211</span> | Test accuracy | Accuracy on data not used for training, an estimate of performance on new data. | [Note ML-079](ML/07-classification/ML-079-polynomial-logistic-regression/ML-079-polynomial-logistic-regression.md#3-an-example-two-half-moons) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-1962">G-1962</span> | Test set | The part hidden during training, used to check the model. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#6-training-and-test-sets) | Train-test split |
| <span id="G-1963">G-1963</span> | Test statistic | The number a test computes from the sample to make its decision, such as $z$ or $t$. | [Note MA-038](MA/04-inference/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses/MA-038-null-and-alternative-hypotheses.md#6-the-eight-steps-of-a-hypothesis-test) | Hypothesis testing: null and alternative |
| <span id="G-1964">G-1964</span> | Text generator | A next-word predictor run in a loop, each predicted word added to the text. | [Note DL-063](DL/05-rnn/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction/DL-063-lstm-next-word-prediction.md#1-overview) | Next-word prediction with an LSTM |
| <span id="G-1966">G-1966</span> | Theoretical (classical) probability | Favourable outcomes divided by all outcomes, for equally likely outcomes. | [Note MA-011](MA/02-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability/MA-011-empirical-and-theoretical-probability.md#1-overview) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-1967">G-1967</span> | Theoretical distribution | The known distribution that data is compared with, for example on a Q-Q plot. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#7-building-a-q-q-plot) | Q-Q plot |
| <span id="G-1968">G-1968</span> | Theoretical quantile | Where a value would sit if the data were perfectly normal (the horizontal axis of a Q-Q plot). | [Note ML-029](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-029-function-transformer/ML-029-function-transformer.md#42-reading-a-q-q-plot) | Q-Q plot |
| <span id="G-1969">G-1969</span> | Thin (reduced) SVD | A smaller form of the SVD that drops the columns of $U$ multiplied by the zero rows of $\Sigma$; those columns never affect the matrix, so it gives the same $A$ from smaller matrices. | [Note MA-057](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-057-svd-geometry/MA-057-svd-geometry.md#62-the-thin-reduced-svd) | Singular value decomposition |
| <span id="G-1971">G-1971</span> | Threshold (binarization) | The value that separates 0 from 1 in binarization. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#12-binarization) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-1970">G-1970</span> | Threshold (classification) | The probability above which a prediction counts as class 1. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#61-from-probabilities-to-classes) | ANN for classification |
| <span id="G-2261">G-2261</span> | Threshold (of a perceptron) | The value the weighted sum must reach for the output to be 1; it equals $-b$. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#34-the-bias-as-a-threshold) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-1972">G-1972</span> | Tidy data | Data laid out with one record (observation) per row and one single value per cell. | [Note ML-044](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting.md#51-tidy-data) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-1973">G-1973</span> | Tied weights | Using one matrix for two jobs: the token embedding matrix turns tokens into vectors at the input, and the same matrix turns the final vector back into one score per token at the output. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#71-the-tied-unembedding) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-1974">G-1974</span> | Time axis and depth axis | The two directions of an unfolded deep RNN: time steps and layers. | [Note DL-065](DL/05-rnn/DL-065-deep-rnns/DL-065-deep-rnns.md#5-notation-time-axis-and-depth-axis) | Deep (stacked) RNNs |
| <span id="G-1975">G-1975</span> | Time series | Data recorded at regular time intervals. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#63-3d-time-series) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-1976">G-1976</span> | Time step | One position in the sequence; word $j$ enters at $t = j$. | [Note DL-056](DL/05-rnn/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation.md#32-time-steps) | Recurrent neural network (RNN) |
| <span id="G-1977">G-1977</span> | Timedelta | A length of time, the result of subtracting two datetimes. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#62-the-timedelta) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-1978">G-1978</span> | Timestamp | pandas' type for a single point in time. | [Note ML-033](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-033-date-and-time/ML-033-date-and-time.md#61-fixing-today) | Date and time features |
| <span id="G-1979">G-1979</span> | Title | The word before a name, such as Mr, Mrs, Miss or Master. | [Note ML-044](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting/ML-044-feature-construction-splitting.md#52-splitting-a-feature) | Feature construction and splitting |
| <span id="G-1981">G-1981</span> | Token | One unit of text the model reads or writes; here, one word. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#4-tokens) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-1980">G-1980</span> | Token embedding matrix $W_E$ | The learned table with one vector per token (50,257 vectors of 768 numbers in GPT-2); in GPT-2 also used as the output matrix. | [Note DL-087](DL/06-transformers/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt/DL-087-decoder-only-gpt.md#5-from-tokens-to-vectors-token-and-position-embeddings) | Decoder-only GPT |
| <span id="G-1983">G-1983</span> | Tokenisation (tokenization) | Splitting a text into tokens (words or word pieces), the units a language model reads and writes, before they are turned into vectors. | [Note DL-081](DL/06-transformers/DL-081-transformer-encoder/DL-081-transformer-encoder.md#10-key-terms) | Tokenization and integer encoding of text |
| <span id="G-1988">G-1988</span> | Top | The dense layers at the end of a CNN, which turn features into classes. | [Note DL-053](DL/04-cnn/DL-053-transfer-learning/DL-053-transfer-learning.md#41-base-and-top) | Transfer learning (feature extraction and fine-tuning) |
| <span id="G-1985">G-1985</span> | Top $k$ sampling | A sampling rule that keeps only the $k$ most likely tokens and samples the next token among them, so very unlikely tokens are never picked. | [Note DL-088](DL/06-transformers/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling.md#62-sampling-at-different-temperatures) | Unembedding, logits, temperature and sampling |
| <span id="G-1986">G-1986</span> | Top activations | The photos (or patches) that give a feature map its largest values. | [Note DL-052](DL/04-cnn/DL-052-visualizing-cnn/DL-052-visualizing-cnn.md#81-the-method-of-zeiler-and-fergus) | Visualising what a CNN learns |
| <span id="G-1987">G-1987</span> | Top categories | Keeping only the most frequent categories and merging the rest into one "uncommon" category. | [Note ML-026](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-026-one-hot-encoding/ML-026-one-hot-encoding.md#4-columns-with-many-categories) | One-hot encoding |
| <span id="G-1989">G-1989</span> | Top-1 error | The share of photos whose true class is not the model's single most likely class. | [Note DL-051](DL/04-cnn/DL-051-pretrained-models/DL-051-pretrained-models.md#51-the-competition) | Pretrained models and ImageNet |
| <span id="G-1990">G-1990</span> | Top-5 error | The share of photos whose true class is not among the model's five most likely classes. | [Note DL-051](DL/04-cnn/DL-051-pretrained-models/DL-051-pretrained-models.md#51-the-competition) | Pretrained models and ImageNet |
| <span id="G-1991">G-1991</span> | Topology | The pattern of connections between a network's layers. | [Note DL-054](DL/04-cnn/DL-054-keras-functional-api/DL-054-keras-functional-api.md#1-overview) | Keras functional API |
| <span id="G-1992">G-1992</span> | Total responsibility $N_k$ | About how many points one component $k$ of a mixture accounts for: the sum, over all points, of that component's share of each point (its responsibility). | [Note MA-073](MA/08-likelihood/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models/MA-073-gaussian-mixture-models.md#41-score-the-points-the-e-step) | Gaussian mixture model (GMM) |
| <span id="G-1993">G-1993</span> | Total sum of squares | The total squared error of always predicting the mean $\bar{y}$; R² compares the model's error with this baseline. | [Note ML-051](ML/06-regression/ML-051-regression-metrics/ML-051-regression-metrics.md#62-the-formula) | Regression metrics |
| <span id="G-1994">G-1994</span> | Toy model of superposition | A tiny network that squeezes more features than it has hidden numbers and is trained to rebuild its input, $x' = \text{ReLU}(W^TWx + b)$; it shows when a network stores more features than dimensions (superposition). | [Note DL-090](DL/06-transformers/DL-090-superposition/DL-090-superposition.md#81-the-toy-model) | Superposition and nearly perpendicular directions |
| <span id="G-1995">G-1995</span> | TPE | Optuna's default way to pick the hyperparameter values to try next from the results of past trials (its sampler, a form of Bayesian optimisation); TPE stands for Tree-structured Parzen Estimator. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#4-optunas-vocabulary) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-1996">G-1996</span> | TPU | Google's chip built only for deep learning maths. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#6-kaggle-notebooks) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
| <span id="G-1998">G-1998</span> | Train-test split | Dividing the data into a training set the model learns from and a test set held back to check it on unseen rows; it comes before scaling or fitting. | [Note ML-023](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-023-standardization/ML-023-standardization.md#62-split-before-scaling) | Train-test split |
| <span id="G-2000">G-2000</span> | Training (a perceptron) | Finding its weights and bias from labelled data. | [Note DL-005](DL/01-basics/DL-005-perceptron-trick/DL-005-perceptron-trick.md#1-overview) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-1255">G-1255</span> | Training (model training) | Giving data to an algorithm so it learns the pattern: the model makes predictions, measures its errors and adjusts to reduce them. | [Note ML-009](ML/01-foundations/ML-009-mldlc/ML-009-mldlc.md#81-training-many-algorithms) | ML development life cycle |
| <span id="G-2001">G-2001</span> | Training curves (learning curves) | Loss or accuracy plotted against the epoch, for the training and validation sets; they show how the model improves as training goes on and when the two sets start to differ. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#8-training-curves) | Training curves (History) |
| <span id="G-2002">G-2002</span> | Training set | The part of the data the model learns from. | [Note ML-012](ML/01-foundations/ML-012-toy-project/ML-012-toy-project.md#6-training-and-test-sets) | Train-test split |
| <span id="G-2004">G-2004</span> | Transfer function | Another name for the activation function: the function a node applies to its weighted sum to give its output. | [Note DL-027](DL/02-training/DL-027-activation-functions/DL-027-activation-functions.md#3-what-an-activation-function-is) | Activation functions |
| <span id="G-2005">G-2005</span> | Transfer learning | Reusing a network trained by others on a big dataset for our own problem. | [Note DL-002](DL/01-basics/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning/DL-002-what-is-deep-learning.md#54-architectures-and-transfer-learning) | Transfer learning (feature extraction and fine-tuning) |
| <span id="G-2006">G-2006</span> | Transformation | A function that takes a vector in and gives a vector out. | [Note MA-053](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices/MA-053-linear-transformations-and-matrices.md#2-transformations-functions-that-move-vectors) | Linear transformations and matrices |
| <span id="G-2007">G-2007</span> | Transformer | A neural network that turns one sequence into another (a seq2seq architecture) built from attention and dense layers, with no RNN, that processes all words in parallel. | [Note DL-067](DL/06-transformers/DL-067-history-of-llms/DL-067-history-of-llms.md#1-overview) | Transformer |
| <span id="G-2008">G-2008</span> | transformers | The `ColumnTransformer` parameter that says which transformer to apply to which columns: a list of (name, transformer, columns) tuples. | [Note ML-027](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-027-column-transformer/ML-027-column-transformer.md#51-the-transformers-list) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-2009">G-2009</span> | transformers_ | The attribute of a fitted column transformer that lists its fitted (name, transformer, columns) tuples; we use it to pull out one fitted step. | [Note ML-028](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-028-pipelines/ML-028-pipelines.md#72-pulling-out-fitted-steps) | Column transformer |
| <span id="G-2010">G-2010</span> | Translation equivariance | The output shifts in the same way as the input; a property of convolution. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#32-features-tied-to-their-location) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-2011">G-2011</span> | Translation invariance | The output stays (almost) the same when the input is shifted slightly. | [Note DL-044](DL/04-cnn/DL-044-pooling/DL-044-pooling.md#32-features-tied-to-their-location) | Pooling |
| <span id="G-2012">G-2012</span> | Transpose | A matrix or vector with rows and columns swapped; it turns a column vector $u$ into a row $u^{\mathsf T}$, so $u^{\mathsf T}x$ is the dot product. | [Note ML-053](ML/06-regression/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths/ML-053-multiple-lr-maths.md#41-two-rules-about-transposes) | Normal equation |
| <span id="G-2013">G-2013</span> | Tree-based algorithm | An algorithm that splits the data with simple conditions; hardly affected by outliers. | [Note ML-037](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample/ML-037-missing-indicator-random-sample.md#23-advantages) | Outliers |
| <span id="G-2014">G-2014</span> | Tree-level feature sampling (tree-level column sampling) | Drawing one random set of features per tree, before the tree is grown; every split of that tree uses only those features (bagging). | [Note ML-104](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-104-bagging-vs-random-forest/ML-104-bagging-vs-random-forest.md#31-bagging-tree-level-sampling) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-2016">G-2016</span> | Trial (hyperparameter tuning) | One run of a tuner (Optuna, Keras Tuner) with one set of hyperparameter values: one model built, trained and scored. | [Note ML-128](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-128-optuna/ML-128-optuna.md#4-optunas-vocabulary) | Optuna |
| <span id="G-2015">G-2015</span> | Trial (probability) | One run of a random experiment; it gives exactly one outcome. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#22-trial) | Events and sample spaces |
| <span id="G-2017">G-2017</span> | Trimmed mean | The mean after removing a fixed share of the smallest and largest values. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#7-trimmed-mean) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-2019">G-2019</span> | Trimming | Removing the rows that hold outliers. | [Note ML-040](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-040-what-are-outliers/ML-040-what-are-outliers.md#71-trimming) | Trimming outliers |
| <span id="G-2018">G-2018</span> | Trimming percentage | The share of values removed from each end for a trimmed mean. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#7-trimmed-mean) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-2020">G-2020</span> | True negative (TN) | A case the model predicts as negative that really is negative: a correct "no". The count of these is one cell of the confusion matrix. | [Note ML-075](ML/07-classification/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix.md#42-reading-it) | Confusion matrix |
| <span id="G-2021">G-2021</span> | True positive (TP) | A case the model predicts as positive that really is positive: a correct "yes". The count of these is one cell of the confusion matrix. | [Note ML-075](ML/07-classification/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix/ML-075-accuracy-confusion-matrix.md#42-reading-it) | Confusion matrix |
| <span id="G-2022">G-2022</span> | True positive rate (TPR) | The fraction of real positives the model flags; the same as recall. | [Note ML-077](ML/07-classification/ML-077-roc-auc/ML-077-roc-auc.md#31-true-positive-rate) | ROC curve and AUC |
| <span id="G-2023">G-2023</span> | Truncated normal | A normal distribution restricted to a range and rescaled so its area is 1; it keeps its bell shape inside the range. Keras' normal initialisers cut at two standard deviations: values beyond are redrawn. | [Note DL-030](DL/02-training/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization.md#62-with-keras-initialisers) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-2024">G-2024</span> | TruncatedSVD | scikit-learn's tool that cuts a matrix down to $k$ columns by keeping only its top $k$ singular directions (a rank $k$ SVD); it does not centre the data, so it works on sparse matrices. | [Note MA-060](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning/MA-060-svd-in-machine-learning.md#32-topics-from-the-svd) | Latent semantic analysis |
| <span id="G-2025">G-2025</span> | TSV file | Like a CSV file, with tabs between values. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#2-csv-and-tsv-files) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-2026">G-2026</span> | Tukey's HSD | A follow-up test (post-hoc test) that compares every pair of groups while keeping the overall chance of a false alarm (Type I error) at $\alpha$. | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#83-which-classes-differ-tukeys-test) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-2027">G-2027</span> | Two-tailed p-value | The p-value of a two-tailed test: the chance, if $H_0$ is true, of a test statistic at least as far from 0 as ours in either direction; for a z-test it is $2\thinspace\Phi(-\lvert z \rvert)$. | [Note MA-041](MA/04-inference/MA-041-p-values/MA-041-p-values.md#62-two-tailed-the-chips-packets) | P-values |
| <span id="G-2028">G-2028</span> | Two-tailed test (two-sided test) | A test that looks for an effect in either direction: $H_1$ uses $\neq$, and the significance level is split into $\alpha/2$ in each tail. | [Note MA-040](MA/04-inference/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails.md#5-one-tailed-and-two-tailed-tests) | Type I and II errors, power, tails |
| <span id="G-2029">G-2029</span> | Two-way ANOVA | A test of whether each of two category columns changes the mean of a number column, and whether their combination (interaction) does; it is ANOVA with one numerical and two categorical columns. | [Note MA-044](MA/04-inference/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test/MA-044-choosing-a-hypothesis-test.md#82-three-or-more-groups-anova) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-2030">G-2030</span> | Type 1 mixed variable | A column whose cells each contain a category and a number together, such as `C85`. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#2-what-a-mixed-variable-is) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-2031">G-2031</span> | Type 2 mixed variable | A column with a number in some rows and a category in others. | [Note ML-032](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-032-mixed-variables/ML-032-mixed-variables.md#2-what-a-mixed-variable-is) | Mixed variables |
| <span id="G-2032">G-2032</span> | Type I error (false positive) | A false alarm: the test declares an effect when there is none (rejecting $H_0$ when it is actually true); its probability is $\alpha$. | [Note MA-040](MA/04-inference/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails.md#21-type-i-error) | Type I and II errors, power, tails |
| <span id="G-2033">G-2033</span> | Type II error (false negative) | A miss: the test finds no effect when there really is one (failing to reject $H_0$ when it is actually false); its probability is $\beta$. | [Note MA-040](MA/04-inference/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails/MA-040-errors-power-and-tails.md#22-type-ii-error) | Type I and II errors, power, tails |
| <span id="G-2034">G-2034</span> | Unbiased estimator | A formula that guesses a true value from a sample (an estimator) and is right on average: its average over many samples equals the true value. | [Note MA-071](MA/08-likelihood/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions/MA-071-mle-for-common-distributions.md#52-biased-but-consistent) | Maximum likelihood estimation (MLE) |
| <span id="G-2035">G-2035</span> | Underfitting | Being too simple to capture the pattern; fails on all data. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#72-underfitting) | Underfitting |
| <span id="G-2036">G-2036</span> | Underflow | A number too close to 0 for the computer to store, which then becomes 0 or loses precision. | [Note ML-072](ML/07-classification/ML-072-log-loss/ML-072-log-loss.md#41-the-problem-with-products) | Logistic regression |
| <span id="G-2037">G-2037</span> | Underlying distribution | The distribution that produced the data points. | [Note MA-023](MA/03-distributions/MA-023-density-estimation-kde/MA-023-density-estimation-kde.md#2-what-density-estimation-is) | Density estimation |
| <span id="G-2038">G-2038</span> | Understanding the data | The project stage where we learn what is in the data before cleaning or modelling. | [Note ML-018](ML/02-getting-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data/ML-018-understanding-your-data.md#1-overview) | Exploratory data analysis |
| <span id="G-2040">G-2040</span> | Unembedding | The last step of a language model: the final vector's dot product with every token's vector, one score per token. | [Note DL-088](DL/06-transformers/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling.md#1-overview) | Unembedding, logits, temperature and sampling |
| <span id="G-2039">G-2039</span> | Unembedding matrix $W_U$ | The matrix that turns a language model's final vector into one score (logit) per vocabulary token; in GPT-2 it is the token embedding matrix $W_E$ itself (tied weights). | [Note DL-088](DL/06-transformers/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling/DL-088-unembedding-and-sampling.md#31-the-formula) | Unembedding, logits, temperature and sampling |
| <span id="G-2041">G-2041</span> | Unfolding (unrolling) | Drawing the recurrent layer once per time step, so the loop becomes a chain. | [Note DL-056](DL/05-rnn/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation/DL-056-rnn-forward-propagation.md#41-the-smallest-rnn-one-node) | Recurrent neural network (RNN) |
| <span id="G-2042">G-2042</span> | Unidirectional RNN | An RNN that reads in one direction only, so its output at $t$ depends only on inputs up to $t$. | [Note DL-066](DL/05-rnn/DL-066-bidirectional-rnn/DL-066-bidirectional-rnn.md#31-the-limit-of-a-unidirectional-rnn) | Bidirectional RNNs |
| <span id="G-2043">G-2043</span> | Uniform distribution | A distribution in which every outcome in a range is equally likely. | [Note MA-029](MA/03-distributions/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal/MA-029-uniform-and-log-normal.md#2-the-uniform-distribution) | Uniform distribution |
| <span id="G-2044">G-2044</span> | Uniform weighting | Every neighbour counts equally: the fill is their plain mean. | [Note ML-038](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-038-knn-imputer/ML-038-knn-imputer.md#75-distance-weighting) | KNN imputer |
| <span id="G-2045">G-2045</span> | Union (A ∪ B) | The event that A or B (or both) happens. | [Note MA-017](MA/02-probability/MA-017-mutually-exclusive-events/MA-017-mutually-exclusive-events.md#5-the-addition-rule-where-mutual-exclusivity-pays-off) | Empirical vs theoretical probability, probability rules |
| <span id="G-2046">G-2046</span> | Unit hypercube | The box from 0 to 1 along every axis in three or more dimensions (a unit cube in three); min-max scaling presses data with many columns into it. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#43-what-min-max-scaling-does-to-the-data) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-2047">G-2047</span> | Unit square | The square from (0, 0) to (1, 1), into which min-max scaling presses two columns. | [Note ML-024](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-024-normalization/ML-024-normalization.md#43-what-min-max-scaling-does-to-the-data) | Normalization |
| <span id="G-2048">G-2048</span> | Unit vector | A vector of length 1, used to describe a direction. | [Note ML-047](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-047-pca-step-by-step/ML-047-pca-step-by-step.md#21-projecting-one-point) | Vector magnitude, distance and scalar operations |
| <span id="G-2049">G-2049</span> | Units | The LSTM setting for how many nodes each of its four internal layers has; it is also the length of the cell state $c_t$, the hidden state $h_t$ and every gate vector. | [Note DL-062](DL/05-rnn/DL-062-lstm-architecture/DL-062-lstm-architecture.md#41-cell-state-and-hidden-state-are-vectors-of-the-same-length) | LSTM gates (forget, input, output) and cell state |
| <span id="G-2050">G-2050</span> | Univariate analysis | Studying one column (variable) on its own, mostly with graphs, to see its values and spread; the column's type decides which graphs to draw. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#1-overview) | Univariate analysis |
| <span id="G-2051">G-2051</span> | Univariate imputation | Filling the gaps in a column (imputation) using only that column's other values. | [Note ML-034](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-034-complete-case-analysis/ML-034-complete-case-analysis.md#32-imputing-filling-in-the-gaps) | Missing values |
| <span id="G-2052">G-2052</span> | Universal approximation theorem | A network with a hidden layer and enough neurons can approximate any continuous function. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#33-universal-approximation) | Universal approximation theorem |
| <span id="G-2053">G-2053</span> | Universal set ($U$) | The rectangle of a Venn diagram; in probability, the sample space, with $P(U) = 1$. | [Note MA-013](MA/02-probability/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables.md#2-venn-diagrams) | Venn diagrams and contingency tables |
| <span id="G-2054">G-2054</span> | Unpaired t-test | Another name for the independent two-sample t-test: a t-test that compares the means of two separate groups whose values are not paired. | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#2-the-independent-two-sample-t-test) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-2055">G-2055</span> | Unreasonable effectiveness of data | With enough data, different algorithms perform about the same. | [Note ML-007](ML/01-foundations/ML-007-challenges-in-ml/ML-007-challenges-in-ml.md#31-more-data-beats-a-better-algorithm) | Enough data |
| <span id="G-2156">G-2156</span> | Unstable model | A model whose fit changes a lot when the training data changes a little; bagging helps it most. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#22-other-base-models) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-2056">G-2056</span> | Unstable training | Training that does not progress because exploding gradients make the updates huge. | [Note DL-060](DL/05-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn/DL-060-problems-with-rnn.md#1-overview) | Long-term dependency problem |
| <span id="G-2057">G-2057</span> | Unsupervised binning | Binning (grouping a column's values into ranges) that sets the bin edges from the column's own values only, without using the target. | [Note ML-031](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-031-binning-binarization/ML-031-binning-binarization.md#4-kinds-of-discretization) | Binning and binarization |
| <span id="G-2058">G-2058</span> | Unsupervised learning | Learning from inputs only, to find structure. | [Note ML-003](ML/01-foundations/ML-003-types-of-ml/ML-003-types-of-ml.md#31-learning-from-inputs-only) | Unsupervised learning |
| <span id="G-2059">G-2059</span> | Unsupervised pre-training | Setting a network's starting weights with a network trained layer by layer, instead of at random. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#35-deep-belief-networks-and-the-name-deep-learning) | Types of neural networks |
| <span id="G-2060">G-2060</span> | Update gate ($z_t$) | In a GRU, numbers between 0 and 1, the output of a sigmoid layer, that decide entry by entry how much of the new candidate memory replaces the old memory. | [Note DL-064](DL/05-rnn/DL-064-gru/DL-064-gru.md#4-the-big-idea-one-state-two-gates) | GRU (gated recurrent unit) |
| <span id="G-2061">G-2061</span> | Updates per epoch | How many times the weights are updated in one pass over the data: one update per batch, so $\lceil n / \text{batch size} \rceil$. | [Note DL-020](DL/02-training/DL-020-gradient-descent-in-neural-networks/DL-020-gradient-descent-in-neural-networks.md#6-choosing-the-variant-in-keras-batch_size) | Batch size in Keras |
| <span id="G-2062">G-2062</span> | Upper / lower limit (outlier detection) | The two cut-offs of the z-score method, $\mu - 3\sigma$ and $\mu + 3\sigma$; a value below the lower limit or above the upper limit counts as an outlier. | [Note ML-041](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-041-outliers-zscore/ML-041-outliers-zscore.md#7-finding-the-limits-and-the-outliers) | Z-score outlier method |
| <span id="G-2063">G-2063</span> | Upsampling (resampling by weight) | Drawing a new dataset of the same size in which each row is picked with probability equal to its weight, so heavily weighted rows appear more often for the next model. | [Note ML-110](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step.md#9-step-7-upsampling-a-new-dataset-drawn-by-weight) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-2064">G-2064</span> | User-Agent | A short text a browser sends with each request to say what it is; some servers refuse requests without one, so scraping code sends one too. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#5-opening-a-file-from-a-url) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-2065">G-2065</span> | UTF-8 | The most common text encoding (the rulebook that maps characters to bytes); it covers almost every character in every language and is `read_csv`'s default. | [Note ML-014](ML/02-getting-data/ML-014-working-with-csv/ML-014-working-with-csv.md#12-text-encoding-encoding) | CSV files |
| <span id="G-2067">G-2067</span> | Validation set | Data held back from training to check and tune a model before the final test. | [Note DL-011](DL/01-basics/DL-011-customer-churn-ann/DL-011-customer-churn-ann.md#73-tracking-accuracy-and-a-validation-set) | Training curves (History) |
| <span id="G-2066">G-2066</span> | Validation set (blending) | In blending, a part of the training data set aside before the base models are trained; the base models predict on it, and the meta-model is trained on those predictions. | [Note ML-121](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-121-stacking-blending/ML-121-stacking-blending.md#6-blending-a-hold-out-set) | Stacking and blending |
| <span id="G-2068">G-2068</span> | Value | In self-attention, the vector each word contributes to the weighted sum that becomes a word's new vector; it comes from the word's embedding through a learned value matrix. | [Note DL-074](DL/06-transformers/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step/DL-074-self-attention-step-by-step.md#1-overview) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-2069">G-2069</span> | Vanilla gradient descent | Another name for batch gradient descent, the plain version, which uses the whole training set for every weight update. | [Note DL-020](DL/02-training/DL-020-gradient-descent-in-neural-networks/DL-020-gradient-descent-in-neural-networks.md#4-batch-gradient-descent-in-a-network) | Batch gradient descent |
| <span id="G-2070">G-2070</span> | Vanishing gradient | Gradients shrinking towards 0 as they pass through many layers, which slows learning. | [Note DL-018](DL/01-basics/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients/DL-018-vanishing-exploding-gradients.md#1-overview) | Vanishing gradient |
| <span id="G-2071">G-2071</span> | Variable | One column of a dataset. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#2-univariate-bivariate-and-multivariate-analysis) | Features |
| <span id="G-2072">G-2072</span> | Variable-length many-to-many | A sequence-to-sequence setup (many-to-many) whose output length can differ from its input length. | [Note DL-058](DL/05-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn/DL-058-types-of-rnn.md#52-different-lengths) | Types of RNN (many-to-one, one-to-many, many-to-many) |
| <span id="G-2073">G-2073</span> | Variance (of a model) | How much a model's predictions change when it is trained on a different sample of the data; a different meaning from the variance of a column. | [Note ML-061](ML/06-regression/ML-061-bias-variance/ML-061-bias-variance.md#1-overview) | Bias-variance trade-off |
| <span id="G-2074">G-2074</span> | Variance (of data) | The average squared distance of the values from their mean; the square of the standard deviation; divide by $n$ for a population (NumPy default) or $n - 1$ for a sample (pandas default). | [Note ML-046](ML/05-dimensionality/ML-046-pca-geometric-intuition/ML-046-pca-geometric-intuition.md#21-the-rule-keep-the-bigger-spread) | Variance |
| <span id="G-2228">G-2228</span> | Variance around a point $v$ | The average squared distance of the values to $v$; smallest when $v$ is the sample mean. | [Note MA-006](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion/MA-006-measures-of-dispersion.md#62-the-sample-mean-is-the-closest-point-to-its-sample) | Variance |
| <span id="G-2075">G-2075</span> | Variance inflation factor (VIF) | A number for each input column that shows how well the other inputs predict it, $1 / (1 - R_j^2)$; above 5 signals multicollinearity (the input is redundant). | [Note ML-055](ML/06-regression/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions/ML-055-linear-regression-assumptions.md#32-checking-it) | Multicollinearity |
| <span id="G-2076">G-2076</span> | Variance of a random variable | How spread out a random variable's values are: the expected squared distance from its expected value, $\mathrm{Var}(X) = E[(X - E[X])^2]$. | [Note MA-012](MA/02-probability/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance/MA-012-expected-value-and-variance.md#4-variance-of-a-random-variable) | Expected value and variance of a random variable |
| <span id="G-2077">G-2077</span> | Variance reduction | The drop in mean squared error from a node to its children; a regression tree uses it to compare splits, as a classification tree uses information gain. | [Note ML-093](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-093-regression-trees/ML-093-regression-trees.md#43-trying-every-threshold) | Regression trees |
| <span id="G-2081">G-2081</span> | Vector | A list of numbers: a 1D tensor. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#2-what-a-tensor-is) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-2079">G-2079</span> | Vector (geometric view) | A point in a coordinate system, drawn as an arrow from the origin. | [Note MA-048](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors/MA-048-vectors-and-feature-vectors.md#23-coordinates-walking-instructions) | Vectors and feature vectors |
| <span id="G-2080">G-2080</span> | Vector addition | Adding matching components; geometrically, placing the second arrow's tail at the first arrow's tip. | [Note MA-052](MA/05-linear-algebra/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis/MA-052-linear-combinations-span-and-basis.md#31-tip-to-tail) | Linear combinations, span and basis |
| <span id="G-2082">G-2082</span> | Vector-valued function | A function that takes several numbers in and gives several numbers out, $\mathbf{f}: \mathbb{R}^n \to \mathbb{R}^m$; we read it as a stack of $m$ ordinary functions. | [Note MA-063](MA/06-calculus/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients/MA-063-jacobian-and-matrix-gradients.md#22-functions-with-several-outputs) | Jacobian and matrix gradients |
| <span id="G-2083">G-2083</span> | Vectorisation (of code) | Writing a computation as operations on whole arrays instead of Python loops, which runs much faster. | [Note ML-057](ML/06-regression/ML-057-batch-gradient-descent/ML-057-batch-gradient-descent.md#33-all-derivatives-at-once) | Batch gradient descent |
| <span id="G-2084">G-2084</span> | Vectorization (of data) | Converting data such as text into vectors of numbers, because ML algorithms work only with numbers. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#62-3d-text) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-2085">G-2085</span> | Velocity $v$ | The direction and size of the current move, built from past gradients: $v_t = \beta v_{t-1} + \eta\thinspace\nabla L(w_t)$. | [Note DL-034](DL/03-optimizers/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum/DL-034-sgd-with-momentum.md#5-the-idea-confidence-builds-speed) | SGD with momentum |
| <span id="G-2086">G-2086</span> | Venn diagram | A picture of events as circles inside a rectangle; overlaps show shared outcomes. | [Note MA-013](MA/02-probability/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables/MA-013-venn-diagrams-and-contingency-tables.md#2-venn-diagrams) | Venn diagrams and contingency tables |
| <span id="G-2087">G-2087</span> | verbose | scikit-learn setting that prints progress messages during training. | [Note ML-100](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-100-bagging-classifier/ML-100-bagging-classifier.md#34-other-base-models-pasting-subspaces-and-patches) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-2258">G-2258</span> | Vertex | A corner of the feasible region; a linear program always has a best point at a vertex. | [Note MA-068](MA/07-optimisation/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming/MA-068-linear-and-quadratic-programming.md#22-the-answer-is-at-a-corner) | Linear and quadratic programming |
| <span id="G-2088">G-2088</span> | VGG16 | An image-recognition network (a CNN) of 13 convolution layers (all 3 × 3) in 5 blocks plus 3 dense layers, trained on ImageNet. | [Note DL-052](DL/04-cnn/DL-052-visualizing-cnn/DL-052-visualizing-cnn.md#4-the-network-vgg16) | Visualising what a CNN learns |
| <span id="G-2089">G-2089</span> | View Page Source | Browser option that shows a page's raw HTML. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#32-looking-like-a-browser) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-2090">G-2090</span> | Virtual environment | A separate folder with its own copy of Python and its own packages, one per project, so the projects' package versions do not clash. | [Note ML-011](ML/01-foundations/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab/ML-011-setup-anaconda-jupyter-colab.md#41-why-one-environment-per-project) | Setup: conda, Jupyter and Colab |
