| <span id="G-2092">G-2092</span> | Vocabulary ($V$) | The list of distinct words (tokens) in a set of texts; in a translation model, those of the target language, with one output node per word. | [Note DL-084](DL/06-transformers/DL-084-transformer-decoder/DL-084-transformer-decoder.md#8-the-output-layer-linear-and-softmax) | Transformer decoder |
| <span id="G-2094">G-2094</span> | Volatile | Changing quickly and unpredictably. | [Note MA-028](MA/03-distributions/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots/MA-028-kurtosis-and-qq-plots.md#5-where-kurtosis-matters-kurtosis-risk) | Kurtosis and moments |
| <span id="G-2095">G-2095</span> | Voting classifier | A classifier that combines several trained classifiers by voting. | [Note ML-097](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-097-voting-classifier/ML-097-voting-classifier.md#2-the-core-idea-on-decision-surfaces) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-2096">G-2096</span> | Voting ensemble | Several models trained on the same data, combined by majority vote (classification) or mean (regression). | [Note ML-096](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-096-voting-ensemble/ML-096-voting-ensemble.md#1-overview) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-2097">G-2097</span> | Voting regressor | A regressor that predicts the mean (or weighted mean) of several trained regressors' predictions. | [Note ML-098](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-098-voting-regressor/ML-098-voting-regressor.md#1-overview) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-2098">G-2098</span> | Vowpal Wabbit | A fast learning library that can learn from data a piece at a time as it arrives (online learning). | [Note ML-005](ML/01-foundations/ML-005-online-learning/ML-005-online-learning.md#4-implementing-online-learning) | Online learning |
| <span id="G-2099">G-2099</span> | Ward linkage | A rule for the distance between two clusters in hierarchical clustering: how much the total squared distance to the centroids grows if the two are merged. | [Note ML-125](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering/ML-125-hierarchical-clustering.md#8-linkage-the-distance-between-two-clusters) | Hierarchical clustering |
| <span id="G-2100">G-2100</span> | warm_start | Setting that keeps already-trained trees and adds new ones on the next fit. | [Note ML-105](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-105-random-forest-hyperparameters/ML-105-random-forest-hyperparameters.md#5-the-general-hyperparameters) | Random forest |
| <span id="G-2101">G-2101</span> | Wayback Machine | A web archive that keeps copies of web pages as they were. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#32-looking-like-a-browser) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-2102">G-2102</span> | WCSS (inertia) | Within-cluster sum of squares: the sum of squared distances from each point to its own centroid; a smaller value means tighter clusters. | [Note ML-122](ML/09-clustering-and-more/ML-122-kmeans-intuition/ML-122-kmeans-intuition.md#51-wcss-how-tight-the-clusters-are) | Elbow method and WCSS |
| <span id="G-2103">G-2103</span> | Weak duality | Every value of the related helper (dual) function is at most the true minimum of the original (primal) problem, so any dual value is a floor (lower bound) on the best answer. | [Note MA-066](MA/07-optimisation/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers/MA-066-lagrange-multipliers.md#62-weak-and-strong-duality) | Lagrange multipliers, KKT and duality |
| <span id="G-2104">G-2104</span> | Weak learner | A model whose accuracy is only a little better than random guessing. | [Note ML-109](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-109-adaboost-intuition/ML-109-adaboost-intuition.md#21-weak-learners) | Boosting |
| <span id="G-2105">G-2105</span> | Web scraping | Writing code that extracts data from web pages. | [Note ML-017](ML/02-getting-data/ML-017-web-scraping/ML-017-web-scraping.md#1-overview) | Web scraping |
| <span id="G-2106">G-2106</span> | Weight (in a network) | A number the network learns on the connection between two neurons; it sets how strongly one neuron's output counts in the next neuron's sum. | [Note DL-004](DL/01-basics/DL-004-perceptron/DL-004-perceptron.md#31-inputs-weights-and-bias) | Perceptron |
| <span id="G-2111">G-2111</span> | Weight (in a weighted mean) | A number saying how much a value counts in a weighted mean. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#6-weighted-mean) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-2108">G-2108</span> | Weight decay | Another name for the L2 penalty: each gradient step shrinks the coefficients by a fixed factor. | [Note ML-064](ML/06-regression/ML-064-ridge-gradient-descent/ML-064-ridge-gradient-descent.md#23-the-update-rule-and-one-update-on-numbers) | Ridge regression |
| <span id="G-2107">G-2107</span> | Weight decay factor | The number $1 - \eta\lambda$, a little below 1, by which L2 regularisation multiplies every weight at each update, so the weights shrink a little every step. | [Note DL-026](DL/02-training/DL-026-regularization-in-dl/DL-026-regularization-in-dl.md#6-why-the-weights-shrink-weight-decay) | L1 and L2 regularisation in neural networks |
| <span id="G-2109">G-2109</span> | Weight matrix ($W^{k}$) | All weights entering layer $k$: one row per node of layer $k-1$, one column per node of layer $k$. | [Note DL-010](DL/01-basics/DL-010-forward-propagation/DL-010-forward-propagation.md#41-the-weight-matrix) | Forward propagation |
| <span id="G-2110">G-2110</span> | Weight update | The AdaBoost step that raises the weights of misclassified rows (times $e^{\alpha}$) and lowers the rest (times $e^{-\alpha}$), so the next model pays more attention to the mistakes. | [Note ML-110](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step.md#7-step-5-update-the-observation-weights) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-2112">G-2112</span> | Weight-based algorithm | An algorithm that learns one number per input column from all the points; sensitive to outliers. | [Note ML-040](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-040-what-are-outliers/ML-040-what-are-outliers.md#5-which-algorithms-outliers-affect) | Outliers |
| <span id="G-2113">G-2113</span> | Weighted average | One number for a per-class score: the mean of the score over classes, each class counted by how many items really belong to it (its support). | [Note ML-076](ML/07-classification/ML-076-precision-recall-f1/ML-076-precision-recall-f1.md#62-combining-the-classes) | Precision, recall and F1 |
| <span id="G-2114">G-2114</span> | Weighted error | The total sample weight of the rows a model misclassifies; AdaBoost uses it to judge how good that model is. | [Note ML-110](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step/ML-110-adaboost-step-by-step.md#5-step-3-the-error-is-a-sum-of-weights) | AdaBoost |
| <span id="G-2115">G-2115</span> | Weighted impurity decrease | How much a split lowers the impurity, weighted by the share of rows that reach the node; trees use it to judge splits (the $\Delta$ of `min_impurity_decrease`) and to score feature importance. | [Note ML-108](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-108-feature-importance/ML-108-feature-importance.md#41-the-decrease-at-one-node) | Feature importance |
| <span id="G-2116">G-2116</span> | Weighted input ($z^{k}$) | The numbers a layer computes before its activation: each node's weighted sum of the previous layer's outputs plus its bias, $z^{k} = W^{k\mathsf T} a^{k-1} + b^{k}$. | [Note DL-010](DL/01-basics/DL-010-forward-propagation/DL-010-forward-propagation.md#4-layer-1-as-one-matrix-product) | Forward propagation |
| <span id="G-2117">G-2117</span> | Weighted mean | A mean in which each value is multiplied by a weight saying how much it counts. | [Note MA-005](MA/01-descriptive-stats/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency/MA-005-measures-of-central-tendency.md#6-weighted-mean) | Measures of central tendency |
| <span id="G-2118">G-2118</span> | Weighted quantile sketch | XGBoost's method for choosing candidate split points in a column: it places bin edges at quantiles in which each row counts by its Hessian (weighted quantiles). | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#84-approximate-split-finding-with-quantile-bins) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-2119">G-2119</span> | Weighted sum | In self-attention, the value vectors multiplied by their weights and added together; the result is the word's new, context-aware vector. | [Note DL-076](DL/06-transformers/DL-076-self-attention-geometric-intuition/DL-076-self-attention-geometric-intuition.md#6-step-3-a-weighted-sum-of-the-value-vectors) | Self-attention (query, key, value) |
| <span id="G-2120">G-2120</span> | weights | VotingClassifier and VotingRegressor setting that gives each base model's vote a different importance. | [Note ML-097](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-097-voting-classifier/ML-097-voting-classifier.md#43-weights) | Voting ensembles |
| <span id="G-2121">G-2121</span> | Welch's ANOVA | A version of ANOVA that works even when the groups' spreads differ (it does not assume equal variances). | [Note MA-046](MA/04-inference/MA-046-one-way-anova/MA-046-one-way-anova.md#7-assumptions) | One-way ANOVA |
| <span id="G-2122">G-2122</span> | Welch's t-test | The two-sample t-test that works even when the two groups' spreads differ (it does not assume equal variances). | [Note MA-043](MA/04-inference/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests/MA-043-two-sample-and-paired-t-tests.md#1-overview) | T-tests: one-sample, two-sample, paired |
| <span id="G-2181">G-2181</span> | Whisker | The line in a box plot from the box to the last value inside the fence; it shows how far the ordinary values reach, and values beyond it are drawn as separate dots. | [Note ML-019](ML/02-getting-data/ML-019-univariate-analysis/ML-019-univariate-analysis.md#81-whiskers-and-outliers) | Percentiles, quartiles and box plots |
| <span id="G-2123">G-2123</span> | Winsorization | Handling outliers by capping them at percentile limits: values beyond a chosen low or high percentile are replaced by that percentile's value. | [Note ML-043](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-043-outliers-percentile/ML-043-outliers-percentile.md#3-trimming-and-capping-winsorization) | Capping (winsorization) |
| <span id="G-2124">G-2124</span> | Wisdom of the crowd | The combined judgement of many is often more accurate than any one member's. | [Note ML-095](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-095-ensemble-learning/ML-095-ensemble-learning.md#2-the-wisdom-of-the-crowd) | Ensemble learning |
| <span id="G-2125">G-2125</span> | With replacement | Sampling in which each drawn item is put back, so it can be drawn again. | [Note ML-099](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-099-bagging-intuition/ML-099-bagging-intuition.md#23-drawing-with-replacement) | Bagging |
| <span id="G-2126">G-2126</span> | Without replacement | Drawing items without putting them back, so later draws depend on earlier ones. | [Note MA-010](MA/02-probability/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events/MA-010-events-and-types-of-events.md#44-dependent-events) | Independent and mutually exclusive events |
| <span id="G-2127">G-2127</span> | Word embedding | A learned real-valued vector for each word; words used in similar ways get nearby vectors. | [Note DL-057](DL/05-rnn/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis/DL-057-rnn-sentiment-analysis.md#61-sparse-and-dense-representations) | Word embeddings |
| <span id="G-2128">G-2128</span> | WordNet | An English dictionary that arranges words from general to specific; ImageNet's categories follow it. | [Note DL-051](DL/04-cnn/DL-051-pretrained-models/DL-051-pretrained-models.md#42-what-it-contains) | Pretrained models and ImageNet |
| <span id="G-2129">G-2129</span> | X, y | Usual names for the input table and the output column. | [Note ML-010](ML/01-foundations/ML-010-tensors/ML-010-tensors.md#61-1d-and-2d-tabular-data) | Tensors |
| <span id="G-2130">G-2130</span> | XAMPP | A free package that runs a web server and a MySQL server on one computer. | [Note ML-015](ML/02-getting-data/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql/ML-015-working-with-json-and-sql.md#6-getting-an-sql-database-ready) | JSON and SQL data |
| <span id="G-2131">G-2131</span> | Xavier (Glorot) normal | Starting weights drawn from a normal distribution whose spread is set by the layer's size, standard deviation $\sqrt{1/\text{fan-in}}$ or $\sqrt{2/(\text{fan-in} + \text{fan-out})}$, so the signal keeps its size from layer to layer. | [Note DL-030](DL/02-training/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization.md#41-xavier-normal) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-2132">G-2132</span> | Xavier (Glorot) uniform | Starting weights drawn evenly between $\pm\sqrt{6/(\text{fan-in} + \text{fan-out})}$, a range set by the layer's size, so the signal keeps its size from layer to layer. | [Note DL-030](DL/02-training/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization/DL-030-xavier-he-initialization.md#42-xavier-uniform) | Xavier and He initialisation |
| <span id="G-2133">G-2133</span> | XGBoost | eXtreme Gradient Boosting: a library that implements gradient boosting with many speed and accuracy optimisations. | [Note ML-117](ML/08-trees-and-ensembles/ML-117-xgboost-intro/ML-117-xgboost-intro.md#1-overview) | XGBoost |
| <span id="G-2134">G-2134</span> | XOR | The logic function that outputs 1 when exactly one of two inputs is 1. | [Note DL-003](DL/01-basics/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications/DL-003-nn-types-history-applications.md#31-the-perceptron-and-the-first-ai-winter) | Problem with the perceptron (XOR) |
| <span id="G-2135">G-2135</span> | Yates' continuity correction | A small adjustment to $\chi^2$ for 2 by 2 tables, applied by default in `chi2_contingency`. | [Note MA-045](MA/04-inference/MA-045-chi-square-tests/MA-045-chi-square-tests.md#61-sex-and-survival) | Chi-square tests |
| <span id="G-2136">G-2136</span> | Yeo-Johnson transform | A power transform that makes a column closer to normal, like Box-Cox but also working on zero and negative values; scikit-learn's default. | [Note ML-030](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-030-power-transformer/ML-030-power-transformer.md#1-overview) | Power transformer |
| <span id="G-2137">G-2137</span> | Z statistic | The number a z-test computes from the sample: how many standard errors the sample mean lies from the mean claimed by $H_0$. | [Note MA-039](MA/04-inference/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test.md#41-the-idea-on-the-rats) | Z-test and rejection regions |
| <span id="G-2138">G-2138</span> | Z-procedure | The way to build a confidence interval for a mean when the population standard deviation $\sigma$ is known: $\bar{x} \pm z_{\alpha/2}\thinspace\sigma/\sqrt{n}$. | [Note MA-035](MA/04-inference/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure/MA-035-confidence-intervals-z-procedure.md#5-two-ways-to-compute-a-confidence-interval-for-a-mean) | Confidence intervals |
| <span id="G-2141">G-2141</span> | Z-score | A value after standardization: how many standard deviations it lies from the mean. | [Note ML-041](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-041-outliers-zscore/ML-041-outliers-zscore.md#4-why-it-is-called-the-z-score-method) | Z-score outlier method |
| <span id="G-2139">G-2139</span> | Z-score method | Outlier detection that flags values more than 3 standard deviations from the mean; for roughly normal columns. | [Note ML-041](ML/04-missing-data-and-outliers/ML-041-outliers-zscore/ML-041-outliers-zscore.md#1-overview) | Z-score outlier method |
| <span id="G-2140">G-2140</span> | Z-score normalization | Another name for standardization: rescaling a column so each value becomes its z-score, how many standard deviations it lies from the mean. | [Note ML-023](ML/03-feature-engineering/ML-023-standardization/ML-023-standardization.md#3-types-of-feature-scaling) | Standardization |
| <span id="G-2142">G-2142</span> | Z-table | A table that gives, for many values of $z$, the probability that a standard normal value falls below $z$, written $\Phi(z)$; used to find normal probabilities. | [Note MA-025](MA/03-distributions/MA-025-standard-normal-and-z-table/MA-025-standard-normal-and-z-table.md#4-the-z-table) | Standard normal and the z-table |
| <span id="G-2143">G-2143</span> | Z-test (one-sample) | A test of whether a population's mean equals a claimed value $\mu_0$, used when the population spread $\sigma$ is known and $\bar{X}$ is normal: $z = (\bar{x} - \mu_0)/(\sigma/\sqrt{n})$. | [Note MA-039](MA/04-inference/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test/MA-039-rejection-region-and-z-test.md#1-overview) | Z-test and rejection regions |
| <span id="G-2144">G-2144</span> | Zero initialisation | Starting every weight (and bias) at 0; with ReLU or tanh nothing trains. | [Note DL-029](DL/02-training/DL-029-weight-initialization/DL-029-weight-initialization.md#42-relu-and-tanh-nothing-moves) | Weight initialisation |
| <span id="G-2146">G-2146</span> | Zero padding (image) | Adding rows and columns of 0-valued pixels around an image, so a convolution's feature map can keep the image's size. | [Note DL-043](DL/04-cnn/DL-043-padding-and-strides/DL-043-padding-and-strides.md#41-what-padding-does) | Padding and strides |
| <span id="G-2145">G-2145</span> | Zero padding (text) | Adding all-zero word vectors to shorter texts so that every text has the same length. | [Note DL-055](DL/05-rnn/DL-055-why-rnn/DL-055-why-rnn.md#52-zero-padding-wastes-most-of-the-network) | Sequence padding |
| <span id="G-2147">G-2147</span> | Zero-ablation | Setting a part's output to 0 and running the rest of the model unchanged, to see what that part does. | [Note DL-089](DL/06-transformers/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts/DL-089-mlp-stores-facts.md#71-switching-mlps-off) | MLP blocks as fact storage |
| <span id="G-2148">G-2148</span> | Zero-centred activation | An activation whose outputs average about 0 over a layer, positive and negative; it feeds the next layer roughly centred inputs, which helps training converge faster. | [Note DL-027](DL/02-training/DL-027-activation-functions/DL-027-activation-functions.md#54-zero-centred) | Activation functions |
| <span id="G-2149">G-2149</span> | Zero-frequency problem | A probability of 0 for a value never seen with a class, which forces that class's score to 0. | [Note ML-083](ML/07-classification/ML-083-naive-bayes-code/ML-083-naive-bayes-code.md#1-overview) | Naive Bayes |
| <span id="G-2150">G-2150</span> | λ (lambda), alpha | The strength of the regularisation penalty; alpha in scikit-learn. | [Note ML-062](ML/06-regression/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition/ML-062-ridge-regression-intuition.md#3-the-idea-penalise-large-coefficients) | Regularisation |
